| `client_player_logging_out` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingOut` | Before client disconnect teardown (`Minecraft.disconnect`, NeoForge's `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingOut`). Fires once per actual client disconnect before the client level, handles, or session are torn down, including no-player disconnect transitions where both `player` and `game_mode` are `None`. Does not fire on a dimension change or reconfiguration. Client-side. |
| `client_resources_reloaded` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientReloadHost` | `ClientResourceLoadFinishedEvent` | Render-thread notification after a resource-pack reload has applied successfully. Atlas, models, renderer and client caches already refer to the new resources. Selection/F3+T and server-pack reloads share this point; initial startup does not (`client_setup` is the once-only moment after the initial load). Receives a `ClientReloadHost` for published asset/data queries; world handles and transactions are not present on this context. |
| `client_setup` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientSetupContext` | `FMLClientSetupEvent` | NeoForge's `FMLClientSetupEvent`: the client has started. It fires once per game process, on the render thread, after the first client load has applied (the atlas, models and every client reload listener's published state are final) and before the title screen's first frame. Returning to the title screen and opening another world is not a new start, so it never fires again. This is the opposite of `client_resources_reloaded`, which never runs at initial startup and runs after every later reload. The listener runs on the main thread; work a mod wants done in parallel is its own. The dedicated server has no client and never fires it (see `server_about_to_start` for its `FMLDedicatedServerSetupEvent`). Receives a `ClientSetupContext` for client setup and published resource queries without world handles or transactions. |
| `client_stopped` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientStoppedEvent` | The client has finished shutting down, after `client_stopping`. Fires once on the render thread for an orderly client exit, not for a dedicated server. NeoForge's `ClientStoppedEvent`. |
| `client_stopping` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientStoppingEvent` | The client is closing: it fires once per game process, on the render thread, when the game shuts down, after the world the player was in is left and saved, while the game still holds its settings and resources, so a listener can read them through the `ClientHost` and save its own client state. It is the closing counterpart of `client_setup`. Closing the window, a quit shortcut and the process's stop signals all reach it; a crash does not. A dedicated server has no client and never fires it (`server_stopping` is its stop). Fabric's `ClientLifecycleEvents.CLIENT_STOPPING` fires earlier, before the integrated server stops, so it is not a counterpart. NeoForge has no event for this moment. A listener is a pure function of its `ClientHost` reads and its own state. |
| `client_take_item_entity` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | This client heard that `collector` picked up `collected` (vanilla's `ClientPacketListener.handleTakeItemEntity`, the `ClientboundTakeItemEntityPacket`): an item, an experience orb, an arrow or a trident, taken by this player, another player or a mob. Told before the client plays the pickup sound, starts the pickup particle and shrinks or removes `collected`, so `host` still reads it (its stack, for an item). `amount` is the packet's count taken (`getAmount`): the items taken from an item's stack, 1 otherwise. |
| `client_tick_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientTickEvent.Post` | Once every client tick, paused or not and on the title screen too, after the window's tick ran (NeoForge's `ClientTickEvent.Post`): the window's `Game` only, so not on a server's own tick (`server_tick` is that moment). Client-side. |
| `client_tick_pre` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientTickEvent.Pre` | Once every client tick, paused or not and on the title screen too, before the window's tick runs (NeoForge's `ClientTickEvent.Pre`): the window's `Game` only, so not on a server's own tick (`server_tick` is that moment). Client-side. |
| `component_hover` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | `GuiGraphicsExtractor.componentHoverEffect`: the hover of the text component under the cursor, about to be drawn, in the chat, a book or a dialog. `hover` is the component's hover action: its text, an item or an entity. `x` and `y` are the cursor in GUI pixels. Set `cancel` and the game draws nothing for it: no text tooltip, no item tooltip and no entity lines. A mod can draw its own with `draw`. Left alone, a text hover is wrapped at half the screen's width (at least 200) as vanilla wraps it, an item hover draws its stack's tooltip (which asks `item_tooltip` and the `render_tooltip_*` hooks as any item tooltip does), and an entity hover shows its name, type and UUID while advanced tooltips are on. Asked each frame a hovered component shows, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `component_types` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Register namespaced NBT component types before content loads. Call `ComponentTypesEvent::register` with each explicit `namespace:path`, such as `"mymod:charge".into()`. Invalid and duplicate IDs, including IDs already used by built-in components, are refused before the component registry freezes. |
| `compost_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player added a compostable item to the composter at `pos`. |
| `compute_camera_angles` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeCameraAngles` | Client camera angles before `Camera.setRotation`, third-person mirroring, detached back-off, and sleeping bed rotation (`ViewportEvent.ComputeCameraAngles` in `Camera.alignWithEntity`). |
| `compute_fog_color` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeFogColor` | Computed world fog RGB, after ordinary effects and before upload. Title panoramas skip it. Only finite RGB components in 0..=1 are accepted; alpha and distances stay unchanged. |
| `compute_fov` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeFov` | World field of view in degrees, after camera easing and fluid/death adjustment, before projection and Nausea spin. There is no separate engine zoom pass. Called for world rendering and near-plane overlays; hand/HUD FOV uses its own path. Only finite values in 1..=179 degrees replace the computed FOV. |
| `compute_fov_modifier` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ComputeFovModifierEvent` | NeoForge's `ComputeFovModifierEvent`: the effective camera player's FOV scale (the local player, or the player being spectated), before camera easing. `player` is that player's handle, `raw` is `AbstractClientPlayer.getFieldOfViewModifier`'s product before its final `Mth.lerp`, and `effect_scale` is the Field of View Effects setting the lerp uses. `modifier` starts as `Mth.lerp(effect_scale, 1, raw)`, the value vanilla uses; write it to change the field of view. At `effect_scale` 0 that start is 1 whatever `raw` is. The hook doesn't fire for a non-player camera, for a player with no handle (a detached spectated snapshot), or while the camera player scopes with a spyglass in first person (`AbstractClientPlayer.getFieldOfViewModifier` returns `0.1` before the event). Invalid values or values outside 0.01..=10 revert to the start. |
| `console_line` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Observe an operator-visible log line on a dedicated or integrated server. `level` is `INFO`, `WARN` or `ERROR`, `thread` names the logging thread, and `message` is the text without its timestamp, thread or level prefix. Lines from every logging thread are delivered in order on the server thread at the start of the next server tick. The tap starts on the first tick with an enabled listener; without one, no log is captured and no queue is allocated or locked in the idle path. A listener's own log calls are not tapped. The queue keeps the oldest 4096 lines and discards later ones until it drains. `dropped` reports the number discarded on the first line of that batch and is zero on all other lines. `message` keeps its first 8192 bytes and `thread` its first 128, at a character boundary. A listener cannot change or suppress the log. |
| `consume_effect` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A custom entry of the stack's effective `consumable`, in list order, interleaved with built-in effects. `stack` is the read-only snapshot before consumption shrinks it, including components. `user` is the player or mob consuming the stack. The kind's subscriber is bound when the definition is decoded. Nested applications run in call order, including another entry of the same kind. A panic in the callback disables that callback for the session; later effects still run. An unwind from delegated engine work propagates without disabling the calling consume callback. Other listeners keep their own containment policy. Register a custom kind's save codec and Wire value with `Registrar::register_consume_effect_type` before placing it in a stack that will be sent to a client. A registered kind's `data` holds the canonical fields returned by its save codec, with their exact NBT types. |
| `container_loot_filled` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Sunrise addition: no NeoForge counterpart. Fired once per container fill, each half of a double chest separately, on the rolled list in roll order before shuffle, splitting and slot placement. Add, replace or remove drops; empty stacks are dropped. Stacks over their item's maximum are trimmed at placement, after the hook; overflow that cannot fit is discarded. The opener is named only when a player's open starts the fill. Other accesses (including an `ItemPlace::EntitySlot` read, even during a player action) give None. The origin is the block's centre or the container entity's position. Already filled containers and block or mob drops do not fire this hook. Listeners run in dispatch order, each seeing the previous list, after loot_table_load's changes. The level Host provides reads only; only the drop list is writable. Non-negative damage within the signed integer range is accepted even above an item's maximum. The drop list checks each edit that supplies a stack. A refusal returns an error and warns without undoing other edits. A panic undoes only the panicking listener's edits. Listeners use their own randomness. With no listener, the roll and placement random draws remain unchanged. |
| `container_opened` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player opens one of the game's own menus (`ServerPlayer.openMenu`; the job of NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent.Open`, which comes after the menu is open and cannot stop it): `kind` is the menu type (`minecraft:generic_9x3`, `minecraft:furnace`, `minecraft:crafting`, `minecraft:merchant`, `minecraft:anvil`, `minecraft:lectern`...; a horse's inventory is `minecraft:horse_inventory`) and `pos` the block it belongs to, none for a villager, a mount or a vehicle's chest. Heard after the checks that refuse the menu (a locked container, a spectator) and before the menu that was open closes. Set `cancel` and nothing opens: the open menu stays as it was and the click is spent. The player's state is swapped in for the call, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Not heard: a menu a mod opens (`PlayerRef::open_chest_menu`, `PlayerRef::open_mod_menu`), the inventory screen, and the editors (sign, book, command block). Server-side, for every player of the world. |
| `crafter_craft` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A crafter at `pos` completed recipe `recipe`. |
| `create_fluid_source` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | `CreateFluidSourceEvent` | A fluid source at `pos` (`state`) is about to count towards turning the flowing fluid beside it into a source (NeoForge's `CreateFluidSourceEvent`, `FlowingFluid.getNewLiquid`): two counted sources beside a block over a solid block or a source make it one. Asked for each horizontal neighbour source, whenever the game works out a block's new fluid, spread checks included: answer fast. `can_convert` starts as the fluid's rule (`water_source_conversion` or `lava_source_conversion`). Server-side. |
| `create_skull_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.CreateSkullModels` | Skull models for a mod's own skull types (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.CreateSkullModels`): call `event.register(model)` with one `SkullModel` per type, giving its `kind`, model layer and default texture. A `minecraft:head` item model may then name the kind, so the head draws in hand, in a GUI and in an item frame. Called once at client startup. Any valid canonical `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:` for non-vanilla paths) is accepted; invalid identifiers, the seven built-in vanilla skull types (`minecraft:skeleton`, `minecraft:wither_skeleton`, `minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:creeper`, `minecraft:piglin`, `minecraft:dragon`), duplicate registrations, entries with a layer neither a mod nor vanilla has, or entries with an empty texture are logged and left out. API @API:create_skull_models@. |
| `create_spawn_position` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.CreateSpawnPosition` | Before a new server level selects its initial world spawn (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.CreateSpawnPosition`), after the Overworld's `level_load` and before the remaining configured levels are opened. The vanilla Debug preset uses its fixed spawn and skips this hook. |
| `creative_slot` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A creative-mode player sets a slot of their inventory from the creative screen (`ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handleSetCreativeModeSlot`): `slot` is the inventory menu's slot (5 to 8 armour, 9 to 35 the inventory, 36 to 44 the hotbar, 45 the off hand; below 0 is a stack thrown out of the screen, which is dropped) and `item` (`minecraft:air` for an emptied slot) and `count` what is put there. Heard once the packet passed vanilla's checks (a creative player, a real slot, a valid stack) and before the slot changes or the stack drops. Set `cancel` and the packet is ignored. The player's state is swapped in for the call, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Not heard: stacks a creative player gets other ways (middle-click pick block, `/give`). Server-side, for every player of the world. |
| `creeper_ignite` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player ignited a creeper with an item. |
| `creeper_power` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Lightning charged a creeper. |
| `critical_hit` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `CriticalHitEvent` | NeoForge's `CriticalHitEvent`: a player's melee hit on a living `target_kind` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:armor_stand`) that deals damage, where `Player.attack` decides whether it is critical. `vanilla_critical` is vanilla's answer; `critical` starts as it and may be forced or denied (as in vanilla a critical hit prevents the sweep, so a forced crit loses it; a denied crit loses the crit sound and particles); `damage_multiplier` starts at 1.5 for vanilla's critical and 1.0 otherwise, and scales the damage only while `critical` ends true. Not fired for a target that is no living entity (a dragon part) or when the hit does no damage. Fires on the Game that runs the attack, and on the attacking client, which only asks: once for each melee attack the local player sends on a living entity, with `vanilla_critical` from what the client knows; what a listener writes there changes no packet, no server decision and nothing drawn. |
| `crop_grow_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `CropGrowEvent.Post` | A crop-like block grew (NeoForge's `CropGrowEvent.Post`). Diverges from NeoForge: original_state is always the pre-growth state. `state` is what is at `pos` now. Sugar cane reports the new cane above it, and kelp and vines the new head, while `original_state` names the growing plant before growth. A full-age stem fires even without fruit; sugar cane fires only when adding a cane, not when only aging. Cactus and chorus report the plant's position. Server-side. |
| `crop_grow_pre` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `CropGrowEvent.Pre` | A crop-like block at `pos` is about to grow on a random tick (NeoForge's `CropGrowEvent.Pre`): crops, stems, nether wart, cocoa, sweet berries and bamboo at their own position; cactus and chorus flowers at the block above; sugar cane at its own position; kelp, weeping and twisting vines and cave vines at the block they grow into. `state` is the growing block. Leave `result` at `Default` for vanilla's own chance, or set `True` to grow now or `False` to stop it (`DO_NOT_GROW`). Vanilla's random roll is drawn either way, in vanilla's order. Server-side. |
| `custom_click_action` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CustomClickActionEvent` | NeoForge's `CustomClickActionEvent`: a server player's client sent a `custom` click action (a dialog button or a text component's click event): its id and optional payload. Vanilla only logs it; set `cancel` to skip that. Only play-phase clicks are reported: one sent during configuration (no player yet) is not. The player is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `customize_boss_event_progress` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.BossEventProgress` | One boss bar is about to be drawn (NeoForge's `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.BossEventProgress`, in `BossHealthOverlay`): `bar` is the bar, `x` and `y` come in as its top-left corner in GUI pixels (91 left of the screen's centre; 12 for the first bar), and `increment` as the spacing to the next bar's `y` (19). Move `x` and `y` to draw this bar, and its title, there; set `increment` to space the next bar, which comes in that far below where this one came in (moving `y` does not move the bars after it). Set `cancel` and this bar is not drawn; the increment is still applied, leaving room for what a mod draws in its place. Asked once a frame per bar, in the order the server added them, until the next bar would start a third of the way down the screen; not under F1. Client-side. A bar the API can't carry (a server's name text the API refuses) isn't heard and is drawn as vanilla draws it. |
| `customize_chat_overlay` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.Chat` | The in-game chat is about to be drawn (NeoForge's `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.Chat`, in `Gui.renderChat`): `x` and `y` come in as the chat's bottom-left corner in GUI pixels (0 and the GUI height minus 40, vanilla's place); move them to draw the chat elsewhere. `focused` is whether the chat screen is open, and `gui_width`/`gui_height` the scaled GUI size. Asked once a frame the chat layer draws, focused or not; not while the chat is hidden (F1 with the chat closed). Only the drawing moves: the chat screen's input box, and clicks and hovers on messages, stay where vanilla puts them, as with NeoForge. Client-side. |
| `data_map_types` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterDataMapTypesEvent` | The server is assembling its data tables, as for `data_pack_files` (a world's first open and every `/reload`): push the data map types your mod registers (NeoForge's `RegisterDataMapTypesEvent`). A type is a namespaced `id` (`namespace:path`) for a `registry`: any the game names. Its values come from the world's data packs' `data/<namespace>/data_maps/<registry folder>/<path>.json` (the registry's path for a `minecraft` registry, `<namespace>/<path>` otherwise), and from files you push through `native_data_pack_files` under the key `data_maps/<namespace>:<registry folder>/<path>`. Each file has a `values` object mapping entry IDs and `#tags` to JSON, with an optional boolean `replace` and a `remove` list. Entry conditions (`neoforge:conditions`) are evaluated before the value, including on direct objects. A malformed layer is logged and skipped as a whole; otherwise newer values override older ones. Push a typed map's `DataMap::ty` and use the key's `data` (`ItemKey::data`, `EntityTypeKey::data`...) for an owned decoded value. By name, `data_map_value` borrows a raw tag and `data_map_entries` borrows the whole slice in entry ID order. Both borrow from the host; copy values only if you need them after the borrow. Server-side, not synced to clients. |
| `data_maps_updated` | both | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `DataMapsUpdatedEvent` | The data maps of a registry were reloaded on the server or synced to the client (NeoForge's `DataMapsUpdatedEvent`): the place to build caches from the new values or post-process them. `registry` is the registry's id (`minecraft:item`, `minecraft:block`) and `cause` says how they arrived. A listener cannot cancel or change anything. |
| `data_pack_files` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AddPackFindersEvent` | The server is assembling its data tables from the world's data packs (`ReloadableServerResources`): a world is opening, or `/reload` was run, and the recipes, tags, loot and advancements are built after this. Push `(path, bytes)` pack files and they join this load. `path` is the overlay's key, `<kind>/<namespace>:<path>` without `.json`, over the kinds the tables read: `recipe/mymod:thing`, `tags/mymod:block/thing`, `loot_table/…`, `advancement/…`, `trade_set/…`, `villager_trade/…`, `dialog/…`. A file that isn't JSON is skipped with a warning. Asked once per load on both paths, so this is where a mod recomputes them. Contributed files sit below the world's own data packs: a pack's file for the same path still overrides a mod's. Server-side (the single player's integrated server included). Listener applies run first. A later reload refusal preserves the old engine generation, but does not undo callback effects. Contributions share one list, so a failed file cannot be attributed to a particular mod. |
| `datapack_sync` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `OnDatapackSyncEvent` | Collect recipe types once the joining player exists, after admission and before that player's recipes are sent. On reload, asked once per online player, at most once per player per reload, before their recipes are re-sent. With nobody online this hook is not asked. `player` always identifies the recipient. Configuration tags precede admission. Only this player's Sunrise client receives the selected recipes; vanilla clients receive nothing new. Recipes using a vanilla serializer carry the server's resolved item candidates, not the source's ingredient definitions or bound predicates. A mod serializer still writes its own recipe content. An empty answer retains the client's own recipes, or restores them after an earlier nonempty answer. |
| `default_components_bound` | both | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `DefaultDataComponentsBoundEvent` | Items' default components are bound and readable (NeoForge's `DefaultDataComponentsBoundEvent`); `cause` says how, as `tags_updated`'s does. The place to build or rebuild caches derived from item defaults (stack sizes, food, tool rules): from now on the default-component reads return the published defaults, `modify_default_components`' edits included. |
| `difficulty_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `DifficultyChangeEvent` | The server's difficulty changed, from `from` to `to` (`peaceful`, `easy`, `normal`, `hard`; NeoForge's `DifficultyChangeEvent`). It is vanilla's `MinecraftServer.setDifficulty`, which `/difficulty`, a dedicated server's management and a test environment's difficulty all go through, and it is told only when the difficulty is another one. It only hears: the change has already happened. Server-side. |
| `dragon_fight_tick` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The End's dragon fight is about to tick (`EnderDragonFight.tick`, which `ServerLevel.tick` runs before the level's entities), on the server, every tick the level ticks. `level` is the End. Set `canceled` and the fight does nothing this tick: no player scan for its boss bar, no arena ticket, no state scan, no respawn stage, no dragon found or made, no crystal count. A mod that runs a fight of its own does it here, with `LevelRef::dragon_fight_try_respawn`, `LevelRef::dragon_fight_reset_spike_crystals` and the dragon's own handle. The dragon and the crystals still tick as entities, and the pillars' crystals are still placed once with the arena, as world generation places them in vanilla. NeoForge has no such event. |
| `dragon_fight_won` | server | replace | `&dyn Host` | — | The End's dragon fight was won: its own dragon died, by its death animation or `/kill` (`EnderDragonFight.setDragonKilled`), on the server. Asked on every win, after the exit portal was activated and the next gateway placed, before the egg and before the fight is marked won. `level` is the End, `egg_pos` the top of the podium's column on the `MOTION_BLOCKING` heightmap (`LevelReader.getHeightmapPos`), where vanilla puts the egg, and `previously_won` whether a dragon of this fight was killed before. Vanilla places the egg on the first win only. |
| `dragon_respawn_stage` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The End's dragon respawn reached `stage` (`EnderDragonFight.setRespawnStage`, and `respawnDragon`'s start), on the server. `level` is the End. Heard once per stage: `DragonRespawnStage::Start` when the crystals on the exit portal start it, each later stage as the sequence moves on, and `DragonRespawnStage::End` once the new dragon flies. A respawn aborted by a destroyed portal crystal reaches no stage (`end_crystal_destroyed` hears it). NeoForge has no such event. |
| `effect_particle_modification` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `EffectParticleModificationEvent` | NeoForge's `EffectParticleModificationEvent`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.updateSynchronizedMobEffectParticles`: asked for each active effect `effect` of the living entity `entity` (a `LivingRef`, a mob or a player), the ones vanilla hides included, as the server is about to send their swirls in its synched data (`DATA_EFFECT_PARTICLES`, `DATA_EFFECT_AMBIENCE_ID`). `original_particle` is vanilla's for the effect: an `entity_effect` in its colour (alpha 38 of 255 when ambient), the omens' own particles. `particle` starts as it: replace it, or set it to `None` to go back to the original. `visible` starts as the effect's own: set it to show a hidden effect's swirl or hide a visible one. The visible effects' particles are sent in the order the effects are kept; particles that need a block state, and item, trail and vibration particles, can't be sent and are left out. A mod's own particle (`ParticleOptions::simple` with its registered `ParticleKey`) goes only to Sunrise clients: the others get the list without it. Whether the swirls are ambient (drawn fainter and rarer) follows from the answers as vanilla's `areAllEffectsAmbient` does: a visible effect that is not ambient makes it false, an effect a mod made visible counts as visible and one it hid doesn't, and it is true when none is visible. Asked when the entity's effects change (one is added, removed, or changes its amplifier, visibility or ambience), as vanilla recomputes them only then, and only while a mod listens; the answers are sent until the next change. The window's own player draws its swirls from its own effects, not from these answers. Server-side. |
| `emissive` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | — | Register emissive overlay sprite pairs (`EmissiveSpriteRegistration`) at client startup and on every resource reload, before terrain baking finishes. Call `event.register(pair)` with typed base and overlay `Identifier`s; per-pixel alpha in the overlay marks full-bright material over the base sprite without illuminating surrounding blocks or changing gameplay light. Resolved pairs stay bound to the loaded render-asset generation until the next reload and share the GPU emissive uniform table with OptiFine overlays. |
| `enchant_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player applied an enchantment at `pos`; `item` is the finished item and `levels` the levels charged. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `enchanted_block_loot` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | `EnchantedBlockLootEvent` | NeoForge's `EnchantedBlockLootEvent`: a block's loot table is reading the level of `enchantment` on `tool` to decide what `state` at `pos` drops, in `ApplyBonusCount.run` or `BonusLevelTableCondition.test`. `level` starts as the tool's level, after `get_enchantment_level`; what is left in it is what that one count or condition uses, and the tool keeps its own. A `u32` can't be negative, the case `setEnchantmentLevel` refuses; one above 1024 counts as 1024, as vanilla's levels stop at 255 and a level sizes a formula's loop. `tool` is none for an empty hand or a break with no tool, and the read is still asked, at level 0. `entity` is `LootContextParams.THIS_ENTITY`: the player mining, the mob breaking the block, the blast's direct source (primed TNT, TNT cart, exploding mob, fireball, wither skull, end crystal), the enderman whose carried block drops, the source of `/loot ... mine`, the projectile that broke a chorus flower or a decorated pot, the villager harvesting, the ravager walking through a crop, or none for a break with no entity (a neighbour update, a piston, water, a trident through a speleothem). A boat through a lily pad also gives none, though vanilla names it. Asked once per read, so a table may ask several times per break, or not at all, and the table's draws stay in vanilla's order. It runs inside the roll, so it gets the read-only level host: no writes, no draws, and `entity` is a handle a listener can keep and compare but not read until it has a `Host`. Authoritative servers only. Use `get_enchantment_level` when the block doesn't matter. Tick-time effect: one `listened` test per roll when no mod listens. |
| `enchanted_entity_loot` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EnchantedEntityLootEvent` | An entity's loot table reads an enchantment's level (NeoForge's `EnchantedEntityLootEvent`): asked in the middle of the roll, each time an `enchanted_count_increase` function or a `random_chance_with_enchanted_bonus` condition is evaluated, for a death, for `/loot ... kill` and for `EntityRef::roll_death_loot`. `entity` is the living entity dropping the loot, `source` the killing blow (`minecraft:magic` for the command, the mod's source for a roll) and `attacker` the attacking entity (`getAttacker`): the one that caused the blow, or the command's source, even when `source` names nobody. It is None without one, and it need not be living: such a read is asked too, from a level of 0. `level` comes in as the living attacker's level of `enchantment`; what a mod leaves in it is what the table uses, so a level raised from 0 counts even with no attacker. A level can't be negative, and one above 1024 counts as 1024: vanilla's levels stop at 255, and a table's count grows with the level. Mods that don't need the entities prefer `get_enchantment_level`; a mob's equipment drop chance isn't this hook's (`get_equipment_drop_chance`). Not asked for a `/loot ... kill` of an entity that isn't living, nor for a table rolled any other way. The draws a mod makes from the level's random stay in order with the roll's. Server-side. |
| `enchantment_level_set` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EnchantmentLevelSetEvent` | An enchanting table at `pos` drew the level of one offer (`EnchantmentMenu.slotsChanged`, after `getEnchantmentCost`): `offer` holds the server player whose table it is, the row (0 to 2), the power (bookshelves counted), the item and the level drawn. Change `level` to change the offer; a level below `row + 1` offers nothing, as vanilla's. Asked for each row in order, before `prepare_item_enchant`. Server-side. |
| `end_crystal_destroyed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An End crystal of the End's dragon fight was destroyed, by damage or `/kill` (`EnderDragonFight.onCrystalDestroyed`), on the server, after the fight answered it. `level` is the End and `pos` the crystal's block. `respawn_crystal` is true for one of the crystals driving a respawn: the respawn was aborted, the pillars' crystals lost their protection and the exit portal is active again. Otherwise the fight counted the pillars' crystals again and its dragon, when it lives, reacted. NeoForge has no such event. |
| `ender_entity_teleport` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | `EntityTeleportEvent.EnderEntity` | An enderman or a shulker is about to teleport on its own (`EntityTeleportEvent.EnderEntity`). A mod may move `target` or cancel. It is given the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity (its registries, settings and mod list); it fires inside the tick's own AI step, before the teleport's own random draws, so it takes no `Host`. |
| `ender_pearl_teleport` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.EnderPearl` | A server player's or mob's ender pearl is about to teleport its `owner` (`EntityTeleportEvent.EnderPearl`, `ThrownEnderpearl.onHit`). `owner` is the live entity handle, `pearl` its projectile id, `from` the owner's feet and `target` the pearl's previous position. A mod may move the target; non-finite changes keep the original spot. Canceling leaves the owner in place with no teleport sound, damage or endermite roll; the pearl is used up. Player owners keep their acting-player context and mutable fall `damage` (5; nonpositive causes no health loss, but zero remains a hit attempt that asks the invulnerability predicate). Mob owners take no fall damage and make no endermite draw; their `damage` starts at zero and is player-only. An owner moved, removed, killed or put to sleep by a listener is not moved again using the old decision. The impact hook still hears the hit. What the pearl hit (`getHitResult`), in `projectile_impact`'s shape: the entity `hit_entity` (a dragon part is the dragon), or, with it None, the block `hit_block` on its `hit_face` (the face's normal); `hit_at` is the hit point. For an entity, `hit_block` is the cell of `hit_at` and `hit_face` is `[0, 0, 0]`. |
| `enderman_anger` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `EndermanAngerEvent` | `player` stares at `enderman` (NeoForge's `EndermanAngerEvent`, at the end of `Enderman.isBeingStaredBy`): the look is within the enderman's cone, in line of sight, and no carved pumpkin hides the face. Asked each time the enderman's goals check the stare: to get angry, to freeze while looked at and to teleport away up close. Set `cancel` and that stare doesn't count. Server-side. |
| `entities_load` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Entities from `chunk` finished loading into the server world. `count` is the number of saved entity records read. This is an entity-storage notice; the terrain need not be loaded for the same column. |
| `entities_unload` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Entities in `chunk` left the active server world and were queued for saving. `count` is the number of entity records. An idle column sweep waits for its writes: a refused record leaves the column and its entities resident instead of sending their unload notices. |
| `entity_break_door` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob broke the door at `pos`. |
| `entity_breed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Two mobs completed a breeding action. `child` is absent when vanilla lays an egg instead of creating a baby. |
| `entity_change_block` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob changed a block at `pos`, after the new state was applied. |
| `entity_change_block_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An entity changes the block at `pos` from `from` to `to`, before its neighbour updates. `player` is the player whose landing changed it; `entity` and `kind` identify a mob for a mob's block-changing action. | `entity_constructing` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `EntityEvent.EntityConstructing` | A new entity is being made (NeoForge's `EntityEvent.EntityConstructing`, vanilla's `Entity.<init>`): `kind` is its type (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player` for a player) and `holder` holds its attachments. The entity may not be in the level yet, so read nothing else of it. Nothing can be cancelled. |
| `entity_drop_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob is about to drop `count` of `item` at `pos` (Bukkit's `EntityDropItemEvent`). |
| `entity_enter_block` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A bee entered the hive at `pos` and was stored there. |
| `entity_enter_love_mode` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob entered love mode after player interaction. `player` is the feeder when known. |
| `entity_entering_section` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `EntityEvent.EnteringSection` | `entity` moved from the 16x16x16 section `old_section` into `new_section` (NeoForge's `EntityEvent.EnteringSection`, vanilla's `EntitySectionStorage` callbacks on `Entity.setPosRaw`). Both are section coordinates (`SectionPos`: each block coordinate shifted right by 4), and `chunk_changed` is NeoForge's `didChunkChange`: the x or z section differs, so the entity is in another chunk column. `host` is the side that saw the move. | `entity_interact` | both | replace | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.EntityInteract` | A player right-clicks the entity `entity` (`Entity.getId`) of type `kind` (`minecraft:cow`) with `hand` (`Hand::Main` or `Hand::Off`), holding `stack` (none in an empty hand): where `Player.interactOn` starts, before the entity's own `interact` and the item's `interactLivingEntity`. Both logical sides answer independently, once per hand, main hand before off hand. A consuming client result ends the loop; only Success swings there. SuccessServer and Consume consume without a client swing. Fail, Pass and TryWithEmptyHand fall through on this entity path. A server-only answer leaves the client's off-hand prediction unchanged. ClientHost permits reads and local effects, not authoritative writes. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `entity_interact_specific` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.EntityInteractSpecific` | a right-click on an entity with the point clicked: `entity_interact_specific` hears the entity, its type, the held stack and the point clicked, measured from the entity's position, before `native_interact_entity` (NeoForge's `PlayerInteractEvent.EntityInteractSpecific` counterpart); a claim swings the hand and ends the click. | `entity_invulnerability_check` | both | listen | `LevelSide` | `EntityInvulnerabilityCheckEvent` | Decide whether an entity is invulnerable to this specific damage source (`Entity.isInvulnerableTo`). `original_invulnerability` is the vanilla answer and `invulnerable` starts with that value. This fires once per player or mob hit that enters the invulnerability predicate, including creative/PvP, death, fire-resistance, shield and cooldown refusals. No amount is supplied: vanilla's predicate takes a source, not an amount. Amount mutation remains on `living_incoming_damage`, before cooldown. | `entity_join_level` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityJoinLevelEvent` | An entity joins the server level `dimension` (NeoForge's `EntityJoinLevelEvent`): vanilla's `PersistentEntitySectionManager.addEntity`. `loaded_from_disk` is true for an entity coming back from saved entity storage, false for one added fresh (`addFreshEntity`: a drop, an orb, `/summon`, an arrival through a portal). `subject` is the joining entity's own handle (NeoForge's `getEntity`) and `kind` its type (`minecraft:item`). It is already in the level's lists, but `Host::entity` finds it only in an accessible section: `subject` reads it, and the listener may veto it, before it is admitted, even into a hidden section; discovery through the host is unchanged. Set `canceled` to keep it out: it is taken back out as if never added, and a `/summon` of it fails. Tells every kind in the game's entity lists (items, orbs, mobs, boats, minecarts, armor stands, primed TNT, falling blocks, projectiles, rockets, tridents, end crystals, the dragon, cushions, paintings, item frames, block/item/text displays, markers and interactions), once each; check `kind`. A drop, an orb, a mob, a `/summon` and a chunk's entities are told at once. Arrows and projectiles in the same spawn list (including thrown potions and fireballs) are told at spawn with their owner already set. Fresh falling blocks are also told at spawn, before the next scheduled block tick or before `LevelRef::spawn_falling_block` returns. A natural fall's source block replacement is not undone by a veto. Other spawns are batched in insertion order between tick phases and a veto removes them before they tick again. Inside a transaction (`Host::transaction`) opened in the entity's own level the join is told at that transaction's root commit, not at the spawn call (an entity spawned in another level, as by a command run `in` it, is told at once): before the commit the entity exists and `entity_constructing` has run, but no one is told it joined, and a listener reads the entity when the commit runs. An aborted transaction tells nothing, and the entity it spawned is gone. A join cancelled at the commit leaves the spawn's handle stale. Joins at one commit come in the order the entities were added, before the removals the transaction asked for take effect. Join notifications in the same level requested while a commit is announcing its joins (from a direct spawn or a committed transaction in that level) are delivered after that announcing batch, in request order. Live fallback-store entities also notify: displays, markers, interactions, and summoned or chunk-restored eyes of ender and explicit leash knots. Gameplay-thrown eyes and naturally tied leash knots live in separate lists and do not notify yet. Saved data kept for a missing mod is not an entity in the level and does not notify. Server-side. |
| `entity_leave_level` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityLeaveLevelEvent` | An entity leaves the server level `dimension` (NeoForge's `EntityLeaveLevelEvent`): vanilla's `onTrackingEnd`, when it is removed for any reason (picked up, merged, despawned, burnt, blown up, killed, taken by a hopper or a mob, gone through a portal) or its chunk unloads, a level closing with nobody in it included. Not for the entities still loaded when the server stops, as in vanilla. `entity` is its ID and `kind` its type (`minecraft:item`); it is no longer in the level's lists. It only hears. It tells every kind `entity_join_level` does (`kind` is the type), each once, when the game next notices it gone (the tick or action that removed it). Inside a transaction (`Host::transaction`) opened in the entity's own level an entity's removal takes effect, and this hook fires, at that transaction's root commit, not at the removal call (an entity in another level, as by a command run `in` it, leaves at once): from the call on its handle and selectors no longer find it. An aborted transaction removes nothing and tells nothing. An entity the transaction spawned and removed is never told to join, so it never leaves. Check `kind`. Server-side. |
| `entity_mob_griefing` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `EntityMobGriefingEvent` | May `entity` change the world? (NeoForge's `EntityMobGriefingEvent`, `EventHooks.canEntityGrief`.) `can_grief` starts as the `mob_griefing` game rule; set it either way. Asked for a mob explosion (`ExplosionInteraction.MOB`, for the creeper, wither, ghast fireball or wither skull), a mob picking up items, an enderman taking or placing a block, a sheep eating grass, a mob trampling farmland or turtle eggs, a ravager breaking leaves or crops, a wither breaking blocks or leaving a wither rose, a silverfish leaving or entering stone, a snow golem's snow, a fox's berries, a rabbit's carrots, a villager's farming, an evoker's sheep, a zombie trampling eggs, an allay's pickup, and a mob's arrow or small fireball changing a block (asked for its owner), a zombie breaking a door, the dragon breaking blocks, a ravager breaking pitcher crops, a burning mob melting powder snow, a weaving mob's cobwebs, and decorations and items in explosions (asked for the exploding entity, and for the mob that causes the blast). Asked often: answer fast. Server-side. |
| `entity_mount` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityMountEvent` | An entity mounts or dismounts a vehicle (NeoForge's `EntityMountEvent`, posted from `Entity.startRiding` and `removeVehicle`): `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`) in the server level `dimension`, getting on (`mounting`) or off the `vehicle` of type `vehicle_kind` (`minecraft:oak_boat`, `minecraft:minecart`, `minecraft:horse`, `minecraft:cushion`). Set `canceled` and it does not: a player stays where they were, a mob does not board, a `/ride` fails, and a rider whose dismount is canceled keeps riding. Players hear boarding and dismounts by input, `/ride`, hits, bucking, teleport or spectator mode; mobs hear boarding and dismounts (jockeys, ravager raiders, `/ride`, hits), including removal cleanup. The departing rider's own dismount is asked first, then its direct passengers in passenger-list order. The removed vehicle is no longer a valid handle or query result during these callbacks. A Mob's canceled dismount keeps the relationship with a removed boat, cart or mob; each later dismount asks again. A resident Mob retries immediately before its next eligible entity tick: its chunk must be entity-ticking and its tick must not have been canceled. A server player retries on its next vehicle tick. Either can be later in the same world tick. A rejected, nonresident passenger does not autonomously tick or retry. A mob riding a player is compulsorily ejected on save, logout, death or dimension change: the dismount notifies this hook, but cancellation cannot keep the mob aboard. Loading a save does not notify. A mob that boards a boat by touching it is checked again after the hook returns without canceling: the boat is found again by id (gone: the contact pass for that boat ends), its passengers are counted again, and the mob must still exist, not be removed and not already ride; a full boat or an unfit mob skips that mob. A player who sits on a cushion has the cushion found again by id after the board and dismount checks: gone, the click is spent and the player is not seated. Server-side. |
| `entity_move` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | One physical `Entity.move` invocation (#2414, design #2308, the maintainer ruling of the same issue): the entity `entity` of `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`) is about to move by `delta` (blocks this tick) for `cause`'s sake - itself, the player behind a position packet, a piston, a shulker box lid, a shulker mob's opening lid, a moving collider carrying or shoving the body. It fires before the move's own handling, no-physics and the stuck multiplier included, in the one gate every kind's physics resolves through, so a forgotten family cannot move around it. Change `delta` to replace the movement (still through every ordinary restriction) or set `event.cancelled` or return `Flow::Cancel` to skip it: no position, velocity, collision-flag or fall change, and no displacement notice. Replacement components must be finite; there is no additional per-axis limit. A non-finite replacement rejects that listener's delta and veto together and logs the mod and field. An unchanged game delta is not revalidated. An earlier accepted veto cannot be cleared. If a listener panics or incurs a phase refusal, its edits and cancel flag are discarded; earlier results stand and later eligible listeners still run. Only a panic disables this mod's callback for the session. It takes no Host - the maintainer ruling, like the enderman's teleport: the listener only decides the delta, and its `context` is the loop `context` of the side that ticks the body. The hook fires only on the server. The client predicts ordinary collider displacement for its own player, not a listener's replacement. The server remains authoritative. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. |
| `entity_outline` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Whether an entity the client draws has the glowing outline, and in which colour (`Entity.isCurrentlyGlowing` and `getTeamColor`, what Fabric's and NeoForge's glowing and team colour overrides change). `outline` comes in as vanilla's answer: `Some(0xRRGGBB)`, the team's colour or white, when the entity glows, None when it doesn't. Set `Some` to make any entity glow in that colour, None to take its outline away. It changes only what this client draws: the entity's `Glowing` flag, what the server and other players see, stays. Asked each frame for every mob, player (yourself in third person when you hold the camera), dropped item and projectile drawn. In the ordered body pass, these queries for mobs, players and dropped items follow level join order, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions. Projectile queries run in a later pass. General entities, the dragon and end crystals add no outline queries. API @API:entity_outline@. |
| `entity_place` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BlockEvent.EntityPlaceEvent, BlockEvent.EntityMultiPlaceEvent` | `BlockEvent.EntityPlaceEvent` (and `EntityMultiPlaceEvent`, which extends it): `placer` places `state` at `pos` against `placed_against`. For `BlockItem.place` and mob placement (an enderman's carried block or a snow golem's trail), the hook fires before the block write and before an item stack shrinks; `None` is reserved for a placement with no entity. `placement` is `Single`, or `Multi` with every block the placement writes (a bed's foot and head, a door's halves, a tall plant), where `state` and `pos` stay the primary block (the foot, the lower half). For players, `placed_against` is the state adjacent to the primary placement position opposite the clicked face, including when the clicked block is replaceable. A failed listener's journaled writes are discarded; a panic does not veto the placement or stop later listeners. |

| `entity_pose_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest player's synchronized pose changed from `from` to `to`. |
| `entity_size` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `EntityEvent.Size` | An entity's dimensions are being resolved for `pose` (NeoForge's `EntityEvent.Size`, fired from vanilla's `Entity.refreshDimensions` and from the entity's constructor): it is new, its pose changed to `pose`, it grew up, its `minecraft:scale` changed, or another value its vanilla size follows changed; or as a player's pre-motion candidate (version 2, below). `size` (width, height and eye height, in blocks) starts as vanilla's new size and `old_size` is the size it had. | `entity_tick_pre` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `EntityTickEvent.Pre` | The entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:item`) is about to tick in the level `dimension` (NeoForge's `EntityTickEvent.Pre`, posted right before each entity's tick in `tickNonPassenger`). Cancel to leave the vanilla overlay out, except that a selected fire renderer still decides whether its vanilla quads follow it. Use `ExtractBlockScreenEffectEvent::set_custom_renderer` to prepend or replace the quads. A failed listener leaves the previous choice and cancellation intact so later eligible listeners still run. A panic disables that mod's listener for the session and is reported in the log and the client error toast. `partial_tick` is world interpolation; `player_partial_tick` is local-player interpolation, which continues when the level is frozen. Client-side. |
| `farmland_trample` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BlockEvent.FarmlandTrampleEvent` | A player or mob that fell onto farmland is about to turn it to its base block (`BlockEvent.FarmlandTrampleEvent`, from `FarmlandBlock.fallOn`). `entity` is the landing living entity: read its pose, sneak state and type through the handle. It fires after vanilla's own chance and checks (the draw, the mob-griefing rule or spawn protection, and the size test), so a listener only ever sees a trample that was going to happen. Cancel and the farmland and its crop stay as they are; the entity takes its fall damage either way. |
| `fill_bucket` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player fills an empty bucket (`stack`) at `pos`: `BucketItem.use`'s pickup branch, once `Level.mayInteract` allows it and before `BucketPickup.pickupBlock`. Set `canceled` and the bucket does nothing. Emptying a bucket is `bucket_empty`'s moment, not this one. Server-side, for every player of the world (`host.players().acting_player()` says who). |
| `finalize_spawn` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `FinalizeSpawnEvent` | NeoForge's `FinalizeSpawnEvent`: the unspawned mob of type `kind` is at `pos`. Where vanilla calls `Mob.finalizeSpawn` (equipment, attributes, variants and class setup), this event precedes it. Monster spawner blocks, spawner minecarts and trial spawners also post this event for configured NBT; they lend the loaded mob and still skip vanilla finalization for those attempts. `spawner` is `Spawner::Block` for a monster spawner block (`SpawnerBlockEntity`), `Spawner::Trial` for a trial spawner block (`TrialSpawnerBlockEntity`), `Spawner::Minecart` for a spawner minecart (`MinecartSpawner`), or `None` otherwise (`FinalizeSpawnEvent.getSpawner`). A minecart handle reads the current cart state through the supplied read-only host. `difficulty` is the local difficulty finalization reads: change it to change what the mob gets. There are two cancels. Return `Flow::Cancel` to skip finalization: no finalizeSpawn runs, and the mob still spawns, without vanilla finalization (configured data and the spawner’s separate equipment table are not finalization). Set `spawn_cancelled` to stop the spawn: no mob is added; on spawners, vanilla finalization (when unconfigured and not skipped with `Flow::Cancel`), trial persistence and any configured equipment table roll still run before `spawn_cancelled` vetoes admission (matching NeoForge's `EventHooks.finalizeMobSpawnSpawner`, `BaseSpawner.serverTick`, `TrialSpawner.spawnMob` and `NeoForgeEventHandler.builtinMobSpawnBlocker`), while outside spawners finalization does not run. It draws nothing itself; skipped finalization skips its draws, and with no listener or an untouched event the draws are vanilla's. Outside spawners, fired only where vanilla 26.3 calls `finalizeSpawn` on a mob the game makes, with vanilla's `reason` at that call: natural spawning, chunk generation's animals, spawners, trial spawners, patrols, raids and sieges, zombie reinforcements, a cat spawner's cat, phantoms, jockeys, `/summon` and `/raid`, spawn eggs and dispensers, the nether portal's piglin (`Structure`), a skeleton trap's horse and riders, an evoker's vexes and wandering traders. A conversion fires with `Conversion`, and a cancel there leaves the converted mob as it is. Not fired where vanilla creates the mob without `finalizeSpawn`: breeding and a baby from a spawn egg, an infested block's or effect's silverfish, a thrown egg's chick, a thrown pearl's endermite, a hatching egg's turtle, sniffer or tadpole, a copper golem built from blocks, a slime from the oozing effect and a lightning trap's horse. Nor for entities loaded from disk or structure mobs placed by world generation. `mob` is a scoped `UnspawnedMobEditor` borrowing the mob itself, not yet in a level: read it directly (`event.mob.kind()`, `event.mob.equipment(slot)`, `event.mob.attribute_base(id)`) and change it directly (`event.mob.set_equipment(slot, item)`, `event.mob.set_attribute_base(id, base)`, tags and the custom name) without passing `host`. The loan lasts only for this hook call; the ender dragon (kept apart from mobs) answers its `kind` and `position` and `Unsupported` for the rest. Listeners run before finalization: finalization's own rolls (equipment, attribute bonuses, variants) still run after them and overwrite what a listener put in the same slot or attribute; `Flow::Cancel` keeps every edit, since no finalization follows. A mob finalization itself makes (a chicken jockey's chicken, a zombie horse's rider) gets its own stack loan. `group` is the pack's spawn group data (`SpawnGroupData`, `SpawnGroup`): what the listener leaves there is what finalization reads for this mob. Chunk generation threads it through the pack; live spawning does not yet thread group data through its packs. The host is the read-only one, and `event.mob` changes nothing but this mob: listeners run on replayed ticks, so they must be pure functions of their inputs and edit only `mob` and `group`. Server-side. |
| `firework_explode` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A firework rocket completed its explosion effects. |
| `fishing_medium` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | A fishing bobber `bobber` at `position` in `level` is flying or floating outside water (`FishingHook.tick`'s fluid test found none), and a mod may say it floats and fishes in a medium there: lava, the void under an island, a cloud. `owner` is the player who cast it and `owner_position` where they stand (their feet); a client may know neither for another player's bobber. Answer a `FishingMedium` to make the spot a medium, or None to leave vanilla's (the bobber falls). The first mod to answer, in dependency order, wins. |

| `fluid_flow` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The current of the fluid block at `pos` (`FlowingFluid.getFlow`, the vector that pushes an entity standing in it) is about to push a player, mob, item, experience orb, armor stand or arrow. `fluid` is the fluid's id (`minecraft:water`, `minecraft:flowing_lava`...); a mod may change `flow`, so it can rotate, strengthen or still a current. It runs for each fluid block the entity touches, so keep it short. The vector the renderer reads to turn a fluid's texture is not changed. Fires on both sides, on the client's replayed ticks too, with the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity (its registries, settings and mod list): register the mod on both, and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs so prediction and the server agree. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |

| `fluid_passage` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | May `fluid` flow from the cell `from` (`from_state`) one step toward `direction`, into the cell `to` (`to_state`)? Vanilla's `FlowingFluid.canPassThrough` family has no event; this is asked where Sunrise checks `canMaybePassThrough`: the downward step of `spread`, the side choice of `getSpread` and every step of the slope search (`getSlopeDistance`), so closing a side changes which sides the fluid picks, not only whether one cell fills. `fluid` is `water`, `lava` or a mod fluid's id; `direction` says which step is asked, and a listener may answer only for the horizontal ones. |

| `fluid_tooltip` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `FluidTooltipEvent` | `FluidTooltipEvent`: the tooltip of `stack`, a fluid the game draws a slot of (the recipe viewer's fluid wells), once its name, its amount in millibuckets and, with F3+H, its id are in `lines`, each with a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own). | `food_level_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The food level of the server player `player` (`name`) is about to change from `from` to `to` (Bukkit's `FoodLevelChangeEvent`): eating, hunger and saturation effects, the exhaustion that costs a point, starving. | `framerate_limit` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The frame rate this window is about to be held to: Max Framerate after the inactivity throttle and Battery Mode (260 is unlimited). `focused` is whether the window has focus, `minimized` whether it is minimised and `in_world` whether a world is open. | `furnace_start_smelt` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A furnace at `pos` began cooking `item` (its cooking timer advanced from zero). Server-side. |

A cancelled use with true requests a local swing only: neither a use packet nor a swing packet is sent, so observers see no swing.
