| `item_consumption_teleport` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.ItemConsumption` | A server player or fox that ate chorus fruit (or another item with the `teleport_randomly` consume effect) is about to try a random spot (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent.ItemConsumption`, vanilla's `TeleportRandomlyConsumeEffect.apply`): once per attempt (up to 16), after they stopped riding. `from` is their feet, `target` the spot drawn; a mod may move it (its y kept inside the level's height, a value that is not a number ignored), and the game still looks down from it for ground it may land on. Canceling ends the effect: no teleport and no more attempts. `consumer` names the living actor through its handle. `consumed` is the stack captured before consumption, including its count and components; it is not reread from the hand after listener writes. A player's state is swapped in (`host.players().acting_player()`). A fox's callback has the full server host before its living AI/movement step and before the next mob ticks. Listener writes survive; changing the consumer's item, position or eligibility refuses the captured effect instead of overwriting that write. Cancellation still finishes consumption. A consumed stack the API can't carry (a saved item whose id isn't registered) isn't heard: the listeners aren't called for that consumption and the teleport goes on as vanilla's. |
| `item_crafted` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.ItemCraftedEvent` | A player took a crafting grid's result (`ResultSlot.onTake`, NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.ItemCraftedEvent`): `player` is the player's uuid, `recipe` the recipe the grid matched (`minecraft:oak_planks`) and `stack` the result it took, with its components. `inputs` is the grid row by row as it was before this take used it (4 cells for an inventory's grid, 9 for a crafting table's; `None` for an empty cell): a shift-click that crafts several times is heard once per craft, each with the grid before that craft. It only hears: the take has already happened. It fires for the crafting grid of an inventory or a crafting table, on the integrated server and a dedicated server, guests included, and not for a stonecutter's, a smithing table's or a furnace's result. Server-side. |
| `item_expire` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemExpireEvent` | A dropped item reaches its lifespan on the server (`ItemEntity.tick`, NeoForge's `EventHooks.onItemExpire` / `ItemExpireEvent`), before removal. Every mod may set or add to `extra_life`, in ticks, which starts at zero, and may query or mutate the live item entity through `event.item` (including lending its stack with `event.item.with_stack(host, ...)` and reading entity state via `event.item.entity()`). This is an expiry attempt, not a removal notice: it runs even when a listener extends the item's life. The item's `lifespan` (default 6000) gets `extra_life` by signed 32-bit wrapping addition, then is clamped to `0..=32766` (`Short.MAX_VALUE - 1`) without rewinding `age`. If `age >= lifespan`, the item is discarded and `entity_leave_level` fires before the next drop ticks; otherwise this hook fires again at the updated lifespan. Extensions through this event cannot exceed a total lifespan of 32766 ticks; use unlimited or extended lifetime (`ItemDrop::UNLIMITED_LIFETIME`) for longer lifetimes. |
| `item_fished` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemFishedEvent` | A server player `player` (UUID) and `player_entity_id` reels in a catch: `ItemFishedEvent` in `FishingHook.retrieve`, after fishing loot and its modifiers roll. `fished` holds the bobber's id and position; listeners may change `drops` and set absolute non-negative `rod_damage` (`damageRodBy`): vanilla uses 1, or 2 with the bobber on the ground. After `player_fish`'s reel-in, before rewards. Server-side. The final list reaches `fishing_rod_hooked`, then each stack spawns its item, one experience orb, and its fish statistic if it belongs to `#minecraft:fishes`. Empty stacks are removed. A cancel removes the bobber and skips the trigger, items, experience and statistic; the rod still takes `rod_damage` last. An emptied list without cancel gives no items, experience or statistic; the trigger still fires with an empty list and the rod is still damaged. An invalid `drops` write (a stack the game refuses, such as a count over the item's maximum) discards the whole event's write-back, every listener's `cancel` and `rod_damage` included, and logs a warning naming the hook and the conversion error. Diverges: Sunrise lets a listener change the drop list itself; the reference lets a listener change the stacks but not the list. |
| `item_model_property` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RegisterConditionalItemModelPropertyEvent` | Client-side evaluation of a mod's namespaced conditional item model property. Set value only for a property your mod owns; unhandled properties stay false. The owned stack snapshot is the one being resolved, including nested bundle items. `bundle_selected` is the runtime bundle selection index; -1 means none. |
| `item_picked_up` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemEntityPickupEvent.Pre` | `player` touches the ground item entity `item` (`ItemEntity.playerTouch`), before its pickup delay and target are checked: it is asked on every tick the two touch, even while the item can't be taken. `Default` requires a live delay of exactly zero and no target or this player as the target. Set `True` to take it despite its delay or another player's target (a full inventory still leaves it), or `False` to veto this pickup attempt without changing the item's pickup delay (matching NeoForge `ItemEntityPickupEvent.Pre`). The last mod to set it decides. The handle reads and mutates the live ground item entity on both the host player and guest paths. Pickup inserts from the stack captured before Pre, without overwriting a replacement installed by the listener. Server-side. |
| `item_picked_up_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemEntityPickupEvent.Post` | `player` picked up all or part of the stack of the ground item entity `item` (`ItemEntity.playerTouch`, right after `Inventory.add` took some of it), the entity `item_picked_up` named. `original` is a copy of the stack as it lay before the pickup: changing it does nothing. `remaining` is the part of the captured `original` that did not fit, or `None` if it all fit. If Pre replaced the live stack, that replacement remains on `item` unless a Post listener changes `remaining`. The amount taken is `original`'s count less `remaining`'s. A mod that removes the item entity meanwhile keeps it removed: no take animation, the stat and criteria still run. Not called when `item_picked_up` refused the pickup or the inventory took nothing; a forced `True` from `item_picked_up` changes none of this. A creative player always takes the whole stack. Server-side. |
| `item_smelted` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerEvent.ItemSmeltedEvent` | `FurnaceResultSlot.checkTakeAchievements` ran at a furnace, blast furnace or smoker's result slot with a nonzero `removeCount` (NeoForge `PlayerEvent.ItemSmeltedEvent`): `player` is the live player handle, `stack` lends the stack vanilla passed there (`getSmelting`), and `amount_removed` is `removeCount` (`getAmountRemoved`). On the server it fires with `HostSide::Server` after the furnace awards its used recipes and pops experience (`AbstractFurnaceBlockEntity.awardUsedRecipesAndPopExperience`); on the client it fires with `HostSide::Client` during predicted menu clicks without awarding experience. A pickup or throw passes the taken stack and its count; a shift-click fires once with the pre-move stack and the count moved (`onQuickCraft`); a hotbar swap has `removeCount == 0` so the server awards experience without firing `item_smelted`. Both logical sides. |
| `item_spawn` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A dropped item entity was successfully added to the server level. |
| `item_stacked_on_other` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` | NeoForge's `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` and vanilla's `Item.overrideStackedOnOther` / `Item.overrideOtherStackedOnMe`, fired inside a resolved pickup click (a direct left/right `Pickup` when no drag is active, or a one-slot left/right `QuickCraft` drag release; never on multi-slot or middle-button drags, active-drag resets, or creative catalogue slots). `click` lends the cursor and the clicked slot for this call: read them with `carried` and `clicked`, change them with `set_carried`, `set_clicked` and `take_clicked`. Reads see what earlier phases and this call changed. |
| `item_tooltip` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ItemTooltipEvent` | `ItemTooltipEvent`: the whole tooltip of `stack` (`ItemStack.getTooltipLines`), once its name, the item's own lines, the components' lines, the advanced lines and the operator warning are in `lines`, each with a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own). Add, change, reorder or remove lines. `player` and `player_entity_id` are the local player's, `stack` borrows the stack lazily (`ItemStackView::project` copies components on demand), `display` borrows the stack's `minecraft:tooltip_display`, `advanced` (F3+H) and `creative` (a creative tab's own slot) are its `TooltipFlag`, and `lines` mutates the live tooltip directly with per-edit `TextFormError` validation so one invalid edit leaves all other lines intact. Asked when item tooltip lines are built, for the game's display or `ItemResource::tooltip`. Never asked for a hidden tooltip, a crossbow's loaded projectile or a query made during item tooltip line construction. Queries outside line construction can dispatch this event, but never gather images or draw. Client-side. This global event is distinct from `ItemPart::tooltip`, which runs at the earlier `Item.appendHoverText` position, before component lines. Parts' lines therefore come first; this event sees the finished list including them, for any item, even one with no attached parts. Replacements are used in memory; they are not subject to serialized component limits. |
| `item_toss` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemTossEvent` | `ItemTossEvent`: `player` tosses the item entity `item` (`Player.drop`: the drop key, a menu's throw, what no longer fits in the inventory), not what scatters when they die. The entity is made, with its thrower, but has not joined the level yet: its handle reads and changes the whole stack, the pickup delay and the target, and those changes stay. Set `canceled` and it never joins the level; the items are already out of the inventory and are lost. A canceled toss awards no drop statistic, as vanilla awards one only when an item entity came of it. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: replaces the listen-only `item_dropped`, which fired for the drop key and menu throws only. |
| `items` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Declare items in code once at startup on each side, before resources load. Pass each `mod_api::Item` to `event.register`. The declaration belongs to the submitting package — in its own namespaces or in a content package it serves (`served_by`) — and works like `data/<id>/sunrise/item/<path>.json` in a content mod. A block's item is declared with its block, not here. `crafting_remainder` may reference items across the host and its served packages; duplicates across layers and declarations into a `side = "server"` package's namespace are refused. `register` returns a typed `DeclarationError` for invalid IDs or fields. The game records the refusal and stops startup even if a returning listener ignores it. A panic discards this listener's declarations and diagnostics without rejecting startup. Bare item IDs use the submitting mod ID; valid explicit names retain their namespace. |
| `key_mapping_pre` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.InteractionKeyMappingTriggered, InputEvent.Key` | A physical key or mouse button bound to the gameplay mapping `mapping` (`key.jump`, `key.attack`...) changes state while playing, before the game acts on it. `pressed` is true for a press (a key's repeat counts as one, as the game takes it) and false for a release. Cancel a press to drop it for every mapping bound to its input: the game holds nothing and acts on nothing. A release cannot be canceled, so what the game holds is always let go. Told for the keys and mouse buttons the window takes (agent control's included), after the window's own shortcuts (fullscreen, screenshot, debug keys), and only with no screen open; not for a controller, whose presses go straight to the mappings, nor for a mod's `crate::VanillaKeyMappingRef::set_down` or `crate::VanillaKeyMappingRef::click`, which do not change physical input. No NeoForge counterpart. Client-side. |
| `leaves_decay` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | A non-persistent leaf block at distance 7 is about to decay at `pos`. Set `cancel` to keep it. Server-side. |
| `left_click_block` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.LeftClickBlock` | A player left-clicks the block `state` at `pos`, `face` the face hit (`[0, 1, 0]` is the top): where `ServerPlayerGameMode.handleBlockBreakAction` takes a dig's start, after its reach, spawn protection and world border checks, before `Block.attack` (a note block plays) or a creative break. Set `canceled` to ignore the click: the block isn't attacked, dug or broken on the server, and the player's client gets it back (its own predicted crack still shows to the clicking player). Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). |
| `left_click_empty` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.LeftClickEmpty` | The local player left-clicks empty space, with any main-hand stack. Client-side only; the server does not receive this event. |
| `level_load` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.Load` | `dimension`'s level opened on the server (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Load`, posted in `MinecraftServer.createLevels`): before any of its chunks (`chunk_load`), so a mod sets up what it keeps for the level here. At startup the Overworld fires first, before its initial world spawn is chosen; the remaining levels follow the configured `LEVEL_STEM` registry traversal. Do not assume any particular order among them. Each fires before the next level is available. Idle column eviction leaves the level open: it does not fire `level_unload`, and revisiting that level does not fire `level_load` again. Explicitly opened runtime levels can fire this while parked: `event.dimension` names the opened level, not the Host's current level. Server-side. |
| `level_save` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.Save` | `dimension`'s level was saved (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Save`, in `ServerLevel.save`): its level data, chunks and entities, by the autosave, `/save-all`, a pause, a closing level or the server stopping, and the integrated server's initial save before `server_starting`. A world-wide save visits levels in their creation order, regardless of which level is active. Idle chunk eviction is not a level save. Individual record failures are reported and do not suppress this hook; a failed awaited storage flush does. Server-side. |
| `level_tick_post` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LevelTickEvent.Post` | After a successful level tick pass on either logical side; never after a failed tick. The server ticks eligible levels in its loaded creation order. `host.server_has_time()` says whether optional work still fits in the tick (`LevelTickEvent.hasTime`); always true on a client. |
| `level_tick_pre` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LevelTickEvent.Pre` | Before a level's tick pass on either logical side. `level` identifies the level. The server ticks eligible levels in its loaded creation order. `host.server_has_time()` says whether optional work still fits in the tick (`LevelTickEvent.hasTime`); always true on a client. |
| `level_unload` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.Unload` | `dimension`'s level closed (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Unload`, posted after `level.close()` in `MinecraftServer.stopServer`): drop what the mod kept for it. Fires when an explicitly unloaded runtime level closes or during `MinecraftServer.stopServer`'s per-level close loop (in creation order, after network shutdown, player save/disconnect, and a successful final world save), only after that level's own close (`save.flush()`) succeeds. Idle non-Overworld levels evict chunks/entities without closing or firing `level_unload`. If the final world save fails/panics or a level's own close fails, its `level_unload` does not fire; `server_stopped` still fires unconditionally after retiring storage. Don't read its blocks. Server-side. |
| `lightning_strike` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server lightning bolt is about to be created. `cause` is `weather`, `trident`, `command` or `mod` (a native mod's `strike_lightning`); `player` is the responsible player when known. Set `cancel` to prevent the bolt and its effects. |
| `living_breathe` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingBreatheEvent` | An entity's breathing this tick (`LivingBreatheEvent`, posted from `LivingEntity.baseTick` every tick for a living entity with the generic air logic): `entity` is the player or mob. `can_breathe`, `consume` and `refill` come in as Sunrise does it (a mod-free game changes nothing); set them to change this tick. With `can_breathe` the air rises by `refill`, up to its maximum (`entity_max_air_supply`: 300 for every entity except dolphins, which have 4800); a dolphin's vanilla `refill` is its maximum minus its current air. Without breath, air falls by `consume`, and at -20 it is set to 0 and the entity takes 2 drowning damage. Negative amounts count as 0. Asked on servers for players and mobs. A water animal, axolotl or nautilus comes in with its own air logic's answer: where it breathes (water, or rain for an axolotl) `can_breathe` and a `refill` to its full air; elsewhere no breath and `consume` 1. Running out deals its own damage (drowning, or drying out). |
| `living_change_target` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `LivingChangeTargetEvent` | A mob is about to change its target. `original_target` is the target requested by the game, or none when it is clearing the target. `new_target` starts as that value; a listener can redirect or clear it. Set `cancel` to retain the mob's existing target. `is_brain` identifies a brain-driven target change; false identifies a goal or other mob target setter. Handles may be read through the host. During the lifted mob tick pass, the scoped view reads the ticking mob directly and peers from the tick snapshot: an already-ticked peer exposes post-turn state, and a not-yet-ticked peer pre-turn state. A mob spawned during that pass does not resolve in the scoped view until the next pass. Removed mobs do not resolve; dead-but-present mobs are distinct from removed mobs. Outside this scoped tick path, dispatch reads the entities available in the game host rather than this snapshot. A stale or invalid replacement, including a fake player, is refused. Authoritative server only. When piglins turn on a player who took guarded gold, the game reads the end of its mob list again at each step and finds each piglin again by its entity id after the hook; a piglin that is removed is skipped. |
| `living_conversion_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingConversionEvent.Post` | A mob converted into another type (NeoForge's `LivingConversionEvent.Post`): `converted` replaces `entity`. Fires after the finalized new mob joined the level and before the old mob is removed, for the conversions `living_conversion_pre` tells. |
| `living_conversion_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingConversionEvent.Pre` | A mob is about to convert into another type (NeoForge's `LivingConversionEvent.Pre`, vanilla's `Mob.convertTo`): a zombie drowning, a piglin or hoglin in the Overworld, a tadpole growing, a mooshroom sheared by a dispenser, a pig or villager struck by lightning. `outcome` is the new type. Set `cancel` to keep the mob as it is; a drowning zombie starts its timers again. Not told: a mooshroom a player shears, a zombie villager's cure, a villager turned by a zombie. |
| `living_damage_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingDamageEvent.Post` | NeoForge's `LivingDamageEvent.Post`: entity `entity` of type `kind` has been hurt by the hit `container` (a read-only live `DamageContainerView`) describes, with its final values (health damage, the reductions, the post-attack invulnerability ticks). It fires once for every hit that passed `living_incoming_damage` and the invulnerability cooldown, at the end of `hurtServer`: after the hurt sounds and after that hit's death processing (even if vetoed) or totem protection, before the hurt caller resumes. The next hazard, poison application or rider's hit sees the preceding hit already completed. It is not delayed to the next tick or the end of a player's turn. A queued mob effect's hurt finishes before the next action; a dolphin's dry-out hurt finishes before the land hop. A bee's drowning hurt finishes before it checks stinger expiry; an expiry hurt finishes before nectar and anger bookkeeping and movement controls. A server player's landing hurt finishes before the landing's `HIT_GROUND` and fall reset, and before the next accepted move. A mob landing in its own tick finishes its hurt before the fall reset, movement restitution, travel's collision hurt or a later goal. This includes a `BreathAir` move from either selector and a dolphin following a rider. A pufferfish sting on a mob finishes the hit before applying poison or starting the next sting, and before the fish's own `entity_tick_post`. A projectile hit finishes before its enchantment effects, levitation or a follow-up explosion. Each mount or passenger's hurt finishes before the fall routine continues to the next hit: a horse is hurt before its riders. `container.inflicted()` is the amount before absorption; `container.health_damage()` is the amount applied to health. A hit that took no health (a shield, absorption, armor or a `living_damage_pre` listener took it all) fires it too, with `container.health_damage()` 0. `container.should_cause_side_effects()` carries `hurtServer`'s fresh-hit decision (`shouldCauseSideEffects`): false for a stronger hit inside the cooldown unless its source bypasses it, independent of the final damage or modded invulnerability ticks. It only listens. It can't write the entity or the world's blocks: the host (`HitHost`) gives the hit's random and level attachments, level sounds, and the own random of the entities the hit lends. |
| `living_damage_pre` | server | listen | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingDamageEvent.Pre` | NeoForge's `LivingDamageEvent.Pre`: `LivingEntity.actuallyHurt` of entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`) by `source` (named as `native_living_incoming_damage` names it) has taken armor and magic, and the absorption hearts are next: `container` is a live mutable borrow of the hit's damage container; it holds the damage it started with, what it is now (`new_damage`, which may be 0) and each reduction so far (armor, enchantments, mob effects). Change its `new_damage` to change what is inflicted before absorption, not what `lastHurt` remembers for the cooldown. Set its post-attack invulnerability ticks to change the cooldown itself, or use `add_reduction_modifier` for subsequent stages (such as `Reduction::Absorption`). The setters refuse negative damage, NaN damage and negative ticks without changing the hit. Pre fires for players, mobs and the ender dragon; a wolf whose armor takes the hit and armor stands do not fire it. The host is the hit's (`HitHost`): its blocks, random and own attachments, level sounds, and the own random of the entities the hit lends; the hurt entity is in the middle of its hit. |
| `living_death` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDeathEvent` | A mob or server player is about to die (NeoForge's `LivingDeathEvent`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.die`): its health has run out, and the loot, experience and death message have not run. `entity` is its id and `kind` its type (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player`). `source` and `weapon` are the complete killing blow, as `living_incoming_damage` saw it, including the causing player, direct projectile and weapon components. A server player's lethal fall is heard on the landing body before its fall distance is reset. A lethal pufferfish sting is heard before the fish applies that sting's poison or stings the next mob. A projectile's killing blow is heard before on-hit follow-ups; a mount or passenger's killing blow is heard before falling on to the next rider. Cancellation skips death and loot without changing the listener's health: heal it to keep it alive; at health <= 0 its body is removed after 20 ticks. Armor stands, boats and other non-mob entities are not told. |
| `living_death_message` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `living_death_message` is asked for the death message a tamed mob sends its owner (`TamableAnimal.die`), once the message is built and only when it would be sent (owner online, `show_death_messages` on). `message` holds the text: replace it, or set it to `None` to tell the owner nothing. Every mod is asked in order. Server and host. |
| `living_death_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob or server player's death has finished (Fabric's `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DEATH`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.die` at its `broadcastEntityEvent`): the loot, equipment and experience dropped and the death message went out. `entity` and `kind` identify the victim. `player` is the victim's UUID for a player death, and `message_key` is present when the player death recorded a known translation key. `damage_type` names the killing blow and `attacker` is the recorded numeric attacker (0 when absent). `credited_player` is the UUID of the player credited for a mob kill; it may differ from the source entity. It only hears. The tail of `living_death`, a separate moment, and not fired when that hook cancelled the death. On a server player's lethal landing this is still inside the hurt, before `HIT_GROUND` and the fall reset. On a pufferfish sting it is before that sting's poison and the next sting. Projectile and mounted-fall deaths likewise finish before their on-hit or next-rider follow-ups. Server-side, once per death, for every ordinary living entity. The ender dragon uses a separate death sequence and fires neither living death hook. Armor stands, boats and other non-mob entities are not told. |
| `living_death_protection` | server | collect | `&mut dyn HitHost` | — | A death protection held somewhere other than the hands, asked in `LivingEntity.checkTotemDeathProtection` once neither hand protected the dying entity: no hand had a `death_protection` stack, or `living_use_totem` refused each. Answer by setting `protector` to a stack carrying `minecraft:death_protection`, such as a totem worn in an accessory slot, and the entity survives as a hand's totem would make it: 1 health, that stack's `death_effects`, entity event 35 (the totem animation and sound), and for a player the `used` statistic and the `used_totem` trigger with that stack. |
| `living_destroy_block` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDestroyBlockEvent` | A mob is about to destroy a block (NeoForge's `LivingDestroyBlockEvent`): a wither breaking blocks around it, a ravager breaking leaves, the ender dragon's wall check, an enderman picking a block up, a silverfish waking its friends (the infested blocks break), a zombie breaking a door or a turtle egg. `entity` is its entity id, `kind` its type (`minecraft:wither`), `pos` the block and `state` its state. Canceling keeps the block (an enderman then holds nothing). Asked once per block, only while a mod is loaded. Server-side. |
| `living_drops` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDropsEvent` | Not `loot_drops`: that is the loot-modifier step, which edits the stacks a table rolled (blocks, shearing, a mob's table). This hook fires once after it, on the entity's whole collected drop list, and nothing is spawned until it returns. A dying mob, server player or armor stand `entity` of `kind` (`player` is a player's UUID) is about to drop `drops`, the items `LivingEntity.dropAllDeathLoot` collected (NeoForge's `LivingDropsEvent`): the loot tables' stacks (after `loot_drops`), the equipment that rolled to drop, a mob's own extras (an enderman's carried block, a fox's mouth item, a piglin's inventory, a copper golem's preserved equipment) and a player's inventory, each with the position, motion and pickup delay its item entity will have. Edit the list (take out, change or add drops) or set `cancel` for no drops at all; empty stacks don't drop. `source` is the killing blow (`causing` and `direct` entity ids, 0 when none or not known); a player who dies with no recorded blow gets the source `living_death` heard (`generic_kill` for `/kill`). `recently_hit` is whether a player hurt it within 100 ticks, which is what lets a mob drop its equipment. An armor stand fires it from `brokenByAnything` with an empty list (its stand item and its equipment pop outside the list, as in vanilla). Experience orbs are not in the list (`living_experience_drop`, heard just before this hook, as in NeoForge; the orbs join the level after the items); a spectator, who drops nothing, doesn't call this. Each `ItemDrop` also sets the item entity's `thrower`, `target` (the only player who may pick it up), `no_gravity` and starting `age` (`ItemDrop::UNLIMITED_LIFETIME` never despawns); a drop left as it was spawns as before. Server-side. |
| `living_drown` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | `LivingDrownEvent` | An entity that can't breathe and has no air (`LivingDrownEvent`, posted from `LivingEntity.baseTick`), with vanilla's answers as the defaults: it drowns at -20 air, for 2 damage and 8 bubbles. Change `is_drowning`, `damage_amount` or `bubble_count`, or cancel to skip drowning this tick. Eight bubbles are the vanilla entity event; another positive count is sent as that many bubble particles, and none sends nothing. Asked on servers, for the entities `living_breathe` is; a water animal, axolotl or nautilus shows no bubbles, as in vanilla. It is given the loop `context` of the server that ticks the entity (its registries, settings and mod list), because it fires inside the tick's own air and damage update. |
| `living_entity_use_item_finish` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Finish` | NeoForge's `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Finish`: `entity`'s use of `stack` in `hand` ran its time (`LivingEntity.completeUsingItem`), `duration` the ticks left, always 0. `entity` is as in `living_entity_use_item_start`. `result` arrives with what `finishUsingItem` left in the hand (the bowl of a stew, none for the last apple; a mod's own item is kept) and may be replaced. Fired for consumables, potions, spyglasses and mods' items. Both sides, as vanilla's `completeUsingItem`: answer the same on both, and change the world only on the server (`native_window` is false there), not on a server's client. On the server it fires for every player of the world, `entity` the acting one's id. On a server's client the result arrives as vanilla's client `finishUsingItem` leaves it: an item that is not a consumable unchanged (a spyglass), a consumable less one, and its use remainder (the bowl of the last stew) once that empties the hand; nothing is used up in creative mode. The hand holds the result, a mod's replacement included, until the server's inventory update. |
| `living_entity_use_item_start` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Start` | NeoForge's `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Start`: `entity` starts using `stack` in `hand` (`LivingEntity.startUsingItem`), for `duration` ticks (`use_duration`'s answer). `entity` is the user's `Entity.getId`: a player's (the acting one, `host.players().acting_player()`, on the server; the local player on a server's client) or a mob's; a mob fires it only for `LivingRef::start_using_item`. Change `duration` (consumables, potions, spyglasses and mods' items count to it; below 0 refuses the use, and 0 runs it out on its first tick, as `living_entity_use_item_tick` running it out, unless the item's own is 0 already), or set `canceled` and the use doesn't start. Both sides, as vanilla's: on the server for every player of the world, and on a server's client for the uses it predicts. Answer the same on both, and change the world only on the server (`native_window` is false there), not on a server's client. |
| `living_entity_use_item_stop` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Stop` | NeoForge's `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Stop`: `entity` lets go of `stack` in `hand` before its use ran its time (`LivingEntity.releaseUsingItem`: the use button let go, a screen opened), `duration` the ticks left. | `living_entity_use_item_tick` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Tick` | Each living tick that `entity` may still use `stack` in `hand`. `entity` is as in `living_entity_use_item_start`; `duration` replaces the remaining-ticks counter, not the original use duration. A canceled tick or a mob discarded before that check reports nothing. An entity that joins wearing something counts from empty on its first body tick. | `living_experience_drop` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingExperienceDropEvent` | A dying mob or player `entity` of `kind` is about to drop `original` points of experience; the vanilla conditions have been checked. `attacking_player` is the player who last hurt it, if any. | `living_get_projectile` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `LivingGetProjectileEvent` | `LivingEntity.getProjectile(weapon)` (NeoForge's `LivingGetProjectileEvent`) for a player (`player` and `name`) or a mob (`entity`, `kind`): a skeleton or illusioner's shot, or the crossbow load of a pillager or piglin. `projectile` is the vanilla ammunition; replace it to choose another. | `living_heal` | both | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | `LivingHealEvent` | A living entity is about to heal. Mods may change the amount or cancel. `entity` identifies the affected entity. | `living_incoming_damage` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingIncomingDamageEvent` | NeoForge's `LivingIncomingDamageEvent`: `LivingEntity.hurtServer`, past its guards (an invulnerable or dead entity, fire on a fire-resistant one), is about to hurt entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player`) by `source`: its damage type (`minecraft:fall`), causing entity (`DamageSource.getEntity`: the shooter of an arrow), direct entity (`getDirectEntity`: the projectile's entity id when the damage type names one, on a player, mob or ender dragon; 0 for `magic()` and any other source with no direct entity, even during that projectile's impact), and where it comes from (`getSourcePosition`). `victim` is where the entity stands and looks, and `weapon` the direct entity's weapon (`getWeaponItem`) when it is known. `causing_weapon` is the causing living entity's current main-hand weapon (`getEntity().getWeaponItem`), read at hit time, not a projectile's saved weapon; None for an absent, unknown or non-living cause, or once the shooter is gone. A melee hit can lend the same view for both fields; unavailable weapons do not suppress the event. `causing_weapon` is an addition: NeoForge's `LivingIncomingDamageEvent` doesn't carry it, and mods there read the source entity's main hand themselves. `container` is a live mutable borrow of the hit's damage container before any reduction; engine sequencing and normalization remain game-owned. Change its `new_damage`, the invulnerability ticks a fresh hit leaves (vanilla's 20), or install an owned reduction modifier closure with `add_reduction_modifier` (run in registration order when that reduction stage executes, receiving a read-only view of the live container and the preceding reduction value). Set `canceled` and nothing of `hurtServer` happens (no damage, hurt sound, knockback, cooldown, statistics or criteria); a canceled hit on the ender dragon still counts as landed (`EnderDragon.hurt` returns true whatever `reallyHurt` did). | `living_knockback` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `LivingKnockBackEvent` | NeoForge's `LivingKnockBackEvent`: `LivingEntity.knockback` is about to push entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player`) `strength` away from the direction (`x`, `z`), on the raw strength, before the entity's knockback resistance takes its share and its motion changes. Vanilla's direction points at the source of a hit (the attacker's side), and the entity is pushed the other way. `source` is the push's `DamageSource`, named as `native_living_incoming_damage` names a hit's. `attacker` is the attacking entity's network id, falling back to the victim's last hurt-by mob, or 0 when unknown; `attacker_main` and `attacker_off` lazily borrow what it holds as `ItemStackView` (a player's selected and off-hand stacks, a mob's main hand; none when unknown, empty or unrepresentable). `LivingRef::knockback` has an unattributed `minecraft:generic` source: this same attacker fallback and the victim's existing kill credit still apply. The call deals no damage and creates no credit. Vanilla's short `LivingEntity.knockback` overload delegates directly to the full overload, firing this event once per knockback operation, matching NeoForge's `CommonHooks.onLivingKnockBack`, which takes the strength as a float: `strength` is an `f32` (the engine's `f64` cast down), widened again after the edit. Change `strength`, `x` or `z`, or set `canceled`, and the push follows them: canceled, the entity's motion is left alone. A `strength` of 0 or less after the hook is no push, and a direction too short to tell draws a random one after it, as in vanilla. A value a mod leaves that is not a finite number is put back as it was. It fires for `LivingRef::knockback` and for the players, mobs and armor stands the game knocks back: a hit's own push, a sprinting or Knockback hit, a sweep, a spear, a shield's push on its holder, a goat's ram and a mob's melee push. The ender dragon can also receive a default knockback when it is not sitting. The event does not fire when `SulfurCube.knockback` launches a cube carrying a block and the source has a causing entity, because it does not call the living implementation. Pushes that do not use `LivingEntity.knockback` also do not fire it: the dragon's wing push, Punch arrows, explosions, wind charges, a mace smash's area and a hoglin's throw. The level is the victim's: its blocks, random and own attachments. `kill_credit` is the pushed living entity's kill credit at this pre-motion moment: a remembered player takes precedence over its last hurting mob, even when that player cannot resolve (then 0, without falling back). `kill_credit_main` lazily borrows that entity's current main-hand stack as `ItemStackView`, or none when unknown, empty or unrepresentable. Call `ItemStackView::project` only when components or an owned stack are needed; unrelated component projection failure never suppresses the event. This is not the immediate damage-source attacker. Accepted-hit memories, including a tame wolf's owner, are committed before this read. Both are additions: NeoForge's `LivingKnockBackEvent` doesn't carry them, and mods there read the pushed entity's kill credit and its holder's main hand themselves. |

| `living_visibility` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `LivingEvent.LivingVisibilityEvent` | How visible `entity` is to `looking` (NeoForge's `LivingVisibilityEvent`, last in `LivingEntity.getVisibilityPercent`): the range a mob's targeting check sees it from is scaled by `visibility`. It hears blocks that a player, an explosion or a mob breaks, not other breaks (leaves decaying, a torch losing its wall, a piston, `/setblock ... destroy`), nor a creative break or a tool that can't harvest the block, nor experience. | `mob_despawn` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `MobDespawnEvent` | NeoForge's `MobDespawnEvent`: the mob `mob` (of type `kind`, at `pos`) is about to run `Mob.checkDespawn`, which fires once per tick for every mob on the entity tick list (its chunk is entity-ticking, which player tickets give across the whole view distance), persistent ones and peaceful-mode ones included. A listed mob beyond the simulation distance is checked without being ticked; a mob in a loaded chunk that is not entity-ticking is neither checked nor told. The hook is told while the game has lent its mob list to the tick, so `mob` resolves through the tick's own read view (#795): the ticking mob itself, and the other present mobs in its tick snapshot, are readable through the host. The ticking mob is read directly; an already-ticked peer exposes its post-turn state and a not-yet-ticked peer its pre-turn state. A mob spawned during the pass does not resolve in this scoped view until the next pass. Removed mobs do not resolve; a dead-but-present mob is not a removed mob. This is the scoped read-host first step for #795, not an in-place ownership redesign. `kind` and `pos` stay as plain data for listeners that only need them. Set `result`: `Allow` discards the mob now, `Deny` keeps it this tick, and `Default` (the starting value) leaves vanilla's rules to decide. Anything but `Default` skips vanilla's checks, including the `nextInt(800)` draw of the idle-despawn roll, so the hook fires before that draw: a mob a mod denies does not consume it. A listener is a pure function of its inputs, because the hook fires on replayed ticks. The wither and the ender dragon override `checkDespawn` without `super`, so the event is not fired for them. | `mob_spawn_placement_check` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `MobSpawnEvent.SpawnPlacementCheck` | NeoForge's `MobSpawnEvent.SpawnPlacementCheck`: may the entity type `kind` spawn at `pos` in `level` for `reason`? It can differ from vanilla even for a vanilla entity type. `result` starts as `Default`, which keeps that answer. Set `Succeed` or `Fail` to override it for this check. The predicates have already drawn from `event.random`, the caller's live stream (NeoForge's `SpawnPlacementCheck.getRandom()`). Natural mobs, chunk-generation packs and `BaseSpawner` ticks (monster spawner blocks and spawner minecarts) fire this hook; trial spawners check obstruction directly. `spawner` is `Spawner::Block` for a monster spawner block, `Spawner::Minecart` for a spawner minecart, and `None` otherwise. `mob` is a scoped read-only `UnspawnedMobView` of the actual new or loaded candidate. A refusal drops it. Otherwise the same body goes to finalization and admission. Creating it does not draw from the level's random. Server-side. |
| `mob_split` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `MobSplitEvent` | A slime, magma cube or sulfur cube is dying and about to split into smaller ones (NeoForge's `MobSplitEvent`, vanilla's `AbstractCubeMob.remove`): `dimension` is the level it dies in (a `PendingMobSpec` for a new child names it), `parent` its entity id (still resolvable with `host.entity(parent)`), `kind` its type, `size` its size and `child_size` the size of the children (half of it). The children vanilla made (2 to 4 for a slime or magma cube, 2 for an unprimed sulfur cube, or 0 for a primed sulfur cube above size 1) are already constructed, in order, and wait in an unbounded mutable list lent as `children` (`MobSplitChildren<'_>`, matching NeoForge's `MobSplitEvent.getChildren()`): inspect a child (`event.children.get(i)`), edit one in place (`event.children.get_mut(i)`), construct a detached `UnspawnedMob` (`event.children.construct(host, spec)?`) and add or swap it in (`event.children.push(child)?`, `event.children.insert(i, child)?`, `event.children.replace(i, child)?`), or remove children (`event.children.remove(i)`, `event.children.clear()`). Each child is a complete mob that has not joined the level. All original proposals have already fired `entity_constructing`; removed and canceled proposals discard their unpublished state but do not undo other Host writes. Any detached `UnspawnedMob` not left in the list is cleanly discarded when dropped. If a listener panics, the shared dispatcher restores its cancellation flag and disables that listener for the session. Direct writes to children and their list are not journaled, so edits made before the panic remain visible to later listeners. Set `cancel` (or return `Flow::Cancel`, or leave the list empty) to spawn no children: the construction draws stay spent and the parent still dies. If accepted, children join once each in list order. The parent's removal effects still run when it is removed, even if the split was canceled. Only for a parent above size 1, once its children are made. With no listener nothing is allocated for the list. On the server. |
| `mod_container_viewers` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The number of players with the mod container at `pos` of `dimension` open went from `previous` to `viewers` (vanilla's `ContainerOpenersCounter`, as a barrel keeps it): 0 to more than 0 is `onOpen`, back to 0 is `onClose`, any change `openerCountChanged`, so a block can show itself open while `viewers` is not 0. Incremented synchronously when a non-spectator opens the menu, decremented when it closes (including disconnect), and rechecked every five ticks while open, including copper golems in range. Rechecks also report unchanged counts. Zero crossings post container_open/container_close game events: the opening or closing player is the source, none on a recheck. Server-side; open/close runs with the player's state in place. |
| `mod_mismatch` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `ModMismatchEvent` | A saved mod list (code and content mods) was read while opening a world. Fires when a nonempty list exists, changed or unchanged, never when the lists are absent or empty (an empty saved list counts as absent), once per open. Versions use the loader's comparison; minecraft is excluded. Newly added mods are not differences. Call `ModMismatchEvent::mark_resolved` with a mismatched mod id to mark a migration; the first resolving listener is recorded and earlier markers cannot be removed or overwritten. When a saved mod is no longer loaded, it fires before the world opens, on the side that asks (the client in singleplayer, the dedicated server), and a resolved id is taken off the question; otherwise it fires on the opening server before world-state restoration, after preparation. |
| `model_baking_completed` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ModelEvent.BakingCompleted` | Every model of a resource load is baked and published (`ModelEvent.BakingCompleted`): the block and item models and the standalone ones mods asked for (`standalone_models`), in the store `model` now reads. Fires once per successful model load, after both `texture_atlas_stitched` calls, at the model manager's apply step on first load and reload. Listeners ordered after the model manager apply after this hook. It precedes `client_setup` on startup and `client_resources_reloaded` on later reloads. A refused prepare fires nothing, and the old handles stay valid. Resolve models once through `render_assets().model` and keep the `ModelRef`s until the next `model_baking_completed`, when the old ones answer `ModelError::StaleModel`. Use `host.render_assets().baked_model(key)` to borrow published geometry during this callback; it does not edit the store. Never look them up by name every frame. Client-side. A panic rolls back the callback's journaled writes; the mod is reported and its callback skipped for the session without suppressing other mods' notifications. |
| `modify_custom_spawners` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `ModifyCustomSpawnersEvent` | NeoForge's `ModifyCustomSpawnersEvent`: the server `level` builds its custom spawner list once when it opens. `spawners` is an editor over that ordered list, which starts with vanilla's five built-ins (`WanderingTrader`, `Patrol`, `Cat`, `VillageSiege` and `Phantom`, in the order Sunrise ticks them) for the Overworld, and starts empty for other dimensions (matching vanilla `MinecraftServer#createLevels`). Inspect every entry, built-in or a mod's, with `get`, `entries` and `position`; `insert` an owned `CustomSpawner` at any place (0 puts it before the built-ins) or `add` it at the end, `remove` a built-in kind, `remove_at` any entry and `move_entry` any entry. The level owns an added instance for its lifetime, ticking it directly once per eligible server tick (outside debug worlds, with `spawn_mobs` enabled, matching `ServerChunkCache#tickChunks`) inside a server transaction and dropping it when the level unloads or the world closes. Every entry in the list runs in one ordered walk (`ServerLevel#tickCustomSpawners`); a removed built-in never ticks, so its random draws are gone with it. The editor does not own the live list: a listener's edits are staged and applied after every listener ran, and a listener that panics has its edits undone, so an instance it removed stays in the level with its state. With no listener, or one that leaves the list alone, the level ticks exactly vanilla's spawners. Server-side, not cancellable. |
| `modify_default_components` | both | collect | `&dyn DataLoadContext` | `ModifyDefaultComponentsEvent` | NeoForge 26.3's ModifyDefaultComponentsEvent: set or remove entries in `components`, the default component map for `item`, including vanilla items. Asked once per item after registries freeze, before the owning load publishes immutable defaults. Receives a read-only DataLoadContext with the actual ServerDataLoad or ClientPreload origin, frozen registry names, settings captured once for loaded packages at this load boundary, and the shared LoadedMods view. Readers and datapack reloads do not replay this event. No runtime Host, world handles or transaction access. |
| `moisture_change` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Farmland at `pos` is about to change moisture from `from` to `to`. Set `cancel` to keep its old moisture. Server-side. |
| `mouse_button_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.MouseButton.Post` | An accepted raw button event finished dispatch, including a screen handler's early return. |
| `mouse_button_pre` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.MouseButton.Pre` | Raw mouse button input on any screen, before dispatch; cancel skips dispatch and Post. Button/modifiers use the same GLFW encoding as screen hooks. Synthetic clicks skip it. |
| `mouse_scrolling` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.MouseScrollingEvent` | Gameplay scrolling after accumulation, before hotbar/spectator handling. `scroll` is scaled x/y, `accumulated` whole x/y steps, `buttons` left/middle/right, and `cursor` the GUI position. Cancel skips gameplay scrolling. |
| `movement_input_update` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `MovementInputUpdateEvent` | Lends the local player's sampled `MovementInput` after sampling, before auto-jump and sprint decisions. Every listener sees the preceding listeners' edits. |
| `music` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `SelectMusicEvent` | Choose this client's music, once a client tick: `music` and `volume` hold what vanilla chose (`Minecraft.getSituationalMusic` and `getMusicVolume`: the biome's track, the creative or underwater one, a boss's, the menu's; None for silence). Set `music` to a track of your own, a sound event of your content mod's `sounds.json` such as `Music::new("mymod:music.spring", 1200, 6000, false)` (the delays in ticks between songs, and whether it cuts off a different song playing), None to keep vanilla's quiet, or change `volume` (0 to 1) to fade it. |
| `music_layers` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The tracks to play over (or instead of) the music on this client, asked once a client tick: push a `MusicLayer` (a sound event, looped, and its volume, 0 to 1) for each. A layer eases to a new volume over up to two seconds, and one left out fades out and stops; layers that first appear in the same tick start together, so list every stem of a piece from the start, the silent ones at volume 0, and raise them (a fuller arrangement as a village grows). Their sound events should be `stream: true` in `sounds.json`. |
| `neighbor_notify` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `BlockEvent.NeighborNotifyEvent` | The block at `pos` (`state`) is about to tell its neighbours it changed (NeoForge's `BlockEvent.NeighborNotifyEvent`): at `ServerLevel.updateNeighborsAt`, which notifies all six sides (`skipped_side` is `None`), and `updateNeighborsAtExceptFromFacing`, which notifies all but `skipped_side`. Set `cancel` and no neighbour hears, on both paths. Asked for every such update, redstone included: answer fast. Server-side. |
| `note_block_change` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `NoteBlockEvent.Change` | A player is tuning a note block on the authoritative server, not in client prediction. `context` gives its dimension, position, instrument and old note; `new_note` starts as vanilla's cycled note. Set it to 0 through 24; an out-of-range value keeps vanilla's. Cancel to leave the note unchanged and prevent playback. |
| `note_block_play` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `NoteBlockEvent.Play` | The note block at `pos` is about to play (NeoForge's `NoteBlockEvent.Play`, in `NoteBlock.playNote`), once a block above no longer mutes it. `player` and `player_entity_id` name the player who tuned or punched it, and are `None` for redstone. `instrument` is its instrument and `note` its note, 0 to 24; change either to play another (a note out of range keeps the block's own). Set `cancel` and it doesn't play: no sound, no particle, no `note_block_play` game event. Server-side. |
| `panel_event` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | `event` happened on mod `mod_id`'s panel (`native_panel_items`): the player's click, key or edit, or (`Search`, since version 2) the owner's own `panel_search` reaching its box, on the first frame that draws it, with no player edit. Since version 3 a left click on an item with nothing carried comes as `Clicked` when the button comes up without a drag, and a drag off the list ends as `Dropped`. Act on it when it is yours. |
| `permissions_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PermissionsChangedEvent` | NeoForge's `PermissionsChangedEvent`: the operator list is about to give player `player` (`name`) the permission level `new` in place of `old`, through `PlayerList.op` (`/op`, the console's `op`, the management API's `operators/add` and `operators/set`) or `PlayerList.deop` (`/deop` and the same on the console and the management API; `new` is then `All`). Set `cancel` and the list keeps the old level. The player need not be online: `player_entity_id` is their server entity id when they are. Clearing the list (`operators/clear`) is not a call to either, as in vanilla. Dedicated servers only; a single-player world has no operator list. Server-side. |
| `pickup_xp` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerXpEvent.PickupXp` | Before `player` picks up `orb` in `dimension` (`ExperienceOrb.playerTouch`, NeoForge's `PlayerXpEvent.PickupXp`). Fires only when `takeXpDelay` is 0. `orb` is the live `ExperienceOrbRef` handle—there is no separate snapshot copy, so reads (`value`, `count`, `age`) and writes (`set_value`, `set_count`, `set_age`, or `orb.entity().kill(host)`) act on the live orb immediately and later listeners observe earlier edits. `set_value` keeps vanilla's signed `int` (`DATA_VALUE`) semantics (zero and negative values repair nothing and award no XP; a surviving orb persists `Value` as a signed short); `set_count` enforces vanilla's `ExtraCodecs.POSITIVE_INT` (`1..=i32::MAX`) and returns `Err(ExperienceOrbWriteError::InvalidCount)` without mutating the orb otherwise. Canceling (`Flow::Cancel`) skips the pickup continuation (`takeXpDelay` stays 0, no take/sound, no Mending/XP award, and no count decrement), leaving any writes already made through `orb` in place. Otherwise, if `orb` is still resident, pickup runs in vanilla `ExperienceOrb.playerTouch` order: sets `takeXpDelay` to 2, takes the orb, repairs Mending and awards remaining positive XP, then decrements `count` and discards at 0. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: renamed from `experience_orb_pickup`, to NeoForge's `PlayerXpEvent.PickupXp`. |
| `piston_post` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | `PistonEvent.Post` | On an authoritative server, the piston at `piston` pushed the blocks at `push` one step `direction` (`[0, 1, 0]` is up; pulling when it retracts, `extending` false) and destroyed those at `destroy` (NeoForge's `PistonEvent.Post`, at the end of `PistonBaseBlock.moveBlocks`, after the neighbour updates). It only hears; `piston_pre` is the veto. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: `piston_move` is split into `piston_pre` and `piston_post`. |
| `piston_pre` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `PistonEvent.Pre` | On an authoritative server, the piston at `piston` is about to push the blocks at `push` one step `direction` (`[0, 1, 0]` is up; pulling when it retracts, `extending` false) and destroy those at `destroy` (NeoForge's `PistonEvent.Pre`, in `PistonBaseBlock.moveBlocks`, once its structure resolved). Set `canceled` and nothing moves, as when the structure can't; a sticky piston then retracts without its block, and clients are sent the blocks rather than the retraction to replay. Diverges from upstream: `piston_move` is renamed to `piston_pre`, and `piston_post` is new. |
| `play_level_sound_at_entity` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtEntity` | A sound bound to `entity` is about to play (NeoForge's `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtEntity`, in `Level.playSeededSound`'s entity overload, `playSound(except, sourceEntity, ...)`): a goat horn, a frog's tongue, eating and spawn, an animal eating (frog, goat, sniffer), a long jump, a goat's ram, a breeze's charge and deflection, shearing (sheep, mooshroom, snow golem, bogged, copper golem), dye on a sheep, an axe on a copper golem, an allay's item and duplication, a ghastling hatching, a trident thrown (the trident) or riptide (the player), and a mod's sound bound to an entity. Queued server sounds are asked as they go out. For a transactional server request, the hook runs synchronously at the call; only the resolved effect is deferred. Sounds emitted by a level-sound listener are not re-dispatched to level-sound listeners. The fields and their rules are `play_level_sound_at_position`'s. Client entity-sound packets ask before playback and keep following their emitter; an absent emitter plays nothing. `LocalPlayer.playSound` also asks this hook, including steps, falls, hurt, bundle clicks and consumption; these remain positional sounds, not following sounds. Both sides, distinguished by `HostSide`. |
| `play_level_sound_at_position` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtPosition` | A sound is about to play at `position` in `level` (NeoForge's `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtPosition`, in `Level.playSeededSound`): every server sound not bound to an entity, mobs' own sounds included (`Entity.playSound` plays at the entity's position). A mod may change `sound`, `source`, `volume` and `pitch`, or set `cancel` and no player hears it; `original_volume` and `original_pitch` are as asked. The sound engine processes the supplied volume and pitch. Sounds the server's level plays during the tick are asked as they go out, at the end of the tick, with the state then. `/playsound` sends its packet directly and isn't asked, as in vanilla on the server. On the client, incoming sound packets ask before playback, including `/playsound`; client-predicted and game-event level requests ask here too. Canceling a sound does not cancel its action or prediction. Server and client requests are separate: `HostSide` identifies which one is being edited. UI and sound-engine-only playback do not ask this hook. For a transactional server request, the hook runs synchronously at the call; only the resolved effect is deferred. Sounds emitted by a level-sound listener are not re-dispatched to level-sound listeners. Both sides. |
| `play_sound` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlaySoundEvent` | `starting` is true for starts and loop replays, false for a live channel refresh. A ticking sound's live refresh applies volume, pitch and `effects` transiently; a refresh for a category volume change applies volume and pitch. `None` stops the channel. Base volume and pitch edits, including zero, negative and infinite values, go through `SoundEngine.calculateVolume` and `calculatePitch`, as they do in `play_local_sound`, at a start and at a live refresh alike. Invalid `effects` (`SoundEffects::valid`) refuse playback. A sound is about to play (`PlaySoundEvent`, vanilla's `SoundEngine.play`): `original` is the sound as asked for, `sound` starts as a copy of it. Edit `sound` (its volume, pitch, source, position, identifier, looping, delay, attenuation, relativeness or `effects`) to change what plays, or set it to `None` to play nothing. `effects` (`SoundEffects`) is the mixer's DSP for this one sound: its gain, a low-pass and a high-pass on the direct sound and sends to the shared `room`, `hall` and `cavern` reverbs, each in a fixed range (`SoundEffects::valid`: a value out of range, NaN or an infinity refuses the play, never clamped). A sound that doesn't tick keeps the effects it started with. It is asked before the engine's own checks (output open, sound resolved, volume, channel pool), for every sound the client plays through the sound engine, so answer fast; later mods see earlier mods' edits in `sound` and the sound as asked for in `original`. A new `identifier` is resolved again, with its own weighted pick. The listener gets the `ClientHost`. A start fires inside the play call itself and plays at once with the answer, as vanilla's `SoundEngine.play` does; a live refresh fires once per audio tick and applies at that tick. Client-side only; a server has no sound. `instance` is an engine-local sound instance id, unchanged across its live updates. |
| `play_sound_source` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlaySoundSourceEvent` | A sound that is not streamed has started on `channel` (`PlaySoundSourceEvent`), after the engine set its pitch, volume, attenuation, looping and position: `sound` is what is playing, after `play_sound`'s edits. A notice: the channel is an id only and gives no control. Streamed sounds (music, records) fire `play_streaming_source` instead. Client-side only. |
| `play_streaming_source` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlayStreamingSourceEvent` | A streamed sound (music, a record) has started on `channel` (`PlayStreamingSourceEvent`): `sound` is what is playing, after `play_sound`'s edits. A notice: the channel is an id only and gives no control. Other sounds fire `play_sound_source` instead. Client-side only. |
| `player_advancement_done` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AdvancementEvent.AdvancementEarnEvent` | A server player completed an advancement, after progress was recorded. |
| `player_advancement_progress` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AdvancementEvent.AdvancementProgressEvent` | A criterion of a server player's advancement was granted or revoked, after its progress changed and before any completion rewards. A grant or revoke that changes nothing is not heard. `progress` is an owned snapshot of every criterion (including its stored grant date), the requirement groups and their completion at this exact mutation. It belongs to the mutated progress, including first-tick and reload grants queued by the server hub. During a nested player turn, a suspended player's hub record may contain displaced progress. A listener may retain a clone; later mutations do not change the snapshot. |
| `player_arm_swing` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's arm swing was handled by the server. `hand` is `mainhand`. Server-side. |
| `player_attack_cooldown_reset` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player reset their attack strength by swinging (`handlePunch`). The swing and reset have already happened. The player's state is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_bed_enter` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player is about to enter a bed after the sleep checks pass. Set `cancel` to keep them out. The player's state is swapped in. |
| `player_bed_leave` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerWakeUpEvent` | `PlayerWakeUpEvent`: a server player is getting out of the bed at `pos` (`Player.stopSleepInBed`), heard first, while they still read as sleeping there. `wake_immediately` is vanilla's flag for skipping the wake-up fade: generic stop-sleeping, death and bed-missing wakes pass true; morning, explicit leave and bed-rule refusal pass false. `update_level` is false for morning, true for those individual wakes. |
| `player_break_speed` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayerEvent.BreakSpeed` | A player's destroy speed for the block at `pos` may be changed or canceled. A client without this mod predicts vanilla destroy speed; the server remains authoritative and corrects the resulting block-breaking progress. |
| `player_brewed_potion` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerBrewedPotionEvent` | A server player took `stack` from a brewing stand bottle slot (`BrewingStandMenu.PotionSlot.onTake`): `player` is their UUID and `player_entity_id` their network id. |
| `player_bucket_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player caught a mob in a bucket, after it was removed and the filled bucket was given. Server-side. |
| `player_bundle_item_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected a bundle item in `slot` at `index`. The guest's state is not swapped in during packet handling. |
| `player_change_beacon_effect` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player changed a beacon's selected effects, after they were stored. Each selected effect is an `Option<EffectKey>`; None means no effect. Server-side. |
| `player_changed_dimension` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerChangedDimensionEvent` | `player` (`name`) changed dimension (NeoForge's `PlayerChangedDimensionEvent`): `from` and `to` are dimension ids (`minecraft:overworld`, `minecraft:the_nether`, `minecraft:the_end`). Fires once a cross-dimension teleport lands: a Nether or End portal (including travel on a vehicle and later End portal exits), or a command such as `/execute in ... run tp`. The destination level is in place and the player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()` and `PlayerRef::tell` reach them. Runs on the logical server, not the client. Does not fire for a move within a level, a player who leaves before arrival, or a replacement created by respawn, even in another level. Use `player_respawned` for respawns after death or after the End credits. |
| `player_client_loaded_world` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player finished loading the world around them after joining, respawning or changing dimension. The guest's state is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_clone` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.Clone (not cancellable)` | A player's data was copied to their new player (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.Clone`, from `ServerPlayer.restoreFrom`): `was_death` is true after a death, false when they left the End after the credits (`respawn` with `keepAllPlayerData`). `original` is a direct, callback-scoped loan (`OriginalPlayer`) of the retained original player, enforced at compile time so it cannot outlive the hook. It supports attachment reads on the old player without default insertion. Saved values decode once, and cache writes, explicit writes and removals are journaled to `host.transaction` without touching the replacement. It also provides `with_inventory` over the 43-slot vanilla inventory, `persistent_data`, health, experience, active effects, position, source dimension and source level. `player` is the replacement's UUID, already assigned its final level, including any `player_respawn_position` redirect. Heard after removal of the old player's menus and after the data copy, before placement or bulk attachment publication. On both routes an installed descriptor must have a persistence codec to copy; on death it must also opt in. Saved data for absent packages remains opaque and uses its stored flag on death. Old bulk tokens are stale. Not cancellable. |
| `player_command` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CommandEvent` | A command a player typed (NeoForge's `CommandEvent`, before `Commands.performCommand` runs it), without its slash: any command, vanilla's and the mods' own, also one the player may not run. Set `canceled` to stop it: nothing runs and nothing is answered but the lines pushed to `replies`, which go to the player as system chat. No player's state is swapped in and the server's player list is busy with this one, so `PlayerRef::tell` and the other player helpers reach nobody here: answer through `replies`. Once for each command a player sends, on a server, and for each line of a quiet command batch (`sunrise:quiet_commands`, whose replies are dropped). A command the server refuses before reading it (unsigned chat arguments, the chat rate limit) never reaches it. |
| `player_commands_sent` | server | collect | `&dyn Host` | — | Filter the command tree just before it is sent to a live server player, including on join, on respawn, on dimension changes and on command refresh. Use `player` with the host to read the recipient and `tree.hide_where(...)` to select roots to omit from the permission-filtered tree. Aliases that redirect to removed commands are omitted too. This only changes command highlighting and completion on that client; it does not prevent execution. A client parses the tree it receives to find the arguments it must sign (NeoForge's `ClientPacketListener.sendCommand` does the same with the client command tree), so hiding a root that has signable arguments (`msg`, `say`, `me`, `teammsg`) leaves the player's client unable to sign them: it sends the command unsigned, and a server that enforces secure chat refuses it. Hide such a root only where that is acceptable; the server's signature enforcement is not relaxed. At join the handle has connection data before appearing in online player lists. A permission update exposes the new level; cross-dimension travel exposes the source dimension and position until the command tree has been sent. |
| `player_container_close` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerContainerEvent.Close` | `player` closed the `menu_type` that `player_container_open` told of (NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent.Close`, posted in `ServerPlayer.doCloseContainer` after the menu's `removed` and state transfer, before `containerMenu` resets to the player's inventory menu): they closed it, walked out of reach, died, changed level, or another menu opened. On disconnect, `PlayerList.remove` posts `player_logged_out` before `Player.remove` closes the menu. Once for each open; also told when the server stops, as each remaining player's menu closes before the player is saved, and a menu a listener opens during that close or save is not published. `menu` is the closing menu handle, still valid during this call with the same support boundary as `player_container_open` (`None` for crafting tables, merchants, workstations, lecterns and mount inventories), and stale once the hook returns. The menu's `removed` cleanup has already run: the cursor stack was returned to inventory or dropped, block/vehicle containers have closed, and any menu-owned transient slots have been detached. State swapped in, when the player has a body. Server-side. |
| `player_container_open` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerContainerEvent.Open` | `player` (`name`) opened a menu (NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent.Open`, posted when `ServerPlayer.openMenu` sets the new `containerMenu`): any menu, vanilla or a mod's. `menu_type` is the menu type's name (`minecraft:generic_9x3`, `minecraft:furnace`, `minecraft:merchant`, `minecraft:crafting`, `minecraft:lectern`, a mod's own), `pos` the block it is of in `dimension` (none for a trade, a vehicle's, a mod's native menu or a mount's inventory, which has no menu type in vanilla and is told as `sunrise:mount_inventory`). The player's own inventory is not one, nor is failed construction. Heard synchronously after construction, the open packet is queued and full contents sent, with the new menu already current. Replacing a menu follows its close; even inside one tick, open precedes `player_container_close`. `menu` is the current menu handle, valid during this call, as returned by `PlayerRef::container_menu`. It is `None` for unsupported menus such as crafting tables, merchants, workstations, lecterns and mount inventories. A supported menu keeps its handle when backing indices are permanently excluded; its slot methods still refuse those indices. The player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_deep_sleep` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player has slept for 100 ticks in a bed and now counts as deeply asleep for the night-skip check. |
| `player_destroy_item` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerDestroyItemEvent` | The player's item was used up or broke. `original` is the complete stack before the use or durability loss, including count, damage and components. `hand` is the hand it was in, or None for worn equipment and ammunition consumed from the inventory. `player` and `player_entity_id` identify the acting player, including the local host. Listen only, after consumption or the break has applied and before a use remainder is installed. A mining break notifies after block drops. Both sides hear their own actions; the client does not report a break inferred from an equipment status packet. Partial consumption, refused actions and creative use that preserves the stack do not fire. |
| `player_destroyed_block` | both | listen | `HostSide` | — | A player's break removed the block `state` from `pos`: the `Block.destroy(level, pos, state)` call `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock` makes only after `Level.removeBlock` succeeded, before a creative player's return and `playerDestroy`'s drops. `state` is the block the removal took out, read just before it: what `playerWillDestroy` left there (a cracked decorated pot; a disarmed tripwire, where vanilla passes `Block.destroy` the armed one) or what a `break_block` listener put there. `block_entity` is the saved data of the block entity that stood at `pos` (`BlockEntity.saveWithoutMetadata`, read with `state`), none for a block without one; the entity itself is gone from the level by now, so a live handle would be stale. The level is writable. On the server, for every player of the world: the helpers read the acting one. Not heard for a break `break_block` canceled, nor for a break the level makes (a piston, water, an explosion). |
| `player_edit_book` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's book edit or signing packet is about to be applied. `pages` is its page count; `signing` means the packet has a title. Set `cancel` to drop the edit. |
| `player_egg_throw` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player threw an egg, after its projectile was launched and the use was applied. Server-side. |
| `player_elytra_boost` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player used a firework rocket to boost while gliding, after the rocket was launched and consumed. Server-side. |
| `player_enchant_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEnchantItemEvent` | A server player enchanted `item` at an enchanting table (`EnchantmentMenu.clickMenuButton`): `enchantments` are the ones it was given, in order, as (id, level). `item` is the enchanted stack (an enchanted book for a book). Fires after the levels are paid, before `enchant_item`. Server-side. |
| `player_exp_cooldown_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's XP orb pickup cooldown changed to `ticks`. It is reported after the value changes. Server-side. |
| `player_fish` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's fishing rod, with `state`: 0 the cast (`FishingRodItem.use` throwing the bobber), 1 a fish biting (`FishingHook.catchingFish`), 2 the reel-in of a bite, which gives the catch, 3 the reel-in with nothing on the line, 4 the reel-in of a bobber hooked to an entity or item (like Bukkit's `PlayerFishEvent`; NeoForge's `ItemFishedEvent` is later, in `FishingHook.retrieve`). Set `cancel` on the cast and nothing is thrown; on a bite the fish gets away and the wait starts over; on a reel-in (2 to 4) nothing happens, the bobber stays out and the rod takes no wear. The bobber ending by itself (too far, the rod put away, the owner dying), nor the host of a LAN world's rod. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Once for each cast, bite and reel-in, on a server. |
| `player_flower_pot_manipulate` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player put a plant into a flower pot, after the potted block state was stored and the item consumed. `plant` is the item id and `pos` the pot position. Server-side. |
| `player_flyable_fall` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerFlyableFallEvent` | NeoForge's `PlayerFlyableFallEvent`: server player `player` (`name`) landed after falling `distance` blocks at damage multiplier `multiplier` while allowed to fly (a creative or spectator player walking), so `Player.causeFallDamage` returned early and nothing was hurt. Listen only. Once for each landing, after the fall's block reaction. The player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_game_mode_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerChangeGameModeEvent` | A server player's game mode is about to change (NeoForge's `PlayerChangeGameModeEvent`): from `from` to `to`, both the shared `GameMode` used by commands and `PlayerRef`. `player` is the UUID and `name` the name. Heard for the `/gamemode` command, the F3+F4 switcher's packet and a hardcore world's respawn into spectator, once for each change to a different mode; not for the world's default mode a player joins with. Set `to` to send the player to another mode instead: listeners run in order and each sees the mode the one before left. Return `Flow::Cancel`, or leave `to` at the player's current mode, and the player keeps their mode (nothing is sent, the command counts it as no change); the command's message names the mode it asked for, as vanilla's does. For a change the game makes inside a player's turn their state is swapped in; for the hardcore respawn it is not. |
| `player_handshake` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A login or allowed transfer reached the server, after the transfer and network protocol checks and before the login hello, authentication, or admission. Runs once on the server thread. There is no player or profile yet. |
