| `player_harvest_check` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `PlayerEvent.HarvestCheck` | A player's ability to harvest the block at `pos` may be changed. |
| `player_heart_type` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlayerHeartTypeEvent` | Which hearts the local player's health bar draws this frame (NeoForge's `PlayerHeartTypeEvent`, from `Hud.HeartType.forPlayer`): `kind` comes in as vanilla's pick (poisoned, else withered, else frozen when fully frozen, else normal); set it to draw another kind. This runs inside the vanilla `minecraft:player_health` body, after `gui_layer_pre`. A canceled or replaced layer does not fire this hook, and a wrapper fires it only by drawing the vanilla body. Only the heart sprites change, not the layout or the separate armour, food, vehicle-health or air layers. Client-side. |
| `player_input` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest sent the server's current `PlayerInput` flags, after they were applied to the hub. The guest's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_insert_lectern_book` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player inserted a book into a lectern, after the block entity and inventory changed. `book` is the item id. Server-side. |
| `player_inventory_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `player_inventory_changed(player, stacks)`: a server player's inventory changed, at the moment vanilla's `InventoryChangeTrigger` is checked (the per-tick check found another item or count in a slot), heard before any criterion and whether or not the tree has `inventory_changed` advancements. `stacks` are the 41 slots in `Inventory` numbering: 0..36 the main inventory (0..9 the hotbar), 36..40 the armour (feet, legs, chest, head), 40 the off hand. It also fires at a player's first check after joining, so a mod sees what they log in with. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers read that player. Single player, LAN guests and a dedicated server's players; never on a client of a remote server. Quest, collection and 'obtain an item' mods need it instead of polling every inventory. Bundles need "hooks": @API:player_inventory_changed@. |
| `player_item_cooldown` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An item cooldown is about to start for a server player. `item` is the item id and `ticks` its duration. The player's state is not swapped in. Set `cancel` to skip starting the cooldown. Server-side. |
| `player_item_frame_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player changed an item frame after its contents or rotation changed. `action` is `put`, `rotate`, `remove_item` or `break`; `item` is empty when no item remains, and `rotation` is the resulting step. Server-side. |
| `player_item_mend` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Mending repaired a server player's item with experience from an orb. `repair` is durability restored and `experience` is the orb's value. Server-side. |
| `player_item_smithed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.ItemSmithedEvent` | `PlayerEvent.ItemSmithedEvent`: a server player took `result` from a smithing table (`SmithingMenu.onTake`, or `onQuickCraft` for a shift-click): `player` is their UUID and `player_entity_id` their network id, `inputs` the template, base and addition as they were before the take used them (`None` for an empty slot). It comes once the click is done, so the menu already holds the used inputs, but before the table sounds. Server-side. |
| `player_kicked` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A network or fake guest was kicked, after the hub recorded the rendered `reason`. The guest's state is swapped in unless they were already removed in the same poll. |
| `player_lectern_page_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player changed the page of a lectern, after its page and redstone pulse changed. `page` is the zero-based new page. Server-side. |
| `player_level_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's experience level actually changed. A signed point grant reports once after it finishes, not once per level; level grants/debits and `ServerPlayer.setExperienceLevels` report their applied changes too. A canceled grant or a write of the same value is not reported. |
| `player_load_from_file` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.LoadFromFile (not cancellable)` | A player was loaded from the world's save (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.LoadFromFile`, `PlayerList.loadPlayerData`): their `.dat` has been read and applied, and they are not placed in a level yet. `directory` is the world's player directory (`players/data`): a mod reads its own file there, named `<uuid>.<suffix>` as `getPlayerFile(suffix)` names it. Heard once for each join, for the owner of an integrated server as for anyone else, on a server, with no player's state swapped in. Not cancellable. |
| `player_logged_in` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerLoggedInEvent` | `player` (`name`) joined the game (NeoForge's `PlayerLoggedInEvent`): placed in the world and listed in `host.players().server_players()`, so `PlayerRef::send_payload` reaches them. Once a join, with no player's state swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_logged_out` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerLoggedOutEvent` | `player` (`name`) left the game (NeoForge's `PlayerLoggedOutEvent`), by any path: a lost connection, a kick, the server stopping or failing. Once for each player `player_logged_in` told of, and also for a kicked fake player without a preceding login. Silent `remove_fake` sends no logout. A real player is still in `host.players().server_players()` at `PlayerList.remove` entry, before their menu closes. A listener's edits persist only on leaves this server saves; a completed proxy hand-over or a crash writes no logout save. Only when the server stops can a player still get a last message. A kicked fake has already left the list and is not saved. Server-side. |
| `player_login` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player whose login the server has accepted (NeoForge's `PlayerNegotiationEvent` stage): the name, whitelist, ban and server-full checks have passed and the player holds a place, but their saved state is not loaded and they are not placed in a level yet. Set `cancel` to refuse them: they are disconnected with `reason` (a plain "Disconnected" if it is left unset) and nothing else of the join (the join message, `player_logged_in`) happens. Once for each player who joins, on a server. The player is not placed yet and their client has no world to show a message in, so no player's state is swapped in and `PlayerRef::tell` reaches nobody: the refusal `reason` is the answer. The checks that need no mod (name, whitelist, bans, a full server, the proxy's forwarding) refuse earlier, on the connection, and this hook does not hear those players. |
| `player_login_profile` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The properties of an admitted login's profile, before `LoginFinished` sends them (`ServerLoginPacketListenerImpl.finishLoginAndWaitForClient`): the name, ban, whitelist, capacity and duplicate-player checks have passed; configuration and `player_login` come after. `properties` starts with the login's own, signed `textures` included when there are some, and none for an ordinary offline login; each mod gets the list the mods before it left, and the final list goes to login success and to the player's profile in the player list. The UUID and name are read-only, the login can't be refused here, and the hook can't wait for a profile lookup. |
| `player_loom_pattern_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected a loom pattern, after the loom menu accepted the button. `pattern` is the zero-based button index. Server-side. |
| `player_map_filled` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player filled an empty map, after the filled map and its saved data were created. Server-side. |
| `player_move` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's movement report is about to be applied: they would move from `from` to `to`, or turn from `from_rotation` to `to_rotation` (`[yaw, pitch]`). Heard once for each validated report whose position or rotation differs from the player's last reported location, after the server's own validation and before the report is committed. That location is kept on the player's connection, apart from the accepted position: it is the position given to the listener before it runs, and the destination of every server-side teleport (a join, a teleport in the level, a level change, a respawn, the resend of an unconfirmed teleport, and the correction after a veto). `from` is that location. Set `cancel` for a real veto and not a rollback: the report is not applied and its bookkeeping is skipped, and the client is corrected to the accepted position and rotation through the existing teleport correction. The report was already seen by the server's own checks before the hook, and what a violation of those would have done still happens. The values are the exact ones: there is no once-per-tick accumulation and no threshold in the engine, so the mod filters (`if (to - from).length() < 1.0 / 16.0 { return; }` skips anything under 1/16 of a block). Their state is swapped in (`host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell`). |
| `player_name_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player named a mob with a name tag, after its name and persistence changed and the tag was consumed. Server-side. |
| `player_name_format` | both | collect | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlayerEvent.NameFormat` | A player's display name was formatted. Replace `displayname` to change it. Both sides: `context.side()` says which. It runs wherever vanilla asks `Player.getDisplayName()`: when a chat line, a join or leave line, a command's feedback, a death message or an advancement message is built, and for the name tag over a player (every frame it shows, so it is not asked when no mod listens). Answer from the arguments and `context` alone, and fast: `context` holds the side, the registries and the settings of the resource generation, and a `/reload` builds the next one. A name the API can't carry (a component tree deeper than the text limit) skips the listeners for that call; a `displayname` the engine can't take back is dropped, and the name stays as it was. |
| `player_open_sign` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player opened a sign for editing, after the editor state was made. `front` selects the front or back side. Server-side. |
| `player_passage_cross` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `player`'s client crossed `passage` (#3775), one of the player's level's, from `from` (its feet before), and the server is about to follow: the move to `from` passed the move checks and both ends are tracked for the player, and the server has not yet worked out where the player comes out. Set `cancel` and the server refuses the crossing (`PassageCrossRefusal::Vetoed`) and puts the player back at `from`, as a portal Immersive Portals' `canTeleportEntity` turns down does. A listener that removes the passage or teleports the player refuses it too. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `player_pick_item` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player picked an item from a block or entity, before it enters the hotbar. `pos` is zero for an entity; `entity_id` is zero for a block. The player's state is swapped in. Set `cancel` to leave the inventory and client unchanged. |
| `player_pickup_arrow` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player picked up an arrow after it was removed and its pickup was applied. `item` is the arrow's pickup item id. Server-side. |
| `player_pong` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A connected `player` sent a play-phase pong (`ServerCommonPacketListenerImpl.handlePong`). Runs on the server thread at the packet's turn in that connection's inbox. `name` is the player's profile name; `id` is the signed 32-bit value the client sent. Unsolicited and repeated IDs are reported as-is: an ID does not prove the client answered a particular `PlayerRef::send_ping`. This notice is not cancellable and does not affect keep-alive latency. Configuration and status traffic do not fire it. Each listener gets its own event. |
| `player_recipe_book_click` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player clicked a known, placeable recipe in their current recipe book. Runs after display, recipe knowledge and live menu checks, for vanilla and mod books. Runs before applying placement, including before a mod menu's `recipe_book_place` decision. `recipe` is its ID and `all` is the Shift-click form. The player is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_recipe_book_settings_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's authoritative recipe book pair changed. `book` is a typed RecipeBookIdentity, never a custom vanilla ordinal. Rejected and unchanged custom requests do not notify. Delivery happens at the outermost commit of a transaction, once per final changed pair, so a set followed by a restore inside one transaction notifies nothing. The guest is not swapped in. |
| `player_recipe_discovered` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A recipe is about to be unlocked for a server player. Set `cancel` to keep it locked. The player's state is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_respawn_position` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerRespawnPositionEvent` | `PlayerRespawnPositionEvent`: where `player` respawns, once the server has found it: the bed or anchor they stand up at (facing it), or the world spawn's free column. `original_level` and `original_position` are that point; `missing_respawn_block` says the bed or anchor was gone, so the player came back at the world spawn. Change `level`, `position`, `yaw` and `pitch` to choose their final destination; no preliminary placement is made. `copy_original_spawn_position` (default: the block wasn't missing) says whether they keep their respawn point. Runs on the removed original player, before `player_clone` and placement. To change the point copied for future respawns, call `event.player.set_respawn_config(host, ...)` here, even on death, and set `copy_original_spawn_position` to true. This does not change the current destination. `from_end_fight` is true for the respawn after the End credits, false for death. An ordinary End portal teleport does not run this hook. Server-side. |
| `player_respawned` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerRespawnEvent` | `player` (`name`) respawned (NeoForge's `PlayerRespawnEvent`): at the end of `PlayerList.respawn`, after placement and initial synchronization, with the replacement in scope. `end_conquered` is true after the End credits (the player keeps health, food and experience), false after death. An ordinary End portal teleport does not fire this hook. Server-side. |
| `player_riptide` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player launched with a riptide trident, after the velocity, spin attack and grounded lift have been applied. The player's state is swapped in. No reply is needed. |
| `player_save_to_file` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.SaveToFile (not cancellable)` | A player was saved to the world's store (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.SaveToFile`, `PlayerList.save`): when they leave or the world closes, when a dedicated or integrated server saves its guests, and for an integrated server's owner when the world saves them. Fires only after successful replacement of the player file, independently of world metadata. `directory` is the world's player directory (`players/data`): write your own file there, named `<uuid>.<suffix>`, never the player's `.dat`. The player may be leaving, so no player's state is swapped in. Not cancellable. On a full world save, including an autosave, successful player writes and these notifications finish before the first `level_save`; placed players stay online. A world save before any player joins has no player to save. |
| `player_set_spawn` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSetSpawnEvent` | NeoForge's `PlayerSetSpawnEvent`: a server player's respawn point is about to be set (`ServerPlayer.setRespawnPosition`), once for each call vanilla makes: every sleep in a bed (the same bed too, which refreshes its angle), an anchor that is a new point, `/spawnpoint`, and the copy to the replacement after `player_clone` when `player_respawn_position` allows copying (by default unless the saved block was missing). `pos` is the block position and `dimension` its level; `pos` is none when no point is set. `forced` is `/spawnpoint`'s flag. Cancel to keep the current point, including a point already set on the replacement by a clone listener. `player_entity_id` is the server's entity id for the player. Server-side. |
| `player_shear_entity` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player shearing a mob is about to do so. Set `cancel` to leave the mob and shears unchanged. The guest's state is swapped in. The listener may change the world: straight after it returns without cancelling, the game finds the mob again by its entity id and checks that it still exists, is not removed, and that the held stack keeps its item and count; if not, the click is spent and nothing is sheared. |
| `player_shield_disable` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's shield was disabled by a blocked melee hit. The shield cooldown is set immediately after this call. Server-side. |
| `player_spawn_phantoms` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSpawnPhantomsEvent` | `PlayerSpawnPhantomsEvent`: the phantom spawner is about to decide whether to try a spawn near a non-spectator server player, before its difficulty and rest rolls. `result` starts empty, meaning `Default` (skylight and sea level check); `Allow` skips that check and `Deny` stops the attempt. `phantoms` starts with a randomly drawn group size; set it to override the count. Without mods the original draw order is kept. |
| `player_sprint` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's sprint started or stopped: their client sent `START_SPRINTING` or `STOP_SPRINTING` (the sprint key, a double tap forward, or letting go) and the server's player is now `sprinting` (vanilla's `setSprinting` in `handlePlayerCommand`). Heard only when it changes. It cannot be stopped, and it hears what the client asks, not what the server does: a sprint that ends by itself (a hit's knockback, hunger, a wall) is not reported. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers and `PlayerRef::tell` work. Once for each change, on a server. |
| `player_start_spectating_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player started spectating `entity_id`, after their camera moved to it (`ServerPlayer.setCamera` with a new target: a spectator's click or `/spectate`) and their client was told. Heard once per change of target, not again while the camera follows it. The mirror of `player_stop_spectating_entity`. |
| `player_start_tracking` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.StartTracking` | A client started tracking an entity (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.StartTracking`, posted at the end of `ServerEntity.addPairing`): `player` (`name`) has just been sent the spawn bundle of `entity` (its ID, `uuid` its UUID, `kind` its type, `minecraft:zombie`) and counts as tracking it. It came into range, its client's chunks reached it, or it was put in the level; another player is an entity too, and a player never tracks their own. The entity is still in the level. What a listener sends the player (`PlayerRef::send_payload`) reaches it right after that bundle, before the entity's next changes. Heard in the tick's tracking pass, after the pass has built every player's packets: the player already counts as tracking the other entities the same pass paired. The engine is still walking its tracker: read the game and send payloads, but don't spawn, remove or teleport entities from here. No player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_stat_award` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `StatAwardEvent` | A statistic is about to be awarded to a server player, before its count and scoreboard criteria change. `stat_type` and `stat` are ids such as `minecraft:mined` and `minecraft:stone`, and `value` the amount. All three may be rewritten; ids must use their canonical namespaced spelling. Unknown types or entries, noncanonical ids and negative amounts are refused together, warning once and keeping the entire original award. Set `cancel` to award nothing. Server-side. Ordinary interactions fire at vanilla's award point; inside a transaction, the award and this hook wait until the outermost successful commit, after tentative world and inventory writes. |
| `player_stays_on_ground_surface` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The player's crouch edge backoff (`Player.maybeBackOffFromEdge`) asks whether the player is staying on the ground surface (`Player.isStayingOnGroundSurface`, vanilla: the shift key is down). `entity` is the player's id; a mod may change `staying`, so a mod can make the player hold to an edge without crouching, or walk off while crouching. Fires on both sides, on the client's replayed ticks too, with the loop `context` of the side that ticks the player (its registries, settings and mod list): register the mod on both, and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs so prediction and the server agree. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `player_stonecutter_recipe_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected a stonecutter recipe, after the menu accepted its button. `recipe` is the zero-based button index. Server-side. |
| `player_stop_spectating_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player stopped spectating `entity_id`, after their camera returned to the player. |
| `player_stop_tracking` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.StopTracking` | A client stopped tracking an entity (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.StopTracking`, posted at the end of `ServerEntity.removePairing`): `entity` went out of range, left the level or died, or `player` (`name`) left, changed level or respawned, which stops everything they tracked. `entity`, `uuid` and `kind` are as `player_start_tracking` told, once for each start; the player no longer counts as tracking it. A stop of the tracking pass is heard right after the removal packet, and what a listener sends the player reaches it after that removal. A leaving player's stops come after `player_logged_out`; those of a respawn or a level change, which send their own removals, at the next tracking pass. The entity is not necessarily still there, and a player who left is gone. As in `player_start_tracking`, the engine is walking its tracker: don't spawn, remove or teleport entities from here. No player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_swap_hands` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingSwapItemsEvent.Hands` | Heard before a non-spectator player's swap-hands action. The two mutable stacks start as the off-hand and main-hand stacks; the hands receive the listener's outputs unless it cancels. slots. Set `cancel` to keep the items where they are: the player's client is sent both slots again. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Once for each press, on a server: a LAN or dedicated server's players, the single-player owner and a LAN host. Not heard: the same swap made in an open inventory (hovering a slot and pressing the key, a menu click). |
| `player_swap_with_equipment_slot` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player swapped their main and off hands, after both inventory slots changed. Server-side. |
| `player_switch_hotbar_slot_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Post` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Post`: a server player switched from hotbar slot `old_slot` to `new_slot` (0 to 8), after the selected slot changed and a main-hand item use stopped. Fired only when the slot differs and `player_switch_hotbar_slot_pre` didn't cancel it. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `player_switch_hotbar_slot_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Pre` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Pre`: a server player is switching from hotbar slot `old_slot` to `new_slot` (0 to 8), after the slot range check and before the selected slot changes. Fired only when the slot differs. The player's state is not swapped in. Set `cancel` to keep the old slot; the client is sent that slot again, and `player_switch_hotbar_slot_post` doesn't fire. |
| `player_tab_list_name_format` | server | collect | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlayerEvent.TabListNameFormat` | ServerPlayer.getTabListDisplayName: the name the Tab list shows for `player`, None to let the client choose it. Replace `display_name` to change it. It runs while the Tab list is built. Answer from the arguments and `context` alone, and fast: `context` holds the side, the registries and the settings of the resource generation, and a `/reload` builds the next one. A name the API can't carry skips the listeners for that call; a `display_name` the engine can't take back is dropped, and the name stays as it was. |
| `player_take_lectern_book` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player took the book from a lectern, after the menu and block entity changed. `book` is the removed item id. Server-side. |
| `player_teleport_end_gateway` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player is about to travel through an End gateway at `gateway`. Set `cancel` to leave them at the entrance; the gateway is not consumed and its cooldown does not start. Server-side. |
| `player_teleported` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player a command teleported (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent`, for a player): `/tp` and `/teleport`, inside their level or into another one. `from` and `to` are their feet before and after, `dimension` the level they are in now. Heard after the move has reached the player's state and their client (an inter-level teleport: when the new level is entered, before it has loaded around them), so it cannot stop it. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers that read the acting player and `PlayerRef::tell` work. Once for each teleported player, on a server. Ender pearls and chorus fruit have their own hooks; a spectator's teleport menu is not heard yet; portals and respawns are not teleports here. |
| `player_tick_post` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerTickEvent.Post` | After a player's tick on both logical sides (NeoForge 26.3 `PlayerTickEvent.Post`). Non-cancellable. On the client, fires through death tick 20, but not after the local player is removed. `player` is the same identity as in `player_tick_pre`; on the server their state is acting. Runs after body work and server-side item, arrow and XP pickups, so reads see the completed tick. Client reads see only the state available to that client, not future server updates. On the server, neither player tick hook fires for a spectator whose bounds touch an unloaded chunk; that player's body tick is skipped. |
| `player_tick_pre` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerTickEvent.Pre` | Before a player's tick on both logical sides (NeoForge 26.3 `PlayerTickEvent.Pre`). Non-cancellable. On the client, fires through death tick 20, but not after the local player is removed. `player` identifies the ticked player; on the server their state is acting. Fires before sleep, cooldowns and body work. Players still tick under `/tick freeze`, but not while the level is paused. On the server, neither player tick hook fires for a spectator whose bounds touch an unloaded chunk; that player's body tick is skipped. |
| `player_toggle_entity_age_lock` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player toggled an ageable mob's age lock with a golden dandelion, after the lock and item use changed. |
| `player_toggle_flight` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's client started (`flying`) or stopped flying (vanilla's `handlePlayerAbilities`, which honours it only for a player who may fly: creative or spectator), heard once for each change, on a server, with the player's state swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` and the other helpers reach them. The player is already flying or not as asked, as vanilla leaves them; set `cancel` to refuse it: they keep the state they had and the server sends their abilities back, as vanilla does for what it refuses, so the client lands or flies again. Only what a client asks for is heard: not the flight a game-mode change starts or ends (`/gamemode`), and not a LAN host's own player. |
| `player_toggle_sneak` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's client changed sneaking in its player input packet, after the hub applied the new state. The guest's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_turn` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Relative turn delta about to be applied to the local player (`Entity.turn`), in `Entity.turn` units where `1.0` equals `0.15` degrees (`x` is yaw, `y` is pitch). Fires on the client for every relative look source that turns the player (mouse after `calculate_player_turn`, controller stick, arrow keys and control-socket `turn`), skipping freecam and direct `ClientHost::set_local_rotation`. Edit `x` and `y` to scale or adjust the turn; an axis whose `(delta as f32) * 0.15` is not finite logs a warning and keeps its original delta. |
| `player_unleash_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player removed an entity's leash, after it was cleared. |
| `player_velocity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server applied `velocity` to a player, when syncing changed motion to that player's client. |
| `player_xp_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerXpEvent.XpChange` | A server player's experience points are about to change through `Player.giveExperiencePoints` (NeoForge's `PlayerXpEvent.XpChange`): an orb pickup after mending, an advancement reward, or `/xp add ... points`. Change the signed `amount` (in points) or set `cancel` to give nothing. Experience bottles and furnaces make orbs, so they come through the orb path. Server-side. |
| `player_xp_level_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerXpEvent.LevelChange` | A server player's level is about to change through `Player.giveExperienceLevels`: once per level gained or lost by points, and for the anvil, the enchanting table's debit, `/experience add ... levels` and `PlayerRef::give_levels`. Absolute `setExperienceLevels` writes do not ask. Change `levels`, or set `cancel` to leave the level unchanged. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Server-side. |
| `portal_create` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A Nether portal was built for a dimension travel, or an End platform was made for an arrival. `pos` is the lower corner of the Nether portal rectangle or the End platform origin. The completed blocks are already in the world. |
| `portal_spawn` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `BlockEvent.PortalSpawnEvent` | A fire placed at `pos` (`state`) found an empty obsidian frame, `shape`, and is about to fill it with a nether portal (`BlockEvent.PortalSpawnEvent`, in `BaseFireBlock.onPlace`), in the Overworld or the Nether. Set `cancel` and no portal forms: the fire stays where it can survive, as without a frame. Server-side. |
| `potential_spawns` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `LevelEvent.PotentialSpawns` | Possible natural spawns at a server position (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.PotentialSpawns`, `NaturalSpawner.mobsAt`). Mods may add or remove entries, or cancel to make the list empty. Mods can read the world, but cannot write it during the spawn pass. |
| `potion_brew_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PotionBrewEvent.Post` | NeoForge's `PotionBrewEvent.Post` in `BrewingStandBlockEntity.doBrew`. `stacks` contains the renewed bottles and the as-yet unconsumed ingredient. The stand takes the edited slots before consuming the original ingredient and producing its remainder and brew sound. If Pre cancels and at least one slot matches its prior value, Post sees the replacement slots; a canceled Pre never consumes the ingredient. Server-side. |
| `potion_brew_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PotionBrewEvent.Pre` | A brewing stand at `pos` is about to brew (`PotionBrewEvent.Pre`, in `BrewingStandBlockEntity.doBrew`): `stacks` is a copy of its five slots (three bottles, the ingredient, the fuel). Set `cancel` to skip vanilla's brew: the stand then takes `stacks` as the mods left them, uses no ingredient and plays no brew sound, and `potion_brew_post` sees those slots if at least one slot still matches its prior value. Uncanceled, changes to `stacks` are dropped. Server-side. |
| `potion_splash` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A thrown splash or lingering potion applied its hit effects. |
| `prepare_hopper_transfer` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A hopper at `pos` began an item transfer attempt. This runs after its cooldown and enabled checks, before pushing or pulling any items. Server-side, including the single-player server. |
| `prepare_item_craft` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A crafting-grid result was prepared for a server player; `pos` is the crafting table, or None for the inventory grid. Change `result` before it is sent. An accepted result retains valid damage even when it exceeds that stack's maximum durability. |
| `prepare_item_enchant` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Enchanting offers were calculated for a server player at `pos`. Change `costs` before they are sent to the menu. |
| `prepare_render_buffers` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `PrepareRenderBuffersEvent` | Prepare geometry and update retained instance data before pass-bearing world stages execute (NeoForge 26.3's `PrepareRenderBuffersEvent`, vanilla `LevelRenderer.prepareTranslucents`). Runs before a classic shader pack's sky or celestial work; `AfterSky` is not a preparation hook. Client-only, noncancellable. `view` lends the frame's camera, world partial tick, game time, improved-transparency state and visible sections. |
| `prepare_smithing` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A smithing result was prepared for a server player at `pos`. Change it before it is sent. |
| `projectile_impact` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ProjectileImpactEvent` | A projectile hit something (NeoForge's `ProjectileImpactEvent`, posted where vanilla's projectile has found what it meets, before the hit acts): `projectile` (its entity id) of type `kind` (`minecraft:arrow`), shot by `owner` (a mob's or a player's entity id, 0 for none) in the level `dimension`. It hit the entity `entity` of type `entity_kind` (a player is `minecraft:player`; a dragon's part is `minecraft:ender_dragon`), or, with `entity` None, the block at `block` on its `face` (the face's normal, `[0, 1, 0]` for the top), at the point `at`. Set `canceled` and the impact does not happen: no damage, no knockback, no sound, no block reaction, the arrow does not stick, a pearl does not teleport, a potion or a fireball does not burst. Cancellation alone leaves the projectile alive. Resolve the live owner with `host.entity(event.projectile)` and call `EntityRef::discard` to consume it instead. Removal suppresses the hit even if the event is not canceled. A decision invalidated by listener writes is not recomputed. A flying bobber skips hooking and redirection to the hit point, but its ordinary move still stops against solid blocks. Water is not an impact. Bobbers notify before vanilla's entity deflection and world-border bounce; rockets notify after deflection and cancellation skips their impact explosion only. Covers arrows, tridents, thrown items, fireballs, wither skulls, wind charges, llama spit, shulker bullets, bobbers and rockets. Other families skip breeze deflections and hits on wind charges. A dragon part or End crystal is told by the id of the dragon or crystal. Server-side. Version 3 fires whenever a mod subscribes to `projectile_impact` directly (the separate `hears_projectile_impacts` opt-in poll hook is removed); version 2 added bobbers and rockets. |
| `raid_spawn_wave` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A raid wave has been spawned at `pos`; `wave` is its one-based wave number and `raiders` counts the raiders, not the mobs they ride. |
| `raid_state_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server raid at `pos` changed to `state` (`triggered`, `victory`, `loss` or `stopped`). `raid` identifies its owning level, including a parked level. A terminal raid already removed from storage cannot be read. |
| `recipe_book_categories` | both | listen | `HostSide` | — | Supply categories and vanilla-shaped displays for custom recipe types. Asked lazily once per recipe table. Use the Host's recipe table to build `RecipeBookCategory::displays` from the type's own opaque recipe JSON. Each category names its menu, book identity and per-book search group. The display/index load boundary returns typed errors; screen integration is separate. This declaration does not register a settings book. |
| `recipe_property_sets` | both | collect | `&dyn DataLoadContext` | `RegisterRecipePropertiesEvent` | Items a mod's own recipe type takes in a station slot, added to the recipe property sets (`RecipeManager.RECIPE_PROPERTY_SETS`): what a furnace, blast furnace, smoker, campfire, smithing table slot or brewing stand slot accepts, and what a client predicts it accepts. Call `RecipePropertySetsEvent::register` once per set to add to; the vanilla recipes' own items stay in. Called once per load, after the recipes are resolved, with the `DataLoadContext` of the load that owns them (`origin()` `ServerDataLoad` on a server, `ClientPreload` on a client's own load), and again after `/reload`. Any valid namespaced key creates or extends a custom set (including non-vanilla `minecraft:` keys); bare keys name vanilla sets. Invalid keys, unknown bare vanilla keys, and air are logged and skipped, naming the contributor; unknown items are ignored. Later additions do not change the first contributor. Custom sets are synced to clients alongside vanilla sets. The answer must be the same every time. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `recipe_sources` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Additional ingredient containers for server player `player` placing or checking a recipe in the mod crafting menu `menu` (`namespace:path`) at `pos`. The player's state is not swapped in. Push block positions to `sources`; containers are used after the player's inventory, in mod order and then push order. The engine silently skips `pos`, duplicates (first wins), and unavailable or non-lendable containers. No separate container-count cap applies. Chest-like block entities and mod containers with declared slots are lendable. No answer preserves vanilla placement. The engine takes ingredients and writes changed containers back, firing `container_changed`; this hook only names sources. Asked once per recipe-book placement/apply or server transfer check, not per tick. |
| `recipes_received` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RecipesReceivedEvent` | The server's recipes of `recipe_types` arrived (NeoForge's `RecipesReceivedEvent`): `host.recipes_of_type` reads the server's recipes of those types in place of the window's own, so rebuild what you made of them. Vanilla serializers expose resolved candidates, not native ingredient predicates; matching on the server remains authoritative. The window retains the resolved recipes of the types not sent. It comes after a join, once the window has its player, and after every `/reload`, on a server whose mods asked for them (`native_datapack_sync`), single player included; with no types when the server stopped sending them and the window is back on its own recipes. A recipe's recipe book ids are not part of what arrives: `recipe_book_ids` answers them. Client-side. |
| `register_block_state_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterBlockStateModels` | Register the block-state definition types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterBlockStateModels`, its `registerDefinition`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterBlockStateModelsEvent::register` to bind each type identifier to its provider callback; a blockstate file whose `neoforge:definition_type` names a registered id invokes that provider directly on every load and reload. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one. |
| `register_block_tint_sources` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.BlockTintSources` | The tint sources of blocks (NeoForge's `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.BlockTintSources`, vanilla's `BlockColors.register`): `register(layers, blocks)`, and a model face with `tintindex` i is multiplied by layer i of its block's list; a face past the list is not tinted. A layer is one of vanilla's sources (`VanillaTint`) or the mod's own `BlockTintSource`. Blocks are ids, a mod's or vanilla's, and a later registration for a block replaces the earlier one, vanilla's own colours included. Asked once at start on the client, before resources load; every resource load, a pack reload included, applies the registrations again. An id no block has is logged and skipped. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_cauldron_fluid_content` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterCauldronFluidContentEvent` | Say which fluid, and how much, one of your cauldron blocks holds (NeoForge `RegisterCauldronFluidContentEvent`), so the fluid capability (`FLUIDS`) fills and drains it as it does vanilla's water and lava cauldrons. Asked once the block registry is resolved, before any world opens, and again on a resource reload. Give `event.contents.register(block, fluid, total, level)` the block and fluid keys your `register` hook requested, the millibuckets the cauldron holds when full, and the name of its integer level property, or none for a cauldron only ever full. The engine refuses, with one logged warning naming both sides, a block that already has a content, a fluid that already has a cauldron, an unknown block or fluid, a level property the block lacks or that isn't an integer, and a total of 0 or one that doesn't divide evenly by the levels; the rest of a mod's contents still apply. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_cauldron_interaction` | both | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterCauldronInteractionEvent` | Register cauldron item interactions, by exact item or by item tag, on both sides before snapshot publication. Vanilla dye tags take precedence, then item tags in registration order, then exact items; registering the same key again overwrites the entry in place. Registered callbacks run on the server through `Interaction::interact`. A panic or phase refusal discards that listener's entries, including queued registrations to all dispatchers, before the next listener runs. |
| `register_client_commands` | client | collect | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RegisterClientCommandsEvent` | The client's own commands (NeoForge's `RegisterClientCommandsEvent`): push each usage with its executor attached by `Command::executes_client` (`Command::new("x <mode:word>").executes_client(...)`). Asked when a connection starts and again when the server sends its own tree. Client and server branches can share a root in the chat's display tree, but run in separate dispatchers. Only an unknown command or argument in the client dispatcher proceeds to the server. A client command has no operator level: one with a nonzero `level` is left out, and server-only handle argument kinds (`player`, `players`, `entity`, `entities`, `dimension`) are refused. |
| `register_client_item_extensions` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterClientExtensionsEvent` | Register worn equipment providers on the client, like NeoForge's RegisterClientExtensionsEvent. Each client resource load bakes the requested models and binds the requested atlas sprites before reload listeners apply. Models may use any valid namespace. Draw callbacks borrow the live stack and asset layer and select from those bindings. An ordinary registration error rejects only that call and preserves other successful registrations. A panic discards only that listener's batch. Do not keep references from a discarded batch. Server-only packages cannot listen. |
| `register_client_tooltip_component_factories` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterClientTooltipComponentFactoriesEvent` | Register client tooltip component factories for custom `TooltipComponentData` types (NeoForge's `RegisterClientTooltipComponentFactoriesEvent`). Asked once at client startup. Call `event.factories.register::<MyData, MyClientComponent>(\|data\| ...)` to map each custom `TooltipComponent` produced by `ItemPart::tooltip_image` or `render_tooltip_gather_components` into a `ClientTooltipComponent` that measures and draws itself. A later registration for the same type replaces the earlier one. A listener that panics leaves all previously registered factories in place and publishes none of its own. |
| `register_color_resolvers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ColorResolvers` | The colour resolvers block tints blend over the biomes around a block (NeoForge's `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ColorResolvers`): `register(resolver)` adds the mod's own `BiomeColorResolver` and returns the `ColorResolver` to pass to `ClientHost::biome_tint` or `BlockAndTintGetter::block_tint`. The game blends a mod's resolver over the biome blend radius and caches it per section while it meshes, as it does vanilla's grass, foliage and water. Asked once at start on the client, before `register_block_tint_sources` and before resources load. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_commands` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterCommandsEvent` | The commands native mods add to the server's command tree: push each usage with its executor attached by `Command::executes`. Asked whenever the server builds its command tree, as the reference loader's `Commands` does: when a world opens (a dedicated server, a LAN host, single player's server thread), before its functions load, so a function line may name your command; on each `/reload` and data pack change, so a command you stop pushing is refused by the next functions loaded; and after a mod calls `host.commands().refresh_commands()`. Every player's chat box completes them as vanilla's, within their level. A usage under a root vanilla owns merges into it, as `RegisterCommandsEvent` registers into vanilla's `CommandDispatcher` (Brigadier's `CommandNode.addChild`): vanilla's usages and permissions stay, the mod's children are added beside them, and an executor at a vanilla node replaces vanilla's (see `Command`). A lower-level child opens traversal through a prefix without changing that prefix's own execution permission. When accepted usages execute at the same node (including optional prefixes), each later accepted registration overwrites that node's executor (matching Brigadier's `CommandNode.addChild`, which sets `child.command = node.getCommand()`) together with Sunrise's paired execution permission level for that node. Only that node's executor runs, attributed to its mod; if the owner is disabled, execution is refused rather than handed to another mod. Clients get the command tree, not executors. An argument's kind is one of `word`, `string` (a word or a quoted phrase), `text`, `integer`, `integer(min,max)`, `long`, `long(min,max)`, `number`, `boolean`, `player`, `item`, `block_pos`, `vec3` and `vec2`, or a command argument type a mod registered (`Registrar::register_command_argument`), named by its namespaced id. |
| `register_configuration_tasks` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterConfigurationTasksEvent` | Append tasks for this connecting profile, on the server thread after the built-in configuration steps. Runs for every peer, including vanilla. The profile has no player handle yet. Use `event.connection.has_channel` to check what the peer negotiated and `event.register(task)` to give this connection an owned task. Tasks start in registration order, including tasks of the same type, and can finish, wait or fail this peer. A panic while reading a task's type refuses admission and disables that task implementation for its mod. If a registration listener itself panics, only its staged tasks, diagnostic and journaled writes are discarded. Other listeners still configure the same peer, and the panicking registration listener is skipped for later peers. Delayed tasks complete via `IPayloadContext::finish_configuration_task` with their task type from either a server main-thread or network-thread configuration payload handler. There is no task timeout: answering keep-alives can hold configuration indefinitely. A silent or disconnected connection still ends normally. |
| `register_debug_entries` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterDebugEntriesEvent` | Register F3 debug entries once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterDebugEntriesEvent`). Each mod receives an empty batch. Push `DebugEntry::text` with its line callback or `DebugEntry::renderer` for a renderer toggle. Call `DebugEntry::with_reduced_debug_info` to opt an entry into showing under reduced debug info (default `false`, matching vanilla's `DebugScreenEntry.isAllowed`). Text callbacks run only while their entry is visible. Malformed or duplicate IDs (including built-in vanilla entry IDs) are logged and left out. |
| `register_debug_renderers` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterDebugRenderersEvent` | Collect world-space debug renderers at each client renderer-list refresh (NeoForge's `RegisterDebugRenderersEvent`). Push `DebugRenderer::new(draw)` for each renderer that should run until the next refresh. Use `event.is_debug_entry_enabled(id)` to gate a renderer on a vanilla or startup `DebugEntry` switch, or push unconditionally. |
| `register_dimension_transition_screen` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterDimensionTransitionScreenEvent` | Register loading-screen factories once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterDimensionTransitionScreenEvent`). Each mod receives an empty batch. Conditional pairs take priority over incoming, then outgoing. The first valid factory for a key wins. Unmatched travel, login and death use vanilla for a new screen. The client keeps whichever presentation is already open on an in-place load update. |
| `register_enchantment_effects` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Register codecs in the enchantment value and entity effect type registries. Selected definitions bind their named codec once during resource loading. The decoded effect runs at its component's stage, not as a global hit listener. A refusing or panicking registration callback publishes none of its codecs. |
| `register_entity_layers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.AddLayers` | The layers a mod inspects, hides or adds on living entity renderers (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.AddLayers`): `add(target, layer)`, `stock_layers(&target)` and `hide(target, stock_layer)`. Every living entity of the type, or every player of the player model, in view draws `EntityRenderLayer::submit` after its model, its unhidden stock layers and the mod layers added before, with `StockLayerDraw` to redraw a hidden stock layer per entity. The final body model is borrowed for `submit`; it is `None` when no body was posed, as after a successful replacement renderer. `entity_types`, `is_living`, `player_models`, `has_mod_renderer` and `stock_layers` tell what renderers this load built. A target that is no living entity type, or a stock layer a renderer lacks, is refused by name. Asked on the client on every resource load, the first one and each reload, after the renderers resolve (`register_entity_renderers`): added layers and hidden stock layers don't carry over from one load to the next, so register them again each time. A mod's layer that panics in `submit` stays disabled when re-added with the same `Arc`. In the ordered body pass, layers submit in level join order, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions. If this registration listener panics or is phase-refused, none of its added layers or stock-layer hides take effect; later listeners still register in the same order. |
| `register_entity_renderers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterRenderers` | The code-defined renderers of entity types (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterRenderers`, `registerEntityRenderer`): `register(type, renderer)`, and every entity of the type `type` (`mymod:bolt`) in view is drawn by `EntityRenderer::submit` in place of the type's own drawing. A later registration for a type replaces the earlier one; a type the game does not know is logged with its id and draws nothing new. Asked once at start on the client, before resources load; the game resolves each type's renderer on every resource load. This build draws the registered renderers of content mods' thrown types, of the living types drawn as mobs (in place of the model and its vanilla layers), of primed TNT (in place of the flashing cube), of experience orbs (in place of the billboarded orb quad) and of paintings (in place of the painting's quads) and of item frames and glow item frames (in place of the frame model and the item or map on its face); general entity types draw only through their renderer, if registered. That renderer reads the published generation of synced attachments through `EntityRenderState::attachments`. Other entity families keep their own drawing, including the dragon and end crystals. General, living, primed TNT and experience orb renderers submit at each entity's slot in level join order. If this listener panics, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_entity_spectator_shaders` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEntitySpectatorShadersEvent` | Register the screen effect spectating an entity type puts on, once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterEntitySpectatorShadersEvent`), as vanilla's creeper, spider and enderman do. Pass each `EntitySpectatorShader` to `event.register(shader)`. The first registration for an entity type wins, vanilla's own counting as first, so a mod can't change what the vanilla mobs show; a skipped or malformed registration is logged. The effect applies while the first-person view follows that entity, and the third-person view or leaving it clears it. |
| `register_fluid_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterFluidModelsEvent` | The models of your world fluids (NeoForge's `RegisterFluidModelsEvent`): call `event.register(model)` with a `FluidModel` per fluid whose look doesn't say all of it. Its sprites are block atlas textures, baked at every resource load from that pack's files; the tint stays the look's. A fluid without one draws its look's sprite on every surface, or water's textures when it has none. Asked at startup and on resource reload before baking, and only on the client. |
| `register_game_tests` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `RegisterGameTestsEvent` | Register namespaced test functions before the world's test definitions load. Each function runs with a ServerHost once per attempt in the real test level. The runner retains ownership of retries, timeout and completion. A listener that panics or incurs a phase refusal publishes neither its definitions nor its functions. |
| `register_gui_layers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterGuiLayersEvent` | Register, wrap or replace a HUD layer with an owned draw callback (NeoForge's `RegisterGuiLayersEvent`). Asked once at client startup. The installed body runs between `gui_layer_pre` and `gui_layer_post` unless Pre cancels it. Use `event.layers.register(id, placement, Box::new(draw))` to insert a new slot, or `replace_layer` and `wrap_layer` to change an existing slot's body. `minecraft:player_health` is the heart slot only. Use `minecraft:air_level` as the `Above` anchor to place a layer above all status rows. A status replacement or wrapper runs even when the original body would not draw, including under F1. A listener sees its pending edits; they publish only after a successful return. A panic during registration, including while dropping a superseded staged body, disables that listener for the session and reports its mod in the log and client error toast. |
| `register_item_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterItemModelsEvent` | Register the item model node types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterItemModelsEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterItemModelsEvent::register` to bind each type identifier directly to its decode-time factory, which parses the node's JSON object through `ItemModelConfig` once on each load/reload, declares any nested child item models through `ItemModelChildren`, and returns the per-stack callback that pushes `ItemModelLayer` choices referencing those child keys. Any valid `Identifier` (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla or engine item model type id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_item_tint_sources` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ItemTintSources` | Register the item tint sources a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ItemTintSources`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterItemTintSourcesEvent::register` to bind each tint source identifier directly to its decode-time factory, which parses the tint entry's JSON object once on each load/reload and returns the per-stack ARGB colour callback. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla tint source id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_json_animation_types` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent` | Register the custom animation targets and interpolations a mod adds for keyframe animation files to name (NeoForge's `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent::register_target` and `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent::register_interpolation` to bind each identifier directly to its callbacks. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; duplicates and collisions with the built-in targets (`minecraft:position`, `minecraft:rotation`, `minecraft:scale`) or built-in interpolations (`minecraft:linear`, `minecraft:catmullrom`) are rejected as registration errors. |
| `register_layer_definitions` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterLayerDefinitions` | Register suppliers for entity model layers (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterLayerDefinitions`). Called once at client startup. Use `RegisterLayerDefinitionsEvent::register` to bind each layer ID to its supplier; accepted suppliers run once when the layer table is frozen. A supplier panic is logged and shown in a client error toast naming its mod; other suppliers still run. Bake the realized definitions through `ClientHost::bake_layer`. API @API:model_layers@. |
| `register_load_conditions` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Register named conditions once at mod load, before server data admission. Each handler is a raw-JSON handler: it reads the condition's JSON value. IDs may use any namespace, but a listener that completes with a built-in or duplicate ID error refuses mod load, even if it ignored the returned error. A listener that panics or incurs a phase refusal publishes neither handlers nor preparation errors. Whether an unknown ID refuses a load or skips a file is determined by the loader, not the namespace. |
| `register_model_loaders` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `ModelEvent.RegisterLoaders` | Register the model loaders a mod adds (NeoForge's `ModelEvent.RegisterLoaders`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterModelLoadersEvent::register` to bind each loader identifier to its schema and bake callback. Each model and set of resolved texture slots invokes that callback once per load or reload. The callback's quads replace the cuboid geometry unless the loader explicitly delegates to the cuboid decoder. An absent required loader rejects its declaring model file. A reference to that file uses the missing model at that link without discarding a descendant's own geometry. Duplicates and collisions with built-ins (`neoforge:composite`, `neoforge:obj`, `neoforge:empty`, `fusion:model`) are registration errors. |
| `register_particle_groups` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterParticleGroupsEvent` | Register the render groups a mod's particles draw in (NeoForge's `RegisterParticleGroupsEvent`). Called once at client startup. Use `RegisterParticleGroupsEvent::register` to bind an ID to a factory run once per particle engine construction. Sprite loads and reloads only resolve each particle type against the constructed groups. |
| `register_particle_providers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterParticleProvidersEvent` | Registers logical-client sprite-set factories for already registered `ParticleType`s (NeoForge's `RegisterParticleProvidersEvent`). Each provider creates owned particles from typed options. The last successful registration per type wins. Sprite reload drops live and pending instances and binds the factories to the new sprite sets. If a listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_pipeline_modifiers` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterPipelineModifiersEvent` | Register pipeline modifiers once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterPipelineModifiersEvent`): call `event.register(modifier)` with each `PipelineModifier`. Modifiers are plain data state overrides (blend, depth compare, depth write, depth bias, face culling, colour write mask, polygon mode, primitive topology) applied during world drawing via `WorldDrawContext::with_pipeline_modifier`. Duplicate modifier IDs and non-finite depth-bias slope scales fail startup. |
| `register_preset_editors` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterPresetEditorsEvent` | Register client editor factories for world-preset keys once at startup (NeoForge's `RegisterPresetEditorsEvent`). Use `RegisterPresetEditorsEvent::register` to bind each preset identifier directly to its factory. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same preset replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a vanilla preset (`minecraft:flat`, `minecraft:single_biome_surface`) overrides its built-in editor. Unregistered presets retain their built-in editor, or their disabled Customize button. |
| `register_range_select_item_model_property` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | Register the numeric `minecraft:range_dispatch` item model properties a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent::register` to bind each property identifier directly to its decode-time factory, which parses the `range_dispatch` node's JSON object once on each load/reload and returns the per-stack numeric callback. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla property id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_render_pipelines` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterRenderPipelinesEvent` | Register render pipelines and custom render types once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterRenderPipelinesEvent`; 1.21.1 mods used `RegisterShadersEvent`): call `event.register(pipeline)` with each `RenderPipeline` and `event.register_render_type(setup)` with each `RenderSetup`. `event.compute_pipeline(pipeline)` with each `ComputePipeline` (a GLSL compute shader `assets/<ns>/shaders/compute/<path>.csh`, its `BindGroupLayout` and defines). Each pipeline carries a `PipelineState` (blend including `PipelineBlend::Opaque` and `PipelineBlend::Custom`, optional `DepthState` with `DepthCompare` and depth bias, culling, colour write mask, polygon mode, and `PrimitiveTopology`), optional multi-target `ColorTargetState` slots up to `MAX_COLOR_TARGETS` (8), optional `.order_independent()` opt-in for `RenderLevelStage::OitTranslucent`, optional separate vertex shader and defines. Each `RenderSetup` wraps a registered mod or vanilla pipeline ID with texture bindings, lightmap/overlay toggles, crumbling/sorting/solid-phase flags, `LayeringTransform`, `TextureTransform`, and `OutlineProperty`. The game builds them with its own at startup and again on every resource reload. Any valid `Identifier` is accepted, and duplicate mod IDs fail startup, regardless of required or optional policy. Collisions with vanilla are checked in the corresponding required or optional table (and custom render types are checked against `BUILTIN_RENDER_TYPE_IDS`). An unavailable vanilla pipeline table fails startup registration. Non-finite depth bias slope scales, colour target indices `>= 8`, and differing blend functions across active colour targets fail startup registration. A required pipeline whose shader (or OIT variant when `.order_independent()`) is missing or doesn't build fails the load: the game does not start, and a reload fails and keeps the previous resources. An optional) one is logged and resolves to `RenderPipelineError::Invalid`. |
| `register_select_item_model_property` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | Register the categorical `minecraft:select` item model properties a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent::register` to bind each property identifier directly to its decode-time factory, which parses the `select` node's JSON object once on each load/reload and returns both the per-case `when` value decoder (`&Tag -> Result<T, String>`) and the per-stack categorical callback (`-> Option<T>`). Any valid `Identifier` (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla property id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_special_model_renderer` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterSpecialModelRendererEvent` | Register factories for special item model types (NeoForge's `RegisterSpecialModelRendererEvent`). Called once at client startup, before resources load. The last registration of a type wins, including for vanilla's types. Each `minecraft:special` node is decoded and baked afresh on resource reload; the baked renderer submits to a scoped collector when the item is drawn. A listener that panics or incurs a phase refusal leaves the previous factories and replacement reports unchanged. |
| `register_sprite_sources` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterSpriteSourcesEvent` | Register the atlas sprite source types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterSpriteSourcesEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterSpriteSourcesEvent::register` to bind each type identifier directly to its provider callback; an entry of an atlas definition's `sources` whose `type` is a registered id invokes that provider at its place in the list, on every load and reload. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one, and registering a built-in vanilla source id overrides that built-in when decoding atlas definitions. |
| `register_texture_atlases` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterTextureAtlasesEvent` | Declare atlases beside the game's (`RegisterTextureAtlasesEvent`). Call `event.register(atlas)` with each `TextureAtlasRegistration`, which names the atlas definition that lists its sprites. Called at first client load and every resource reload, before textures are stitched. |
| `register_villager_professions` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Declare villager professions once per mod load, before world admission. The engine resolves every reference atomically with the owning resource tables. Vanilla replacement and overlapping custom acquisition POIs are refused. NeoForge adds a profession through `RegisterEvent` on `Registries.VILLAGER_PROFESSION`; this hook declares it as data, so only the registration moment corresponds. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `render_arm` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderArmEvent` | Before this window's player's first-person `left` or right arm is drawn (`RenderArmEvent`, in `renderPlayerArm` and `renderMapHand`): the empty main hand's arm, and the arms under a held map, never while the player is invisible. `player` and `player_entity_id` are the local player's UUID and entity id. `draw`'s pose stack starts at the arm model's origin, in blocks, posed as vanilla's arm is (with any `render_hand`), lit with the light at the eyes; add geometry with `draw.cube`, `draw.quad`, `draw.block` (taking a resolved `BlockState`) and `draw.item`. Set `canceled` to leave out vanilla's arm; what the mods drew stays. Client-side. |
| `render_fog` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.RenderFog` | Final world fog, after the computed color hook, ordinary calculations and Sunrise's fog option, before upload. Title panoramas skip it. Invalid color or distance values retain the original whole fog. |
| `render_frame_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RenderFrameEvent.Post` | Once a frame, in a world or not, right after the frame was rendered (NeoForge's `RenderFrameEvent.Post`). `elapsed` is the frame's time. It fires for a frame that was skipped too; `flip_frame` is the moment a frame was presented. `world_partial_tick` is the interpolation used by that frame, held while paused or frozen; `partial_tick` is the advancing window clock. Outside a world both use the window clock. Neither is recomputed after listeners run. Client-side. |
| `render_frame_pre` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RenderFrameEvent.Pre` | Once a frame, in a world or not, after Sunrise's extras and just before the frame is rendered (NeoForge's `RenderFrameEvent.Pre`). `elapsed` is the frame's time. `world_partial_tick` is the interpolation about to be drawn, held while paused or frozen; `partial_tick` is the window clock, which keeps advancing. Outside a world both use the window clock. Listener writes do not change this frame's captured interpolation. Client-side. |
| `render_gui_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiEvent.Post` | After the complete in-world HUD layer walk (NeoForge's `RenderGuiEvent.Post`), including under F1, and before screens, toasts, debug overlays and IME draw. Use `event.draw` to draw in GUI pixels and `ClientHost::hud_layer_bounds` to read the clipped bounds of earlier layers (e.g. `"minecraft:effects"`). Use `ClientHost::debug_overlay_visible()` to leave room for the debug overlay. Skipped during replay, when there is no in-world HUD pass, or when a mod cancels `render_gui_pre`. A panicking listener's drawing is discarded. Client-side. |
| `render_gui_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiEvent.Pre` | Before the in-world HUD layer walk (NeoForge's `RenderGuiEvent.Pre`), including under F1. Return `Flow::Cancel` to skip the walk (including layer Pre and Post), `render_gui_post` and the HUD chat layer. Focused chat is drawn by its screen, not the layer walk. Toasts draw after the active screen or overlay and are outside this veto. F1 hides ordinary toasts and audio stream failure notices, but not other callback error toasts. Fires before Options over a world. Does not fire during replay or when a server dialog or the connection screen covers the world HUD. Client-side. |
| `render_hand` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderHandEvent` | Before one first-person hand of this window's player is drawn (`RenderHandEvent`, client): a hand that holds an item or a map, the empty main hand's arm, and the empty off hand, where vanilla draws nothing. Adjust `render`'s pose, draw in the hand with `draw`, or cancel the default geometry; what the mods drew stays. Equip progress includes the item's swap scale. `draw`'s pose stack starts in the hands' view space, in blocks, +x right, +y up, -z into the screen, under the hand sway and before vanilla's per-item transforms; both hands share it, and `render.hand` says which one is asked. It is lit with the light at the eyes. Add geometry with `draw.cube`, `draw.quad`, `draw.block` (taking a resolved `BlockState`) and `draw.item`. `HandDrawContext::render_arm` submits either physical arm with the local avatar's skin, sleeve and model width, at the current draw pose and light. The submitted geometry joins the hand's own vertices, ahead of vanilla's: the hand's field of view, the view bob, a shader pack's hand pass and `hand_drawn` see it as they see vanilla's hand. Unlike NeoForge, the pose stack is `draw`'s own: turning it moves what you draw, `render`'s pose moves the default geometry, and player render state is not exposed. |
| `render_item_in_frame` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderItemInFrameEvent` | Before a visible item frame's item or map is drawn. Cancel hides the item, not the frame. |
| `render_level_stage` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderLevelStageEvent` | Observe or draw world geometry at an active stage of a world view (NeoForge 26.3's `RenderLevelStageEvent`). NeoForge's `RenderLevelStageEvent.AfterSky` runs once when `LevelRenderer.addSkyPass` adds the sky pass, after the engine or classic pack's sky work even if no geometry was visible. A classic pack's `AfterLevel` follows its world passes, before composition and the engine hand overlay. `context` exposes `StagePass::None` for those two stages, `StagePass::World` for standard pass stages, and `StagePass::Oit` for `OitTranslucent` sub-passes. Client-only, noncancellable. |
| `render_living_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderLivingEvent.Post` | After a living entity is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderLivingEvent.Post`): once a frame for each mob and player in view whose `render_living_pre` wasn't canceled, after its model, its vanilla layers and the mods' layers. `entity` is its render state, as `render_living_pre` has it; `draw` is posed at its feet, turned with its body, and what it draws is kept. `event.model` borrows the final `crate::FrameModel` the entity's layers saw, including custom pack models and the pose under layers-only draws. It is read-only and scoped to this callback. Use `part(name)` or the hierarchy to read a part's local `pose()` and effective `visible` flag after animation and Pre edits. A successful replacement renderer or other draw without a body pose supplies `None`. The order today: before this hook its model, its vanilla layers and the mods' layers are drawn; after it, the frame's later passes draw its name tag, its leash, and the flames of a burning entity and its shadow, each as its own pass. This hook neither sees nor replaces the flames and the shadow, and a canceled `render_living_pre` doesn't stop them. A type with a mod's registered renderer (`register_entity_renderers`) has the same later passes, the renderer in the model's place. The reference draws the leash and the name tag before this hook: that move is the follow-up in #1285. For a player, its `render_player_post` follows. In the ordered body pass, each entity's hooks finish before the next entity's submission begins, including any general entity renderer at its own level join slot. |
| `render_living_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderLivingEvent.Pre` | Before a living entity is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderLivingEvent.Pre`): once a frame for each mob and player in view (one the frame culls is not asked), before its pose, model and layers. `entity` is its render state (its id, kind, custom name, the frame's partial tick, its body and head turns and walk cycle, `LivingEntityRenderState`). `draw` is posed at its feet, turned with its body: +z the way it faces, +x its left, +y up, in blocks, with the helpers block entities draw with (`cube`, `quad`, `block`, `item`, `model_part`); what it draws is kept. Set `canceled` to skip all of the entity's drawing this frame: its model, armour, held items and layers, the mods' layers, its name tag, leash and glowing outline, and `render_living_post`, and the renderer a mod registered for its type (`register_entity_renderers`), which draws in the model's place; a mod drawing a model of its own draws it here and cancels. The flames of a burning entity and its shadow, which the frame queues after the entity's drawing, still draw. A player's `render_player_pre` comes first and edits the same `event.model`; a cancel here still calls `render_player_post` with `model: None`. `event.model` lends the selected body's baked hierarchy and bind pose as a `crate::FrameModelEdit`. Use `model.part("nose")` to get a villager's nose index, then `model.hide(index)` to leave it and its descendants out of the body and layers drawn on that model (such as a spider's eyes). Armour, held items and independent layers are unaffected. Missing names return `None`; invalid indices return `false` without an edit. Edits last for this draw only; `draw.keep()` does nothing here. In the ordered body pass, this hook follows level join order, including this window's player at its own slot, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions. |
| `render_name_tag_can_render` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderNameTagEvent.CanRender` | Name tag visibility and optional replacement text. |
