| `render_name_tag_do_render` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderNameTagEvent.DoRender` | Before one line of an entity's name tag is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderNameTagEvent.DoRender`): once a frame for each line of each tag the frame shows, after `render_name_tag_can_render` decided the tag shows and what it says. Mobs, players, armour stands, item frames and display entities ask it. The score line (`NameTagLine::Score`: the below-name score, or a mannequin's description) comes first, then the name (`NameTagLine::Name`). |
| `render_player_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderPlayerEvent.Post` | After a player is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderPlayerEvent.Post`): once a frame for each player whose `render_player_pre` wasn't canceled, after its `render_living_post`, or right after its `render_living_pre` when only that one was canceled. `player`, `who` and `draw` are as `render_player_pre` has them, and what it draws is kept. `event.model` borrows the same final `crate::FrameModel` as `render_living_post`, read-only for the callback. Its poses and effective visibility include this draw's animation, Skin Customization and Pre edits. It is `None` if no body pose was produced, including when only Living Pre canceled. The player's name tag, leash, and the flames of a burning player and its shadow are each drawn in a later pass of the same frame, as in `render_living_post`. A canceled pre hook doesn't stop the flames or the shadow. In the ordered body pass, this player's pair finishes at its slot in level join order, before the next body submits, including bodies drawn by a general entity renderer. |
| `render_player_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderPlayerEvent.Pre` | Before a player is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderPlayerEvent.Pre`): once a frame for each other player in view and for this window's player in third person (including front view) when it holds the camera, before its `render_living_pre`. `player` is its render state, `who` its profile name and whether it is this window's own player, and `draw` the frame `render_living_pre` gets. Set `canceled` to skip the player's own drawing this frame, `render_living_pre`, `render_living_post` and `render_player_post` included. Canceling only `render_living_pre` skips Living Post but still calls Player Post with `model: None`. Either cancel leaves eligible flames and shadows to the frame; a replacement body should not submit them again. `event.model` borrows the selected player's hierarchy and bind pose as a `crate::FrameModelEdit`. Look up `model.part("head")` and call `model.hide(index)` if it exists. That leaves the head and its children out for this draw after Skin Customization. Living Pre sees the same draw-local edits. Armour, held items and other independent layers, including the cape, are unaffected. In the ordered body pass, the pair wraps this player's living hooks at its slot in level join order, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions, for local and remote players alike. |
| `render_tooltip_gather_components` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderTooltipEvent.GatherComponents` | `RenderTooltipEvent.GatherComponents`: the elements of `stack`'s tooltip (`ItemStack.getTooltipLines` as `TooltipElement::Text` lines, plus any `TooltipElement::Component(TooltipComponent)` supplied by the built-in bundle tooltip or the item's `ItemPart::tooltip_image`), once `item_tooltip` has run and before any wrapping, factory preparation, or measuring. Add, change, reorder or remove elements; each element is then prepared into a `PreparedTooltipComponent` (custom components via factories registered in `register_client_tooltip_component_factories`) before `render_tooltip_pre` runs. Gather wraps logical text in the item's tooltip font when it cannot fit on either side of the cursor. Assign a positive GUI-pixel width to `event.max_width` to further constrain wrapping (`-1` by default). Return `Flow::Cancel` and nothing is drawn; neither `render_tooltip_pre` nor `render_tooltip_texture` is asked. A panicking destructor of custom data published here disables this listener for the session without disabling other listeners. Asked each frame the tooltip shows, never for one the stack hides. An input conversion refusal skips Gather and preserves the unwrapped engine tooltip. Text is used in memory without serialized component limits. Client-side. |
| `render_tooltip_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Pre` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Pre`: a tooltip whose components have been prepared and are about to be measured, positioned and drawn. `stack` is the item the tooltip describes, or `None` for a non-item tooltip. `components` are the read-only prepared components (`PreparedTooltipComponent`, in display order, built-in and custom alike) as `render_tooltip_gather_components` and component preparation left them; measure them with a font borrowed via `draw.with_tooltip_font(font, ...)` or inspect them with `as_text`, `as_bundle`, and `downcast_ref`. `place` holds the anchor point in GUI pixels before `positioner` adds its offset and keeps the box on screen; cursor anchors truncate to whole GUI pixels, and a widget positioner may ignore the anchor. `screen` is `[screen_width, screen_height]` in GUI pixels. `positioner` is the active `ClientTooltipPositioner` that places the tooltip box once measured. Text lines are wrapped only where `render_tooltip_gather_components` ran: with no Gather listener, each line arrives whole, as vanilla passes it. A stack that can't be converted skips Gather and Pre, and the engine's prepared tooltip is drawn as it is. `font` is the font identifier used for measurement and drawing (`minecraft:default`, or the item font, queried again before Pre); change it to measure and draw with a replacement font without rewrapping the lines. A prepared component's `width` and `height` return `None` on failure, distinct from a valid size of zero. A failed component measurement or draw aborts the whole tooltip. `draw` is the tooltip's GUI drawing context; paint from Pre stays visible on cancellation or failure. Return `Flow::Cancel` and the tooltip box and components are not drawn and `render_tooltip_texture` is not asked. Asked each frame a tooltip shows, only while a mod listens. An input conversion refusal skips Pre and leaves the prepared tooltip intact. Client-side. |
| `render_tooltip_texture` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Texture` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Texture`: the look of a tooltip box about to be drawn. Set `texture` to a tooltip style (`<namespace>:<name>`) and the box draws as vanilla draws a `tooltip_style`: the GUI sprites `<namespace>:tooltip/<name>_background` and `<namespace>:tooltip/<name>_frame` from the resource packs, each nine-sliced as its `gui.scaling` says or stretched without one. A style whose background or frame no pack has keeps the fills, where vanilla draws the missing texture. Left `None`, the box is Sunrise's solid fills, in `background` and the `border_top` to `border_bottom` gradient, as ARGB. `stack` is the item the tooltip describes, `None` for a text tooltip. Asked each frame a tooltip shows, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `render_view_transform` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Presentation-only view transform applied at vanilla's `GameRenderer.bobHurt` / `bobView` stage, after hurt tilt and view bobbing and before rendering the world. It also runs when a screen has input focus and this world render stage still runs. |
| `resolve_player_skin` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Select the local player's skin texture and model together, at vanilla's `AbstractClientPlayer.getSkin` moment. Asked once per frame with a local avatar, before the third-person body and skin layers, first-person arms and inventory previews are extracted. Client-side, with a read-only host; it does not change the player's profile, cape, tab-list entry or what other clients see. |
| `resource_pack_status` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's client answered a resource pack in play (vanilla's `ServerboundResourcePackPacket`): `status` is `Action`'s number, 0 successfully loaded, 1 declined, 2 failed download, 3 accepted, 4 downloaded, 5 invalid URL, 6 failed reload, 7 discarded. Heard when the packet arrives, after the server has handled it; it cannot be stopped. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers and `PlayerRef::tell` work. The pack a dedicated server offers in the configuration (`resource-pack` in `server.properties`) is answered before the player has joined, and those answers are not heard. Once for each answer, on a server. |
| `right_click_block` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickBlock` | A player right clicks a block (NeoForge's `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickBlock`): once per hand, where `useItemOn` starts, on the clicking client and on the server (`click` says which, with the block, the hit, the hand and its stack, and the player), for a spectator too (the server then only lets one open a menu). Before the block's own use (a chest, a door, a lever) and the item's (`Item.useOn`, `native_use_on`). |
| `right_click_empty` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickEmpty` | The local player right-clicks empty space with an empty `hand`. Client-side only; the server does not receive this event. |
| `right_click_item` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickItem` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickItem`, immediately before `Item.use`. Cancel skips its Part and vanilla behaviour; its result defaults to `Pass`. `Success`, `SuccessServer` and `Consume` end the client's hand loop. `Success` swings on the client, `SuccessServer` on the server, and `Consume` has no swing. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `screen_char_typed_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Post` | A typed character that neither the receiving screen nor its mounted widgets took (`ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.charTyped` returning false), once per printable character that `screen_char_typed_pre` let through: `c` and `modifiers` as there. Not cancellable (matching NeoForge's `ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Post`, which is an unhandled post-dispatch notice). Window characters (including IME commits and text without a physical key) and the control socket's `type` fire it on the receiving screen when the matching Pre did not cancel. A held edit modifier makes a physical key a shortcut instead of text; a controller's keyboard does not synthesize this hook. |
| `screen_char_typed_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Pre` | A character was typed at the open screen (`ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.charTyped`): `screen` is the screen it is, `c` the character, `modifiers` GLFW's bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8), and `field` a copy of the focused text box. Set `canceled` and that one character never reaches the focused child or text box. |
| `screen_closing` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Closing` | The foreground `screen` is being replaced (`ScreenEvent.Closing`), after `screen_opening` let it. This includes a prompt or editor replaced by chat, and an outgoing chat instance even when the new one has the same ID. |
| `screen_exclusion_areas` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The hook `native_screen_exclusion` asks: a mod adds the rectangles it draws over `screen` (a side panel, a button column) to `areas`, in GUI pixels, `[x, y, width, height]`. NeoForge has no counterpart in the game; recipe viewers ask for it through their own API (`EmiExclusionArea`, JEI's `IGuiContainerHandler.getGuiExtraAreas`). Client-side. |
| `screen_files_dropped` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Files were dropped on the window while a screen is open (vanilla's `Screen.onFilesDrop`, called by `MouseHandler.onDrop` with the whole drop as one list). NeoForge's `ScreenEvent` has no event for it, so this hook has no NeoForge name. `paths` are the dropped files as the operating system gave them, in the order it gave them: a path that is not valid UTF-8 is passed through unchanged, so compare and open paths as `Path`, not as text. The game reads no file; the mod reads the ones it wants. `screen` is the screen that is open when the drop ends, a vanilla screen or a mod's (`screen.mod_screen` is its mod). Observe only: the hook cannot cancel or mark the drop handled, and the game's own handling (the resource-pack screens take a dropped pack) always runs. | `screen_init_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Init.Post` | A screen initialized or rebuilt its children (`ScreenEvent.Init.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.init` / `Screen.rebuildWidgets`). Use `event.widgets` to list and remove children or mount buttons, text fields, and custom widgets. A layout-only resize keeps the existing children and focus and does not fire this hook. A new chat instance initializes once even when it replaces one with the same ID and rectangle. A vetoed opening does not initialize a screen. A panicking listener publishes no edits. The pause and title screens list their own controls first, in vanilla's order. |
| `screen_key_pressed_post` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Post` | A key press that neither the receiving screen nor its mounted widgets took (`ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.keyPressed` returning false): `key`, `repeat` and `modifiers` as in `screen_key_pressed_pre`. If the mod browser's search editor declines, the container still handles raw bindings (including rebound inventory close and sort) before this hook can fire. Slot swap, pick and drop can act before an unhandled result; a translated mapping reached after Post cannot sort the container. Set `canceled` to keep the game's own key handling (bindings, hotbar and toggles) from seeing it. A key that has no vanilla name fires nothing. Fires for window key presses and the control socket's `key ... down`, `key ... repeat`, and the press phase of `key ... tap`, on the receiving screen and only when the matching Pre did not cancel it. A controller does not synthesize this hook. |
| `screen_key_pressed_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Pre` | A key went down over the open screen (`ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.keyPressed`): `screen` is the screen it is, `key` vanilla's name for the key (`key.keyboard.e`) — a key with no name fires nothing — `repeat` whether it is the keyboard's auto-repeat of a held press (Sunrise's extra; NeoForge folds repeats into the event), and `modifiers` GLFW's bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8). Set `canceled` and the press goes no further: no focus move, no Escape-close, no inventory or hotbar key, no typing by the key itself. |
| `screen_key_released_post` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyReleased.Post` | A key release that neither the receiving screen nor its mounted widgets took (`ScreenEvent.KeyReleased.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.keyReleased` returning false): `key` and `modifiers` as in `screen_key_released_pre`. Set `canceled` and the release counts as handled: the game's own key handling never sees it, so a held mapping stays down; cancel a release only when you cancelled the press it ends. A controller does not synthesize this hook. |
| `screen_key_released_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyReleased.Pre` | Before the currently open screen handles a named key's release. Window releases, control-socket `key ... up`, and the release phase of `key ... tap` reach the current receiving screen, even if it did not see the press. Cancel skips the screen handler and Post. A cancelled window Pre still releases the device key and applies mapped keyboard release cleanup. A cancelled socket Pre skips the ordinary control-key release; a held mapping can stay down. Cancelling an unhandled release in Post instead skips normal mapped release on either route, after the screen has declined. |
| `screen_mouse_button_pressed_post` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Post` | A mouse press after the receiving screen handled or declined it (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Post`). Every listener sees `was_click_handled`, the receiving handler's original answer. A hit on the first child counts as handled even when that child declines and does not take focus. |
| `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Pre` | A press about to go to `Screen.mouseClicked` (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Pre`). `screen` is the receiving screen and `screen.mouse` is the pointer position. `button` is the GLFW index (0 left, 1 right, 2 middle, 3 back, 4 forward; beyond those, the platform's own number). `modifiers` are GLFW bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8). The receiver and press Post get the same `double_click`: true for a real press when the last handled real press was of this button on this screen instance less than 250 ms ago. Cancel to skip the screen handler, Post and gameplay mappings; a cancelled real press still counts as handled for double-click detection. |
| `screen_mouse_button_released_post` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Post` | A mouse release after the receiving screen handled or declined it (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Post`). The matching Pre must not have cancelled. `screen`, `button` and `modifiers` come from the release's Pre, not the earlier press or a screen the release opened. Every listener sees `was_release_handled`, the original answer from the receiving handler. Completing quick-craft is not itself a handled release: the child still answers. An early swallowed container release is handled. |
| `screen_mouse_button_released_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Pre` | A mouse button went up over the open screen (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseReleased`), with the same `screen`, `button` and `modifiers` as `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre` and fired at the same point. Set `canceled` and the release goes no further: a pressed slot keeps what it took and no drag, slider or scrollbar lets go. |
| `screen_mouse_dragged_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseDragged.Post` | A held-button pointer movement that the receiving screen (and its mounted widgets) did not handle (`ScreenEvent.MouseDragged.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseDragged` returning false): `button`, `delta` (GUI pixels, `screen.mouse` the new position) and `modifiers` as in `screen_mouse_dragged_pre`. Not cancellable. Fires on the receiving screen only when unhandled and when the matching Pre did not cancel it, on the window only, for real mouse input and not a pad's or keyboard focus's. |
| `screen_mouse_dragged_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseDragged.Pre` | Before a real held-button movement reaches screen drag handlers. `screen.mouse` is the current GUI position; `delta` is its GUI-space movement. | `screen_mouse_moved_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The pointer moved over the open screen (vanilla's `Screen.mouseMoved`, called by `MouseHandler.handleAccumulatedMovement` for every movement, whether or not a button is held, and before `Screen.mouseDragged`). It fires first: with a button held, `screen_mouse_dragged_pre` follows for the same move. Cancel keeps the new cursor position and skips the rest of this move: the drag hook and the screen's drag handlers do not run (Sunrise's screens do no other work per move; hover is read from the cursor when the screen draws). Same receiving screens as `screen_mouse_dragged_pre`, including dialogs, confirmation prompts, books, lecterns and sign editors. A move with no change in position, a controller's or the keyboard focus's pointer, and a screen that holds the pointer grab (`ScreensClientHost::set_pointer_grab`: its motion is `screen_pointer_motion`) fire nothing. With no listener nothing is built. |
| `screen_mouse_scrolled_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Post` | A mouse wheel scroll that the receiving screen (and its mounted widgets) did not handle (`ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseScrolled` returning false): `scroll` and `modifiers` as in `screen_mouse_scrolled_pre`. Not cancellable. Fires on the receiving screen only when unhandled and when the matching Pre did not cancel it, on the window only, for real mouse input and not a pad's or keyboard focus's. |
| `screen_mouse_scrolled_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Pre` | The wheel turned over the open screen (`ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseScrolled`): `screen` is the screen it is, `screen.mouse` where the pointer is (so there is no position of its own), `scroll` the wheel's movement in x and y as the game's own scroll handler scales it (a line times the wheel sensitivity: with Discrete Scrolling on, a notch is ±1, its sign only; a touchpad's pixels are divided by 30), and `modifiers` GLFW's bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8). | `screen_opening` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Opening` | `Gui.setScreen` is about to replace the `current` screen with `new` (`ScreenEvent.Opening`; the screen `playing` is none: vanilla's null). A new chat screen instance can replace another chat screen; `current` is the foreground screen, including a prompt or editor it will replace. The waiting screen and a warning's live return dialog can replace a receiver with the same `dialog` id. `Cancel` keeps its mounted widgets and focus, without emitting Closing or the replacement's Init.Post. A different moment from `screen_mouse_moved_pre`, which reports the cursor's movement in GUI pixels and does not fire while the grab holds. Fires only while that screen holds the grab, for the real pointer: a controller sends no motion, and a zero delta is not reported. Not cancellable. |
| `screen_preedit_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Preedit.Post` | After a screen's preedit handler (`ScreenEvent.Preedit.Post`) and only when no text field took the composition (the handler returned false: vanilla's focused field takes it, so this is a preedit no field wanted) and `screen_preedit_pre` did not cancel. | `screen_preedit_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Preedit.Pre` | Before a screen's preedit handler (`ScreenEvent.Preedit.Pre`, vanilla's `GuiEventListener.preeditUpdated`): the input method is composing `full_text` (empty when the composition ended), with the caret at byte `caret_position`, split into `blocks` of which `focused_block` is the one being converted. | `screen_render_background` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Background` | The open screen is about to draw its contents (`ScreenEvent.Render.Background`, after vanilla's `renderBackground` and before `render`): draw here for what must sit under the screen's widgets, items and tooltips. It does not fire when `screen_render_pre` cancelled. Fires once a frame on the window, for the screens `screen_render_post` covers: a screen of the game's, the title's own screens, Win and Options. Not while playing with no screen, nor for a server dialog, the server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book or a sign editor drawn over gameplay. Draw with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`; `screen.rect` is the screen's container panel (or the whole window). |
| `screen_render_foreground` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Foreground` | The open screen has drawn its contents (`ScreenEvent.Render.Foreground`, vanilla's point before the deferred tooltips and the preedit overlay). Not cancellable. Tooltips, the dragged stack and the preedit overlay draw over this hook; use `screen_render_post` for drawing over the screen's tooltips. It does not fire when `screen_render_pre` cancelled. Fires once a frame on the window, for the screens `screen_render_post` covers: a screen of the game's, the title's own screens, Win and Options. Not while playing with no screen, nor for a server dialog, the server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book or a sign editor drawn over gameplay. Draw with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`; `screen.rect` is the screen's container panel (or the whole window). |
| `screen_render_inventory_mob_effects` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.RenderInventoryMobEffects` | Ahead of the effect panels beside the survival and creative inventories (`ScreenEvent.RenderInventoryMobEffects`, vanilla's `EffectsInInventory.extractRenderState`): `available_space` is the width in GUI pixels right of the container, at least 32 (with no effect to show, or less room, vanilla draws nothing and this does not fire). `compact` picks icons over full panels (vanilla's choice is compact under 120) and `horizontal_offset` shifts the stack right (negative left). Set `canceled` and the panels are not drawn: draw your own with `screen_render_post`. Once a frame while the screen draws them, on the window only; if the effects space changes within a frame (the recipe book opening between two layout builds), it is asked again for the new space. The exclusion areas follow this choice in the same frame. |
| `screen_render_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Post` | The open screen has drawn itself and its tooltips (`Screen.render`, the point of `ScreenEvent.Render.Post`): draw over it with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`, `screen.rect` being its container panel (or the whole window) and `screen.id` the screen it is. Once a frame on the window, never while playing with no screen; `&self`, so it reads and draws only. The Win and Options screens call it on their own path, after their drawing. |
| `screen_render_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Pre` | Before the open screen draws itself (`ScreenEvent.Render.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.renderWithTooltips`): draw to put something under it, or set `canceled` and the screen is not drawn, and neither is its `screen_render_background` or `screen_render_foreground`. The world, the HUD layers and the toasts still draw, and `screen_render_post` still fires. Fires once a frame on the window, for the screens `screen_render_post` covers: a screen of the game's, the title's own screens, Win and Options. Not while playing with no screen, nor for a server dialog, the server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book or a sign editor drawn over gameplay. Draw with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`; `screen.rect` is the screen's container panel (or the whole window). |
| `screen_room` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Room for a mod's own panel right of the open container screen's window, in GUI pixels: `right` starts at 0; raise it to the width your panel takes beside the window, its gap included, and leave it be otherwise. Later mods see earlier mods' answers: keep the larger (`event.right = event.right.max(mine)`). When the window, the recipe book open beside it and that room all fit across the screen, the window and the book move left by half the room (rounded down), so the three stay centred as vanilla's recipe book moves the window right to make room for itself (`RecipeBookComponent.updateScreenPosition`); when they don't fit, nothing moves. This API is right-only: mod panels reserve the right while the existing recipe book occupies the left; left reservations are not part of this hook. The query's `ScreenView::rect` is pre-reservation. Later draw/input views expose the shifted window; check that rectangle's right edge plus the room against the viewport. Keep this query pure and cheap. Its answer is memoized for the frame and screen; recursive queries during dispatch see zero. Asked once a frame while the player's inventory, a container or a mod's menu screen is open, not for the creative inventory, a merchant's or a beacon's screen, nor while the recipe book covers the window of a narrow screen; an answer that changes geometry builds the layout again in the same frame (slots, book and effect panels move with the window). Not finite or below 0 counts as 0. Beyond NeoForge: a mod there reads the container screen's `leftPos` but has no event to move it, so it draws in the space that's left. |
| `screen_transparent_background` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Pre (transparent background)` | `Screen.renderTransparentBackground`: draws the translucent dark background behind an in-game screen (inventory, container, creative, book, sign, editor). Set `canceled` (or return `Flow::Cancel`) to suppress the vanilla dark gradient, allowing a mod to draw its own background or apply menu background blur via `Host::apply_screen_blur`. API @API:screen_transparent_background@. |
| `screenshot` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenshotEvent` | An F2 screenshot has been read back, before either PNG is written (`ScreenshotEvent`). Runs on the client thread with a `ClientHost`. `image` is the in-memory frame (`getImage`): RGBA8, top to bottom, `width * height * 4` bytes. Change it and the normal PNG is that image. A length other than `width * height * 4` writes nothing and reports screenshot failure. The HDR copy is not this image. Change `file_name`, a relative path inside the player's screenshots folder, or set `message` to replace the success chat line. `None` uses the normal translated, clickable line for the final file name. Return `Flow::Cancel` to write no files and post `message`, or the translated cancellation line when `message` is `None`. Invalid paths (including symlinks) report screenshot failure; parent folders are created. HDR copies follow the final name with `-hdr.png`. Agent captures and world icons do not fire this hook. |
| `sculk_bloom` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A sculk catalyst at `pos` has begun blooming after taking experience. The catalyst block state has changed. Server-side. |
| `section_visibility` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | A chunk section in the player's current level is about to mesh: return one `SectionVisibility` or `None`. `input` is the raw section with its one-block border, read before any mod hid a state. `SectionCell::State` gives the canonical `BlockState`. Biome reads give the stored registered key, or `None` for a missing column or an unresolved biome slot. Asked on the client world thread before the section goes to a meshing worker, including remeshes after a neighbouring column arrives or unloads, a resource reload, or `ClientHost::refresh_render_visibility`. A first all-air compile is skipped if no mod contributes section geometry. Other levels drawn through level views do not call this hook. Answers only take away, and every mod's are combined, so no mod brings back what another hid. An answer that breaks a bound is refused whole, logged once, and the section draws without it. With no mod listening, the mesher pays nothing. |
| `server_about_to_start` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerAboutToStartEvent` | The server is about to open its worlds: nothing of the world is loaded yet (NeoForge's `ServerAboutToStartEvent`, "called before the server begins loading anything"). Vanilla's `MinecraftServer.loadLevel` start. Server-side: the single player server thread once Play is pressed, a dedicated server after its listener has bound. A bind failure skips this event and still reaches `server_stopped`. On a successful open this is followed by `level_load`, `server_starting`, and `server_started`; a failed open skips `server_starting`, `server_started`, and `server_stopping`, still runs `stopServer` teardown (`level_unload` for opened levels), and reaches `server_stopped`. `dedicated` tells a dedicated server from the single player one. |
| `server_chat` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerChatEvent` | NeoForge's `ServerChatEvent`: a player's chat message, through their chain, is about to be broadcast. `player_id` is the server's entity id for the player, `player` their UUID, `username` their name and `raw_text` what they typed. `message` starts as that text; replace it and players see the new message (`withUnsignedContent`: a signed message keeps its signature), and the log shows it. Set `cancel` and nobody sees it, and it doesn't count toward the chat spam limit. Commands (`/say`, `/msg`...) are not chat here. Server-side, with no player's state swapped in. A component deeper than 512 nodes refuses capture or the whole writeback; no event field is applied if any Text field fails conversion. |
| `server_started` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStartedEvent` | The server has opened its worlds and is about to run its tick loop (NeoForge's `ServerStartedEvent`): every level is loaded, no player has joined, and the first tick has not run. The integrated server's initial world save has completed before `server_starting` and this notice. It saves the world, not an already joined player. Fired once per successful open; failed startup skips it but still reaches `server_stopped`. A listener panic does not undo the initial save or fail startup. It discards that callback's journaled server writes, including nested commits, but not effects made outside the Host transaction journal. `dedicated` distinguishes a dedicated from an integrated server. |
| `server_starting` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStartingEvent` | Fired once on both dedicated and integrated servers after configured world and level loading (`level_load`), adopted datapack and text setup, spawn preparation, and the initial world save, before `server_started` and before accepting play (matching NeoForge's `ServerStartingEvent` at the end of `DedicatedServer.initServer` and `IntegratedServer.initServer`). Does not fire if startup fails before that point; a failed startup still runs teardown and reaches `server_stopped`. |
| `server_stopped` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStoppedEvent` | Fired once at the end of server teardown (NeoForge's `ServerStoppedEvent` via `ServerLifecycleHooks.handleServerStopped`), after `stopServer` completes (network admission stop, player save and disconnect, final world save for an adopted world, per-level close and `level_unload` when the save and level close succeed, and storage release), on both integrated and dedicated servers. Always fires once `runServer` enters, even when startup failed (including a dedicated TCP port bind failure before `server_about_to_start`), the tick loop errored or panicked, the final save failed, or a shutdown listener panicked. The world's Server configs are still loaded; `config_unloading` follows this event. Failed opens save only worlds already adopted by the server (the prepared level is installed; the session lock alone is not adoption). |
| `server_stopping` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStoppingEvent` | Fired once at the start of an orderly server shutdown (NeoForge's `ServerStoppingEvent` in `MinecraftServer.runServer`, after the tick loop exits normally and before `stopServer`), before network shutdown, player disconnect and save, final world save, level unloads, and `server_stopped`. Worlds are still loaded and players are still connected so a mod can save its own data or notify them. Not fired on a failed startup or when the tick loop exits with an error or panic: those paths jump directly to `stopServer` teardown and `server_stopped`. |
| `server_tick` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerTickEvent.Pre, ServerTickEvent.Post` | The authoritative server tick's phases: `ServerTickStart` before any level is ticked (NeoForge's `ServerTickEvent.Pre`), `ServerTickEnd` after the last (`.Post`). Runs on integrated and dedicated servers only. To read a loaded block, resolve the dimension with `host.level(dimension)` and call `level.block_state(host, pos)` on the returned handle. A paused pass fires nothing; a frozen world carries `world_running` false except during a requested step. `host.server_has_time()` says whether optional work still fits before the next tick is due (`ServerTickEvent.hasTime`). |
| `server_tick_rate_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `MinecraftServer.onTickRateChanged`, before the server adjusts its autosave wait. Runs after every successful tick-rate write, including an equal value, before the server sends the resulting clock state. Also runs when a `/tick sprint` ends: on its own, by `/tick sprint stop` or by `/tick freeze`. Read the current rate with `host.tick_stats()`. Server-only: a client receiving the rate or reporting a sprint does not fire it. |
| `setting_changed` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `ModConfigEvent.Reloading` | A registered setting changed through a typed write, Options > Mods, or a settings reload. Runs on the side that changed the value, after publishing it. Only the setting's owner hears it. `value` is the new value. The listener gets a read-only `Host`: the write that caused the notice is already committed, and a listener that wrote its own key from here would notify itself without end. Make a follow-up write from the callback that made the first one. |
| `sign_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player finished editing a sign: `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.updateSignText` has taken their update packet and every check has passed (they were editing that sign, within reach, the sign is not waxed), right before the lines go on the sign. `pos` in `dimension`, the `front` or back side, and the four `lines` as written, plain text with the formatting codes still in (vanilla strips them afterwards). Rewrite `lines` (made four lines again) and that is what the sign gets, or set `cancel` and the sign keeps its old text. With the player's state swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` and the helpers that act on the player reach them. Once for each update a player sends on a server. Not told: the host's own editing of a sign on a LAN or single-player world (the editor writes into their world directly), and the sign changes that are no edit of the text (dyeing, glow ink, waxing, `/data`, `/setblock`, or a mod using `BlockEntityRef::replace_data`). |
| `sleep_finished_time` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `SleepFinishedTimeEvent` | Enough players have slept through the night in level `dimension` and the clock is about to move to the morning (NeoForge's `SleepFinishedTimeEvent`, vanilla's `ServerLevel.tick` when it moves the clock to `wake_up_from_sleep`). `adjustment` says where the clock goes and may be changed: it comes in as `Marker(minecraft:wake_up_from_sleep)`, vanilla's own, and a mod may set `Absolute`, `Relative` (from `current`, the day time now) or another `Marker`; a later mod reads what an earlier one left. `new_time` is the morning vanilla resolved, read only, for a mod that computes from it. Contract version `2`: in version `1`, `new_time` was the value a mod wrote; a time is now written as `Absolute`. `current` and the result are times of the level's own clock (its dimension type's default clock, else the Overworld's). The game resolves `adjustment` once after every mod: a time before `current`, a time past `i64::MAX`, or a marker the level's clock does not define is refused, as `setTimeAddition` refuses one, and the clock moves to the morning. Only the Overworld's clock defines markers today, so on any other clock every `Marker`, vanilla's included, falls back to the morning. When even the morning is past `i64::MAX`, `new_time` is `current` and the clock stays. On the server, only when the `advance_time` rule is on. Set `canceled` to leave the clock where it is; the players wake and weather resets as usual either way. This is the only wake-up clock event. |
| `sleep_skip_night` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Enough players sleep in level `dimension` to skip the night (vanilla's `ServerLevel.tick`, once `SleepStatus.areEnoughSleeping` and `areEnoughDeepSleeping` both pass): the game is about to move the clock to the morning when `advance_time` is on (`sleep_finished_time`), wake every sleeping player, and end the rain when `advance_weather` is on. `percentage` is the `players_sleeping_percentage` game rule, `active` the players that count toward sleeping (not spectators) and `sleeping` those in bed. Asked once per tick while this holds, on the server, before the clock moves. |
| `sleep_status_announce` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The players or the sleepers of level `dimension` changed, and the game is about to show everyone the action-bar line "n/m players sleeping", or "Sleeping through this night" once enough are in bed (vanilla's `ServerLevel.announceSleepStatus`, reached from `updateSleepingPlayerList`). Asked after vanilla's own checks passed: the `players_sleeping_percentage` rule allows sleeping through nights (`canSleepThroughNights`) and the world is not a single-player one that is not published. It is not asked when nothing changed or the line would not be sent. |
| `sleepers_needed` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | How many players must sleep in level `dimension` to skip the night: vanilla's `SleepStatus.sleepersNeeded`. `needed` arrives with vanilla's answer, `percentage` (the `players_sleeping_percentage` game rule) of the `active` players that count toward sleeping (not spectators), rounded up and at least 1; set it to your own count for this level and tick. An answer below 1 counts as 1. Asked on the server wherever vanilla reads the count: the night-skip check of `ServerLevel.tick` (`areEnoughSleeping` and `areEnoughDeepSleeping`, before `sleep_skip_night`) and the "n/m players sleeping" announcement, which shows the answered count. Not asked when `percentage` is over 100, where vanilla never skips the night. Asked each tick, so answer from what you already hold; don't change the world here. |
| `sound_engine_load` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `SoundEngineLoadEvent` | The sound engine opened its output (`SoundEngineLoadEvent`): once at window start, after each resource reload (with the `context` of the new resource generation) and when the output device changes. Nothing fires with no sound output (no device, or audio turned off). Client-side only. |
| `spawn_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The world's spawn point changed to `pos` in level `dimension`, facing `angle`. |
| `sponge_absorb` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | A sponge at `pos` absorbed water and became wet. Server-side. |
| `spread_players_teleport` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.SpreadPlayersCommand` | A server entity is about to be placed by `/spreadplayers` (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent.SpreadPlayersCommand`, vanilla's `SpreadPlayersCommand.setPlayerPositions`): once per entity, after the spread worked out its spot. `entity` is its network id, `player` its UUID (None for a non-player), `from` where it stands and `target` the spot. A mod may move `target` (a value that is not a number is ignored). Canceling leaves the entity where it is; the spread's other entities, its random draws and its feedback are unchanged. |
| `standalone_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `ModelEvent.RegisterStandalone` | Models no blockstate names, registered once on the client before resources load. Use `StandaloneModelsEvent::register_model` for plain files or `StandaloneModelsEvent::register` to bind a baker and its file dependencies. Every resource load rebakes both from the active pack files. Failed bakes publish no model and log the registering mod. Look up results through `RenderAssets::model` in or after `model_baking_completed`. |
| `submit_custom_geometry` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `SubmitCustomGeometryEvent` | Submit unlit geometry after particle submission, before opaque rendering (NeoForge 26.3 `SubmitCustomGeometryEvent`, vanilla `LevelRenderer.submitFeatures`). Client-only, noncancellable. `view` lends the frame's `Camera`, world partial tick, game time, improved-transparency state and visible `RenderableSection` slice. Submit from extracted state. |
| `substitute_inline_images` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Propose inline images for one nonempty, translated text run in chat history or on either face of a sign, after legacy formatting and before wrapping, measuring and bidi reordering. Existing inline objects are not passed to the hook. The `run` is one resolved style span; ranges cannot cross spans or line breaks. Push UTF-8 byte ranges into `images`, in priority order. The first valid, nonoverlapping proposal wins in hook dispatch order. Missing sprites and invalid ranges are ignored, leaving the original characters available to later proposals. |
| `sweep_attack` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `SweepAttackEvent` | NeoForge's `SweepAttackEvent`: a player's damaging melee hit on a `target` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:oak_boat`) is about to sweep or not, after `critical_hit`. `vanilla_sweep` is `Player.isSweepAttack` (a charged, grounded, not critical sword hit below the speed limit); `sweeping` starts as it and may force or stop the sweep; `canceled` skips the later mods and keeps `sweeping` as it is (a mod doing its own sweep cancels with `sweeping` false). A critical hit that sweeps has both sounds. Fires on the Game that runs the attack, and on the attacking client, which only asks. |
| `tab_complete` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest asked to complete `command`; owned API `Suggestion` records may be added to or filtered from `suggestions`. `range` is the replaced byte range. The game snapshots completions only when a listener subscribes. |
| `tags_updated` | both | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `TagsUpdatedEvent` | Tags changed, and what depends on them should be rebuilt (NeoForge's `TagsUpdatedEvent`); `cause` says how. The place to drop caches derived from the data and to rebind what depends on it. |
| `target_hit` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A projectile hit the target block at `pos`, which now has `strength` output power. Server-side. |
| `teleport_command` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.TeleportCommand` | A server entity is about to be teleported by `/tp` or `/teleport` (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent.TeleportCommand`, in `TeleportCommand.performTeleport`, once per teleported entity): `dimension` is the level it arrives in, `entity` its network id, `from` its feet now and `to` where the command sends it. A mod may move `to` (a value that is not a number is ignored). Canceling leaves the entity where it is; the command still reports success. `entity_teleport` and `player_teleported` hear it after. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `texture_atlas_stitched` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `TextureAtlasStitchedEvent` | An atlas was stitched and published (`TextureAtlasStitchedEvent`): `atlas` names it. Fires once per atlas, the blocks' first and then the GUI's, during atlas publication before the model manager applies. Model lookups still see the previous generation (none at startup). It precedes `client_setup` on startup and `client_resources_reloaded` on later reloads. Listeners ordered after the model manager apply after both stitches. A refused prepare fires nothing. Look the sprites a mod draws up here, once, and keep what it finds until the next stitch of that atlas: `texture_sprites` for `TextureAtlas::Blocks`, `gui_sprite` for `TextureAtlas::Gui`. Never look them up by name every frame. A sprite of an atlas the mod registered (`register_texture_atlases`) is stitched into its own texture, not the shared GUI atlas: the `AtlasRegion` `gui_sprite` answers for it holds UVs local to that texture, so draw it by its name (`TextureAtlasRegistration::sprite_name`), which selects the owning texture, and never combine its UVs with another sprite's atlas. Client-side. A panic rolls back the callback's journaled writes; the mod is reported and its callback skipped for the session without suppressing other mods' notifications. |
| `tick_target_millis` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | How many milliseconds a client tick lasts (`Minecraft.getTickTargetMillis`, the target the window's `DeltaTracker.Timer` divides the frame's time by): `millis` comes in as vanilla's (50 at 20 ticks a second, longer when the server's `/tick rate` is slower); set it to speed up or slow down the client's fixed-step clock, a "timer". Asked once a frame, in menus too, before the frame's client ticks run (at most ten a frame). An answer that is not a finite number above zero keeps vanilla's. Only the window's clock changes: a server still ticks at its own rate. Client-side. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `time_skip` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A manual clock write is about to move the clock: `cause` is `command` for `/time` or `mod` for a level clock setter. Set `cancel` to keep `from`. Waking sleepers uses `sleep_finished_time` alone. |
| `tnt_primed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | TNT at `pos` is about to be lit (primed): a flint and steel or a fire charge on it, fire burning it, a redstone signal, an arrow, or a dispenser's flint and steel or TNT item. `player` is the server player whose click lit it, with their state swapped in (so `PlayerRef::tell` answers them), None when no player is behind it (fire, redstone, an arrow, a dispenser). Set `cancel` and it is not primed: a TNT block stays where it is (one that fire or a signal had already taken out is put back, without a block update), a dispenser's TNT item stays in it. Not asked: TNT set off by a blast (`explosion_detonate` spares blocks), a TNT minecart and a sulfur cube. Server-side and in single player. |
| `toast_add` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ToastAddEvent` | A client toast is about to enter its queue (`ToastAddEvent`). Return `Flow::Cancel` to discard only this candidate, before slot allocation, animation or sound. `kind` identifies its family; music is `Other`. Replace `title` and `description` to change the displayed components. For advancements the title is the frame label and the description is the advancement name. Existing recipe/system toasts updated in place do not fire another add event. Requests from `ClientHost::add_toast` enter as `ToastKind::Mod`, including requests that would replace an existing mod toast. Integrated-server error notices are new system toast candidates on the owning client, not on the server or its guests. Pending chunk I/O reports stay with that window when the world closes. Client-lib and title producers prepare candidates for the game to admit with a `ClientHost` before applying. Diagnostics naming a mod's panicking callback are not candidates for this hook and cannot be edited or cancelled. |
| `trade_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected trade `index` from `villager` in the trading menu. |
| `trade_with_villager` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `TradeWithVillagerEvent` | A server player's take from a villager or wandering trader, once per trade, after its statistic and `villager_trade` criterion. `merchant` is the trader's entity id, `kind` its type, and `offer` the post-take snapshot: the use is counted and the result is what that iteration took. `host.players().acting_player()` names the player. |
| `use_item_on_block` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `UseItemOnBlockEvent` | `UseItemOnBlockEvent`: after a phase was chosen, before its Part or vanilla behaviour. Cancel skips that behaviour and returns `cancellation_result` (initially `TryWithEmptyHand`). In `ItemBeforeBlock`, `Pass` continues to the block, skipping the item's `on_item_use_first` Part; any result other than `Pass` ends the block interaction. A block-phase `TryWithEmptyHand` can still try the empty-hand behaviour. On a server-registered cauldron click, the client waits for block fallthrough before `ItemAfterBlock`; a consuming server item result doesn't skip this local phase. Other clicks can predict vanilla on a client without the mod; the server corrects it. |
| `vanilla_game_event` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `VanillaGameEvent` | A vanilla game event is about to reach its listeners (NeoForge's `VanillaGameEvent`, vanilla's `Level.gameEvent`): every event, in `dimension`. `event` is its id (`minecraft:step`), `pos` where it happened and `cause` the network id of the entity that caused it (None for none). `affected_state` is the block state the event touched (`GameEvent.Context.affectedState`, with `cause` its `sourceEntity`): the block a player broke or placed, the block a mob stepped on; None where vanilla passes none (`Context.of(entity)`). Canceling keeps the sculk sensors, wardens and allays from hearing it. A dispenser fluid transfer with no open transaction in its level publishes before overflow insertion or preparation. With a caller-owned transaction in that level, publication waits for the outermost commit. Server-side. |
| `vault_change_state` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A vault at `pos` changed to `state`. Its block state is updated. Server-side. |
| `vault_loot` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A vault at `pos` is about to eject a loot item. |
| `vehicle_create` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A boat or minecart was created at `pos`, after it joined the level. `kind` is the vehicle type. Server-side. |
| `vehicle_damage` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A vehicle is about to take damage from a player's attack. `kind` is `boat` or the minecart type; mods may change `damage` or set `cancel` to leave its damage and wobble unchanged. The attack itself still completes. Server-side. |
| `vehicle_destroy` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A boat or minecart is about to be destroyed at `pos`. TNT minecarts that prime instead of breaking are not heard. Server-side. |
| `vehicle_moved` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A boat or minecart (`kind`, `minecraft:oak_boat`) moved since the last tick (`VehicleMoveEvent`): `from` and `to` are its positions, heard after the tick moved it. Once a tick for each vehicle that carries a server player, whose state is then swapped in (`host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them; `player` is their UUID), and for one a server player rode and left, until it has stood still for ten seconds (`player` is none, nobody is swapped in). It only hears. Cheap when nothing moves: one position compare a tick for each watched vehicle. Not heard: a vehicle moved before anyone rode it, a mount (horse, pig...) or a vehicle carrying only a mob. Server-side, in the level the vehicle is in. |
| `viewed_level_sound` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | A sound of a level the server opened for viewing (#3716) reached this client, and the client asks where in the player's own level to play it. `level` is the viewed level's handle (what `host.level` gives for it) and `position` the sound's position there; `sound`, `source` and `seed` are the server's. `play_at` starts as None, and a sound left None is not played. Set it to a position in the player's level and the sound plays there, as the player's own level plays a sound packet (no distance delay), with the `volume` and `pitch` the listeners leave and the same seed. A non-finite `play_at` is not a placement. Volume and pitch are passed to the sound engine without a separate veto. Only a server of `sunrise:levels` revision 3 sends these. A sound bound to an entity of the viewed level has that entity's position there and plays at `play_at` without following it. Sounds of the player's own level ask `play_level_sound_at_position` and `play_level_sound_at_entity`, never this hook. With no listener the sound is dropped after one check, with nothing decoded or allocated. Client-side. Diverges from upstream: new hook, a Sunrise addition with no NeoForge counterpart. |
| `village_siege` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `VillageSiegeEvent` | A village siege found a spawn position on the server. Set `canceled` to stop the siege. There is one siege per dimension. |
| `visuals` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The world frame's local, world-space rim and colored-light sources. Push requests; Sunrise validates and limits them before rendering. Called only while a world is visible. |
| `weather_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Before an explicit server-wide weather parameter write by `/weather`, a mod or a GameTest weather environment. GameTest saves its level's actual weather type and proposes it again on teardown. `dimension` is the origin of the write, not its scope. `clear_ticks` is the requested clear timer and `weather_ticks` sets both the rain and thunder timers. These timers and the `raining` and `thundering` flags are read-only proposals: editing your event copy changes neither the write nor what other listeners see. Natural weather ticks and direct strength changes do not fire this hook. |
| `whitelist_state_update` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The dedicated server's whitelist changed to `enabled`. |
| `wind_burst` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Before a wind burst interacts with blocks, `wind_burst(dimension, center, blocks)` receives the shuffled candidate positions. Remove positions to spare those blocks; entity knockback and the shuffle's random draws are unchanged. Not called when mob_griefing prevents a breeze from interacting with blocks. Bundles need "hooks": @API:wind_burst@. |
| `window_resize` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `WindowResizeEvent` | After a window resize or applying a changed GUI scale, fullscreen, or Unicode font setting. `width` and `height` are physical pixels; `gui_width` and `gui_height` are scaled GUI pixels. Fires only when physical or scaled dimensions change. |
| `world_border_bounds_change_finish` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | When a moving server border reaches its target size at the start of `level`'s normal tick, after the server's tick functions, including on an idle parked level. Starting or replacing an extent does not finish it. Read the final bounds with `event.level.world_border(host)`. A panic is logged with the mod's ID, shown in an error toast and disables just that mod's callback for the session. Nested dispatch skips callbacks for this hook already running on the thread. Other refusals are logged each time. |
| `world_border_bounds_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | After the server sets `level`'s border size. `lerp_ticks` is `None` for a fixed size and `Some(requested_ticks)` for a timed write, including a timed equal-size write. A timed equal-size write doesn't start a moving extent. Loads and client packets do not fire this hook. A panic is logged with the mod's ID, shown in an error toast and disables just that mod's callback for the session. Nested dispatch skips callbacks for this hook already running on the thread. Other refusals are logged each time. |
| `world_border_center_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | After the server sets `level`'s border center, including an equal-value direct write. Read the named border with `event.level.world_border(host)`. Loads and client packets do not fire this hook. A panic is logged with the mod's ID, shown in an error toast and disables just that mod's callback for the session. Nested dispatch skips callbacks for this hook already running on the thread. Other refusals are logged each time. |
| `world_saved` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The server completed a world save cycle. `wait` says chunk writes were flushed before returning. This includes the integrated server's initial save before `server_starting`; the final save comes after player removal. A listener panic discards that listener's journaled writes and disables this callback for the session. It does not fail the save or skip level close. |
| `xp_orb_targeting` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `XpOrbTargetingEvent` | An experience orb looks for a player to follow (NeoForge's `XpOrbTargetingEvent`, `ExperienceOrb.followNearbyPlayer`), on a server. `following` starts as the default: the nearest living player within `scan_distance` (8) blocks who isn't a spectator, or `None`. Set another player, or `None` to follow no one. The orb only goes for a living player who isn't a spectator, in its level, and only moves once they are within 8 blocks. Sunrise looks again on every orb tick (vanilla keeps the player it follows while they stay within 8 blocks), so this is asked every tick for each orb: answer fast. |

## Part hooks

### `BlockEntityPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `block_entity` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | Initial saved data for a declared block entity (`EntityBlock.newBlockEntity`), fired inside the write that creates it (`LevelChunk.setBlockState`), on a server and on a client of one. `state` is the actual placed state for a block declared through `stateful_block_entity`; its properties are available through the Host's state registry. The declaration owns the type id. No answer creates no entity for a state-aware factory; other declared types start with an empty compound. The level is read-only, as `newBlockEntity` gets no level. |
| `block_entity_client_tick` | client | listen | — | — | A client tick of this client's copy of the mod block entity (`ClientLevel.tickBlockEntities`), when its type is yours and its registered type's ticker accepts this state on the client: once per client tick while the level runs, in vanilla's ticker order among the others, whether or not the copy is drawn. `event.subject` is a `BlockEntityClientScope`: borrow a read-only `BlockEntityReadView` through `with_view` for the resident copy's handle, position, level, block state, data slots, item slots and merged NBT (`block_entity_update_tag`), and borrow `ClientHost` sequentially through `host()` to start, stop and fade sounds or add particles. Never called on a server, and never again for a copy removed or whose chunk unloaded. |
| `block_entity_data_received` | client | listen | — | — | The synchronized `tag` has been merged into this client's block entity. `source` distinguishes chunk data from a later update packet. Empty chunk compounds send no notice; empty update-packet compounds do. Absent tags send no notice. Called once for each accepted tag, only for a matching mod block entity that the client created. `event.subject` is a `BlockEntityClientReadScope`: `with_view` lends a read-only view of the resident block entity with the merged data already applied, and `host()` borrows `&dyn ClientHost`. This notice cannot change the received tag. Missing tags and vanilla block entities call nothing. Related NeoForge receive paths `IBlockEntityExtension.onDataPacket` and `handleUpdateTag` perform loading; they are not counterparts of this notice, which runs after the merge. |
| `block_entity_load_additional` | server | listen | — | — | The engine applied saved data to this mod block entity on the logical server (`BlockEntity.loadAdditional`). `tag` is the applied mod payload, and `event.subject` is a `BlockEntityLevelReadScope`: `with_view` lends a read-only `BlockEntityReadView` carrying the instance's handle, `worldPosition`, level, block state and updated data, and `host()` borrows `&dyn LevelHost`, so a listener can store the applied data under its dimension and position, also after a later `/data merge`. The notice cannot change the tag or write to the world. On a holder without declared inventory slots, `tag` includes any saved `Items` field as mod-owned NBT, even rows that look like canonical item stacks. Lists keep their saved order. For a declared inventory, modeled stacks remain separate from the mod payload. |
| `block_entity_on_load` | both | listen | — | `IBlockEntityExtension.onLoad` | The mod block entity has been added to its level, with saved data already applied, before its first tick (`IBlockEntityExtension.onLoad`). Fires once for each instance on both logical sides, including placement. A chunk loaded again creates new instances and calls this hook again. On a server, `block_entity_load_additional` reports the saved-data application first. `event.subject` is a `BlockEntitySideScope`: `Server(scope)` lends a mutable `BlockEntityView` and `&mut dyn ServerHost` sequentially, while `Client(scope)` lends a read-only `BlockEntityReadView` and `&mut dyn ClientHost` sequentially. `block_entity_save_additional` is the separate server-side serialization hook. |
| `block_entity_render_bounding_box` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IBlockEntityRendererExtension.getRenderBoundingBox` | Where the mod block entity at `pos` draws, for culling (NeoForge's `getRenderBoundingBox`): `bounds` is min x, y, z then max x, y, z, in blocks from the block's lower north-west corner. It starts as the block with a block of margin around it; widen it when your `block_entity_submit` draws further (a tall shaft, a beam), or set an infinite value for one never culled. Asked once a frame for each within its view distance (`block_entity_view_distance`) whose chunk is drawn. `entity` borrows the same resident data as `block_entity_submit`. |
| `block_entity_save_additional` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Write live mod state into `tag`, a copy of the instance's stored payload (`BlockEntity.saveAdditional`). Edits affect only the output; the entity, its inventory, identity, position, and engine-managed attachments and shapes are not changed. Parts of the entity's type run in dispatch order. For a holder without declared inventory slots, `tag` holds any persisted `Items` as mod-owned NBT. Canonical-looking item rows keep their saved list order; they are not reconstructed in slot order. The listener may replace or remove this field. Declared slots remain engine-owned inventory, serialized by the engine after the listeners run. `event.pos` and `event.level` are the instance's own `worldPosition` and level, read-only and filled by the engine, so a listener can find its live state by dimension and position, including during unload or a removed block's loot roll. Every call has a level: the hook is not asked without one. Malformed payloads, including overlong modified-UTF-8 names and strings, fall back to the stored payload before any output is used. If the region writer cannot encode or admit the complete chunk record, it retries that snapshot with stored block-entity payloads instead of listener edits. |
| `block_entity_submit` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterRenderers` | Draw the mod block entity at `pos`, of block `block` (`mymod:machine`), on this client: once a frame while it is in view (vanilla's `BlockEntityRenderer.submit`: within its view distance, 64 blocks unless `block_entity_view_distance` says otherwise, its chunk faded in, and its bounds, `block_entity_render_bounding_box`, in the frustum), when its `id` is yours. `state` is the block's state (`blockEntity.getBlockState()`: read its `facing` with `native_block_state_property`), `entity` holds what the server sent (`block_entity_update_tag`) without copying it. Add geometry with `draw.quad`, `draw.cube`, `draw.block` (taking a resolved `BlockState`) and `draw.item` under `draw`'s pose stack, which starts at the block's lower north-west corner, in blocks; `draw` also has the partial tick, the game time and the block's light. When `DrawContext::draw_scene` reuses this renderer, `entity` is supplied by the scene, the draw is full bright, and `pos` is scene-local, not a position to look up in the client level. |
| `block_entity_tick` | server | listen | — | — | A server tick of a resident mod block entity of your registered `kind`. The game skips the callback if the current block does not support that entity type, as in `LevelChunk.BoundTickingBlockEntity.tick`. The first invalid visit logs a warning; a valid tick clears it for the next episode. `event.subject.with_view` lends its live storage. To reach the level or another object, return from the loan and call `event.subject.host()`. Ask for another loan afterwards; it returns `None` if the level, chunk load, generation or block-entity type validity (`BlockEntityType.isValid` for the live block state) no longer matches. In-place block-state updates to another valid state remain borrowable with the live state. For a Part-state edit, first get the handle with `event.subject.with_view(\|view\| view.handle())`, then borrow `event.subject.host()` to call `edit_state`. Keep a handle only to use across calls. |
| `block_entity_update_tag` | server | replace | — | — | What clients get of the block entity at `pos` when its `id` is yours (`BlockEntity.getUpdateTag`): set `tag` to it, read from `entity` alone. Asked on the server whenever a chunk holding it is sent and whenever its block changes, on the server thread; a client's copy of the block entity (made by `block_entity` there) merges it into its `nbt`. Leave it unset to send no tag of yours. A block change puts the one-shot fields of `BlockEntityView::set_client_update_fields` over it, and sends them alone when it is unset. |
| `block_entity_view_distance` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | How far from the camera the mod block entity of block `block` draws, in blocks (vanilla's `BlockEntityRenderer.getViewDistance`): 64 unless a part answers. Set `distance` for your own block: further for something seen from afar (a beacon-like beam, a large display), nearer for small detail. It is asked once for each mod block, the first time this client draws block entities, and kept: never per frame. A value that isn't finite and above 0 is ignored. Beyond NeoForge: the renderer's own method, no event. |
| `can_place_item` | server | replace | — | — | `Container.canPlaceItem`: whether `slot` of this mod block entity's container admits the offered nonempty `stack`. `Some(true)` or `Some(false)` replaces the declared `ModContainer::insert_rules` answer; `None` keeps it. A subscribed slot refuses insertion if the entity snapshot, block state or offered stack cannot be represented. Face, input-slot and stack limits still apply. |
| `can_take_item_through_face` | server | replace | `&dyn Host` | — | `WorldlyContainer.canTakeItemThroughFace`: whether the nonempty `stack` in `slot` of this mod block entity's container may leave through `face`. `Some(true)` or `Some(false)` replaces the declared answer, which is whether `ModContainer::outputs` lists the slot; `None` keeps it. A subscribed container refuses the take if the entity snapshot, block state or stack cannot be represented. The face's slots (`ModContainer::faces`, `getSlotsForFace`) still bound which slots are asked. |
| `menu_quick_move` | server | cancel | — | — | A player shift-clicked slot `slot` of the menu of the mod block entity (`AbstractContainerMenu.quickMoveStack`, the method a Java menu overrides), on the server, before the game's own routing: move the stack where your block wants it. `subject` lends sequential access to the live resident block entity (`subject.with_view(...)`), the server `Host` (`subject.host()`), and atomic transactions (`subject.transaction(...)`); `with_view` returns `None` if the block entity was removed or replaced during the call. `slot` is the menu's index: below `container_slots` it is a container slot (the block's own, a linked slot, or a crafting result); from there on the player's inventory (27 main slots, then the hotbar). After the live slot allows pickup, `stack` copies what it holds. Take what you move out of `stack` and return `Flow::Cancel`. The same source keeps the remainder (`None` empties it) and the client gets the menu again. A result handler consumes its own inputs; the remainder stays available for the inputs it leaves until the grid changes. Left unhandled, the game undoes the listener's journaled writes before routing the same menu click without a second pickup check. A later Part sees accepted proposals. |
| `on_chunk_unloaded` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `IBlockEntityExtension.onChunkUnloaded` | The chunk holding this mod block entity is unloading (`IBlockEntityExtension.onChunkUnloaded`): told once for each of its mod block entities, on each side that holds a copy of it, before the copy is dropped. `pos` is where it stands, `block_entity` its handle (the one `host.block_entity` gave while it was loaded) and `entity` its data. Release what your mod keeps for it here. Use the borrowed `entity` to read its data while the row leaves the world; the handle identifies it, but need not resolve through the Host during eviction. As with `chunk_unload`, changes made in it aren't saved: the chunk was saved before it unloads. Not told when the world closes, or on a client when its level goes (a respawn, a dimension change, leaving). |
| `pre_remove_side_effects` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | On the authoritative server, the block at `pos` is being replaced. `state` is its outgoing state. Read the retiring block entity through `entity`; its attachments are still available through the level host. Use `effects` to suppress the base container scatter, scatter additional slots, spawn positioned item entities or award experience. An invalid contribution does not change any other contribution. |
| `stateful_block_entity` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | Opt a block with a registered block entity type into state-aware creation. Asked once per block when a level's immutable factory ownership table is built. Set true and never clear an earlier answer. For these blocks, `block_entity` receives the actual state and no answer creates no entity. Existing entities survive property changes within the same block. |

### `BlockPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `add_destroy_effects` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `IClientBlockExtensions.addDestroyEffects` | Asked on the client before a non-air state's destruction debris. `state` is the destroyed state, not what the client now sees at `pos`; an invisible state can still be asked. Return `Flow::Cancel` to replace or omit the burst, or `Flow::Continue` for the normal burst (even if the state normally suppresses terrain particles). The break sound is independent. `random` is the client level's random. |
| `add_hit_effects` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `IClientBlockExtensions.addHitEffects` | Asked on the client for the non-air, visible block being hit, after its optional hit sound and before any outline sampling. Return `Flow::Cancel` to replace or omit the crumb, or `Flow::Continue` for the normal crumb (even if the state normally suppresses terrain particles). The supplied face is the one being hit. `random` is the level's random, also used by a call to `LevelRef::add_breaking_particles` during this callback. |
| `adjacent_block_path_type` | server | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getAdjacentBlockPathType` | The optional contribution of the neighbour `event.state` at `event.pos` (`IBlockExtension.getAdjacentBlockPathType`). `event.path_type` is that neighbour's own classified type, not the node's type. The readonly host reads the owning level. `Some` immediately becomes the node's type and ends the scan, even if equal to its current type; `None` keeps that neighbour's fluid and vanilla fallback, never suppressing it. Vanilla water contributes through its classified path type: a solid waterlogged block remains `Blocked`. Custom fluids default to `WaterBorder`. Neighbours are visited x, then y, then z, each from -1 to 1, excluding the node's own column. No mob is supplied. These answers run after static classification; per-search evaluator caches remain. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `affect_neighbors_after_removal` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | `Block.affectNeighborsAfterRemoval` for a mod's block: an authoritative server removed the block `state` from `pos` (`moved`: a piston pushed it) and has run its template class's own updates. Push onto `updates` the neighbour updates it wants, in order: `OutputSignal` (`updateNeighbourForOutputSignal`), `Neighbours` (`updateNeighborsAt`) and `NeighboursExcept(direction)` (`updateNeighborsAtExceptFromFacing`); the level runs each at once, as the block's own override would. Answered from inside the level's write: it reads, and cannot write blocks or spawn entities. |
| `analog_output` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | `BlockState.getAnalogOutputSignal`: the signal a comparator reads from the block `state` at `pos`, `face` the side that touches the comparator (or the conductor in front of it). `output` holds vanilla's answer: change it when the block is yours (0 to 15; anything else is clamped). Asked only for blocks `has_analog_output` answered for, from inside the level's update: don't change the world here. When the answer changes without the block's state changing, call `level.update_neighbors(host, pos, state, NeighbourUpdate::OutputSignal)`; a change of state through `LevelRef::set_block` or `LevelRef::set_block_property` (or `LevelWriteHost` inside a block tick, with neighbour updates on) tells the comparators itself, as vanilla's `Level.setBlock` does. Authoritative servers only. Version 2 supplies `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost`: use `container_signal` for vanilla's comparator read, which may unpack unopened loot. The level's random stream remains in vanilla order. Other world edits stay forbidden. |
| `animate_tick` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | A client's random display tick of the block `state` at `pos` (`Block.animateTick`): `ClientLevel.animateTick` picked `pos` among the 667 random positions within 16 and the 667 within 32 blocks of the player it samples each client tick. Add particles with `host.add_particle` and play sounds with `host.play_local_sound`: a torch's smoke, a furnace's flames, redstone ore's dust. `level` is the client's level, `random` its random (`ClientLevel.random`, the one vanilla lends `animateTick`), so a port keeps Java's odds: `random.next_float() < 0.04` as `random.nextFloat() < 0.04f`. Only parts on the picked state's block are called, inside the pick: after the block's own vanilla effects, before its fluid's and the next pick. A block with no part answering costs the sampler one table lookup. Never called on a server, nor for a screen's scene. A resource pack's Polytone `disable_particles` on the block stops the part's call too, as it stops the block's own. |
| `beacon_color_multiplier` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getBeaconColorMultiplier, IBlockStateExtension.getBeaconColorMultiplier` | The colour the block `state` at `pos` gives the beam of the beacon at `beacon_pos` (`IBlockExtension.getBeaconColorMultiplier`), asked as the beacon builds its beam sections one block at a time. `color` starts at the block's own answer: the beacon's white and a stained glass block's or pane's dye colour, empty for the rest.
