Set `Some(rgb)` to tint the beam as stained glass does (a colour unlike the section below starts a new section mixed half and half with it), or `None` to let the beam pass untinted, or stop at an opaque block other than bedrock, as vanilla does. The beam takes the red, green and blue channels; alpha is ignored. Asked on both sides, since a client builds the beam it draws from the blocks it has, with the level read-only: register the mod on both and answer the same on each. With no part attached to the state, the game checks the hook subscription and the block key before any event conversion, so the cost is one table lookup per block above a beacon. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `block_jump_factor` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The jump factor of a block state this part is attached to (`Block.getJumpFactor`, which `Entity.getBlockJumpFactor` reads for the player, the cube, the panda, the rabbit and every other living jumper) is being read; vanilla's values are 0.5 for honey and 1.0 for the rest. A part may change `factor` (`event.factor`). The entity reads the block at its feet and, when that gives 1.0, the block below it, so a changed factor takes part in that choice exactly as the block's own would. It runs for each read, on both sides and on the client's replayed ticks, so register the mod on both and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs. It is given the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity, and takes no `Host`. NeoForge's counterpart `Block.getJumpFactor()` is a method of the block, with no bus event, so this is a part method only; a mod reacting to its own blocks uses this part. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `block_path_type` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.getBlockPathType` | The optional classification of `event.state` at `event.pos` (`IBlockExtension.getBlockPathType`). Asked before every vanilla rule, including air and `is_pathfindable`. `Some` replaces the whole static classification; `None` declines to vanilla. The read-only level host is the level that owns `pos`, so the same state may answer differently at different positions. No mob is supplied: engine callers in NeoForge pass null. The server's 4096-slot positional cache stores the final static classification, including Open, until a matching block-updated notification or slot collision. It survives time, reload and chunk lifetime. Ordinary writes clear only on actual change with UPDATE_CLIENTS and full status at least BLOCK_TICKING; direct block-entity notifications clear even unchanged state. Neighbour and mob-specific adjustments run afterwards, outside that cache. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `block_render_visibility` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Hides a block state from the mesher: set `event.hidden` and the state draws nothing, as if its blocks were air. Its neighbours' faces toward it show, it no longer hides what is behind it, and its fluid layer and block entity are not drawn; light, collision and the server are untouched. The answer depends on the state alone and is resolved at mesh time, never per frame: the game asks once for every state when the client joins a world, and again for every state each time a mod calls `ClientHost::refresh_render_visibility` (a toggle, an ore scanner). Attach the part to vanilla blocks with `block_part_tag`. With no mod answering, the mesher pays nothing. |
| `block_signal` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | The redstone signal the block `state` at `pos` gives on its `face`, the side that touches the block reading it: `BlockState.getDirectSignal` when `direct` (what passes through a block it touches, as a lever's does), else `getSignal`. `signal` holds vanilla's answer: change it when the block is yours (0 to 15; anything else is clamped). Asked very often, from inside the level's update, so answer from your own state: check the block first, and don't read or change the world here. When the answer changes, and once the block is placed, call `level.update_neighbors(host, pos, state, NeighbourUpdate::Neighbours)`. Authoritative servers only. |
| `block_speed_factor` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The speed factor of a block state this part is attached to (`Block.getSpeedFactor`, which `Entity.getBlockSpeedFactor` reads for the player, mobs, boats and minecarts where vanilla reads it) is being read; vanilla's values are 0.4 for honey and soul sand and 1.0 for the rest. A part may change `factor` (`event.factor`). The entity reads the block at its feet and, when that is not water or a bubble column and gives 1.0, the block below it, so a changed factor takes part in that choice exactly as the block's own would. It runs for each read, on both sides and on the client's replayed ticks, so register the mod on both and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs. It is given the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity, and takes no `Host`. NeoForge's counterpart `Block.getSpeedFactor()` is a method of the block, with no bus event, so this is a part method only; a mod reacting to its own blocks uses this part. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `bounce_on` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | An entity bounced off a block state this part is attached to (`Block.bounceOn`). `event.fall_distance` is the distance stored after fall handling, so a grounded boat can report zero. It fires only when the entity bounced, just before vanilla sets its movement. `event.movement` is the movement the bounce leaves it (`x`, `y`, `z`): change it to change what the entity keeps. It runs inside the entity's move, on the level random host: it reads blocks and draws the level's random in vanilla's order, but writes nothing. Runs on the server for players, mobs and other bodies that bounce in the base move. |
| `bounce_restitution` | both | collect | `LevelSide` | `IBlockExtension.getBounceRestitution` | A simulating entity's downward block collision asks for the contacted state's restitution (`IBlockExtension.getBounceRestitution`, through `IEntityExtension.getBlockBounciness`). `event.state` and `event.pos` are the pair selected by `Entity.move` at `getOnPosLegacy`. That position uses the supporting block's column but need not be the supporting cell. `event.entity` is the entity's id and `event.kind` is its type. |
| `break_progress_positions` | client | collect | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | When a crack is set or changes stage, supply additional positions drawn at that same stage. Only parts on the cracked `state` are called, for both the local player's predicted crack and other players' cracks. `positions` starts empty; the original `pos` is always drawn separately. Answers are stored with the crack, not requested during rendering, and disappear when the crack clears, expires or its client level closes. |
| `can_be_connected_to` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.canBeConnectedTo` | On an authoritative server, whether the block `state` at `pos` lets the fence, wall, or iron bars or pane `connector` beside it connect to it (`IBlockExtension.canBeConnectedTo`): the block being connected to answers, not the connecting one. `kind` names the connector's vanilla rule, `direction` points from the block toward the connector and `face_solid` is whether the block's face toward it is sturdy. Asked once the connector's vanilla rule answered, each time the connector is placed or settles its shape toward the block. `Some(true)` connects, `Some(false)` refuses and `None` keeps vanilla's rule. The connector's own `connects_to` is asked after, starting from this answer, and has the last word. A panicking part is contained and vanilla's answer is used. The level is read-only. With no part attached to the state, the cost is one table lookup. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `can_be_hydrated` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.canBeHydrated` | Whether `fluid` at `fluid_pos` waters the farmland this part is attached to, the block `state` at `pos` (`IBlockExtension.canBeHydrated`): set `hydrates` to `Some(true)` or `Some(false)`, or leave it `None` for vanilla's rule, a fluid in `#minecraft:water`. `FarmlandBlock.randomTick` scans the 9×2×9 box from `pos` (x and z from −4 to 4, y 0 and 1) and asks only where the fluid is not empty, in x, then y, then z order; the first position that hydrates ends the scan, and the farmland goes to moisture 7. A session water ticket waters the farmland without scanning. Asked on an authoritative server; the level is read-only. With no Part attached to the farmland, the scan is vanilla's. |
| `can_drop_from_explosion` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.canDropFromExplosion` | On an authoritative server, whether the block `state` at `pos`, destroyed by `explosion`, drops (`IBlockExtension.canDropFromExplosion`), asked where `BlockBehaviour.onExplosionHit` asks `Block.dropFromExplosion`. `Some(false)` skips what the drop runs: the block's `spawnAfterBreak` (an infested block's silverfish, an ore's experience) and its loot; `Some(true)` runs them, even for TNT; `None` keeps vanilla's answer, where only TNT drops nothing. The block is removed either way, and a TNT still primes (`TntBlock.wasExploded` is not part of the answer). A panicking part is contained and vanilla's answer is used. The level is read-only. With no part attached to the state, the cost is one table lookup. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `can_entity_destroy` | server | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.canEntityDestroy` | On an authoritative server, whether `entity` may destroy the block `state` at `pos` (`IBlockExtension.canEntityDestroy`), asked where vanilla asks its per-block rule: the wither's block break (`WitherBoss.canDestroy`: not `#wither_immune`), a dangerous wither skull's blast (the entity is the skull, readable during the blast; only a block it may destroy has its resistance capped at 0.8), the dragon's `checkWalls` (a `#dragon_transparent` block is skipped before the part is asked, and a `#dragon_immune` block stays even on a yes), a zombie's door breaking (asked as the goal starts) and the turtle-egg `RemoveBlockGoal`'s target choice. `Some(true)` makes a block vanilla spares destroyable for that entity, `Some(false)` spares one it would break (the dragon is then in a wall), and `None` keeps vanilla's rule. The griefing gate (`entity_mob_griefing`) runs first, so with griefing off nothing breaks whatever the part says, and `living_destroy_block` can still keep a block the part allowed (the egg goal's target choice fires no such event). Ravager: griefing event only. A panicking part is contained and vanilla's rule is used. Asked every tick for each block in the dragon's head, neck and body: answer fast, from the inputs. The host is read-only. With no part attached to the state, the cost is one table lookup. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `can_harvest_block` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `IBlockExtension.canHarvestBlock` | Whether the player `player`'s tool harvests the block `state` at `pos` (`IBlockExtension.canHarvestBlock`, what `Player.hasCorrectToolForDrops` asks of the block). `can_harvest` starts with vanilla's answer: false for a block that needs a tool the player's held item is not, true otherwise. Set it for your own block; false breaks the block and drops nothing, and the dig is slowed as vanilla slows a wrong tool. The part answers first and the global `player_harvest_check` hears the result, so a mod reacting to every block still has the last word. Asked wherever the harvest rule is: the dig's progress (both sides, so a client predicts it), the break and a door's break. |
| `can_place_liquid` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `LiquidBlockContainer.canPlaceLiquid`: may the block at `pos` take `fluid`? The first answering `BlockPart` returns `Some(true)` to accept as a container on any template, or `Some(false)` to refuse as a container. `None` leaves it to the next part, then to the template. A bucket may still replace a replaceable block, or try the block beside the hit. |
| `can_stick_to` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.canStickTo` | Whether the block state this part is attached to sticks to `other`, the state beside it (`IBlockExtension.canStickTo`): set `sticks` to decide. `sticks` starts with vanilla's answer: false for honey beside slime (as `is_slime_block` tells slime), otherwise true when either state is sticky (`is_sticky_block`). Two blocks stick when each says it sticks to the other, as `PistonStructureResolver` asks (NeoForge's `canStickToEachOther`), so a part that answers false for one neighbour is enough to let go of it and a part cannot make a block stick to one that refuses. The pair is asked from both sides, and the other block's part, if it has one, answers its own side. Asked on an authoritative server while a piston resolves its structure: answer from the two states, without touching the world. With no part attached to the state, the cost is one table lookup. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `can_survive` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `BlockState.canSurvive` for `state` at `pos` (`BlockBehaviour.canSurvive`), before the block answers it: as it is placed (`BlockItem.canPlace`), from a shape update of a block whose template checks it (a plant, a torch, a sign: false breaks the block with its drops), and wherever else vanilla asks. `state` may not be in the world yet, so decide from `state`. Answer only for your own blocks: `Some(false)` refuses the spot, `Some(true)` allows it, and the block's own rule doesn't run. The level is read-only. Asked on every shape update of such a block: keep it short. Asked on both sides, as vanilla asks it: a client asks it from its placement prediction and its own shape updates, with its loaded copy of the level (match `LevelSide`). Reads a client can't answer, tag tests among them, are `None` there, and `BlockEntityPartState::state_in` gives its default: never answer from those as if they were the server's. Answer `None` on a client where only a server can decide: the template's rule then predicts, and the server still decides. Version 2 is catalog metadata; rebuild for the `LevelSide` signature. A disabled owner's subscribed rule keeps that block type where it is already present or settling after a piston move or fall, but refuses a new placement. |
| `can_sustain_plant` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | `IBlockExtension.canSustainPlant` | `IBlockExtension.canSustainPlant` for the soil `ground` at `ground_pos` and the plant `state` above it, asked before the plant answers its own `mayPlaceOn` (`may_place_on`) wherever vanilla's plant rule reads its ground: when the plant is placed, from its shape update and wherever else vanilla asks. `Some(true)` lets the plant stand there, `Some(false)` refuses it (a planted one pops off when its ground changes to this block), and left `None` the plant's own `may_place_on` answers, as vanilla's does. Answer only for your own soil. The level is read-only. Runs on every shape update of a plant over a part's block: keep it short. Asked on both sides, as vanilla asks it: a client asks it from its placement prediction and its own shape updates, with its loaded copy of the level (match `LevelSide`). Reads a client can't answer, tag tests among them, are `None` there, and `BlockEntityPartState::state_in` gives its default: never answer from those as if they were the server's. Answer `None` on a client where only a server can decide: the template's rule then predicts, and the server still decides. Version 2 is catalog metadata; rebuild for the `LevelSide` signature. A disabled owner's subscribed soil rule supports plants of this type already above it or settling there, but does not authorize a new planting. |
| `clone_item_stack` | server | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getCloneItemStack` | `IBlockExtension.getCloneItemStack`, asked by `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handlePickItemFromBlock` on the server. `player` is the requesting player, available for reads through the Host. `include_data` is true for a creative player picking with Ctrl. The request carries no client hit face. Leave `stack` unset to keep the game's item. Creative breaking, such as a beehive's `playerWillDestroy`, does not ask this query. |
| `collision_shape` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The collision shape of the block state `state` at `pos` is being read for the collision query `query` (vanilla's `BlockBehaviour.getCollisionShape(state, level, pos, context)`), by an entity's move, a placement check or a projectile's path. `query` is vanilla's `EntityCollisionContext`, captured when the query was made: the entity's id and class, descending, placement, always-fluid, the main-hand item, and `is_above`. `shape` starts as `None`, which keeps the shape the block would otherwise have (its declared one, a context-keyed one, or the one its block entity published); a part that answers `Some(shape)` replaces it for this query with a block-local `VoxelShape`. Build it with `VoxelShape::try_from_boxes`, which rejects invalid geometry rather than imposing a box count. A Part that panics or traps loses only its answer; the previously accepted shape stands, and later Parts can still answer. It is asked on the owning tick thread for every query that meets the state, as vanilla asks `getCollisionShape` for every block of every sweep, so a change to the part's own state is seen by the next query. Fluid passage and face support never ask. It is given the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity and takes no `Host`: the part answers from `query` and the state it keeps itself, keyed by the entity's id and synced to the client, which asks the same question for its own prediction. A collision query made inside the callback gets the empty-context answer, never a nested call. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `connects_to` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Whether a fence, wall, or iron bars or pane of yours connects a side: set `connected` to say whether the block `state` at `pos` connects toward `neighbour`, the state beside it, `direction` pointing from the block to that neighbour and `face_solid` whether the neighbour's face toward the block is sturdy. `kind` names vanilla's rule, and `connected` holds its answer: `ConnectionKind::Fence` is `FenceBlock.connectsTo` (a solid face, a fence of the same kind or a gate across the arm), `Wall` is `WallBlock.connectsTo` (a wall, a solid face, iron bars or a pane, or a gate across the side; a connected side is still low or tall from the block above) and `Bars` is `IronBarsBlock.attachsTo` for iron bars and glass panes (a solid face, bars or a pane, or a wall; vanilla's rule ignores `direction`). Change it, to false as well as to true, when the block is your own. The neighbour's part answers first, in `can_be_connected_to`, and `connected` starts from its answer: this hook has the last word. Asked whenever the block settles its shape toward a side or is placed, inside the level's update: answer from the arguments without touching the world. Only the block's own part is asked, so vanilla blocks cost nothing. Authoritative servers only. Contract version `2`: version `1` was `fence_connects_to`, for fences alone and without `kind`. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `contents_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server-only, non-cancellable backing-block notice at `Container.setChanged` / `BlockEntity.setChanged`, after the write and before dirty/comparator updates. Default `ITEMS` and item `STORAGE` handlers coalesce implicit notices per backing block at root commit, for vanilla containers and engine-managed mod slots. During `menu_quick_move`, implicit notices publish only after a committed move settles its source slot (including when the source is emptied), and never after an unhandled or refused move. If the original backing container was removed or replaced, neither its replacement nor watchers receive the deferred notice. Explicit `set_changed` notifies immediately, even for equal contents and inside a transaction; the notice survives abort. Direct `set_item_slot`, writes to storage a mod provides itself, and rollback restores synthesize no notice. Nested writes to the backing block whose notice is active still apply, with dirty/comparator updates, but do not re-enter or queue a second notice for that block. Another block notifies normally. Double containers notify each backing component, not the other half merely refreshed for comparators. Vehicle/menu placeholder coordinates are excluded. The source level, pos and state always name the changed backing block: `level` and `pos` are the `StorageSlot::level` and `StorageSlot::pos` of any slot of the container that changed, so a double chest notifies the owning half's `pos`. `observer` is `None` for its own Parts, or `Some` for Parts of a block entity watching that source through `watch_contents`. Source Parts run first, then a snapshot of observers in registration order; a stale observer is skipped. A self-watch never duplicates the source callback. Registration changes affect the next notice. Replaces the Create port positional `contents_changed` mixin for depot, package and schematic storage; there is no matching NeoForge bus event. Each Part runs in the existing storage journal. A panic discards that Part's tentative writes (including successful nested transactions), not the backing change that raised the notice or another Part's writes. |
| `destroy_progress` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | `BlockBehaviour.getDestroyProgress(state, player, level, pos)`: the share of `state` that one tick of mining by `player` removes at `pos`, which the dig adds up until it reaches 1. Answer for your own block in place of the game's math (hardness, tool, harvest rule, efficiency, effects, block-break speed, submerged mining, the on-ground penalty and `player_break_speed`), as a block that mines like another overrides it in vanilla: leave `progress` unset to keep that math. `0.0` never breaks; `1.0` or more breaks at once. To mine as another block, ask `PlayerRef::destroy_progress` for that block's position and state from here, on the server (it takes a server host): inside this answer that query gives the game's own math for the state and asks no Part again, so a chain of answers cannot run without bound. Asked on both sides wherever the dig's progress is, so a client predicts it: register the mod on both and answer the same from both. A client without the mod predicts the game's math, and the server's dig decides. Every tick of a dig asks it: keep it short. |
| `dispense_method` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `DispenserBlock.getDispenseMethod`, before the item's `dispense_item`: the block the dispenser faces chooses what the dispense does, for any item, including vanilla items, on the authoritative server (not droppers). `state` is the block at `pos` moved by `facing` (air included), and the part is asked only for that block. An answer wins: no item part is asked and vanilla's behaviour doesn't run. The answer is `dispense_item`'s: a remainder of None empties the slot, `Some(stack)` replaces it (the hook owns consumption: no extra item is removed afterwards), and `event` is the level event played with the smoke: 1000 the click, 1001 the fail sound, or 0 for no sound or smoke. Leave answer None and `dispense_item` decides. In an open transaction in the dispenser's level, sound and smoke follow the behaviour's published effects at the outermost commit and are discarded on abort. The remainder is detached until the dispenser writes its slot. An invalid remainder is reported without writing or notifying the slot, and no other behaviour runs. A disabled owner's subscribed method skips the slot write and dispense events unless an earlier enabled Part supplies a reply the engine accepts. |
| `enchant_power_bonus` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getEnchantPowerBonus, IBlockStateExtension.getEnchantPowerBonus` | The enchanting power the supplied `state` at `pos` gives the table at `table_pos` (`IBlockExtension.getEnchantPowerBonus`). `power` starts at 1.0 for a block in `#minecraft:enchantment_power_provider`, 0.0 otherwise. Explicit reads return the answer unchanged; offers and particle sampling ignore a non-finite override. |
| `entity_inside` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | An entity's block sweep crossed a block state this part is attached to (`Block.entityInside`), and the part is told mid-sweep, where vanilla runs the block's own `entityInside`. `event.precise` is `Entity.checkInsideBlocks`' flag: the entity moved nearly a block, or its box at the target touches the block. It runs inside the entity's move on the level random host (`&mut dyn LevelRandomHost`): it reads blocks and draws the level's random in vanilla's order, interleaved with the vanilla blocks the same walk visits. `event.apply` queues one of the five `InsideBlockEffectType`s, and `event.run_before`/`event.run_after` queue owned callbacks around an effect slot. When the sweep flushes (`InsideBlockEffectApplier.StepBasedCollector.applyAndClear`), each callback is lent an `&mut InsideEntityView<'_>` over the swept entity and its live level, stopping as soon as the entity dies. Level writes (`set_block`, `destroy_block`) apply directly to the live level with no touched-block state-unchanged skip or sibling-write rollback, and `hurt_with_source` resolves optional source handles directly without exposing a `Taken` refusal. A panic in the part call drops its staged work; a panic in a queued callback leaves completed live writes in place unless an existing transaction journals them. Either panic disables that mod's `entity_inside` callback for the session, logs the mod's ID, and queues a client error toast. Other mods' callbacks and built-in effects continue while the entity is alive. Fires for players, mobs, items and projectiles alike, each on the shared walk inside its own tick or batch. An item runs one sweep, after `step_on`; the same ordered slots hold built-in effects and Part callbacks. |
| `exp_drop` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.getExpDrop` | Experience before enchantments (`IBlockExtension.getExpDrop`). First answer replaces vanilla sampling; the global break_block event may override it. Negative answers are clamped to 0; positive values are awarded as given. A panic unwinds before the answer is applied. |
| `explosion_resistance` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.getExplosionResistance` | On an authoritative server, the explosion resistance of the block `state` at `pos` against `explosion` (`IBlockExtension.getExplosionResistance`), asked as a ray of the blast enters the block (`ExplosionDamageCalculator.getBlockExplosionResistance`). The answer replaces only the block's own value (`BlockProperties.explosion_resistance`); vanilla combines it with the cell's fluid via `max` (`0.0` for empty fluid, `100.0` for water or lava). What vanilla applies after the block's value keeps winning: a respawn anchor's water cell, a TNT minecart's rails and the blocks under them (never asked) and a dangerous wither skull's 0.8 cap. Any finite answer is accepted (negative answers combine with the cell's fluid resistance via `max`); a non-finite answer is refused and the block's own value is used; a panicking part is contained and the block's own value is used. A ray asks once per block it enters, so keep the answer a pure function of the inputs. The level is read-only. With no part attached to the state, the cost is one table lookup. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `fall_on` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `LivingFallEvent` | An entity landed on a block state this part is attached to (`Block.fallOn`), after falling `event.distance` blocks. Writable `event.distance` and `event.damage_multiplier` feed the fall calculation before hurt and sounds. The multiplier starts at the block's vanilla value (normally 1.0). Damage is `floor((distance + 1e-6 - safe_fall_distance) * damage_multiplier * fall_damage_multiplier_attribute)`, with safe fall distance 3 by default. Halving distance is not halving damage. Zero multiplier or a distance within the safe fall prevents damage and generic fall sounds. An entity that cannot be hurt by the fall (creative, fall immune) is told with zero multiplier and keeps its immunity, and a cancelled fall still lands. It runs inside the entity's move, on the level random host: it reads blocks and draws the level's random in vanilla's order, and cannot write. A write the override makes (farmland turning to dirt, a turtle egg breaking) is asked with `event.request(LandingWrite::SetBlock(state))` or `LandingWrite::DestroyBlock { drop }` and lands at the block the entity landed on once the entity's batch has ticked, in the order asked, if the block is still the state it landed on. SetBlock takes an API BlockState. The complete requested batch is checked before queuing: an invalid key or ordinal refuses all writes and sibling outputs from that callback, including distance and multiplier. The original landed-state guard is checked once per batch, not between its ordered writes. Players, mobs and dropped items land here (an item as an `EntityRef` of kind `minecraft:item`, with zero multiplier: items take none); projectiles and the other entities do not. |
| `falling_tick` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IFallableExtension.fallingTick` | A falling block of `state` ticked and is still in the air (`IFallableExtension.fallingTick`): `entity` is the falling block, and `pos` the block it is in (`Entity.blockPosition`) after its move. Heard once for each tick that ends with it still falling, not in the tick it lands, drops as an item or is removed (that is `Fallable.onLand`'s moment). The part can write to the level and, through `entity`, to the falling block: its velocity, its position. The tick is recorded as it ends and delivered once the level's falling blocks, primed TNT and orbs have ticked in this pass, so a write lands on the stored falling block; a falling block gone by then is skipped. Diverges from NeoForge: server only, where NeoForge also calls it on the client. Only a state a part listens to is recorded; with no such part, one table lookup per falling block per tick. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `fence_gate_in_wall` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | Whether `state`, the neighbour of a fence gate, counts as a wall for that gate's `in_wall` property (`FenceGateBlock.isWall`). Asked of the neighbour's block part, on placement and horizontal shape updates on both sides. `Some(true)` lowers the gate, `Some(false)` rejects this neighbour, and `None` keeps vanilla's `minecraft:walls` tag check. This does not change wall connections or pathfinding. Answer from the state alone; the host is read-only. |
| `fire_odds` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | `IBlockExtension.getFlammability, IBlockExtension.getFireSpreadSpeed, IBlockExtension.isFlammable` | How fire treats the block `state` at `pos` on either side (`FireBlock.setFlammable(block, igniteOdds, burnOdds)`). The client reads ignite odds for placement and shape prediction; only the server burns blocks. From the block's side `face`, the one facing the fire or the air fire may take, put `(ignite, burn)` in `odds` for your own blocks, and vanilla's table isn't read. Ignite odds above zero let fire stand beside the block and spread into the air around it (planks 5, wool 30, leaves 30); burn odds are how fast fire destroys it (planks 20, wool 60, leaves 60). `(0, 0)` never burns, and a face with ignite odds of zero isn't flammable. Negative answers become zero; burn odds cap at 300 (horizontal rolls are 300, vertical rolls 250), and spread speed uses saturating arithmetic. A waterlogged state never burns and isn't asked. Answer only for your own blocks and leave `odds` empty otherwise; the level is read-only. Asked hundreds of times per fire tick: keep it short. |
| `friction` | both | listen | `LevelSide` | `IBlockExtension.getFriction` | The friction of `state` at `pos` (`IBlockExtension.getFriction`), for an optional entity handle. The state need not occupy that position. The read-only level host reads the side that made the query: blocks, states, light, fluids, weather, enabled features, tags and resident block entities, as the other level hosts do, without loading chunks. It has no container, shape or signal view and answers `None` for those. Block-entity handles are the side's own: a client's is not the server's, so answer from state both sides hold. Set `event.friction` to answer; it starts at the state's own coefficient. Any finite answer is used as given, as vanilla uses what `getFriction` returns; a non-finite one keeps that coefficient. Runs on both sides, including during client replay. Keep the answer a pure function of the event and the level reads. |
| `has_analog_output` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | `BlockState.hasAnalogOutputSignal`: set `output` for a block of yours that comparators read (how full or far along it is, as a composter's level), so they ask `analog_output`. | `ignited_by_lava` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.ignitedByLava` | On an authoritative server, whether lava sets fire beside the block `state` at `pos` (`LavaFluid.isFlammable`, the only reader of `BlockStateBase.ignitedByLava`), asked from its side `face`, the one that faces the air the fire would take, as lava's random tick looks for something to light. `Some(true)` lets lava light it, `Some(false)` refuses it, and left `None` the block's own flag answers, as vanilla's does (planks, wool and leaves yes; stone no). | `is_bonemeal_success` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | `BonemealableBlock.isBonemealSuccess` for a block that answered `is_valid_bonemeal_target`, after it did: set `success` to grow it this time. | `is_fertile` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.isFertile` | Whether the soil this part is attached to, the block `state` at `pos` under a growing crop, counts as moist (`IBlockExtension.isFertile`): set `fertile` to decide. `fertile` starts with vanilla's answer, `moisture > 0`. `CropBlock.getGrowthSpeed` asks it for each of the 3×3 blocks under a crop, stem or pitcher crop on its random tick, but only for a block in `#minecraft:grows_crops`: a fertile one adds 3 to the speed, any other tagged block 1 (a quarter of that off-centre), so a block outside the tag is never asked. | `is_fire_source` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.isFireSource` | On an authoritative server, whether the block `state` at `pos`, under a fire, keeps that fire burning (`FireBlock.tick`'s check of the dimension's `infiniburn` tag), asked on every tick of the fire above it: `Some(true)` makes the fire endless, as netherrack does in the overworld (rain doesn't put it out and it never burns out), and `Some(false)` refuses a block the tag lists. | `is_ladder` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IBlockExtension.isLadder` | Whether a living entity climbs the block state this part is attached to (`IBlockExtension.isLadder`, which `LivingEntity.onClimbable` asks of the block the entity stands in). `entity` is the climber's id and `pos` the block it stands in; `state` is that block's state. `ladder` starts with vanilla's answer, whether the block is in `#minecraft:climbable`, and a part may set either value: `true` makes the block climbable as a ladder is, `false` refuses it though the tag lists it. | `light_emission` | both | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getLightEmission, IBlockStateExtension.getLightEmission` | The light the block `state` emits at `pos` in `dimension` (`IBlockExtension.getLightEmission`), for a block that declares `dynamic_light: true`. | `minecart_pass` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A minecart passed over a rail block state this part is attached to (the moment `AbstractMinecart` calls `activateMinecart` on an activator rail, for every rail; Forge's `IBaseRailExtension.onMinecartPass`, which NeoForge 26.3 no longer has, so this hook has no counterpart). `event.entity` is the cart, `event.pos` the rail. It fires after the cart's batch has ticked, not mid-move, so a write the part makes (a velocity change through the cart handle) lands on the stored cart, and a pass whose cart is gone or whose rail changed is dropped. Only a rail state a part listens for is recorded; with no such part nothing is kept. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `neighbor_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelWriteHost` | — | An authoritative server is about to deliver `neighborChanged`. State and block IDs are valid in this level's registries for this call. The host is the level writer vanilla's `neighborChanged` gets: a part may read its inputs (`signal`, `has_neighbor_signal`), schedule its tick, as a diode does (`DiodeBlock.checkTickOnNeighbor`), and set blocks, as `RedstoneLampBlock` lights itself at once (`level.setBlock(pos, state.cycle(LIT), 2)`). A write's own neighbour updates run next, before the rest of the update that delivered this call (`CollectingNeighborUpdater`), as `Level.setBlock` from inside `neighborChanged` runs them. The block's own handling then runs with the state it was called with. |
| `on_block_exploded` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IBlockExtension.onBlockExploded` | An explosion destroys this block, before removal and while its live block entity is readable. | `on_block_state_change` | both | listen | `LevelSide` | `IBlockExtension.onBlockStateChange` | A write through `Level.setBlock` held and the block at `pos` changed from `old` to `new` (`IBlockExtension.onBlockStateChange`), heard at the end of the write, where vanilla runs `updatePOIOnBlockStateChange`: after the block-entity removal, `on_place`, the client update, the neighbour updates and the neighbours' shape updates. The parts of `old`'s block hear it, then, when the block changed, the parts of `new`'s block; `state` is the state whose block's parts are asked. A write that `on_place` replaced with another block is not heard; the write that replaced it is. Heard on both sides: a server's writes, a client's replay of a piston's move and the blocks a client takes from the server (`LevelSide` names which). Applied local predictions are heard at once with `predicted: true`; server writes, settled corrections and all server-side writes have `predicted: false`. A held server update is unheard until acknowledgement; a confirmed prediction is not heard twice, and a denial hears predicted-to-server once. Equal-state writes and unapplied guesses are not heard. The level is read-only. A panicking part is contained. With no listener, one flag check builds nothing. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `on_caught_fire` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelWriteHost` | `IBlockExtension.onCaughtFire` | On an authoritative server, a neighbour of fire passed its burn roll. `state` is still at `pos`; `face` points toward the fire. Lava, lightning, spread into air, flint and steel, TNT use, burning projectiles, redstone and dispensers are outside this spread-only hook. |
| `on_destroyed_by_player` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IBlockExtension.onDestroyedByPlayer` | A player removes the block `state` at `pos` (`IBlockExtension.onDestroyedByPlayer`, called from NeoForge's `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock` as the removal), with `tool`, a copy of the stack in the player's hand taken before the tool wore (`None` for a bare hand), and `will_harvest`, whether the break will drop loot (never in creative). `state` is the state `playerWillDestroy` left, which has already run (a pot's cracking, piglin anger, a tripwire's disarming, a creative shulker box's or beehive's drop). `will_harvest` is the one harvest answer of the break: the tool's `hasCorrectToolForDrops` with the mods' harvest listeners (`can_harvest_block`, then `player_harvest_check`) already applied, asked once before the tool wore, as NeoForge's `canHarvestBlock`. Asked after `break_block` allowed the break and after the tool wore, so the stack in the hand is no longer `tool`. `destroyed` is whether the block was actually destroyed: `Some(true)` says the part removed it itself, `Some(false)` that it stays (a block of layers losing one layer: change it with the `host` and leave it standing), and either answer replaces the game's own removal. `Some(false)` ends the break there: no drops or statistic, though the tool has worn. | `piston_carries_block_entity` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | Whether a piston carries your block `state` at `pos` with its block entity: asked at the last step of `PistonBaseBlock.isPushable`, after the block's push reaction, for a block that is otherwise pushable, whenever a piston's structure takes the block in. If the block's owner subscribes and is disabled, the occurrence is held before dispatch, including for any enabled Parts attached to the block. Once re-enabled, the held occurrence is dispatched once with its original deadline, priority and order. |
| `tool_modified_state` | both | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getToolModifiedState` | The state the block `state` becomes under a tool action (`IBlockExtension.getToolModifiedState`: an axe's strip, scrape or wax-off, a shovel's flatten or douse, a hoe's till), asked of the block a tool is used on (`tool.pos`). `final_state` starts with vanilla's answer, `None` for a block the tool does nothing to: set it to `Some(new)` to allow the action or replace its result (a mod log an axe strips, a path a shovel makes), and `Cancel` it to deny the action, so the tool does nothing. `tool.simulated` says the game is only asking what the tool would do (`LevelRef::block_transform`, NeoForge's `simulate`): answer the same and change nothing in the world. A part answers before the global `block_tool_modification`, which still hears the result and may change or cancel it; a cancelled part skips it. Asked for the block's own parts only, so a block with none costs nothing. A disabled owner's subscribed transform suppresses the unchanged template result, not the ability's notification or a listener's replacement. The global hook still sees the candidate, and later abilities can be tried if this one has no result. |
| `update_shape` | server | replace | `&mut dyn LevelTicksHost` | — | An authoritative server is about to compute the state the block `state` at `pos` takes now that its neighbour in `direction` is `neighbour` (`BlockBehaviour.updateShape`). `state` may not be in the world yet (a piston landing it): decide from `state`, not from the block at `pos`. Answer only for your own blocks: put the new state in `new` (`state` keeps the block as it is; air breaks it, with its drops, on a neighbour update) and the block's own `updateShape` doesn't run, nor the ticks it would schedule. The level is read-only, as vanilla's `LevelReader`; ticks go through `native_schedule_block_tick` and `native_schedule_fluid_tick` with `ticks` (`ScheduledTickAccess`). It runs often, at least six times per `setBlock` that updates shapes (more for redstone wire's diagonals and knock-on updates): keep it short. A disabled owner's subscribed update keeps the input state instead of running the template. |
| `use_item_on` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | `BlockBehaviour.useItemOn`: the block's own behaviour, with the held item, on either hand and both logical sides, after the `Block` phase. `None` defers to the template; `TryWithEmptyHand` selects `useWithoutItem` on the main hand, and `Pass` or `Fail` lets the item try its `useOn`. `Success` predicts a swing; `SuccessServer` and `Consume` do not. The client host allows reads and local effects, not server writes. A disabled owner's subscribed interaction passes without running the block template. |
| `use_without_item` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | `BlockBehaviour.useWithoutItem`: the captured state's empty-hand method, on the main hand of either logical side when the selected block-use result is `TryWithEmptyHand`, including from cancellation of the `Block` phase. `event.click.stack` still describes what the hand holds. `None` defers to the template; a supplied result replaces it. `Pass`, `Fail` and `TryWithEmptyHand` proceed to the item without retrying the empty method. Only `Success` predicts a swing; the client host allows reads and local effects. A disabled owner's subscribed interaction passes without running the block template. |
| `weak_changes` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.getWeakChanges` | Whether this block receives block entity changes through an adjacent conductor. Starts false. |

### `EntityPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `can_rider_interact` | client | collect | — | `IEntityExtension.canRiderInteract` | Whether this candidate counts normally in the client's ordinary entity pick despite sharing the camera's root vehicle. Mirrors NeoForge's `IEntityExtension.canRiderInteract`, false by default. Only parts attached to the candidate's type are asked, in dependency order; `allowed` starts false and the last write wins. `event.subject` is an `EntityClientScope`: `with_view` lends a read-only `EntityClientView` of the candidate (handle, kind, position, rotation, UUID), and `host()` borrows `&mut dyn ClientHost` sequentially. Asked only at the same-vehicle branch of `ProjectileUtil.getEntityHitResult`, after a non-inside ray intersection qualifies against the running nearest distance. True lets this candidate update that distance normally. Inside-box selection does not ask it. Both the tick and frame pick use this rule. Attack-range component rays have no same-vehicle restriction and do not ask it. No server reach or interaction checks change. |
| `general_entity_tick` | server | listen | — | — | The authoritative tick of one general entity row of type `kind` (`mymod:robot`) in the server level's entity phase: `ServerLevel.tickNonPassenger`'s `entity.tick()` for a row the type registry owns (#792 stage 4). The row's common bookkeeping has run (its previous position is its current one); a rider is ticked after its vehicle, depth-first as `tickPassenger` recurses. A passenger dismounted before its join-order slot can tick as a root if it has not already ticked this pass. Rows that joined this tick first tick next pass; parked levels and rows outside entity-ticking chunks do not tick. Canceling `entity_tick_pre` skips the row's own tick; cancellation alone does not skip its passengers' ticks. A listener can separately change the riding graph. `event.subject` is an `EntityServerScope`: `with_view` lends a live `EntitySubjectView` of the row (handle, kind, position, velocity, rotation, UUID and checked velocity, rotation and fall-distance writes), and `host()` borrows `&mut dyn ServerHost` sequentially for attachments (`view.handle()` is their holder) and other world operations. Told only for general entity types: vanilla families tick their own paths (#6509 owns bringing them to one join-order walk), so a part attached to `minecraft:zombie` hears no tick from this hook. Server-side. |
| `is_shearable` | both | replace | `HostSide` | `IShearable.isShearable` | Whether `entity` can be sheared now with `shears` (NeoForge's `IShearable.isShearable`), asked where a player's shears (`player` is who holds them) or a dispenser's (`player` none) meet it, before the `player_shear_entity` or `block_shear_entity` hook. The first part to answer wins, in dispatch order: `Some(true)` shears it through `EntityPart::on_sheared` in place of the species' own shearing, `Some(false)` leaves it unsheared (a player's click then does nothing), and `None` leaves it to the next part and then to the species. `shears` is a read-only view over the resident stack; call `shears.project()` only when components or an owned snapshot are needed. Both sides ask it, as `ShearsItem.interactLivingEntity` does (a client predicting a player's click answers the same question the server does); `host` says which side asks, and a dispenser's ask is the server's alone. |
| `on_sheared` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `IShearable.onSheared` | Shears `entity` (NeoForge's `IShearable.onSheared`): called on the part whose `EntityPart::is_shearable` said `Some(true)`, once the shear hooks let it through. Change the entity and play its sound here, and append drops to `event.drops`: the game spawns each as `IShearable.spawnShearedDrop` does (a snow golem's at its eye height, a bogged's and a mooshroom's at the top of its box, a mooshroom's with no pickup delay, any other one block above its feet with a small random push drawn only once the item joined), posts the shear game event and damages the shears. `shears` is a read-only view over the resident stack; call `shears.project()` only when components or an owned snapshot are needed. Both sides run it (`ShearsItem.interactLivingEntity` calls `onSheared` on both): a client predicting the click changes its copy of the entity and plays its sound, and the `drops` it fills are discarded there, since only the server spawns drops, damages the shears and shears for a dispenser (`host` says which side runs). |
| `register_behaviors` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The mob's mod brain behaviors, joined into the species' brain beside vanilla's (#824): built wherever the brain is built — its first tick and every rebuild — started and stopped in `Brain.tick`'s order, fresh state each build, nothing saved. Branch on the mob with `event.behaviors.mob()`. |
| `register_goals` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The mob's mod goals, added to its two selectors as `GoalSelector.addGoal` does in vanilla's `registerGoals`: fresh at every construction, so a goal's state is never saved. Branch on the mob with `event.goals.mob()`. |
| `spawn_cluster_size` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `SpawnClusterSizeEvent` | NeoForge's `SpawnClusterSizeEvent`: how many mobs one natural spawn group may contain. Runs at the group-size comparison in `NaturalSpawner.spawnCategoryForPosition` (`EventHooks.getMaxSpawnClusterSize`), after the mob was finalized, added to the level with its passengers and counted by the spawn state, so `mob` is a resident mob: `host` reads it as the level holds it, and a mod that took it out in its join listener leaves a handle that no longer resolves. `vanilla` is the species' `Mob.getMaxSpawnClusterSize`; `size` starts there. Every part of the mob's type sees `size` as the parts before it left it, in dispatch order, and writes the one signed size the group is compared with: the group stops growing once its count reaches `size`, so a size of 0 or less ends it with the mob just spawned. The host is the read-only one: a listener runs on replayed ticks and must be a pure function of its inputs. |

### `FluidPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fluid_rules` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | How a mod's world fluid flows at `pos`, starting from what it declared (NeoForge's `BaseFlowingFluid` and `FluidType` getters: `getTickDelay`, `getDropOff`, `getSlopeFindDistance`, `canConvertToSource`). `fluid` is the id it was declared with. Asked for every flow step and every cell a spread looks at: answer fast. Answers take NeoForge's domain, any `int`, except a negative `drop_off`, which is refused (the declared value stands) because the level a spread computes must stay within `FlowingFluid.LEVEL`. Server-side. |

### `ItemPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `apply_enchantments` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.applyEnchantments` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.applyEnchantments`: the enchanting table applies `enchantments`, the list it chose for the clicked row as (id, level), to `stack`, the item in its input slot (`EnchantmentMenu.clickMenuButton`). `result` arrives with vanilla's result: a book becomes an enchanted book with the same count and components, then each level is added as `ItemStack.enchant` adds it, keeping the higher one. Replace or change it, and the input slot holds it; `player_enchant_item`, the `enchanted_item` trigger and `enchant_item` then see it. The levels and lapis paid stay as without the part. The result goes through the stack boundary: an unknown item, invalid damage or an oversized count is refused, and so is a count other than the input's, since the table holds one input; a refused result keeps vanilla's. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on the server, with a read-only `Host`. Loot, trades and spawn gear (`EnchantmentHelper.enchantItem`) don't ask. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `can_continue_using` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.canContinueUsing` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canContinueUsing`: does the use of `stack`, the stack it began with (vanilla's `useItem`), go on now that the used `hand` holds `held`? `entity` is the user's id, as in `living_entity_use_item_start`. `can_continue` arrives with vanilla's answer (`ItemStack.isSameItem(held, stack)`). `true` goes on with `held` as the use's stack; `false` stops the use without a release. Asked each tick of a use, where `LivingEntity.updatingUsingItem` starts and before `living_entity_use_item_tick`, on the server and on a server's client. Not asked for an emptied hand, which stops the use, nor for a hotbar switch, which stops a main-hand use before any item check. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. |
| `can_equip` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.canEquip` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canEquip`: may `stack` go into the equipment slot `slot` of the living entity `entity`, of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`)? `slot` is an `EquipmentSlot` name: `head`, `chest`, `legs`, `feet`, `mainhand` or `offhand`, and `body` or `saddle` from a dispenser. `can_equip` arrives holding the data answer, as `LivingEntity.isEquippableInSlot` and `Equippable.canBeEquippedBy` read the stack's own `equippable` component (a stack's override replaces the item's): yes when it names that slot and its `allowed_entities` is absent or lists the entity type (an empty list allows none); with no component, removed or never there, yes for `mainhand` only. Set it either way. Asked where vanilla asks: the armour slots of a player's inventory menu (`ArmorSlot.mayPlace`, which a shift-click into one reaches too), a dispenser equipping what stands in front of it (`EquipmentDispenseItemBehavior`, one entity at a time) and a mob picking an item up off the ground (`Mob.equipItemIfPossible`). It decides whether the stack fits the slot, not what the slot holds, whether the entity may wear one at all (`canUseSlot`) or the dispenser's own rules for the entity. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on both sides, as the client's menu predicts the slot. `host` is read-only and reads live entity and world state directly, for example `entity.entity().position(host)`. A missing or despawned target refuses wherever a part is asked; the no-listener data path performs no conversion or entity lookup. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `can_fit_inside_container_items` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IItemExtension.canFitInsideContainerItems` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canFitInsideContainerItems`: may `stack` go inside an item that holds items? `fits` arrives with vanilla's answer (`Item.canFitInsideContainerItems`): every stack fits except a shulker box. Set it `true` or `false`. It is asked, on both sides, wherever that rule is: - a shulker box's slots (`ShulkerBoxSlot.mayPlace`), in its menu and a guest's; - a bundle (`BundleContents.canItemBeInBundle`): a click that puts the stack in a bundle, and the `/item` bundle contents; - the item slots a mod gives an item (`item_container`) and its bundle slots. |
| `can_grindstone_repair` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IItemExtension.canGrindstoneRepair` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canGrindstoneRepair`: does the grindstone repair `stack` as it repairs a damageable item? `can_repair` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is damageable (`ItemStack.isDamageableItem`); set it `true` or `false`. It is asked at two places, on both sides (the server's menus and the client's own result): - `GrindstoneMenu.mergeItems`, for the top stack when both inputs are single stacks of the same item. `true` takes the repair branch: one item, the enchantments of both merged, then all but the curses removed. Durability is merged only when the result has durability, as vanilla checks it again on the copy. `false` takes the other branch: two matching stacks of an item that stacks give two, and anything else gives nothing. - The input slots' `mayPlace` (damageable or enchanted): a granted stack can be placed. This site is Sunrise's reading. |
| `can_perform_action` | both | listen | `&ItemAbilityContext<'_>` | `IItemExtension.canPerformAction` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canPerformAction`: can `stack` perform the item ability `ability`, a plain, case-sensitive name such as `shield_block` or `sword_sweep` (a mod's own abilities also use plain names, such as `my_mod_chop`; `host.item_abilities()` lists the game's)? `can_perform` arrives with vanilla's answer for that ability: the item kind's (shears shear), its tag's (`#minecraft:swords` sweep) or the stack's component (`blocks_attacks`, a shield's, blocks); set it either way and the stack grants or loses the ability. `stack` is a borrowed `ItemStackView` (call `ItemStackView::project` only when you need components or an owned copy). The answer depends only on the stack and the ability: the hook is asked from gameplay and from loot predicates (`neoforge:can_item_perform_ability`) and must give the same answer in both. So it has no host or level, by design, as NeoForge's has none; the `ItemAbilityContext` lends only the merged tags (`context.tags()`). Behaviour that depends on the world belongs in the hook for that moment (block breaking, item use). Loot conditions use the shared item defaults. Asked where vanilla checks the item for an action: a raised item blocking attacks (`shield_block`: a granted stack blocks as a bare `blocks_attacks` component does, a denied one never blocks nor is raised); shearing a sheep, mooshroom, snow golem, copper golem or bogged, cutting a mob's, boat's or knot's leads, a dispenser's shears and a bee nest's honeycombs (`shears_harvest`); carving a pumpkin (`shears_carve`); shearing a mob's saddle, carpet, harness, body block or wolf armor off (`shears_remove_armor`); cropping a growing plant's tip (`shears_trim`); disarming a broken tripwire (`shears_disarm`); a mined block's wear as shears wear (`shears_dig`); brushing an armadillo (`brush_brush`); priming TNT and lighting a campfire, candle or candle cake, by hand or from a dispenser (`firestarter_light`); putting out a campfire (`shovel_douse`); a cast line staying out and drawn from the hand, and which hand another player's cast rod shows in (`fishing_rod_cast`); a trident's throw (`trident_throw`, a trident's own use, so only a denial counts); a charged grounded hit sweeping (`sword_sweep`); and the spyglass's zoom, overlay, view and held-to-the-eye pose (`spyglass_scope`, of the item in use). It decides whether the stack may do the action, not what the action does to the world or the tool (`block_tool_modification` is the block's side of a tool action; item wear is its own). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on both sides, so the client's prediction agrees with the server: answer from the stack and `ability` alone, and fast. An item with no such part costs one table lookup and does not project the stack. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `contained_fluid` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The fluid a stack of a mod's own container item holds, as its `neoforge:fluid_container` item model draws it (`FluidUtil.getFirstStackContained`): `fluid` arrives `None` (holds none); set it to the fluid's key, taken once at registration with `r.fluid_key`. Asked on the client each time a stack of an item outside `minecraft:` is drawn through a fluid container model; vanilla's items keep their own answer and never ask. A fluid with no look in an item draws the missing texture; a key no fluid is registered under draws the stack as holding none, and the game logs it once per item. Answer from the stack alone, and fast. |
| `crafting_remainder` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.getCraftingRemainder(ItemStack)` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.getCraftingRemainder(ItemStack)`: what `stack` leaves when consumed by crafting, furnace fuel or brewing. `remainder` arrives holding the item's data remainder. Replace it, or set it to `None` to leave nothing. A part that leaves it alone preserves the data remainder. Recipe-specific book and banner copies are handled by the recipe. Asked for each consumption on the authoritative server, with a read-only `Host`. Oversized counts, unknown items and invalid damage are refused by the stack boundary, retaining the original data remainder. API stacks cannot have count zero; use `None` to leave nothing. Native panics follow the normal Part crash path, attributed to the owning mod and Part. |
| `creator_mod_id` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.getCreatorModId` | `IItemExtension.getCreatorModId`: the mod that made `stack`, which a recipe viewer or tooltip names, instead of the mod that registered its item. `mod_id` arrives as the item id's namespace; set it to another mod (a universal bucket holding that mod's fluid, a facade of its block) or `None` for no mod. Asked by `ItemResource::creator_mod_id`, from client tooltips and viewers as well as the server: answer from the stack alone. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked: an item with none costs one table lookup. |
| `damage_item` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IItemExtension.damageItem` | `IItemExtension.damageItem`: the wear `stack` is about to take (`ItemStack.hurtAndBreak`: a tool that mines or hits, armour that takes a hit, a shield, a rod, an elytra, a tool a dispenser uses, a mob's armour), after Unbreaking has had its say, as NeoForge passes it. `entity` is who wears or uses it: the acting player on a player's paths, the mob, armour stand or dragon wearing it, `None` for a dispenser's tool. `amount` is the points about to be lost; change it (0 or less leaves the item whole) or set `canceled` for no wear at all. Set `break_now` and the item breaks whatever durability it has left, through the vanilla break (the sound and particles, the stack emptied, `player_destroy_item` for a player): NeoForge's `onBroken`. It wins over `canceled`. Not asked when Unbreaking already saved the item, nor for a creative player's tools (they take no wear). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item hear it: an item with none costs one table lookup, and a game with no such part none at all. On the paths of a player the player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()` answers them. Once for each wear, on a server. |
| `dispense_item` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `DispenserBlock.getDispenseMethod`: choose a behaviour for any item, including vanilla items, on the authoritative server (not droppers). The block the dispenser faces is asked first (`dispense_method`), and its answer keeps this part from being asked. Leave answer None for vanilla. Otherwise answer `(remainder, event)`: a remainder of None empties the slot, `Some(stack)` replaces it (the hook owns consumption: no extra item is removed afterwards). `event` is the level event the dispenser plays with its smoke: 1000 the click, 1001 the fail sound (`OptionalDispenseItemBehavior` without success), or 0 for no sound or smoke. In an open transaction in the dispenser's level, sound and smoke follow the behaviour's published effects at the outermost commit and are discarded on abort. The remainder is detached until the dispenser writes its slot. An invalid remainder is reported without writing or notifying the slot, and no other behaviour runs. To drop one normally, resolve `host.level(dimension)`, call `level.dispense_default(host, pos, facing, stack)`, and answer `(remainder, 1000)` on `Ok(remainder)`. |
| `empty_contents` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IDispensibleContainerItemExtension.emptyContents` | `IDispensibleContainerItemExtension.emptyContents`: `stack` (a bucket of a mod fluid, a jar, a canister) is about to be emptied at `pos`, by `player` (the acting player) who clicked `hit` (the block and face), or by a dispenser (`player` and `hit` are `None`). Leave `answer` `None` for vanilla's emptying. Answer `Some(true)` when the part placed the contents itself through the host: the stack becomes an empty `minecraft:bucket` (a creative player keeps it), no vanilla sound plays and the use counts as a success. Answer `Some(false)` for a failure: nothing is placed, a player keeps the full stack and a dispenser drops it, as vanilla's does when emptying fails. Asked for a player once `bucket_empty` let the pour go on, and for a dispenser before its own placement; an item with this part reaches the dispenser's bucket behaviour even when it is no bucket of a mod fluid, and a `dispense_item` answer still comes first. A server's client does not predict the pour of an item with this part. The part is asked in a transaction with the game's apply: a part that changes the player's hand or the dispenser's slots while answering `Some(true)` is refused and its journaled writes are undone, as a failed use (the player keeps the stack, the dispenser fails). A player's successful pour counts for the `placed_block` criterion. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Server-side. |
| `held_item_tint` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Tint one third-person held-item submission in the world, for a player, mob or armor stand. `entity_id` is the holder's current client-level entity id, `hand` is the logical main or off hand (independent of the dominant arm), `stack` is the rendered stack, and `partial_tick` is the frame's tick fraction. |
| `highlight_tip` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemExtension.getHighlightTip` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.getHighlightTip`: the name drawn above the hotbar when `stack` is selected. `name` arrives with what `Hud.extractSelectedItemName` draws (the hover name in its rarity colour, italic when the stack has a `custom_name`); the text the part leaves is drawn in its place, centred at the same height and fade, cut at the screen edge as a long custom name is. Asked once each client tick while the name shows. The display timer still restarts only when the selected item or its hover name changes, so a tip that changes every tick doesn't keep the name up. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Client-side. |
| `hurt_enemy` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's hit landed on the mob `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`), holding `stack`: where `Item.hurtEnemy` is called, before the post-attack enchantments and the weapon's wear. It only hears. Another player as the target is not told. Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). Read the borrowed stack with `get`; call `project` only to keep an owned copy. |
| `inventory_tick` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server tick of the stack a player carries in `slot`, where `Inventory.tick` calls `Item.inventoryTick`. `slot` uses the saved inventory numbers (0 to 35; armor 100 feet to 103 head; -106 off hand), and `selected` marks the held slot. `place` addresses that live slot: `ItemPlace::with_stack` lends its current stack, `ItemPlace::edit` edits its components within a transaction, and the ordinary slot operations can empty it. Later reads see earlier writes, but only Parts of the original `item` are called for this tick. Server-side, including player ticks while the world is frozen. |
| `is_combine_repairable` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IItemExtension.isCombineRepairable` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isCombineRepairable`: may two of `stack`'s item be combined in a crafting grid to repair it (`RepairItemRecipe.canCombine`)? `repairable` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack has durability (`minecraft:max_damage` and `minecraft:damage`); set it `true` or `false`. It is asked for each of the two stacks, once they are found to be single stacks of the same item, and both must answer `true`. A `true` for a stack with no durability (a maximum damage of 0) is refused with a warning, and vanilla's `false` is kept, because the result needs a positive maximum. The anvil's combining of two of the same item keeps vanilla's test and doesn't ask. It is asked on both sides, wherever the grid's result is matched: a crafting table, the inventory grid, a crafter and the client's own result. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. It is given the loop `context` of the side that runs the match and takes no `Host`, so answer from the stack and `context` alone, and fast: a match may ask it many times. With no part on the stack's item the cost is one table lookup, and the stack isn't converted. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. Diverges from upstream: new hook. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. Read the borrowed stack with `get`; call `project` only to keep an owned copy. |
| `is_not_replaceable_by_pick_action` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.isNotReplaceableByPickAction` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isNotReplaceableByPickAction`: must a pick (the pick-block key) leave `stack`, in hotbar `slot` (0 to 8), where it is? `player` is the player picking. `not_replaceable` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is enchanted; set it `true` to keep the stack in place or `false` to let the pick swap it out. Asked in `Inventory.getSuitableHotbarSlot`'s second pass, when a pick moves an item into the hotbar and no hotbar slot is empty: from the selected slot on, for each hotbar stack, until one answers `false`, which is the slot the pick uses; when all answer `true`, the selected slot is used. A pick that selects a matching hotbar stack, or finds an empty slot, doesn't ask, nor one `player_pick_item` cancelled. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on the server (the host's own player and its guests), with a read-only `Host`. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `is_piglin_currency` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.isPiglinCurrency` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isPiglinCurrency`: is `stack` barter currency to a piglin (`PiglinAi.isBarterCurrency`)? `currency` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is a `minecraft:gold_ingot`; set it `true` to make the stack currency, `false` to refuse it (a gold ingot included), or leave it to keep vanilla's answer. Asked where vanilla asks: whether a piglin wants a stack on the ground (currency is wanted whenever its off hand holds no `#minecraft:piglin_loved` item, with no inventory-space check); whether an adult piglin that isn't admiring takes the stack from a player's hand, on the server and in the client's prediction of that click; and when a piglin stops holding the item it admired, where an adult barters currency (`minecraft:gameplay/piglin_bartering`, thrown toward the nearest player) after admiring it, loses it when hit, and equips or keeps anything else. A currency stack picked up from the ground is admired, and so bartered, only when it is also in `#minecraft:piglin_loved`, as vanilla admires loved items only; otherwise the piglin equips or keeps it. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on both sides, so register the part on both for the client's prediction to agree with the server: answer from the stack alone, and fast. An item with no such part costs one table lookup. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `is_primary_item_for` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.isPrimaryItemFor` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isPrimaryItemFor`: is `stack`'s item primary for `enchantment` (an id such as `minecraft:sharpness`), so enchantment selection may offer it? Selection chooses the enchanting table's offers and clues, loot `enchant_with_levels`, trades and spawn gear. `primary` arrives true for a plain book; otherwise it is the resolved `supports_enchantment` answer and membership in `primary_items` when that set is present. Set it true to offer the pair or false to withhold it, including for a plain book. |
| `item_bar` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The small bar `GuiGraphicsExtractor.itemDecorations` draws under an item, wherever the game shows one: `bar` arrives holding vanilla's value (the damage bar, or a bundle's fullness already applied) and may be replaced (signed pixel width and RGB colour) or set to `None` to hide it. Vanilla's bar is 13 pixels wide when full; the foreground endpoint uses wrapping integer addition, as in `GuiGraphicsExtractor.itemBar`. Asked each frame a stack is drawn. Client-side. |
| `item_decorations` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemDecorator.render` | Append an item's custom decorations after its default bar, cooldown and count. `draw` lends a `GuiCanvas`, not a full drawing context. The icon's top-left `(x, y)` is in the caller's local GUI pixels, outside the model-only squeeze. Inventory, hotbar and tooltip draws lend an identity pose. Appended draws belong to the enclosing group; use `draw.scope` to make just your decoration draws all-or-nothing. `draw.item_decorations` on this canvas or its children paints only the standard suffix, including the `item_bar` override. It does not invoke item decorators again. Use ordinary HUD or screen hooks for widgets, scenes, focus and narration. The NeoForge counterpart is `IItemDecorator.render`; registration through `RegisterItemDecorationsEvent` is a separate moment. |
| `item_destroyed` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IItemExtension.onDestroyed` | `IItemExtension.onDestroyed` (vanilla `ItemStack.onDestroyed`): a dropped item entity holding `stack` has reached zero health from `damage_type` (fire, lava, cactus, explosion, `/damage`, or block-contact damage) and is about to be discarded. |
| `item_interact_mob` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | `ItemStack.interactLivingEntity`: after ordinary entity interactions pass, for an alive native Mob and a nonempty stack in `hand`. Both logical sides answer independently. A consuming client result ends the hand loop; only Success swings there. SuccessServer and Consume consume without a client swing; Fail, Pass and TryWithEmptyHand fall through. A server-only answer leaves client off-hand prediction unchanged. ClientHost permits reads and local effects, not authoritative writes. Armor stands, other players and dragon parts are not covered. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `item_name` | both | collect | `&dyn LoopContext` | `Item.getName` | Vanilla's `Item.getName(ItemStack)`: the name of `stack` from its components, so one mod item has many names (a data model named after the mob in it, a filled container after its contents). `name` arrives as `None`, which keeps the default: the stack's `minecraft:item_name` component, else the item's translated name. Set it to `Some(text)` to name the stack; a translatable `Text` (`Text::translate`) renders in each viewer's own language. It replaces the default rather than filling in for a missing `item_name`, as the vanilla items that override `getName` do. A `minecraft:custom_name`, or a written book's title, still wins over it (`ItemStack.getHoverName`), and the part isn't asked then. |
| `item_stacked_on_other` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` | NeoForge's `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` and vanilla's `Item.overrideStackedOnOther` / `Item.overrideOtherStackedOnMe`, fired inside a resolved pickup click (a direct left/right `Pickup` when no drag is active, or a one-slot left/right `QuickCraft` drag release; never on multi-slot or middle-button drags, active-drag resets, or creative catalogue slots). `click` lends the cursor and the clicked slot for this call: read them with `carried` and `clicked`, change them with `set_carried`, `set_clicked` and `take_clicked`. Reads see what earlier phases and this call changed. |
| `makes_piglins_neutral` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.makesPiglinsNeutral` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.makesPiglinsNeutral`: does `stack`, worn by `wearer`, keep calm piglins from targeting it, as gold armour does (`PiglinAi.isWearingSafeArmor`)? `neutral` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is in `#minecraft:piglin_safe_armor`; set it `true` to make the piece count as gold, `false` to make it not count though the tag lists it, or leave it to keep the tag's answer. Asked for each stack in a player's armour slots, in the order feet, legs, chest, head, until one counts. A player wearing a piece that counts is not the target a calm piglin picks (`NEAREST_TARGETABLE_PLAYER_NOT_WEARING_GOLD`); an angry piglin attacks anyway. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. It is asked whenever the server builds a player's view for the mobs, several times a tick, so the answer must be a pure, fast function of `stack` and `wearer`. An item with no such part costs one table lookup. Server-side. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `must_survive` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `BlockItem.mustSurvive`: whether placing this item must pass the block's `can_survive` predicate in the final `BlockItem.canPlace` check. Asked on both sides after placement state selection, with a read-only level host. Standing/wall items select candidates using unconditional survival checks and their own placement-state override; this hook does not skip them. `Some(false)` skips survival only, not replaceability, bounds or entity obstruction. `Some(true)` requires survival. `None` keeps vanilla's requirement (false for scaffolding, true for other block items). Dispatch is by `item` id; this event has no held-stack components. |
| `on_block_placed_by` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BlockItemExtension.onBlockPlacedBy` | `BlockItemExtension.onBlockPlacedBy`: the selected block has been placed, its final block state and the original item's block-entity data applied, and `Block.setPlacedBy` completed (including companion blocks). Runs once, at the primary position, before the item's placement sound, game event, placement trigger and stack consumption. `state` is the live block state after the callback, which can have changed the block or run its own effects. `stack` is the pre-shrink stack; `placer` is the placing player, or None for a dispenser. `level` is the level the block was placed in. `face` and `click` are the placement context's clicked face and world-space hit point.
