Failed, refused and canceled placements never notify, nor do successful writes that leave a different block at the primary position. Only Parts attached to the original item hear this server notice. |
| `on_dropped_by_player` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.onDroppedByPlayer` | `IItemExtension.onDroppedByPlayer`: `player` (the acting player's handle) pressed the drop key on `stack`, the whole selected stack before anything leaves it; `all` is true when the whole stack goes, false for one item (`ServerPlayer.drop(boolean)`). Set `canceled` and the stack stays in the slot: nothing is taken out, no item entity is made, no drop statistic is awarded and `item_toss` does not fire; a server's player is sent the slot back, undoing their predicted drop. Not asked for a menu's throw, nor for a spectator (who drops nothing). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked: an item with none costs one table lookup. Server-side, with a read-only `Host`. |
| `on_entity_item_update` | server | cancel | — | `IItemExtension.onEntityItemUpdate` | `IItemExtension.onEntityItemUpdate`: each tick of a dropped item entity, before the entity's own tick (`ItemEntity.tick`: its fluids, movement, pickup delay, merging and lifetime), and after `entity_tick_pre`. `event.subject` is an `ItemEntityServerScope`: `with_view` lends a live `ItemEntitySubjectView` for the dropped item's handle, position, velocity, stack, pickup delay, age, target, owner and checked writes, and `host()` borrows `&mut dyn ServerHost` sequentially for attachments and world operations. Return `Flow::Cancel` to skip that tick's vanilla work, as NeoForge's `true` return does. The item skips movement and its own merge pass, but another item can still merge into it. `entity_tick_post` still hears it. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to the item ID held at dispatch entry hear it. Read the live stack via `event.subject.with_view` and `view.with_stack`; component conversion is deferred until that stack view is projected. Server-side, for every dropped item that ticks. |
| `on_entity_swing` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `IItemExtension.onEntitySwing` | `IItemExtension.onEntitySwing`: the player in place (`entity`, its `Entity.getId` when known) is about to swing `stack` in `hand`: where `LivingEntity.swing` starts, for an attack, a use or a swing the server asked for. Set `canceled` and no swing starts: no animation and no swing packet. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Both sides, as vanilla's: answer the same on both, and change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. Read the borrowed stack with `get`; call `project` only to keep an owned copy. |
| `on_item_use_first` | both | replace | `HostSide` | `IItemExtension.onItemUseFirst` | `IItemExtension.onItemUseFirst`: the held item's first say on a click on a block, on both logical sides, after `use_item_on_block` (`ItemBeforeBlock`) let it through and before the block's own `useItemOn`. `None` defers; `Pass` goes on to the block. Any other result skips the block and the item's `useOn`. Only `Success` swings on the client. A client answer changes prediction, not the authoritative request; its host allows reads and local effects. |
| `on_left_click_entity` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IItemExtension.onLeftClickEntity` | `IItemExtension.onLeftClickEntity`: `player` (the acting player's UUID), holding `stack` in the main hand, is about to attack the entity `entity` (`Entity.getId`) of type `kind` (`minecraft:cow`, `minecraft:player`, `minecraft:item_frame`): where `Player.attack` starts, once the global `attack_entity` let the blow through and before any damage, knockback, enchantment effect, `hurt_enemy` or the weapon's wear. Set `canceled` and the attack ends there: the target is not hurt, `hurt_enemy` does not fire and the stack takes no wear. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). Read the borrowed stack with `get`; call `project` only to keep an owned copy. |
| `on_stop_using` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `IItemExtension.onStopUsing` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.onStopUsing`: the use of `stack` by `entity` (a player's id or a mob's) in `hand` is over, with `time_left` ticks of it left (0 for a finished use). Where `LivingEntity.stopUsingItem` runs, once, after the use is cleared: a listener that releases again finds no use. It comes after `release_using`, `living_entity_use_item_finish` and the item's own release, whatever stopped the use: a release (even one `living_entity_use_item_stop` canceled), a finish, a hand that changed item, a brush, a mob's release, and the server's other stops (a hotbar switch, swapped hands, a disabled shield, spectator mode, a dimension trip). An emptied hand is a used-up stack and isn't a stop. On a server's client it comes where the client ends its use (a finish, a release, a hand that changed item), not where the synced flags clear it: the server heard that stop. Both sides, as `release_using`: change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are told. |
| `on_use_tick` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Tick` | `Item.onUseTick` of `stack`, used by `entity` with `remaining` ticks left, always above 0: what a mod's own item does each tick it is used (cook, charge, drain). It comes where `ItemStack.onUseTick` calls the item, once `living_entity_use_item_tick` let the use go on: after a consumable's crumbs, for consumables, shields and mods' items (vanilla's other items do their own, and a kinetic weapon has none on the server). Both sides, as `living_entity_use_item_tick`: change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. Runs every tick of every use: check the item first. |
| `placement_context` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `BlockItem.updatePlacementContext`: replace `context` before selecting the block's placement state, on both sides with a read-only level host. `Some(Some(context))` rewrites the target and click, `Some(None)` refuses placement, and `None` keeps the item's vanilla rewrite (including scaffolding's column walk). The placing item cannot be changed. The actual pre-use `stack` supplies the item key and components. `context.source` distinguishes a player placement (`PlacementSource::Player { yaw, pitch, sneaking }`) from a directional non-player placement (`PlacementSource::Directional { direction }`): the effective placement rules, which a rewrite can change independently of `placer`. `placer` supplies the actual player's handle and eyes on this side, or is absent for a dispenser. A player's original destination may be occupied: a part can redirect the placement even when vanilla would refuse it there. A dispenser still needs a replaceable front cell before asking the part. With no answer, vanilla checks the original destination before its own rewrite. A part's rewritten destination must be loaded, in bounds, and replaceable with the supplied context. Non-finite click or player view values are refused. A rewritten directional context's `replace_clicked` is read from the block at `hit_pos`, not from the answer, and a directional question vanilla throws on (`PlacementStateError::NoVerticalLook` or `ReplacingClickedCycle`) refuses the placement. Survival and obstruction still apply at the final position. |
| `release_using` | both | listen | `HostSide` | — | `Item.releaseUsing` of `stack`: `entity` let it go with `time_left` ticks of its `use_duration` left, so it was held for `duration - time_left` (a bow-like item shoots by that charge). Set `used` as vanilla's return, and the stack's use cooldown starts (`ItemStack.releaseUsing`). After the vanilla item's own release, once `living_entity_use_item_stop` let it through. Both sides, as vanilla's: change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. Vanilla's, with no NeoForge counterpart (`LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Stop` is `living_entity_use_item_stop`); `on_stop_using` hears every stop, this one included. |
| `should_cause_block_break_reset` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemExtension.shouldCauseBlockBreakReset` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.shouldCauseBlockBreakReset`: does a break restart because the main hand changed? `stack` is the stack the break began with (vanilla's `destroyingItem`), `held` the main hand now (`None` for an empty hand). `reset` arrives with vanilla's answer: not `ItemStack.isSameItemSameComponents(held, stack)` (count left out, damage compared). `false` keeps the break and its progress going; `true` aborts it and starts it again from 0. A `true` for an unchanged stack restarts the break every tick, so it never finishes. Asked on each `continueDestroyBlock` and `startDestroyBlock` of the block the break targets, on the local player's client (single player and a server's client alike). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Client-side. |
| `should_cause_reequip_animation` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemExtension.shouldCauseReequipAnimation` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.shouldCauseReequipAnimation`: does the first-person hand lower and raise again to show `stack`, the stack the hand holds now? `old` is the stack the hand shows (`None` for an empty hand), `slot_changed` whether `stack` came from another slot (always false for the off hand). `reequip` arrives with vanilla's answer (`!FirstPersonHandsAndItems.shouldInstantlyReplaceVisibleItem`: the same stack changed in place, or an equal one but for its damage, is shown at once). `true` lowers the hand and raises it with `stack`; `false` shows `stack` at once. Asked each client tick, for each hand, while the shown stack differs from the held one by slot or by value; an unchanged hand and an empty hand aren't asked. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Client-side. |
| `supports_enchantment` | both | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.supportsEnchantment` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.supportsEnchantment`: may this item's stack receive `enchantment` (an id such as `minecraft:sharpness`)? The first `Some(bool)` replaces support; `None` tries the next part and then keeps the caller's vanilla rule. Anvils accept enchanted books; `/enchant` and compatibility-filtered loot use `supported_items`. Creative anvil use still bypasses support, and enchantment conflicts remain separate. Both anvil calculations, `/enchant` and compatibility-filtered `enchant_randomly` read the answer. The default for `is_primary_item_for` reads it before testing the enchantment's `primary_items`. |
| `tooltip` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ItemTooltipEvent` | `Item.appendHoverText`: an item's own lines in its tooltip, after its name and before its components' (enchantments, lore, attributes). Add lines to `lines`, which arrives empty, each with a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own); `Text::legacy` reads `§` codes. `context` is the same `TooltipAppendContext` passed to component appenders: player, advanced/creative/nested flags and vanilla `Item.TooltipContext.tickRate()` (the client level's rate, or 20 without a level). The stack has its components (`stack.data()`). Asked when lines are built for display or `ItemResource::tooltip`, except during reentrant line queries, when the stack hides its tooltip or for a crossbow's loaded projectile. NeoForge's `ItemTooltipEvent` is the corresponding line-building event; this scoped hook adds attached parts' lines before component lines are assembled. Afterwards `Hooks::item_tooltip` sees the finished list, including these parts' lines. |
| `tooltip_image` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Optional tooltip component data for this item stack (`Item.getTooltipImage`). Answer `Some(TooltipComponent::custom(data))` (or `Some(TooltipComponent::bundle(stack))`) to insert a component after the item's name line before `render_tooltip_gather_components` runs. Register a client factory for custom data types in `register_client_tooltip_component_factories`. Answer `None` to leave the vanilla tooltip component unchanged. A callback or retained payload destructor panic disables only this item-part registration for this hook. Client-side. |
| `use_animation` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | `Item.getUseAnimation` of `stack`: the animation the renderer draws while the stack is in use, which `ItemStack::use_animation` also reads. `animation` arrives with the item's vanilla answer; the item's Part may change it. A `Spear` override on an item without kinetic data is refused as `None`. Asked on the client when each render consumer updates, and on either side when a mod reads `ItemStack::use_animation`, with `host` read-only: answer from the stack alone, the same on both sides. From inside this callback, a read of `ItemStack::use_animation` for any stack of the same item (a copy with another count or components included) does not ask its Part again: it gets the item's own answer. Another item's read asks that item's Parts. Contract version 3: version 2 asked no Part again for any read from inside the callback; version 1 was asked on the client only, with a `ClientHost`. |
| `use_duration` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | `Item.getUseDuration` of `stack`, in ticks: `duration` arrives with vanilla's (a consumable's time, 72000 for a shield, a spear, a bow, 0 for an item with no use over time). A mod's own item with no `consumable`, `blocks_attacks` or kinetic weapon that answers above 0 is used over time on a right click, as an `Item.use` that calls `startUsingItem`, until it is let go or the time runs out (`living_entity_use_item_finish`). A consumable's time changes too, potions and the other vanilla items keep their own. Asked when a use starts, each tick a mod's item is in use, and each frame the hand draws one in use, on both sides. It has no user, as it is asked so often: answer from the stack alone. |
| `use_item` | both | replace | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickItem` | A player uses `stack` (right click) with `self.hand`: in the air, or on a block once `native_use_on` let it through, where `Item.use` starts. Runs on both logical sides after the global right-click event. Return an interaction result: Success swings on the client, SuccessServer swings on the server, Consume handles without swinging. Fail skips this item's use without swinging, then the off hand may be tried. None lets later Parts answer; if none answers, the item's own use runs. Some(Pass) skips later Parts and continues to the item's own use. TryWithEmptyHand is block-phase-only and is treated as Pass here. Cooldown items are skipped. A server-only answer does not stop the client's off-hand prediction. ClientHost permits reads and local effects, not authoritative writes. `hand` is the interaction hand of THIS click, including an instantaneous air use. Unlike this field, `living_used_item_hand` answers only during active item use. This is `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickItem.getHand`. `player` is the clicking player's UUID and `player_entity_id` that player's entity id on this logical side: the acting player's on a server (a guest's own, never the host's), this client's own player on a client. They name who clicked from the event alone, unlike `players().acting_player()`, which only answers while the host has set the acting player. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `use_on` | both | replace | `HostSide` | `UseItemOnBlockEvent` | A player uses `stack` (right click) with `self.hand` on the block at `pos`, `face` the face clicked (`[0, 1, 0]` is the top), once the block itself let the click through (a chest still opens): where `ItemStack.useOn` starts. Both logical sides answer independently: a consuming client result ends the hand loop; only Success swings there. SuccessServer and Consume consume without a client swing. Fail ends the block path; Pass and TryWithEmptyHand fall through. A server-only answer does not stop the client's off-hand prediction. ClientHost permits reads and local effects, not authoritative writes. Cooldown items are skipped. `hand` and `hit` describe this click; `player` and `player_entity_id` identify its player on this side. |
| `xp_repair_ratio` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.getXpRepairRatio` | `IItemExtension.getXpRepairRatio`: the durability points Mending restores to `stack` per point of experience, where `ExperienceOrb.repairPlayerItems` repairs the damaged Mending item it picked, once per item it repairs. `experience` is the points being spent (never 0) and `player` the player they go to. `ratio` arrives holding vanilla's: the stack's `minecraft:repair_with_xp` repair divided by `experience`, 2.0 for vanilla Mending. The repair becomes `floor(experience × ratio)`, capped at the stack's damage; a ratio left as it arrived keeps vanilla's repair exactly. A ratio that isn't finite is refused and vanilla's repair is used; a negative one counts as 0. A ratio that takes a positive repair to 0 leaves the experience unspent, for the player. Only the parts attached to `stack`'s item are asked: to change Mending for every item, change the enchantment's `minecraft:repair_with_xp` effect in a data pack. Server-side at orb pickup; direct experience writes do not ask this part. |

### `MenuPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `menu_button` | server | listen | — | — | A player pressed a mod menu's button (`clickMenuButton`), on the server. `subject` lends sequential access to the live resident block entity (when present) and the server `Host`: `subject.with_view(...)` returns `None` on a blockless menu or after the block entity is removed or replaced, while `subject.host()` and `subject.transaction(...)` stay usable on both. Mods may change the live block entity in place (each write is validated at the boundary) and the clicking player's carried stack. A panicking listener is contained and disabled for `menu_button` for the rest of the session, discarding its carried-stack edit and rolling back any open transaction. A panic in a different hook does not discard the cursor edit of a button callback that returns normally. |
| `menu_button_valid` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | — | Whether this menu's owner accepts a button id before `menu_button` runs. The owner chooses the ids for its own menu; there is no shared numeric range. Leave `accepted` false for ids the active menu does not support. |
| `menu_clicked` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A click in `menu`, before its default action (`ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handleContainerClick`, `AbstractContainerMenu.clicked`). Only parts registered for that menu type are asked. `slot` is in menu order (container slots, then any player inventory; -999 outside), `button` and `mode` are the click input, and `uuid` identifies the clicking player. Menu-button clicks, recipe placement and the creative set-slot packet do not call it. Return `Flow::Cancel` after applying the click yourself to skip the default: for example, copy an item into a filter slot without consuming the cursor. A display-only menu has no default moves. Other menus keep their ordinary slot rules when unhandled. The client predicts the ordinary click and the server corrects it. No player's state is swapped in. Use their current `PlayerRef::container_menu` to reach its slots and cursor, or `MenuRef::set_items` to replace a display menu's contents. Runs directly on the receiving menu without a synthetic decided-action wrapper or whole-inventory rollback: open a transaction explicitly with `ServerHost::transaction` when a callback wants atomic multi-step edits, and prior unrelated writes stand even if the default click is cancelled or refused. On `Flow::Continue`, the server rechecks receiver preconditions (live player, same menu generation and window, non-spectator, the menu's `stillValid` answer and slot index) before running default click execution on the live menu. Closing or replacing it prevents the default; inside a transaction, the close or replacement takes effect only when the outermost transaction commits. A panic aborts any transaction still open in that callback and disables only that mod's `menu_clicked` callback for the session, with an error log and toast naming the mod. Remaining parts continue, but the failed click's default never runs. The server resyncs the still-live menu and keeps committed edits. |
| `menu_removed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A menu's owner teardown (`AbstractContainerMenu.removed`): the player closed it, walked out of reach, died, changed level, opened another menu, or left. Routed to the `MenuPart` owning `menu` (or `block` for a stock menu opened by a mod block). `player` is the live player handle when available, or `None` if the player is already gone (for example when a deferred teardown runs after disconnect). `uuid` always identifies the player whose menu closed, and `window` is the closed container id. `opening_hand` is the hand the opener supplied, if any. `owned_slots` contains the transient container's detached final values in slot order, or an empty slice when there was no such container; those stacks are not returned or dropped as entities by the game. The menu's transient storage is already detached before this callback runs, and `player_container_close` follows once teardown finishes. Server-side. |
| `menu_slot_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The non-result slots of an open mod menu of type `menu` changed on the server, so its outputs can follow its inputs, as vanilla processing menus do in `AbstractContainerMenu.slotsChanged`. Nothing is asked when no part listens for `menu`. |
| `menu_slot_rules` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | Slot placement, pickup and stack limits for registered vanilla and mod menus; mounts use `sunrise:mount_inventory`, while player inventory and the creative picker have no registered type. `slot` is the backing index, including player inventory slots. The slot's own answers arrive first; the maximum is bounded to 0–99 and the item limit (matching vanilla `Slot.getMaxStackSize` and `Container.getMaxStackSize`). Refusal also prevents quick-move top-ups. Runs on server and client prediction with the read `Host`: it is a query, so it can neither write nor cancel. Each live `may_place`, `may_pickup` and `getMaxStackSize` check during a click asks the parts directly against the current menu slots (matching vanilla `AbstractContainerMenu.doClick` and `Slot`). Permission and capacity are separate checks. During shift-click a later destination sees the source remainder and the earlier destinations' updates. A nested `MenuRef::slot_holds` or `MenuRef::slot_gives` read uses the slot's own rules without asking menu Parts again, even for another slot or menu. `context.slot` still reads the slots of the ongoing click. A panicking part's answers are discarded and its callback is disabled for the session, with the mod named in the log and error toast. The other parts still answer the check. Answer quickly from the question and the read-only Host. On a LAN host's own click the LAN host is not reachable through the `Host` (it answers as if no LAN were open). `container` is the container `slot` indexes (`MenuContainer`): a mod crafting grid's slots are `Grid`'s and its result `Result`'s slot 0. `context` lends the player, the menu's block position and the live slots for this ask (`MenuSlotContext`). Read slots on demand with `context.slot(container, index)`, covering both the menu's backing containers (`Main`, `Grid`, `Result`) and the player's inventory (`Player`: `0..36` inventory, `36..40` armor feet-to-head, `40` offhand, matching vanilla `Inventory`). Bound linked slots read their actual storage; a prepared crafting result belongs to the grid that was shown with it. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `menu_slot_take` | server | listen | — | — | A player took a stack out of slot `slot` of the mod menu type `menu` (`Slot.onTake`), on the server: a notice after the item left the slot, which refuses nothing (the pickup rule, `menu_slot_rules`' `may_pickup`, refuses). It reaches the parts registered for `menu`, on a block-backed mod menu and on a blockless owned menu alike. `subject` lends the server `Host` (`subject.host()`) and atomic transactions (`subject.transaction(...)`) on both; on a block-backed menu it also lends sequential access to the live resident block entity (`subject.with_view(...)`), which returns `None` on a blockless menu or once the block entity was removed or replaced during the call. `player` is the player's UUID; `slot` is the menu's index, the menu's own slots first (linked containers and the player's inventory are never heard). `taken` is a copy of the stack that left the slot, components included, and `left` what the slot holds afterwards (`None` when it was emptied). Vanilla hands `onTake` the taken stack on a click but the stack left in the slot on a quick move, so both are carried: for a quick move `taken` is the amount moved (the slot's stack before, less `left`). |
| `menu_still_valid` | server | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | Whether a player's mod menu stays open (`AbstractContainerMenu.stillValid`). Asked on the server each tick the menu is open and before processing a matching non-spectator container click. If `menu_clicked` listeners run and leave the menu open and the default unhandled, this check runs again before the default action. `pos` is the backing block's position, or `None` for a blockless menu even if its opener supplied a position for the client's screen. `opening_item` is present when the opener supplied a hand; its `stack_matches` says whether that hand holds the same item with the same components as at open (count ignored). Neither affects the initial validity answer. `valid` starts true without a backing block; for a block menu it is whether `eye` is within the player's block interaction range plus 4 of each backing block. Set it to keep the menu open or close it. Vanilla menu types are never asked, and a menu whose block entity is gone closes without asking. Answer from the event and loop context alone. |
| `menu_transfer` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A recipe viewer's "+" on a mod menu (JEI's `IRecipeTransferHandler`): move one set of `recipe`'s ingredients (as many as the items allow with `max`) from `player`'s inventory into the open menu of a mod menu type, or with `dry_run` only say what that would do. Asked on the server before the game's own grid transfer, only for a mod menu type whose part answers, with `menu` its type, `pos` its block (None for a menu with no block) and `recipe` the recipe's id as the viewer sent it. Return `Some(Moves)` once moved, or `Missing`, `NoRoom` or `NotIntoMenu`; a part that returns None lets the game try its own crafting-grid transfer. A dry run must move nothing. With `dry_run` a part may also push the recipe's cells nothing fills to `missing_cells`, and the cells it lacks but could craft to `craftable_cells` (both empty by default), in `RecipeTransferCheck`'s cell order; the game keeps the ones below the recipe's cell count (its known layout: one for stonecutting, 256 for a recipe it can't inspect), each once, in ascending order, and ignores both outside a dry run. |
| `recipe_book_ghost` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The server answered a recipe click in a mod menu's recipe book with missing items, and the client is about to draw the recipe's ghosts: the place vanilla calls the component's `fillGhostRecipe(ghostSlots, display, context)`. `display` is the alternative the player clicked, sent directly by the server for the current opening. `ghosts` arrives holding the game's own ghosts for the menu's layout (a crafting, furnace, stonecutter or smithing layout maps the display to its slots; a `Mod` layout arrives empty); add, change or clear them. Following vanilla's `GhostSlots.setSlot`, each ghost's `display` resolves to stacks as the book's own displays do against the screen's actual shown slots: a nonempty resolved display replaces any prior ghost for the same physical slot in place (preserving slot order), while an unresolved empty display is dropped without erasing a prior ghost, and a slot the menu's screen hides or lacks is omitted. There is no separate ghost-count cap beyond the screen's actual shown slots. A listener that pushes nothing leaves what arrived. Asked for the open menu's book only, once per server ghost response, by the part of the menu's type; a menu with none skips the hook. Client-side, with the `ClientHost`. |
| `recipe_book_place` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player clicked a recipe in the recipe book of a mod menu whose `RecipeBookLayout` is `Mod`, on the server (`RecipeBookMenu.handlePlacement`, the method a Java menu overrides). The part of the open menu's type answers by writing `placement` (which arrives as `RecipeBookPlacement::Nothing`): |

### `MobEffectPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `apply_effect_tick` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | `MobEffect.applyEffectTick`: the content mod's resolved `EffectKey` `effect` acts on `entity` in `dimension` (`minecraft:overworld`). Clear `keep` and the effect ends now, before it counts down. `entity` is a mob's `Entity.getId`, None for a player (`host.players().acting_player()` says who). It runs inside the entity's tick: a mob's world borrows the game there, so the game is read-only, and it does not hold the ticking mobs, nor the player's effects. `holder` identifies the holder; Host reads of a mob holder are unavailable while it is ticking. Queued changes apply after the hook; clearing `keep` ends the effect. `active_effects` is the holder's live active-effect snapshot at this point in the pass; `has_effect(key)` reads it, reflecting removals earlier in the pass. The current level's game rules remain readable through `host.level(dimension)`. Called only when this Part's own cadence requests it. All Parts see the same snapshots; their queued changes apply in Part order afterward (with the declaring package's — or, for a `served_by` content package, its serving native host's — Parts running first). On mobs, each queued hurt finishes its damage, death or protection, and `living_damage_post` before the next queued change or the effect countdown. Clearing `keep` skips later Parts. Server-side. |
| `on_effect_added` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `MobEffect.onEffectAdded`: called on the `MobEffectPart`s attached to the mod effect `effect` after `LivingEntity.addEffect` inserts an instance of `effect` onto `entity` that the entity did not already have (after attribute modifiers and the vanilla added sound, before `onEffectStarted`). Does not fire on reapplications, `forceAddEffect`, world load or client sync. The declaring package's parts run first, then dependent packages in dispatch and registration order. Server-side. |
| `should_apply_effect_tick_this_tick` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | `MobEffect.shouldApplyEffectTickThisTick` for this Part's effect. Each Part starts with `apply = false`; only that Part's answer decides whether its `apply_effect_tick` runs. `tick_count` is the remaining duration, or the holder's tick count for an infinite effect. For an effect declared by a `served_by` content package, its serving native host is the owning package (admitted without an extra dependency, and its Parts run first). Server only. |

## Host methods (`Host`, `ServerHost`, `ClientHost`)

### Shared (`Host`)

| Method | Signature | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `attachment_read_scope` | `fn attachment_read_scope (&self) -> Result<AttachmentReadScope<'_>, AttachmentRowError>` | Borrow this host for an attachment snapshot read in its transaction. Pass the result to `row.with_value` or `query.get`; it does not open a transaction. |
| `attachments` | `fn attachments (&mut self) -> &mut dyn AttachmentStore` | The server-owned holders' attachments; `key.get(host, holder, read)` reads one through an issued `AttachmentKey`. On a client it is the copy of what the server synced: `set` and `remove` change only the copy. |
| `biome` | `fn biome (&self, id: &str) -> Result<BiomeView<'_>, BiomeKeyError>` | installed world-data generation as a scoped `BiomeView`. Returns `BiomeKeyError::Unavailable` on a client or before world data is installed, `BiomeKeyError::InvalidIdentifier` for an invalid id, and `BiomeKeyError::UnknownBiome` for an id absent from the installed generation. |
| `block_entity` | `fn block_entity (&self, level: LevelRef, pos: [i32; 3]) -> Option<BlockEntityRef>` | Find a block entity in a loaded chunk of an open level, or on a client in the level it shows. Tick and menu callbacks keep the entity resident and readable until removal. |
| `block_entity_identity` | `fn block_entity_identity (&self, level: LevelRef, pos: [i32; 3]) -> Option<BlockEntityRef>` | The handle `BlockEntitiesHost::block_entity` gives, and also for a block entity temporarily taken out by an engine operation. Its identity and attachments stay in place, so its Part state (`BlockEntityPartState`) can still check its handle even though data reads find nothing until the row is put back. |
| `block_item` | `fn block_item (&self, block: BlockKey) -> Option<ItemKey>` | `Block.asItem`: the block's item identity, including air for a block with no item; None if either resolved identity is unavailable. |
| `block_key` | `fn block_key (&self, id: &str) -> Option<BlockKey>` | The key of the block named `id`, for a name that only arrives at run time (data, config, a command, a network message). `id` is the full registry name, `minecraft:stone` or `mymod:machine`. None for a name the registry does not hold, and before the registry is complete (during registration, use `Registrar::block_key`). Does not allocate. |
| `block_keys` | `fn block_keys (&self) -> Vec<BlockKey>` | Every block key in the registry's registration order: vanilla's first, then mod blocks in the order they registered. A block registry lists a name once per state; this yields it once. Empty before the block table completes. |
| `block_state_default` | `fn block_state_default (&self, block: BlockKey) -> Option<BlockState>` | The default state of the resolved `block` key: `Block.defaultBlockState`, the one a block takes when nothing else says. None for an unknown block. |
| `block_state_id` | `fn block_state_id (&self, name: &str) -> Option<BlockState>` | The state `name` names, read as `/setblock` reads a block (`BlockStateParser.parseForBlock`): `minecraft:oak_stairs[facing=east]`, `stone` for `minecraft:stone`, the properties left out at their default. Returns an owned block key and canonical local ordinal, never vanilla's numeric `Block.getId`. None for an unknown block, a property it doesn't have or a value it can't take, or block NBT (`{...}`); and, as `/setblock`'s argument, for anything after the block. |
| `block_state_string` | `fn block_state_string (&self, state: BlockState) -> Option<String>` | The state `state` as `/setblock` takes it back (`BlockStateParser.serialize`): its block, then each property in name order, `minecraft:oak_stairs[facing=east,half=bottom,...]`, or the block alone for one with no properties. None for an unknown key or local ordinal; an invalid value never falls back to air. |
| `built_creative_tabs` | `fn built_creative_tabs (&self ,) -> Result<Option<std::sync::Arc<CreativeModeTabs>>, CreativeTabReadError>` | `CreativeModeTab.getDisplayItems` reads it, for a reader that only has `&dyn Host`. It never builds and never fires `build_creative_mode_tab_contents`. `Ok(None)` before this side has built once (a read through `creative_tabs`, or the creative screen, builds it); `CreativeTabReadError::Unavailable` in setup or reload and without a live world. The snapshot keeps the permissions of that build and stays immutable: a later build publishes a new one. |
| `cauldron_content_for_block` | `fn cauldron_content_for_block (&self, block: BlockKey) -> Option<CauldronContent>` | Contents registered for `block`, or none if absent or no table has been built. Reads the current resource generation, including after reload. |
| `cauldron_content_for_fluid` | `fn cauldron_content_for_fluid (&self, fluid: FluidKey) -> Option<CauldronContent>` | Contents registered for `fluid`, or none if absent or no table has been built. |
| `cauldron_level` | `fn cauldron_level (&self, state: BlockState) -> Option<u32>` | Current level of `state`, or none without registered contents or a built table. Always-full contents answer their maximum level. |
| `check_payload_token` | `fn check_payload_token (&self, token: &PayloadToken) -> Result<(), PayloadSendError>` | Whether `token` is one this load's registrar handed out: the frozen declaration at its slot has its id, protocol, direction and schema, and its owning mod is on. Anything else (forged, from another load, of another type) is `StaleToken`, and nothing is sent. |
| `chunk` | `fn chunk (&self, level: LevelRef, pos: [i32; 2]) -> Option<ChunkRef>` | Find a loaded chunk in an open server level (`ServerChunkCache.getChunkNow`) or in a logged-in client's loaded level. On a server, a FULL chunk found becomes one of the level's recent FULL lookups, so a `level.load_chunk` of it before the end of the level's next chunk tick places no ticket. |
| `config_dir` | `fn config_dir (&self) -> &std::path::Path` | The game's `config/` folder beside `options.txt` (beside `server.properties` on a dedicated server), as on NeoForge (`FMLPaths.CONFIGDIR`). Shared by every mod; mods read and write their own config files with ordinary `std::fs` calls. Nothing is created here. |
| `creative_tabs` | `fn creative_tabs (&mut self, permissions: CreativeTabPermissions ,) -> Result<std::sync::Arc<CreativeModeTabs>, CreativeTabReadError>` | On a cache miss, build it with explicit `permissions` and dispatch `build_creative_mode_tab_contents`. It does not require an open creative screen. A same-parameter read from a listener sees tabs already built and the previous contents of tabs not yet built. Reentering with different parameters is refused. Published snapshots stay immutable. Permission is a build parameter, not a grant. During setup, reload, or without a live world, returns `CreativeTabReadError::Unavailable`. |
| `dimension` | `fn dimension (&self) -> &'static str` | The level in place (`minecraft:overworld`), for a hook that isn't told which it runs in. |
| `dist` | `fn dist (&self) -> Dist` | Physical process, not the logical side of this hook. |
| `effect_key` | `fn effect_key (&self, id: &str) -> Option<EffectKey>` | The key of the effect named `id`: the full registry name, `minecraft:speed` or `mymod:rage`. None for a name the registry does not hold, and before the registry is complete (during registration, use `Registrar::effect_key`). Does not allocate. |
| `enchantment_keys` | `fn enchantment_keys (&self) -> Vec<EnchantmentKey>` | Every enchantment key in global sorted loaded-registry order, including vanilla and mod data-pack entries together (`RegistryDataLoader` sorts by id). |
| `enchantment_min_cost` | `fn enchantment_min_cost (&self, id: &str, level: u32) -> Option<i32>` | `Enchantment.getMinCost(level)`: the enchanting power needed, or None for an unknown id. |
| `enchantment_supports` | `fn enchantment_supports (&self, id: &str, item: ItemKey) -> bool` | Whether `item` is in enchantment `id`'s `supported_items` (`Enchantment.isSupportedItem`); false for an unknown id. Does not consult Item Part overrides. |
| `enchantments` | `fn enchantments (&self) -> Vec<EnchantmentInfo>` | Registered enchantments (`Registries.ENCHANTMENT`) in registry order, with `max_level` (`Enchantment.getMaxLevel`), selection `weight`, `anvil_cost` (the base anvil fee per enchantment level) and whether each is a curse. A server answers from its own registry, data packs included; a client from the server's synced entries when it has them, and the local table otherwise. The other enchantment reads use the same table. |
| `enchantments_compatible` | `fn enchantments_compatible (&self, a: &str, b: &str) -> bool` | Whether enchantments `a` and `b` may share an item (`Enchantment.areCompatible`), by the world's enchantment definitions. API @API:item_components@. |
| `entity` | `fn entity (&self, id: u32) -> Option<EntityRef>` | The entity `id` (the id events carry) as a handle for the `entity_*` methods: None with no world open, or for no such entity in the accessible sections of the level in place or among the server's players. On a client (API @API:client_entity_refs@), any entity the server shows this window, this window's own player included. API 373. |
| `entity_type_default_attribute` | `fn entity_type_default_attribute (&self, entity_type: &str, attribute: &str) -> Option<f64>` | An entity type's default value of an attribute, with no entity spawned (`DefaultAttributes.getSupplier(type).getValue(attribute)`): what a new entity of `entity_type` (`minecraft:zombie`) has for `attribute` (`minecraft:max_health`) before any modifier, its supplier's base inside the attribute's range, for vanilla's types and mods' (a mod type's declared row, NeoForge's `EntityAttributeCreationEvent` and `EntityAttributeModificationEvent` included). Both names are read as `Identifier.parse` reads them: `max_health` and `:max_health` are `minecraft:`'s. The Host and typed reload/setup contexts share this read on both sides; it never unwinds. `None` before publication, for an unregistered type or one with no supplier (such as `minecraft:item`), or for a missing attribute. |
| `entity_type_in_tag` | `fn entity_type_in_tag (&self, entity_type: &str, tag: &str) -> bool` | `EntityType.is(TagKey)`: whether `entity_type` (`minecraft:player`) is in the entity type tag `tag` (`minecraft:cannot_be_pushed_onto_boats`). |
| `entity_type_keys` | `fn entity_type_keys (&self) -> Vec<EntityTypeKey>` | Every entity-type key: built-in and content types in registry order (`BuiltInRegistries.ENTITY_TYPE`), then native general types ordered by declaring mod and type id. |
| `fluid_key` | `fn fluid_key (&self, id: &str) -> Option<FluidKey>` | Resolve a registered fluid identifier. |
| `game_event_key` | `fn game_event_key (&self, id: &str) -> Option<GameEventKey>` |  |
| `game_version` | `fn game_version (&self) -> &'static str` | Exact Minecraft version supported by this process. |
| `hover_name` | `fn hover_name (&self, stack: &ItemStack) -> Result<Text, TextFormError>` | `ItemStack.getHoverName()` as a component. The custom name wins, then a written book's nonblank raw title. Otherwise the item's name is used: a mod item's `ItemPart::item_name` answer replaces its default; `None` keeps it. Vanilla item overrides (such as potion contents or a profiled player head) participate in the item-name step; the base item uses the stack's `item_name` patch or inherits its prototype's value. A removal in the stack's patch reads empty; if a mod item's prototype has no `item_name` (including when the mod removes it), the game uses its item or block translation key. Only a fallback written into a component is kept. It carries no rarity colour or italics (`getStyledHoverName` adds those). Each viewer renders a translatable in their own language; call `text_string` on it for a string in this side's language. An invalid stack is a `TextFormError`. |
| `instance_dir` | `fn instance_dir (&self) -> PathBuf` | The instance (game) folder, which its players share: `saves/`, `resourcepacks/`, `shaderpacks/` and `sunrise-mods/` are in it, and the title's screens create worlds and read packs there. A world's own `datapacks/` is in that world's folder (`saves/<world>/datapacks`). The folder the game runs in (`--player-dir` moves a player's options and servers only, not the instance). Nothing is created. |
| `is_loaded` | `fn is_loaded (&self, id: &str) -> bool` | Whether a mod with the `sunrise-mod.toml` id `id` is installed: a native or content-only package (Fabric's `FabricLoader.isModLoaded`, NeoForge's `ModList.isLoaded`). The list is fixed at startup. |
| `item_abilities` | `fn item_abilities (&self) -> Vec<String>` | Every item ability the game asks `can_perform_action` about (NeoForge's `ItemAbility.getActions`): plain names such as `sword_sweep` and `shears_trim`, sorted. A mod's own abilities are ids it owns and are not listed. |
| `item_block` | `fn item_block (&self, item: ItemKey) -> Option<BlockKey>` | The block `item` places (`BlockItem.getBlock`, as `Block.byItem` reads it): `minecraft:wheat_seeds` places `minecraft:wheat`, and a standing/wall item places its standing block, not the wall variant. None for an item that places no block, and for an unknown item. |
| `item_burn_time` | `fn item_burn_time (&self, item: ItemKey) -> Option<i32>` | Item `item`'s cooking-fuel burn time in ticks (`FuelValues.burnDuration` of its `cooking_fuel` component, the data pack's context int provider resolved), None where it is no fuel. API @API:item_components@. |
| `item_compost_chance` | `fn item_compost_chance (&self, item: ItemKey) -> Option<f32>` | Item `item`'s chance to raise a composter's level (its `compostable` component's layers provider resolved), None where it is not compostable. API @API:item_components@. |
| `item_cooldown_group` | `fn item_cooldown_group (&self, stack: &ItemStack) -> String` | `ItemCooldowns.getCooldownGroup(stack)`: the group `stack`'s cooldown is filed under, the group its `use_cooldown` component names (its own, else its item's default), else its item id. |
| `item_crafting_remainder` | `fn item_crafting_remainder (&self, stack: &ItemStack) -> Option<ItemStack>` | `Item.getCraftingRemainder`: Sunrise currently stores the remainder's item id, so the result is a single plain stack. |
| `item_default_component` | `fn item_default_component (&self, item: ItemKey, component: &str) -> Option<Tag>` | Item `item`'s default value of component `component`, in its prototype (`Item.components().get`): `minecraft:max_damage` on an iron sword is `250`, data packs' and native mods' changes included. Read from the item report's JSON, so a number is an `Int`, `Long` or `Double` and a boolean a `Byte`. None where the item hasn't the component, and for an unknown item. |
| `item_entity` | `fn item_entity (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<ItemEntityRef>` | Narrows an entity to a dropped item, or None for another kind, a stale handle, a client, or an item temporarily taken out for ticking. |
| `item_from_json` | `fn item_from_json (&self, json: &str) -> Result<Option<ItemStack>, ItemJsonError>` | Decode one complete JSON value as an optional item stack with vanilla's `ItemStack.OPTIONAL_CODEC` (`ItemStack.CODEC` wrapped in `ExtraCodecs.optionalEmptyMap`) over `JsonOps`: `{}` decodes as `Ok(None)`, and `{"id":"...","count":N,"components":{...}}` decodes as `Ok(Some(stack))`, resolving item identifiers (outer and nested) and persistent component types against this side's registries. `count` defaults to 1 and must be in `1..=99` after Java's `Number.intValue`; bare identifiers default to `minecraft:`; component removals use `"!id":{}`. |
| `item_from_snbt` | `fn item_from_snbt (&self, snbt: &str) -> Option<ItemStack>` | Parse one complete SNBT compound as a saved `ItemStack.CODEC` item: `{id:"minecraft:diamond_sword",count:1,components:{"minecraft:damage":5}}` (`stone` is `minecraft:stone`). `count` defaults to 1; a present number must be in 1..=99 after Java’s `Number.intValue`. Unknown fields such as `Count` are ignored. Trailing whitespace is allowed. The shared SNBT parser's structural rules apply, but no binary NBT byte quota is applied to the text. Bad syntax, an invalid item or component patch, and an unknown item return `None` and log the failure reason. |
| `item_key` | `fn item_key (&self, id: &Identifier) -> Option<ItemKey>` | Resolve an item once at load, including vanilla items, to its process-local identity. None if the completed registry does not contain the identifier. |
| `item_keys` | `fn item_keys (&self) -> Vec<ItemKey>` | Every item key in the registry's registration order: vanilla's first, then mod items in the order they registered. Empty before the item table completes. |
| `item_to_json` | `fn item_to_json (&self, stack: Option<&ItemStack>) -> Result<String, ItemJsonError>` | Encode an optional item stack as JSON with vanilla's `ItemStack.OPTIONAL_CODEC` (`ItemStack.CODEC` wrapped in `ExtraCodecs.optionalEmptyMap`) over `JsonOps`: `None` (an empty stack) encodes as `{}`, and `Some(stack)` encodes as `{"id":"...","count":N}` with `"components":{...}` when the stack carries non-default persistent components or explicit removals (`"!minecraft:damage":{}`), resolved against this side's item and component registries. |
| `known_recipes` | `fn known_recipes (&mut self) -> Arc<[i32]>` | The `book_id`s of the recipes the player's recipe book holds now (the server's book on a server); empty outside a world. |
| `level` | `fn level (&self, dimension: &str) -> Option<LevelRef>` | The level `dimension` (`minecraft:the_nether`) as a handle: on a client, the level it shows, whatever the server calls it, and each level the server opened for viewing (`sunrise:levels`) while that view is open. A viewed level's handle answers its block reads from its own world: `block_state`, `block`, `block_property`, `clip`, `visit_block_collisions`, `has_collisions`, and the fluid reads built on `block_state` (`fluid_shape`, `fluid_height`, `fluid_height_for_camera`, `fluid_can_be_replaced_with`). `get_enchant_power_bonus` uses the viewed level as context. Its other reads, its entity reads and every write refuse it. It answers None while the server has the level closed; a close and a later open within the same client level make the old handle read again. A login, a reconfiguration or a respawn into another level replaces the client level: the old handle stays stale and the mod asks `host.level` again. None with no world open, or for a dimension the server hasn't loaded. In `client_level_load`, the new level resolves by the event's dimension even before it is installed; in `client_level_unload`, the retiring level still resolves. API 373. |
| `list_resources` | `fn list_resources (&self, pack_type: PackType, directory: &str, suffix: &str) -> Vec<Identifier>` | The resources of `pack_type`'s stack under folder `directory` of every namespace whose path ends in `suffix`, one each, in identifier order, as `ResourceManager.listResources` answers it: `list_resources(PackType::ClientResources, "textures/item", ".png")` gives `minecraft:textures/item/apple.png` and the rest. An empty `directory` lists a whole namespace. Metadata files (`.mcmeta`) are never listed, and a name that isn't a valid identifier is skipped. A side without that stack, or a `directory` that leaves its namespace, lists nothing. This read never reloads packs. |
| `living` | `fn living (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<LivingRef>` | The entity as a living entity (vanilla's `LivingEntity`: a mob, a player, an armor stand or the dragon), or None for another kind or a stale handle. |
| `math_profile` | `fn math_profile (&self) -> MathProfile` | The native engine's fixed selection for the seven platform-dependent Java Math operations. This read allocates nothing on the native path. It identifies behavior, not the machine. See `MathProfile` for the algorithms selected by each profile. |
| `max_damage` | `fn max_damage (&self, item: ItemKey) -> Option<u32>` | Default durability of the resolved item, or none if its metadata is unavailable. Stack-specific component overrides are checked at the game's write boundary. |
| `max_stack_size` | `fn max_stack_size (&self, item: ItemKey) -> Option<u32>` | The most of the item `item` one slot holds (`Item.getDefaultMaxStackSize`), or `None` for an unknown item: for a mod's container that fills or merges stacks itself. |
| `menu_keys` | `fn menu_keys (&self) -> Vec<MenuKey>` | Every built-in menu-type key in registry order (`BuiltInRegistries.MENU` order): vanilla's first, then mod types in the order they registered. |
| `mob` | `fn mob (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<MobRef>` | The entity as a vanilla Mob: a mob or the ender dragon. Players, armor stands, stale handles and clients are rejected. Mobs taken out for ticking are unavailable. |
| `mod_recipe_property_contains` | `fn mod_recipe_property_contains (&self, set: &str, item: ItemKey) -> bool` | Whether a mod-created recipe property set accepts an item ID. Unknown sets/items and vanilla keys return false. The server uses its current load, and clients use the server's last synchronized generation (local preload before the first packet). The same read on `LoopContext` lets `menu_slot_rules` filter a slot identically for server and prediction. Membership reads allocate nothing and perform no registry scans. |
| `on` | `fn on (&self, mod_id: &str) -> bool` | Whether the player left mod `mod_id` on in Options > Mods. A mod turned off does nothing (check this first in each hook) until the launcher's next build leaves it out. |
| `open_resource` | `fn open_resource (&self, pack_type: PackType, identifier: &Identifier ,) -> Result<Option<Box<dyn std::io::Read + '_>>, ResourceOpenError>` | Open the original file from the winning pack, as `ResourceManager.getResource` and `Resource.open` do, not the bytes merged for a renderer or reload listener. In priority order, each pack can supply its own file or block lower files with `filter.block`. Client resources come from the selected asset packs over the game's assets; server data comes from the selected world data packs over the game's and mods' data, with `minecraft:vanilla` kept unless disabled. `identifier` is the resource ID (e.g. `minecraft:models/block/stone.json` or `mymod:machines/furnace.json`), without the pack's directory prefix. |
| `payload_drop_counts` | `fn payload_drop_counts (&self, id: &PayloadId ,) -> Result<Option<PayloadDropCounts>, LevelReadError>` | The drops of `Droppable` channel `id` so far, by cause, over every connection of this game (a server's clientbound drops, a client's serverbound ones, and the server's refusals of a client over its byte budget). `None` for a channel that is not `Droppable` or not declared by an enabled mod. `Err(Unavailable)` when the host has no backend. |
| `player` | `fn player (&self, uuid: u128) -> Option<PlayerRef>` | Resolve a real online player by UUID, never a fake. A fake is reached through the handle returned by its factory. During a nested player swap, outer players whose records are displaced return None. |
| `recipe_book_category` | `fn recipe_book_category (&mut self, kind: &str) -> Option<RecipeBookCategory>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. The validated category and icon of a custom recipe type, or None for an undeclared/invalid type, before resources load, or outside a world. Returns the first category in category-id order when a type has several. This local declaration may include placement predicates; it is not the category data sent to a remote client. Any invalid declaration refuses the complete generation. Rebuilt when the recipe table changes. Does not unlock any recipes. |
| `recipe_ingredient` | `fn recipe_ingredient (&self, json: &str) -> Result<Option<Ingredient>, RecipeJsonError>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. An ingredient from recipe-file JSON text: an item, a `#tag`, or a list of them, a bare id as `minecraft:` (as vanilla reads it); `test` says whether a stack fits. Items the game doesn't know are left out; None for missing tags, invalid ingredients or no known named items, and outside a world. An existing-empty tag returns an empty ingredient. This helper parses standalone JSON; it does not bind a mod's custom kind. To test a custom ingredient already resolved at load, use `Ingredient::test`. Invalid JSON is refused with `RecipeJsonError::InvalidJson`. |
| `recipe_result` | `fn recipe_result (&self, json: &str) -> Result<Option<ItemStack>, RecipeJsonError>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. A stack from recipe-file `result` JSON text: `id` (a bare one as `minecraft:`), `count` (1 by default, up to 99) and `components`. None without an `id`, for an item the game doesn't know or a count outside that, and outside a world. Invalid JSON is refused with `RecipeJsonError::InvalidJson`. |
| `recipes_of_type` | `fn recipes_of_type (&self, kind: &str) -> Arc<[Recipe]>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. The recipes of registered recipe type `kind` (`minecraft:crafting`, `mymod:crusher`), vanilla's types and mods' own alike, from `data/<namespace>/recipe/*.json` of vanilla, the content mods (`.smod`) and the world's data packs, in file order: each one typed (`Recipe`: its ingredients, results, group, cooking time and experience, or its stonecutting, smithing or special crafting rule), together with its JSON. Locally loaded recipes give the original recipe JSON; synced vanilla serializers give the server's resolved item lists, and synced mod serializers give the data written by that serializer. Bound native predicates aren't written into the JSON or sent to clients. The `type` field names the serializer; this can differ from the reported recipe kind. Dynamic rules are not fixed outputs; use `LevelRef::recipe_for` for engine-matchable recipe types. Registered crafting kinds match through LevelRef::recipe_for. Cache other mod-owned types once per load/reload and match those in mod memory; those return RecipeLookupError::NotMatchable. These are the recipes in force (`RecipeManager.getAllRecipesFor`): data packs merged and load conditions held, so a pack's override or removal shows. A loaded recipe the API cannot represent is left out of this listing and logged at debug level with its id and reason; the rest remain in order. This does not change recipe loading. A data pack changes them on `/reload`, so look them up again when you need them. Empty for a type nobody uses and outside a world. These are the server's table on the server thread, a dedicated server or a LAN host; a client, the single-player window included, sees vanilla's and the content mods' recipes, not the world's data packs', unless a mod had the server send it the type (`native_datapack_sync`). |
| `registry_entry` | `fn registry_entry (&self, registry: &str, id: &str) -> Option<RegistryEntry>` | Resolve a canonical entry in the current side's live registry. None for an unknown registry or entry, or before that registry is available. |
| `registry_entry_in_tag` | `fn registry_entry_in_tag (&self, entry: &RegistryEntry, tag: &str) -> Option<bool>` | Membership in the current side's merged tags. None for an unavailable registry or entry; an absent tag, or a tag not containing the entry, returns false. |
| `registry_protocol_id` | `fn registry_protocol_id (&self, registry: &Identifier, name: &Identifier) -> Option<i32>` | The network id of `name` in `registry` (`Registry.getId`): the number a packet carries for it, for a mod that builds or reads raw packets (`minecraft:particle_type` and `minecraft:flame`). Built-in registries answer their fixed ids, mod entries included; data-driven ones (`minecraft:damage_type`, `minecraft:biome`, ...) answer the order the server synced them in; custom data component types are included. A client reads its own connection's registries, a server the ones it sends. The id holds for one connection and one data reload: do not keep it across a reconnect, a reconfiguration or `/reload`. None for an unknown registry or name, on a client before that registry was synced, and with no connection. Allocates nothing; each registry is searched in order. Use `ItemKey::registry_id` for the local prepared item number used by scatter rendering; that read is available without a connection and is not a packet lookup. |
| `resource_stack` | `fn resource_stack (&self, pack_type: PackType, identifier: &Identifier) -> Vec<Vec<u8>>` | Every selected pack's copy of resource `identifier` in `pack_type`'s stack, lowest first and the highest pack's last, as `ResourceManager.getResourceStack` answers it; empty where no pack supplies it. The stack and the names are those of the selected packs; `open_resource` opens the highest pack's own file. A copy that can't be read is left out, so the last entry can be a lower pack's copy than the one `open_resource` opens. This read never reloads packs. |
| `server_data_version` | `fn server_data_version (&self) -> i32` | The engine's `DataVersion` written into level data and chunks. Constant on both sides, including before a world is open; this describes this game build, not the version of a remote server or an older save. |
| `server_has_time` | `fn server_has_time (&self) -> bool` | Whether the server's current tick still has time before the next one is due (`MinecraftServer.haveTime`, NeoForge's `ServerTickEvent.hasTime` and `LevelTickEvent.hasTime`): a mod doing optional work in `server_tick` or `level_tick_pre`/`level_tick_post` skips it when this is false. Always true on a client, and outside a scheduled tick. |
| `setting` | `fn setting (&self, key: SettingKey) -> Result<SettingValue, SettingAccessError>` | Read this side's live value of an owner-bound setting registered through `SettingsRegistrar::register_setting`. Writes are the side's own (`set_setting` on `ClientHost` or `ServerHost`). |
| `setting_bool` | `fn setting_bool (&self, key: SettingKey) -> Result<bool, SettingAccessError>` | Read a toggle setting through its owner-bound `SettingKey`. |
| `setting_choice` | `fn setting_choice (&self, key: SettingKey) -> Result<u32, SettingAccessError>` | Read a choice setting's selected index through its owner-bound `SettingKey`. |
| `setting_color` | `fn setting_color (&self, key: SettingKey) -> Result<u32, SettingAccessError>` | Read a colour setting (`0xRRGGBB`) through its owner-bound `SettingKey`. |
| `setting_f64` | `fn setting_f64 (&self, key: SettingKey) -> Result<f64, SettingAccessError>` | Read a range setting's number through its owner-bound `SettingKey`. |
| `sunrise_version` | `fn sunrise_version (&self) -> &'static str` | Sunrise version printed in crash reports. |
| `tags` | `fn tags (&self) -> &dyn Tags` | The merged-tag service, using load-resolved block, item, fluid and tag keys. |
| `text_from_json` | `fn text_from_json (&self, json: &str) -> Result<Option<Text>, TextFormError>` | Reads a text component as `/tellraw` and `ComponentSerialization.CODEC` take it: one JSON or SNBT value (`"plain"`, `{"text":"Hi","color":"gold"}`, `["a",{"text":"b"}]`, `translate`, `extra`, click and hover events), with nothing but whitespace after it (Paper's `GsonComponentSerializer.deserialize`). None when it does not parse as one component: an object needs one content key (a `type` binds alone, a null `type` counts as absent), `text` must be a string, `extra` a non-empty list, and a style field that is present and wrong refuses the whole component. Command, URL and page click actions use `command`, `url` and `page`, respectively; a legacy `value`-only click for those actions is refused. `open_url` requires a well-formed HTTP(S) URI; a malformed URI or another scheme refuses the component (`Ok(None)`). `show_item` refuses `minecraft:air`; a component removal (`!id`) requires an object value. An object whose only content key is `index` is refused, as in vanilla's codec; Sunrise follows vanilla there. |
| `text_from_legacy` | `fn text_from_legacy (&self, legacy: &str) -> Result<Text, TextFormError>` | Reads legacy formatting codes (`§a`, `§l`, `§r`...) as the font draws them (`Style.applyLegacyFormat`; Bukkit's `ChatColor` strings): a colour code resets the decorations, `§r` resets everything, an unknown code is dropped. `&` codes are not read; a mod that takes them replaces `&` with `§` first, as `ChatColor.translateAlternateColorCodes` does. |
| `text_from_nbt` | `fn text_from_nbt (&self, tag: &Tag) -> Result<Option<Text>, TextFormError>` | Read a component with `ComponentSerialization.CODEC` and `NbtOps`. A string is literal, including `§` codes. Numeric style flags are true when nonzero, and a custom click payload keeps its original NBT tag types. `Ok(None)` means the tag does not decode as a component; nesting beyond the 512 containers allowed by vanilla NBT returns `TextFormError::TooDeep`. |
| `text_string` | `fn text_string (&self, text: &Text) -> String` | `Component.getString()`: resolve translations, arguments, keybinds and siblings to plain characters. Styles do not add characters; literal legacy formatting codes remain. |
| `text_to_nbt` | `fn text_to_nbt (&self, text: &Text) -> Result<Tag, TextFormError>` | Encode one component with `ComponentSerialization.CODEC` and `NbtOps`. An unstyled literal with no siblings encodes as a string. Translations and styles are stored, not resolved. Refuses malformed fields and nesting beyond vanilla NBT's 512 containers. |
| `upgrade_nbt` | `fn upgrade_nbt (&self, _kind: NbtDataType, _data_version: i32, _nbt: Compound ,) -> Result<Compound, NbtUpgradeError>` | Run the game's data fixers on one saved compound, from `data_version` to the current game data version. This needs no world, adds no `DataVersion` field, and runs no fixes that operate on the enclosing chunk or file. Use `NbtDataType::EntityTree` if the upgrade should include riders and passengers as well as the root entity. Like vanilla's standalone fixers, entity and block-entity roots can retain legacy keys alongside newly written ones. |
| `visit_registry_entries` | `fn visit_registry_entries (&self, registry: &str, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (& str) -> core::ops::ControlFlow<()> ,) -> Result<core::ops::ControlFlow<()>, RegistryReadError>` | Visit the canonical entry IDs in the current side's registry, in the table's existing order: numeric IDs for protocol tables, lexical order for name-keyed local tables. IDs are borrowed for each call; use `Identifier::parse` if you need to retain a typed ID. Return `ControlFlow::Break(())` to stop, or `ControlFlow::Continue(())` to keep visiting. Returns `Ok(Continue(()))` on completion, including for an empty registry, or `Ok(Break(()))` on early stop. An unknown registry or one not exposed on this side returns `RegistryReadError::UnknownRegistry`. Without a live session, returns `RegistryReadError::Unavailable`. Neither calls `visit`. |

### Server (`ServerHost`)

| Method | Signature | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `add_objective` | `fn add_objective (&mut self, name: &str, criteria: &str, display_name: Text, render_type: ObjectiveRenderType ,) -> Result<ObjectiveRef, ObjectiveError>` | `Scoreboard.addObjective`: a new objective with no number format that does not update scores' display names, shown nowhere. The name is any string, as the board takes it; the `/scoreboard` command only reads a single word. Refused with `ObjectiveError::NameTaken` or `ObjectiveError::UnknownCriteria`. |
| `add_team` | `fn add_team (&mut self, name: &str) -> Result<TeamRef, TeamError>` | `/team add`: a new team with its name as display name, no color, no members and vanilla's default options. The name is any string, as the board takes it. Refused with `TeamError::NameTaken` when a team has it. |
| `advancement` | `fn advancement (&mut self, id: &Identifier) -> Option<AdvancementInfo>` | `ServerAdvancementManager.get(id)`: the loaded advancement `id`, from the set the last data-pack load or `/reload` built (loaded on first use). None when no advancement has that id. |
| `attachment_validate_write` | `fn attachment_validate_write (&self, token: AttachmentWriteToken ,) -> Result<(), AttachmentRowError>` | Validate the capture's transaction, if any, and residency in the current dimension immediately before an ordinary journaled attachment write. The token comes from a captured row, not from disk or the network. Returns `Unavailable` outside the world phase. In the world phase, `OutsideTransaction` for an expired frame takes precedence over `StaleHolder` for missing or mismatched residency. This does not open a transaction. |
| `boss_bar` | `fn boss_bar (&self, id: &str) -> Option<BossBarRef>` | An existing custom boss bar by id (`minecraft:` when no namespace), or None. Saved bars are there from the world's start, as vanilla's `CustomBossEvents`. Boss bars of mobs and raids are not custom boss events. |
| `boss_bars` | `fn boss_bars (&self) -> Vec<BossBarRef>` | Every custom boss bar, in the order `/bossbar list` shows them. |
| `cancel_task` | `fn cancel_task (&mut self, id: TaskId) -> bool` | Withdraws the waiting task identified by the opaque capability `id`: returns `true` if it was still waiting. A panic while dropping an attributed task's captures is reported against the scheduling mod, disables that mod's delayed callbacks and does not unwind through the caller. |
| `commands` | `fn commands (&mut self) -> &mut dyn Commands` | The commands service. |
| `configuration_has_channel` | `fn configuration_has_channel (&self, connection: ConfigurationConnection, channel: &PayloadId ,) -> bool` |  |
| `configuration_send_payload` | `fn configuration_send_payload (&mut self, connection: ConfigurationConnection, channel: &PayloadId, bytes: PayloadBytes ,) -> Result<(), ConfigurationTaskError>` | connection while its configuration epoch is active, regardless of current task state. A saved connection returns `ConfigurationTaskError::Disconnected` after it moves to Play. Prefer `ConfigurationTaskContext::send_payload` in start and tick callbacks. |
| `create_boss_bar` | `fn create_boss_bar (&mut self, id: &str, name: Text ,) -> Result<Option<BossBarRef>, TextFormError>` | `/bossbar add`: a new bar, visible, white, `progress`, at 0 of 100, with nobody added. None if the id is taken or invalid, or outside a server. Refuses text deeper than 512 component nodes before applying any changes. |
| `display` | `fn display (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<DisplayRef>` | Narrows an entity to a display, or None for another kind, a stale handle or a client. |
| `display_slot_objective` | `fn display_slot_objective (&self, slot: DisplaySlot) -> Option<ObjectiveRef>` | `Scoreboard.getDisplayObjective`: the objective shown in the slot, or None. |
| `dragon` | `fn dragon (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<DragonRef>` | The entity as the ender dragon, or None for another kind, a stale handle or a client. |
| `experience_orb` | `fn experience_orb (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Result<Option<ExperienceOrbRef>, LevelReadError>` | Narrows `entity` to an `ExperienceOrbRef` across the active and parked server levels. Returns `Ok(None)` when the handle is stale, removed, held out, or names a non-orb entity; returns `Err(LevelReadError::Unavailable)` on a client or outside the world phase. |
| `fake_player` | `fn fake_player (&mut self, level: LevelRef, uuid: u128, name: &str) -> Option<PlayerRef>` | Create or retrieve a connectionless player for the level currently in place. |
| `fishing_hook` | `fn fishing_hook (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<FishingHookRef>` | Narrows an entity to a cast fishing hook. None for another kind, a stale handle, a client, or a hook taken out for its tick. |
| `fluid_interaction_result` | `fn fluid_interaction_result (&self, source: FluidState, neighbour: BlockState, below: BlockState ,) -> Option<BlockState>` | What a liquid cell of `source` becomes beside one cell (`neighbour`, a block state whose fluid is read through it, so a waterlogged block holds water) with `below` under it: the open world's fluid interaction table (vanilla's lava rules, then every mod's entries, as `LiquidBlock.shouldSpreadLiquid` and NeoForge's `FluidInteractionRegistry` walk it) for that one neighbour, with the same exact-tag and family matching, a source cell's result for a source and the flowing result otherwise. `None` is no interaction, and for a fluid with no entries or one that has no world form. A pure read: it changes no world, fires no `fluid_place_block` and plays no fizz. A liquid checks its five neighbours in vanilla's order, above, north, south, west, east (never below), and the first that answers wins: loop over them in that order to ask the same. |
| `game_rule` | `fn game_rule (&self, id: &str) -> Result<GameRuleValue, GameRuleReadError>` | Read the server-wide rule from the first server hook onward. Returns `UnknownRule` for an unknown id; level reads use the same store. |
| `game_tests_enabled` | `fn game_tests_enabled (&self) -> bool` | Startup opt-in for test-only content. Test registration runs regardless. |
| `holder_team` | `fn holder_team (&self, holder: ScoreHolder) -> Option<TeamRef>` | `Scoreboard.getPlayersTeam`: the team the holder is on, or None when it is on none or does not resolve. Membership is by the holder's key, so an offline player's name works. |
| `levels` | `fn levels (&self) -> &dyn Levels` | The levels service. |
| `merchant` | `fn merchant (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<MerchantRef>` | Narrows an entity to a villager or wandering trader, or None for another kind, a stale handle, or a client. |
| `messages` | `fn messages (&self) -> &dyn Messages` | The messages service. |
| `minecart` | `fn minecart (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<MinecartRef>` | Narrows an entity to a minecart of any kind, or None for another kind of entity, a stale handle, a client, or an entity held out of storage. |
| `objective` | `fn objective (&self, name: &str) -> Option<ObjectiveRef>` | `Scoreboard.getObjective`: the objective with that name, or None. The name is any string; saved objectives are there from the world's start. |
| `players` | `fn players (&self) -> &dyn Players` | The players service. |
| `players_send_payload` | `fn players_send_payload (&mut self, players: &[PlayerRef], id: &PayloadId, bytes: PayloadBytes ,) -> u32` | Queue the same application bytes on each of `players`' connections: one crossing and one buffer for the whole group, where `player_send_payload` repeats both per player. Each send follows `player_send_payload`'s rules; a player whose send is refused (offline or stale, no channel, wrong phase, backpressure, framing) is skipped and not counted. Returns how many sends were accepted, which is 0 for an empty list; a player listed twice is sent to twice. Publication waits for root commit; abort publishes nothing. |
| `profiles` | `fn profiles (&self) -> &dyn Profiles` | The profiles service. |
| `projectile` | `fn projectile (&self, entity: EntityRef) -> Option<ProjectileRef>` | Narrows an entity to a projectile: one in the projectile list, a trident a player threw or a firework rocket. None for another kind, a stale handle or a client. |
| `reload_resources` | `fn reload_resources (&self) -> &dyn ServerReloadResources` | The retained server reload listeners of the last successful reload. |
| `runtime_levels` | `fn runtime_levels (&mut self) -> &mut dyn RuntimeLevels` | The runtime_levels service. |
| `schedule_task` | `fn schedule_task (&mut self, delay_ticks: u32, task: Box<dyn FnOnce (&mut dyn ServerHost)> ,) -> Result<TaskId, ScheduleError>` | Runs `task` once after `delay_ticks` server ticks, matching vanilla's `MinecraftServer.schedule(TickTask)` / `BlockableEventLoop.runAllTasks`. Due tasks drain to exhaustion after both the `server_tick` start hook and the `server_tick` end hook, so `delay_ticks == 0` runs in the current tick (including `0`-delay tasks scheduled from inside a running task). A paused server does not advance the delay. Tasks due together run in the order they were scheduled. |
| `server_is_published` | `fn server_is_published (&self) -> bool` | Whether the server is open to players other than a single-player world's owner (`MinecraftServer.isPublished`): always true on a dedicated server; on a single-player world's server, true while it is open to LAN (the Open to LAN screen or `/publish`) and false again once that ends (`/unpublish`). No hook fires when it changes: read it when the answer matters. |
| `server_max_players` | `fn server_max_players (&self) -> Option<u32>` | The most players who may be on at once (`PlayerList.getMaxPlayers`, `max-players`), as the server list shows it. Logins at or above the limit are refused unless they bypass it. None where no server is open to players (a client, or a server nobody can join). |
| `server_set_max_players` | `fn server_set_max_players (&mut self, max_players: u32) -> Result<(), PlayerLimitRefusal>` | Sets the player limit from the next login and server list ping on (Paper's `Server.setMaxPlayers`); players already on stay, even when they are more than the new limit. The existing login bypass still applies (dedicated operators flagged `bypassesPlayerLimit`). `server.properties` is not changed, so a restart goes back to its `max-players`. Writes nothing and refuses with `PlayerLimitRefusal::NotOpen` where no server is open to players, and with `TooHigh { maximum }` for a limit above `i32::MAX`, as vanilla keeps the limit in an `int` (Paper's `setMaxPlayers` takes one). Zero is taken: it refuses all new logins that do not bypass the limit. |
| `server_set_simulation_distance` | `fn server_set_simulation_distance (&mut self, distance: i32) -> bool` | Sets the live simulation distance through `PlayerList.setSimulationDistance` on either server, including unpublished worlds. Players get the new distance and simulation tickets use it. Returns `false` and logs the refusal if the player list is unavailable or the input is outside the 2–32 supported endpoints of `Options.simulationDistance`. An unchanged owner option does not override this write; a changed one does. This does not change `server.properties` or the owner's options. |
| `server_set_view_distance` | `fn server_set_view_distance (&mut self, distance: i32) -> bool` | Sets the live view distance through `PlayerList.setViewDistance` on either server, including unpublished worlds. Players get the new chunk radius, and chunk tracking and loading tickets use the new distance. Returns `false` and logs the refusal if the player list is unavailable or the input is outside `ChunkMap`'s 2–32 supported range. The integrated server follows the owner's render distance at the end of an unpaused tick. A view-distance write from a server-tick listener can be superseded at the end of that tick. This does not change `server.properties` or the owner's options. |
| `server_simulation_distance` | `fn server_simulation_distance (&self) -> Option<i32>` | The live simulation distance in chunks advertised by `PlayerList.getSimulationDistance`. Available on dedicated and integrated servers, including unpublished worlds; `None` when no logical server player list is available. |
| `server_view_distance` | `fn server_view_distance (&self) -> Option<i32>` | The live view distance in chunks advertised by `PlayerList.getViewDistance`. Available on dedicated and integrated servers, including unpublished worlds; `None` when no logical server player list is available. |
| `set_display_slot` | `fn set_display_slot (&mut self, slot: DisplaySlot, objective: Option<ObjectiveRef>) -> bool` | `Scoreboard.setDisplayObjective`: shows the objective in the slot, or clears the slot with None. Clients are sent the change by the board's usual per-tick sync. False for a stale objective. |
