| `set_setting` | `fn set_setting (&mut self, key: SettingKey, value: SettingValue ,) -> Result<bool, SettingAccessError>` | Persist and publish a setting on this server and synchronously notify its owner. Returns `Ok(true)` if the value changed (`Range` values compare by bits), or `Ok(false)` if it was already equal. |
| `set_tick_rate` | `fn set_tick_rate (&mut self, rate: f32) -> bool` | Sets the logical server's tick rate (`ServerTickRateManager.setTickRate`). `TickRateManager.setTickRate` raises finite values below 1 to 1; `/tick rate`'s upper bound is only a command argument limit. Every accepted write, even an equal one, fires `server_tick_rate_changed`, shortens the autosave wait if needed, then sends the final rate and freeze state to clients. A nested write skips callbacks for this hook already running on this thread. A non-finite rate or a call off the logical server returns `false` and logs the refusal without changing the clock. |
| `spawn_projectile_from_rotation` | `fn spawn_projectile_from_rotation (&mut self, owner: LivingRef, entity_type: &Identifier, stack: &ItemStack, offset: f32, power: f32, inaccuracy: f32 ,) -> Option<ProjectileRef>` | projectile: snowball, egg, pearl, potion, experience bottle, wind charge, or a registered mod thrown type. Constructs at the owner's eyes minus 0.1F blocks (wind charges use eye height directly), sets ownership before shooting, inherits known movement (vertical only while airborne), adds to the level and asks `entity_join_level` whether to admit it. Spread draws x, y, z triangular noise from the new projectile's own random. Once it is in the level, `stack`'s enchantments run their `projectile_spawned` effects on it (`Projectile.applyOnProjectileSpawned`): in the stack's order, each effect's requirements tested with the level random just before it applies, its own draws from the projectile's random. Does not consume the stack or apply item-use cooldowns or statistics. None for an unavailable owner or one in another level, unsupported type, invalid stack or non-finite shot parameter. Also refuses a stack with a `projectile_spawned` effect the engine does not apply: `ignite`, `apply_impulse`, `play_sound` and `all_of` of those apply (`apply_mob_effect` and `apply_exhaustion` do nothing to a projectile, as in vanilla); the block, damage, explosion, particle, summon, function and item-damage effects are refused rather than skipped. No random is drawn and nothing spawns on those refusals. Unsupported requirement contexts (including entity flags) and invalid clamped level-value ranges also refuse before insertion. Type predicates, random chance and their boolean combinations are supported. The original stack is unchanged; carried count is one; the effects see the stack as given. |
| `team` | `fn team (&self, name: &str) -> Option<TeamRef>` | `Scoreboard.getPlayerTeam`: the team with that name, or None. |
| `tick_rate_is_frozen` | `fn tick_rate_is_frozen (&self) -> Option<bool>` | Whether the server's ticking is frozen (`TickRateManager.isFrozen`, `/tick freeze`). A frozen server still runs the ticks `/tick step` asks for, so `server_tick_start`'s `world_running` can be true while this is true. None on a client. |
| `tick_stats` | `fn tick_stats (&self) -> (f32, f64)` | The server's tick rate (20 by default) and the milliseconds its ticks took on average lately, as `/tick query` reports them. The ticks a second it keeps up is the rate, or 1000 / milliseconds when that is lower. |
| `transaction_lifecycle` | `fn transaction_lifecycle (&self) -> TransactionLifecycle` | Where the transaction system is (`Transaction.getLifecycle`): none open, one open, a nested one closing, or the outermost closing while the game tells the world of a commit. |
| `visit_game_rules` | `fn visit_game_rules (&self, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (& str, GameRuleValue) -> core::ops::ControlFlow<()> ,) -> Result<core::ops::ControlFlow<()>, ServerReadError>` | Visit the available rules in the server-wide store, with their borrowed namespaced IDs and typed values. Readable from `server_about_to_start`, before any level exists; level visits use the same store. Return `Break(())` to stop. An unavailable rule store returns `Unavailable`. |
| `visit_objectives` | `fn visit_objectives (&self, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (ObjectiveRef) -> core::ops::ControlFlow<()> ,) -> Result<core::ops::ControlFlow<()>, ServerReadError>` | `Scoreboard.getObjectives`: visits every objective in the resident board's order. Vanilla leaves that order unspecified. Return `ControlFlow::Break(())` to stop; the result is `Ok(Break(()))` on a stop or `Ok(Continue(()))` on completion, including an empty board. An unavailable board does not call the visitor. |
| `visit_teams` | `fn visit_teams (&self, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (TeamRef) -> core::ops::ControlFlow<()> ,) -> Result<core::ops::ControlFlow<()>, ServerReadError>` | `Scoreboard.getPlayerTeams`: visits every team in the resident board's order. Vanilla leaves that order unspecified. Return `ControlFlow::Break(())` to stop; the result is `Ok(Break(()))` on a stop or `Ok(Continue(()))` on completion, including an empty board. An unavailable board does not call the visitor. |
| `visit_tick_times` | `fn visit_tick_times (&self, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (u64) -> core::ops::ControlFlow<()> ,) -> Result<core::ops::ControlFlow<()>, ServerReadError>` | The last 100 server ticks' durations in nanoseconds, as `MinecraftServer.getTickTimesNanos` lays them out: the tick numbered `n` at index `n % 100`, 0 where none was recorded yet. The visit is in slot order; return `Break(())` to stop, or collect the values to sort them for percentiles. `tick_stats` has the average. A host without a server clock returns `Unavailable`. |
| `world_dir` | `fn world_dir (&self) -> Option<PathBuf>` | The open world's folder (`world/`, beside its `level.dat`): a server mod keeps its data in a folder of its own there, so the world's backups carry it. None when no world is open. |

### Client (`ClientHost`)

| Method | Signature | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `add_particle` | `fn add_particle (&mut self, options: &ParticleOptions, at: [f64; 3], velocity: [f64; 3]) -> bool` | ClientLevel.addParticle: submits one local particle through the ordinary provider, camera-distance, setting and population limits. False without a loaded client world, for non-finite input or for an item crumb whose colour has a component outside 0 to 1. True means submitted, not necessarily visible. For a registered `ParticleType`, pass `ty.options(value)`; the matching client factory receives the value and original auxiliary inputs before the particle is queued. |
| `add_toast` | `fn add_toast (&self, toast: ModToast) -> Result<(), AddToastError>` | Submit a notification to the window's toast manager. The next toast update offers it to `toast_add` as `ToastKind::Mod` before inserting or replacing anything. An accepted matching kind restarts the display time in place; a veto leaves any existing toast untouched. Calls from `toast_add` itself wait for the following update. Refuses the whole request if its text or item icon cannot be represented. |
| `apply_screen_blur` | `fn apply_screen_blur (&self) -> bool` | Requests vanilla's menu background blur for this frame, with a per-frame guard so blur is applied at most once per frame. Returns true if blur was newly requested this frame, or false if it was already applied/requested this frame. API @API:screen_transparent_background@. |
| `audio_device` | `fn audio_device (&self) -> String` | The output device the options ask for (`Options.soundDevice`). |
| `audio_devices` | `fn audio_devices (&self) -> Vec<String>` | The output devices sound can play on (`SoundEngine.getAvailableSoundDevices`). |
| `audio_options` | `fn audio_options (&self) -> ClientAudioOptions` | A snapshot of the current audio preferences. |
| `bake_layer` | `fn bake_layer (&self, layer: &str) -> Option<ModelPart>` | Entity model layer `layer` baked into its `ModelPart` tree, each part at its pose, as `EntityModelSet.bakeLayer` bakes it: one a native mod registered (`register_layer_definitions`), or a prepared vanilla definition by its `entity-models.json` name (`minecraft:zombie#main`, `minecraft:player#main`). Layers are indexed once per client resource load and reload rather than read from disk on each call. The tree is the caller's own copy: bake it once, as a renderer does, and reuse it while setting its parts' poses each frame. None for a layer neither has. API @API:model_layers@. |
| `biome_tint` | `fn biome_tint (&self, pos: [i32; 3], resolver: ColorResolver) -> Result<u32, LevelReadError>` | The resolver's blended colour at `pos` in the current client level, as opaque ARGB. Like `ClientLevel.getBlockTint` (`BiomeColors.getAverageGrassColor` and its counterparts), this samples a square at the block's height using the client's biome blend radius and the same biome colours as the mesher, including updates from `Self::refresh_biome_colors`. Use `ColorResolver::Grass` or `ColorResolver::Foliage` without looking up a block state or a model. For a specific block or fluid face, use `Self::block_tint_color`. Returns `LevelReadError::Unavailable` outside the World phase or when the host has no client level to read. |
| `block_sprites` | `fn block_sprites (&self, blocks: &[BlockState]) -> Vec<Option<BlockSprites>>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. For a client mod: what each resolved block state draws on its six sides, preserving its properties without reparsing names. Resolve states once at load. Each face's sprite (array and layer, as `WorldDrawn::block_sprites` cuts them) and its texture by the name `cube` and `quad` take, where it lies on the side, how its texture runs across it (`FaceSprite::quad_on` draws it on another block's box, turned as its model turns it), its tint source and index (`block_tint_color`) and whether it is cut out, translucent or animated; its particle sprite; whether it is a plain cube. None for a block the game does not know. The model is the state's first random variant. API 380. |
| `block_sprites_version` | `fn block_sprites_version (&self) -> u64` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. For a client mod: changes each time the resources load (a resource pack turned on or off can move every sprite): ask `native_block_sprites` again then. API 380. |
| `block_tint_color` | `fn block_tint_color (&self, state: BlockState, tint_index: i32, pos: [i32; 3]) -> Option<u32>` | For a client mod drawing a block's faces itself (a copycat drawing its material's, from `block_sprites`): the 0xRRGGBB colour a face of `state` with tint index `tint_index` (`FaceSprite::tint_index`) is multiplied by at `pos`, as the chunk mesher tints it there (biome blend included). Grass, foliage and water use the client's current biome colours, including updates from `Self::refresh_biome_colors`. A face's own tint source or a pack colormap can give it a different colour. Returns `None` for an untinted face (-1, or an index past the state's tints). For a biome colour independent of the face's tint source, use `Self::biome_tint`. |
| `build_play_packet` | `fn build_play_packet (&self, request: &PlayPacketRequest ,) -> Result<ServerboundPlayPacket, PacketBuildError>` | Encodes a typed serverbound play packet with the client's vanilla protocol encoder. Available in client host contexts including setup, without reading a connection or changing local state. Validates vanilla wire field bounds and applies no gameplay or local effects; submit the result with `send_packet`. |
| `camera` | `fn camera (&self) -> Option<Camera>` | The most recently extracted world frame's camera, if one exists. During GUI rendering this is the current frame, including bob/spin and FOV. |
| `camera_entity` | `fn camera_entity (&self) -> Option<EntityRef>` | The entity the view follows (`Minecraft.getCameraEntity`): the entity a server attached the camera to, else the local player. None outside a client world. |
| `camera_type` | `fn camera_type (&self) -> CameraType` | The window's camera type (`Options.getCameraType`), including its latest pending request. |
| `chat_history_limit` | `fn chat_history_limit (&self) -> u32` | How many messages, wrapped lines and sent lines (`recentChat`) the chat keeps (`ChatComponent.MAX_CHAT_HISTORY`): vanilla's 100 unless a mod set another cap. |
| `chat_line` | `fn chat_line (&mut self, line: Text) -> Result<(), TextFormError>` | Adds `line` to this client's chat HUD and history (vanilla's `ChatComponent.addClientSystemMessage`): it shows here only, so nothing is sent to the server. The Chat option's server-message filter doesn't hide it (`GuiMessageSource.SYSTEM_CLIENT`), and `client_chat_received` is not told: vanilla fires that event from `ChatListener` for lines the server sent, not for lines the client adds itself. Returns an error if the text exceeds the boundary's size or nesting limits; rejected text is not added. |
| `chat_options` | `fn chat_options (&self) -> ClientChatOptions` | A snapshot of the current chat preferences. |
| `clear_particles` | `fn clear_particles (&self) -> bool` | `ParticleEngine.clearParticles`: every particle and emitter goes before the next frame is drawn or the next particle tick runs, whichever comes first. It takes `&self`, so a screen or render hook can ask, as with `native_play_local_sound`. False on a server. |
| `client_executor` | `fn client_executor (&self) -> Option<ClientExecutor>` | The calling mod's handle for submitting work to the client thread from any thread. Obtain it in `client_setup` and clone it into workers that cannot borrow a host. Returns `None` when the client loop is unavailable or closing, on restricted hosts such as the client-side `entity_entering_section` callback, or after a task from the calling mod panicked in this client session. |
| `client_scoreboard` | `fn client_scoreboard (&self) -> Result<Option<&dyn ClientScoreboard>, ClientSessionReadError>` | Borrows the live board sent to this client (`ClientLevel.getScoreboard`). `Ok(None)` means no logged-in client world; a logged-in world with an empty board still returns a view. An unavailable read returns `ClientSessionReadError::Unavailable`. Only an explicit text or format copy can fail to represent one of the board's fields. |
| `client_session` | `fn client_session (&self) -> &dyn ClientSession` | The client_session service. |
| `clipboard_text` | `fn clipboard_text (&self) -> Option<String>` | The clipboard's text (`KeyboardHandler.getClipboard`), empty when it holds none, while the game handles a key, mouse or typed-text event: a mod's key or screen hook pasting into its own field. None at any other moment (a tick, a frame, a packet), so no mod reads the clipboard without the player doing something, and None on a server. |
| `clock` | `fn clock (&self) -> Option<Clock>` | The clock of the world this window shows, for client mods: its game time and time of day as the server last sent them (`ClientboundSetTimePacket`), advanced each tick between packets; none on the title screen. |
| `close_screen` | `fn close_screen (&mut self) -> bool` | Closes the open screen as its Escape key does: a container screen closes as the player's container close (`ServerboundContainerClosePacket` with the menu's id, then the client menu closed), a pause menu closes to the game, and another screen runs its own close. The sign editor sends its sign update (`ServerboundSignUpdatePacket`), as Escape does. A book closes with nothing sent; a lectern's book closes its menu (`ServerboundContainerClosePacket`). A confirmation, a server's pack prompt or a dialog open over another screen is the one that closes. Declining a required pack disconnects rather than returning to the screen underneath. The screen hooks fire as for Escape. The death screen, the code of conduct and a dialog Escape cannot close do not close this way. False when no screen is open (or, outside a world, when it cannot act). |
| `cloud_status` | `fn cloud_status (&self) -> CloudStatus` | How clouds are drawn now: the session's choice (`set_session_clouds`), else the option. |
| `container_click` | `fn container_click (&self, request: &ContainerClickRequest ,) -> Result<ServerboundPlayPacket, PacketBuildError>` | Builds a vanilla container click; it needs the host because the packet carries hashed stacks. Uses this connection's registries and component codecs, refusing components that cannot be encoded or hashed, unrepresentable IDs/slots and more than 128 changed slots. Applies no menu checks or local effects; submit the result with `send_packet`. |
| `create_dynamic_texture` | `fn create_dynamic_texture (&mut self, id: &Identifier, width: u32, height: u32, rgba: &[u8] ,) -> Result<(), DynamicTextureError>` | Vanilla's `TextureManager.register` of a `DynamicTexture` under `id`, then its `upload`: the calling mod's RGBA texture `id`, in its own namespace (`mymod:dynamic/panel`), made from `rgba`, `width * height` pixels of four bytes, row by row from the top left. The texture is a GPU texture of its own, as `DynamicTexture` makes one: `RGBA8_UNORM`, one mip level, sampled nearest with repeat, with no choice of format or sampler. Draw it with `GuiCanvas::texture` under `id` (`region.tex` its size), from the next frame on; any mod may draw the id, only the owner changes it. It survives resource reloads (F3+T, a pack change) and a world closing, and lasts until `release_dynamic_texture` or the mod's unload; if the game loses its graphics device the texture is gone and the next update reads `DynamicTextureError::Unknown`, so create it again. |
| `create_fireworks` | `fn create_fireworks (&mut self, request: &FireworksRequest) -> bool` | `ClientLevel.createFireworks`: draws a firework's explosions in this client's world as a bursting rocket draws them (`FireworkParticles.Starter`): each explosion's shape, colours, fade colours, trail and twinkle, one every other tick. The blast, and the crackle after a twinkling explosion, play only when `request.audible`, as for a rocket that isn't silent. Only the picture and the sound: no entity, no damage, nothing sent to the server, and other players see nothing (a mod's server half tells each client half to draw it). True means queued; false on a server, without a client player or world, or for non-finite input. API @API:client_fireworks@. |
| `create_mesh` | `fn create_mesh (&self, desc: &MeshDescriptor<'_>) -> Result<MeshRef, MeshError>` | Allocate retained GPU vertex buffer(s) and an optional index buffer (`GpuBuffer`), returning an owner-checked generational `MeshRef` handle. Buffer sizes must be non-zero, stride/index/4-byte aligned, and at most `device.limits().max_buffer_size`. Handles are retired automatically on mod disable, world close, resource reload, or `MeshRef::release`. |
| `create_render_target` | `fn create_render_target (&self, desc: &RenderTargetDescriptor ,) -> Result<RenderTargetRef, RenderTargetError>` | Allocate a mod-owned offscreen GPU render target (`RenderTarget` / `TextureTarget`), returning an owner-checked generational `RenderTargetRef` handle. |
| `create_storage_buffer` | `fn create_storage_buffer (&self, desc: &StorageBufferDescriptor ,) -> Result<StorageBufferRef, BufferError>` | Allocate a retained GPU storage buffer (vanilla's `GpuBuffer`), returning an owner-checked generational `StorageBufferRef`. `desc.size` is at least 4, a multiple of 4 and at most `device.limits().max_buffer_size`; a binding takes a range of at most `max_storage_buffer_binding_size` bytes (`StorageBufferRef::slice`). The buffer survives a resource reload and a world close, and is freed by `StorageBufferRef::release` or when the mod unloads. Refused, never panicking, for a size the device cannot hold. |
| `create_uniform_buffer` | `fn create_uniform_buffer (&self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<UniformBufferRef, BufferError>` | Allocate a retained GPU std140 uniform buffer initialized with `bytes` (non-empty, 4-byte aligned, and at most `device.limits().max_uniform_buffer_binding_size`), returning an owner-checked generational `UniformBufferRef` handle. Retired automatically on mod disable, world close, resource reload, or `UniformBufferRef::release`. |
| `creative_search_items` | `fn creative_search_items (&mut self) -> Vec<ItemStack>` | The creative search tab's final display order, for ranking stacks in inventory sorters. This requests the same catalog as the creative screen (using the player's permission and Operator Items Tab option). On a miss it dispatches the creative build hook. During setup, reload, a different build, or without a live catalog, this returns an empty list. |
| `cross_passage` | `fn cross_passage (&mut self, passage: PassageId) -> Result<(), PassageCrossError>` | Moves the window's player through `passage` (#3775), one its server told it of, as Immersive Portals' client does: its feet, where it was a tick ago (so the frames between two ticks do not jump), its velocity and its look come out at the far side, turned with the passage, and the server is told the passage and where the feet were. Nothing else of the player changes: on ground, fall distance, sprinting, what it uses. The server checks the move to there as any move, then answers with a position packet that changes nothing (all relative and zero), so the player keeps its velocity; as any position packet does, it stops mining (a passage crossed while mining aborts the dig). A refused crossing puts the player back where the server has it (`Self::last_passage_refusal` says why). One crossing at a time: the next waits for the server's answer. Refused, with nothing moved or sent, when not connected to a server, with no player, while riding, for a passage the window does not hold, when the feet are more than 20 blocks from the opening, and when a mod's `client_packet_send` cancels the crossing's payload. |
| `crosshair_target` | `fn crosshair_target (&self) -> Option<CrosshairTarget>` | The committed `client_pick` result (Minecraft.hitResult), computed at partial tick 1.0 before each tick's inputs, including while paused, and at the frame's partial tick before extraction. Each input reads its tick's result; the highlight reads the frame's result. Reads during dispatch see original. None before the first pick, without a client world or on a miss. Consumption never recasts or rechecks target liveness; a block broken earlier in a tick remains that tick's use target. |
| `cursor` | `fn cursor (&self) -> [f32; 2]` | Where the mouse is, in GUI pixels. |
| `debug_overlay_visible` | `fn debug_overlay_visible (&self) -> bool` | Whether the client draws its debug overlay this frame. Always-on debug entries can make it visible with F3 off. F1 hides the overlay unless a screen is open. |
| `disconnect` | `fn disconnect (&mut self, reason: Option<Text>) -> Result<(), DisconnectError>` | Leaves the server or the single-player world (NeoForge's `IClientCommonPacketListenerExtension.disconnect(Component)`), at the end of this frame, not inside the calling hook. With a reason, the disconnect screen shows it (a single-player world is saved first); with none, it leaves as the pause menu's Disconnect does. The request belongs to the world it was made in: one made while that world closes is dropped, never carried into the next. A second call in the same frame replaces the reason. The retained reason renders in the active language at draw and narration time; literal text stays unchanged. `DisconnectError::Unavailable` when no server or world is joined. The reason is local to the client, not encoded into a packet. |
| `drop_held` | `fn drop_held (&mut self, hold: HoldId) -> Result<(), HoldError>` | including overlapping packets. It publishes nothing and keeps the other holds active. |
| `fluid_name` | `fn fluid_name (&self, fluid: &str) -> String` | Fluid `fluid`'s name as the lang names it (`fluid_type.<mod>.<name>`, vanilla's by its block), else its path in words. |
| `graphics_options` | `fn graphics_options (&self) -> ClientGraphicsOptions` | A snapshot of the current graphics preferences. |
| `gui_options` | `fn gui_options (&self) -> ClientGuiOptions` | A snapshot of the current GUI and accessibility preferences. Its `gui_scale` is only the option, where 0 means automatic; read `Self::gui_scale` for the scale the window applies. |
| `gui_scale` | `fn gui_scale (&self) -> Option<u32>` | The GUI scale in effect (`Window.getGuiScale`), at least 1; none on a server or when no window exists. Vanilla's `Window.calculateScale` resolves it from the GUI Scale option and the window size: the option is a maximum (0 = automatic, not a guarantee), and with the Unicode font forced an odd result is raised by one. This read returns the scale the window applies, never a separate recomputation. Divide `Self::window_size` by this scale, rounding up, for the GUI-scaled width and height (`Window.getGuiScaledWidth/Height`); draw callbacks read their GUI dimensions from `DrawContext::size`. |
| `gui_sprite` | `fn gui_sprite (&self, sprite: &Identifier) -> Option<AtlasRegion>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. For a client mod: where GUI sprite `sprite` lies in the GUI atlas and its size, none for one the atlas lacks or whose entry its definition removed (a server's metrics-only atlas lacks them all). The atlas names its sprites by GUI id, the id its definition lists them under: `minecraft:container/slot` (also given as `container/slot`), a mod's `mymod:panel`; a sprite a registered sprite source made in `gui.json` is found by the id it named; a client mod's dynamic texture is not a GUI sprite (draw it with `GuiCanvas::texture`). |
| `gui_status_heights` | `fn gui_status_heights (&self) -> Option<[i32; 2]>` | How far above the bottom edge the HUD's status rows reach, left and right in GUI pixels (NeoForge's `Hud.leftHeight` and `rightHeight`), as they stand when the current HUD layer starts: both are 39 (the hotbar and experience bar) before `minecraft:player_health`, and each vanilla row adds its height as it draws (health and armour on the left, food, a vehicle's hearts and air on the right). A layer placed `Below` a vanilla row sees the height that row draws at, and one placed `Above` it the height after it. A vanilla row that is hidden, canceled or replaced adds nothing. None outside the HUD layer pass. |
| `gui_ticks` | `fn gui_ticks (&self) -> i32` | The HUD tick counter (`Hud.getGuiTicks`): advances once per unpaused client tick, including on the title screen, independently of server tick freeze. Wraps as a Java int. |
| `has_channel` | `fn has_channel (&self, id: &PayloadId) -> bool` | Whether the connected server has channel `id` for the connection's current phase (NeoForge's `ICommonPacketListener.hasChannel`): the test `send_payload_to_server` makes before it queues anything, so true means that send won't return `UnsupportedPeer` (it may still refuse the bytes, for their size or under backpressure). False with no connection, for a channel declared for the other phase or direction, and for an optional channel the server did not negotiate. |
| `has_waypoints` | `fn has_waypoints (&self) -> bool` | Whether the server tracks any waypoint for this client (`ClientWaypointManager.hasWaypoints`), the waypoints the locator bar draws. The HUD's contextual bar reads the same fact. Which bar the HUD shows is not exposed: vanilla's `Hud.nextContextualInfoState` is private. |
| `hold_packets` | `fn hold_packets (&mut self) -> Result<HoldId, HoldError>` | physical play connection. It covers subsequent uncancelled engine play sends and mod packets committed while it is active. Holds can overlap. A pending packet retains its ordinary writer credit; pressure never releases a hold. Control replies (including keep-alives, chunk batch acknowledgements and cookies) bypass holds. Disconnect, protocol changes and aborted travel invalidate the id. |
| `hud_layer_bounds` | `fn hud_layer_bounds (&self, layer: &str) -> Result<Option<[f32; 4]>, LevelReadError>` | Bounding rectangle `[x, y, width, height]` in GUI pixels of a HUD layer's geometry after viewport and scissor clipping, before other layers can cover it. Use a vanilla id such as `"minecraft:effects"` or a registered mod layer id. Returns `Ok(None)` if the layer has not painted visible geometry, including before it draws, if it is canceled, or outside the HUD pass (including screen render hooks). Returns `Err(LevelReadError::Unavailable)` outside a client window. |
| `hud_mode` | `fn hud_mode (&self) -> HudMode` | Read effective HUD visibility independently of first-person hands. |
| `input_options` | `fn input_options (&self) -> ClientInputOptions` | A snapshot of the current input preferences. |
| `instance_groups` | `fn instance_groups (&self) -> &dyn InstanceGroups` | Persistent model groups. |
| `is_key_down` | `fn is_key_down (&self, key: &str) -> bool` | Whether a specific key is currently held down. `key` is the vanilla physical key or mouse button name (`key.keyboard.left.shift`, `key.mouse.left`, `key.mouse.4`, for example). Unknown names are not down. Modifier combinations use `ClientHost::modifiers`. |
| `is_mouse_grabbed` | `fn is_mouse_grabbed (&self) -> bool` | Whether the window holds the pointer (`MouseHandler.isMouseGrabbed`): true while the player looks around in a world, false with a screen open or the window released. |
| `is_paused` | `fn is_paused (&self) -> bool` | The applied client pause state, not whether a pause screen is open. |
| `item_particle_options` | `fn item_particle_options (&self, stack: &ItemStack) -> Option<ParticleOptions>` | Resolve vanilla's `ItemParticleOption` for this stack using the ground display context and seed 0, exactly like the game's eating particles. Includes every resolved model layer's particle sprite, respecting resource packs and stack component overrides. Returns `None` for an invalid stack or unavailable resources/provider. Available in Setup, Reloading and World. Resolve again after a resource reload; this read allocates the sprite list. |
| `item_uses_block_light` | `fn item_uses_block_light (&self, stack: &ItemStack, context: ItemDisplayContext) -> bool` | `ItemStackRenderState.usesBlockLight` of `stack`'s model resolved for `context` (`ItemModelResolver.updateForTopItem`, no owner or level): whether the model is lit as a block is, from the side (`gui_light: side`, vanilla's 3D item models), where a flat item is lit from the front. It is what the 1.21.3 `BakedModel.isGui3d` became: a mod uses it to draw a stack as a flat sprite or as a 3D model. False for an empty or invalid stack, and for a model that draws nothing. Reads the model the client resolved from its resource packs. |
| `jump_riding_scale` | `fn jump_riding_scale (&self) -> f32` | How far the local player has charged its mount's jump (`LocalPlayer.getJumpRidingScale`), 0.0 to 1.0: the value the HUD's jump bar draws and `Hud.willPrioritizeJumpInfo` tests (`> 0`). It rises while the jump key is held on a `Self::jumpable_vehicle` and drops back to 0.0 when the jump is released and its cooldown runs out. 0.0 once a client tick has run with the player controlling no mount that can jump (that tick resets it), and outside a client world. Read-only: the jump key drives it, and the server checks the jump it is sent. |
| `jumpable_vehicle` | `fn jumpable_vehicle (&self) -> Option<EntityRef>` | The vehicle the local player controls that can jump now (`LocalPlayer.jumpableVehicle`): the player is its controlling passenger (`getControlledVehicle`: the first one, on its saddle) and it can jump (`PlayerRideableJumping.canJump`): a saddled horse, donkey, mule, nautilus or camel, the camel neither sitting nor getting up or down; a pig or a strider never jumps. The HUD's jump bar makes the same decision. None when the player controls no such vehicle (a second passenger included), or outside a client world. |
| `language_right_to_left` | `fn language_right_to_left (&self) -> bool` | `Language.isDefaultRightToLeft`, the flag `Font.isBidirectional` reads: whether the active language reads right to left (its `bidirectional` in a pack's `language` section), false for the default language. Lay out a row of GUI or tooltip pieces in reading order with it. Available outside drawing and outside a logged-in world, including client setup, as the other text reads are. |
| `last_passage_refusal` | `fn last_passage_refusal (&self) -> Option<(PassageId, PassageCrossRefusal)>` | The server's refusal of the window's last crossing (`Self::cross_passage`) and the passage it named, until the next crossing; None when it was not refused or no answer came yet. |
| `local_item_use` | `fn local_item_use (&self) -> Option<ItemUse>` | The local player's ordinary item use as the client sees it: the hand, ticks used and ticks left (`isUsingItem`, `getUsedItemHand`, `getTicksUsingItem`, `getUseItemRemainingTicks`). None when the player uses no item or no client world is open. Does not report input intercepted by `crate::ClientItemUseBehavior`. Sends nothing. |
| `local_player` | `fn local_player (&self) -> Option<PlayerRef>` | The local player in a logged-in client world. |
| `menu_blur_radius` | `fn menu_blur_radius (&self) -> f32` | The user's configured menu background blur radius (`menuBackgroundBlurriness` in options.txt, usually 0.0 to 10.0; default 5.0). 0.0 means blur is turned off. API @API:screen_transparent_background@. |
| `microphone_devices` | `fn microphone_devices (&self) -> Result<Vec<MicDevice>, MicRefusal>` | Lists the client's available audio input devices, each with a distinct opaque selector (`MicDevice::id`) and a human-readable display label (`MicDevice::label`). Returns an empty list when no input devices exist or client audio capture is disabled. An individual inaccessible endpoint does not hide other available inputs. On macOS, listing does not request microphone permission. |
| `millis` | `fn millis (&self) -> u64` | Milliseconds on the game's own clock (`Util.getMillis`), counting from the game's start: what a text field's cursor blinks by and what tells a double click from two clicks. Only differences between two readings mean anything. |
| `mining_destroy_delay` | `fn mining_destroy_delay (&self) -> u32` | The ticks left before the local player's next block starts breaking (`MultiPlayerGameMode.destroyDelay`): 5 after a block breaks or creative's instant break starts, then one less for each tick the attack key keeps continuing the destroy (`continueDestroyBlock`), until 0 lets the next block start. While it is above 0 a held attack starts no break. Read-only: vanilla has no public setter. 0 outside a client world. |
| `mod_key_mapping` | `fn mod_key_mapping (&self, owner: &str, id: &str) -> Result<ModKeyMappingRef, ModKeyRefusal>` | Resolve `owner`'s registered mapping `id` once after registration, usually in `client_setup`. Keep the handle for subsequent calls; save the owner and id, not the handle. Any mod can resolve, read, rebind or reset any registered mapping. Returns `ModKeyRefusal::NotRegistered` for an unknown owner or id and `ModKeyRefusal::Unavailable` when there is no client backend in this host. |
| `mod_screen` | `fn mod_screen (&self) -> Option<&str>` | The mod whose own screen is currently open, if one is (Escape, or another screen replacing it, closes it). This is a live query for the screen open right now; inside `screen_closing` or `screen_*_post` hooks, read `event.screen.mod_screen` to identify the screen that received the event even after a synchronous replacement or close. |
| `mod_screen_close` | `fn mod_screen_close (&mut self, mod_id: &str)` | Closes mod `mod_id`'s own screen, if it is open: to the Mods screen when its `ConfigScreenFactory` opened it, otherwise as Escape closes a screen (back to the game, or to the title screen with no world). |
| `mod_screen_open` | `fn mod_screen_open (&mut self, mod_id: &str) -> bool` | Opens a screen of mod `mod_id`'s own through the normal screen-transition path (`Gui.setScreen`): `screen_opening` and `screen_closing` fire for the transition and `screen_init_post` runs once it opens, so the mod mounts widgets in `screen_init_post`, draws in `screen_render_post`, and takes its clicks, scroll, keys, and typing there; the mouse is freed, and Escape closes it. Every mod's own screen has the `ScreenView.id` `mod` and sets `ScreenView.mod_screen` to its owning mod id, so a mod's screen hooks check `event.screen.mod_screen == Some("my_mod")` (or live `mod_screen()` to query the currently open screen). It doesn't pause a single-player game (as the inventory doesn't). Can replace any open vanilla screen, title screen, overlay (book, sign editor, dialog, or prompt), or another mod's screen, running the replaced screen's close cleanup once hooks allow the transition. Replacing a server-pack prompt leaves the server's request unanswered, as in vanilla `Gui.setScreen`. Returns `true` when `mod_id`'s screen is open, and `false` when cancelled or redirected by `screen_opening`, while a world or connection is loading, or without a client window (a headless/dedicated server, the server thread, or guest-acting contexts). A gamepad can close it (B, Start) and activate focused mounted controls. That activation does not dispatch screen mouse hooks. |
| `modifiers` | `fn modifiers (&self) -> ClientModifiers` | The current window modifier keys in the same GLFW encoding as input hooks. Cleared when the window loses focus; available even outside a world. |
| `move_local_player_by` | `fn move_local_player_by (&mut self, delta: [f64; 3] ,) -> Result<Option<[f64; 3]>, LevelWriteError>` | Moves the local player by `delta` in blocks, as `Entity.move(MoverType.SELF, delta)`. The move uses the player's usual collisions and step height, and updates its ground and fall state, including the usual effects of collisions and blocks on velocity. Available to client hooks with world access. For per-tick movement call from the local player's client-side `player_tick_pre`, before its own movement. This sends no packet and fires no `entity_move` hook (that hook is server-only). A section change is reported through `entity_entering_section` at the position write, before the move updates collision flags, ground, fall distance and velocity. The normal position report carries the movement and ground flag, and the server checks the report as usual. Returns the displacement actually made, or `Ok(None)` without moving if there is no local player, the player is dead, sleeping or riding, or a component is non-finite or outside ±64 blocks. `Err(LevelWriteError::Unavailable)` means the call is outside a world phase or the engine couldn't finish the move. |
| `muffle_sounds` | `fn muffle_sounds (&mut self, amount: f32, lowpass: f32, ticks: u32, exempt: &[& str]) -> bool` | Muffles the game's sounds for a while, as ears ringing after a close blast hear them: `amount` (0 to 1) of every sound's volume is taken away and `lowpass` (0 to 1) dulls it, from untouched to a 300 Hz low-pass. Both start in full and fade back in a straight line over `ticks` client ticks, held while the game is paused. The sound events in `exempt` (`mymod:ear.ringing`) play clearly through it, those already playing too, so the mod's own ringing stays sharp. Everything else is muffled, music and the interface's clicks included. A new call replaces the muffle in place; `ticks` 0 ends it at once. Nothing changes while no mod calls it. False, with nothing changed, on a server, for an amount or strength outside 0 to 1, or for an exempt event that isn't `namespace:path`. API @API:muffle_sounds@. |
| `narrate` | `fn narrate (&mut self, text: &Text, when: Narration) -> Result<(), NarrateError>` | Has the narrator say `text` as a system message, through the same `GameNarrator` the game's own narration uses: `Narration::Now` is `saySystemNow` (cuts off what is being said), `Narration::Queued` is `saySystemQueued`. The player's Narrator option, the voice volume and the narrator's log of recent lines apply as to vanilla's lines. The text is the component's plain string (`Component.getString`) in the current language, bounded only by the component's own form limits. Nothing reaches the server. Refused with nothing said, by name, as `NarrateError` lists: an empty line, the option Off or Chat only, or no speech. Available from client setup onward. |
| `observed_screen_id` | `fn observed_screen_id (&self) -> Option<ScreenId>` | The same screen identifier provided to screen hooks. |
| `open_audio_stream` | `fn open_audio_stream (&self, _spec: &StreamSpec, _source: Box<dyn AudioStream> ,) -> Result<AudioStreamRef, AudioRefusal>` | Gives the mixer a native source of interleaved signed 16-bit PCM, like NeoForge's `SoundInstance.getStream`. The mixer takes ownership and calls `AudioStream::read` serially on the output's shared worker as it needs frames, with no `Host` or world borrow. The device callback requests PCM but never calls or waits for the source. Returning `0` signals EOF so already-queued frames drain before playback stops; return `n > 0` without writing to the zeroed buffer for temporary silence. An async or network producer bridges through its own nonblocking buffer inside `AudioStream::read`. |
| `open_chat` | `fn open_chat (&mut self, text: &str) -> Result<(), String>` | Opens the chat screen with `text` typed in for the player to finish and send; nothing is sent. It can replace an open screen, including another chat screen or foreground prompt. A new chat instance fires normal opening, closing and initialization hooks; a veto leaves the old screen untouched. Returns an error when there is no local player. |
| `open_inventory` | `fn open_inventory (&mut self) -> bool` | Opens the player's inventory as the inventory key does: locally, with no packet (the player menu is always open on the server). Riding a vehicle with its own inventory sends the open-inventory player command (`ServerboundPlayerCommandPacket`), as the key does. False with a screen already open, for a spectator, in a portal transition, or with no world. |
| `open_microphone` | `fn open_microphone (&self, _spec: &MicSpec) -> Result<MicRef, MicRefusal>` | Opens live PCM capture from the system default or selected input device directly on the client. Returns a client-local `MicRef` readable from any thread off the OS audio callback: `MicRef::read` writes only validated whole interleaved signed 16-bit PCM frames from a bounded `MIC_RING_MAX_MS` ms ring that drops the oldest frames on overflow, leaving any unreturned suffix untouched and returning `0` immediately on concurrent reader contention. Calling `MicRef::close` or dropping the handle stops capture and waits for the stream's teardown thread to release the OS input device before returning; a backend device error also releases the OS stream promptly without waiting for `close`, drop, or a tick. The engine never records or transmits captured audio. On macOS, opening a matching input can wait for the operating system's first microphone permission decision, before probing the input configuration. A label matching only an output does not request microphone permission. |
| `panel_items` | `fn panel_items (&mut self, mod_id: &str, items: Vec<Stack>)` | Shows the panel beside container screens with these items (at most 8192), as the game's own slots in pages, with a search box; the player's clicks, searches and keys over items, and items dragged off the panel onto the screen (`PanelEvent::Dropped`), come back as `panel_event`. The panel is the last mod's to show one; empty items and no recipe page take yours away. |
| `panel_recipes` | `fn panel_recipes (&mut self, mod_id: &str, page: Option<RecipePage>)` | Shows `page` in mod `mod_id`'s item panel instead of its items, drawn by the game with its own slots (`RecipePage`, `RecipeView`): a title and a back button, tabs, and the recipes paged to the panel's height. `None`, or a page without recipes, shows the items again. The renderer's limits are cut, never refused: 256 recipes and 16 tabs a page, 27 slots, 4 results and 8 fluids each way a recipe, 64 stacks a slot. The same title and tab again keep the page the player turned to. Clicks on its slots, actions, page turns, the back button and tabs come back as `panel_event`. |
| `panel_search` | `fn panel_search (&mut self, mod_id: &str, text: &str)` | Sets the text in mod `mod_id`'s item panel search box (`panel_items`), called by the panel's owner itself: `mod_id` must be the mod whose hook or part is making the call and whose panel is showing; any other call changes nothing, so one mod can't rewrite another's search (another mod asks the owner, through the owner's published integration). The text, cut to the box's 50 characters counted in UTF-16 code units as vanilla's box counts them, goes into the box the next time the owner's panel draws its box, never during this call: the box's value is replaced, the cursor goes to its end, the panel goes back to its first page, and the owner hears `panel_event` `Search(text)`, the event the player's typing sends. No keystrokes are simulated. A later call before that replaces an earlier one; the player editing the box first, or another mod taking the panel, drops it; text the box already holds changes nothing, its cursor and selection included. Set while no container screen is open, it waits for the panel to appear. A viewer `jei` clearing its own box calls `host.panel_search("jei", "")` from one of its hooks. JEI's `IIngredientFilter.setFilterText`. |
| `panel_style` | `fn panel_style (&mut self, mod_id: &str, style: PanelStyle)` | How mod `mod_id`'s item panel (`panel_items`) looks from now on: `PanelStyle::RecipeBook` (the default: the recipe book's page arrows and a plain search box), `PanelStyle::Viewer` (square page buttons on a bar over the items and the viewer's search box, as Sunrise's Recipes panel draws them) or `PanelStyle::Classic` (JEI's list on Minecraft 1.12: flush right under page buttons that always show and wrap around, the search box at the foot of the screen with a settings button that opens the mod's page in Options > Mods). The viewer's look changes only the drawing; the classic one also lays the panel out. Kept for the session, whether or not the panel is showing. |
| `particle_count` | `fn particle_count (&self) -> u32` | The particles alive in the client's level (`ParticleEngine.countParticles`): 0 once a clear is asked, and on a server. |
| `place_recipe` | `fn place_recipe (&mut self, book_id: i32, all: bool) -> Result<(), String>` | Places a recipe in the open screen's grid as a click in the recipe book does (`ServerboundPlaceRecipePacket`): the inventory's grid or a crafting table's. `all` places as many as the items allow, as with Shift. Refused while no such screen is open and for a recipe the player's book doesn't hold; one missing items shows as a ghost. |
| `play_local_sound` | `fn play_local_sound (&self, sound: &SoundPlay, after: u32) -> Option<SoundRef>` | Plays `sound` for this player only (`SoundPlay`'s event: `minecraft:ui.button.click`, or a mod's own from its `assets/<id>/sounds.json`): nothing is sent to the server, and other players don't hear it. It takes `&self`, so a screen, key or mouse hook can play one: the sound is queued and starts at the next audio update (once a client tick), `after` unpaused sound-engine ticks from then (`SoundEngine.playDelayed`). `SoundPlay::ui` starts at the listener (`SimpleSoundInstance.forUI`); `SoundPlay::at` starts at a position in the client's level with distance attenuation. The sound engine computes final volume and pitch; a volume above 1 can carry farther. |
| `playing` | `fn playing (&self) -> bool` | Playing, with nothing over the world: in-game keys and camera effects apply. |
| `pointer_grabbed` | `fn pointer_grabbed (&self) -> bool` | Whether the calling mod's own open screen holds the pointer grab (`set_pointer_grab`). `false` once the grab has ended by itself, and `false` for a mod whose screen is not the open one. |
| `potions` | `fn potions (&self) -> Vec<PotionInfo>` | Registered potions (`Registries.POTION`), in registry order, each by its registry id: vanilla's 46, from `minecraft:water` to `minecraft:infested` (content mods cannot declare potions yet). The game builds the list once; each call copies it. A mod builds the potion, splash, lingering or tipped-arrow stack itself with the `minecraft:potion_contents` component. |
| `prepare_item` | `fn prepare_item (&self, stack: &ItemStack, display: ItemDisplayContext, seed: i32 ,) -> Result<PreparedItemRef, ModelError>` | Resolve `stack`'s item model once for `display` and `seed` (`ItemModelResolver.updateForTopItem`, no owner), returning a retained `PreparedItemRef` for `BlockEntityDrawContext::prepared_item`. Returns `ModelError::UnknownModel` for an empty stack, an unknown/invalid stack, or a model that draws nothing. The handle goes stale on a successful resource reload, world close, owner mod disable, or `PreparedItemRef::destroy`. |
| `preset_editor_dimensions` | `fn preset_editor_dimensions (&self) -> Option<Compound>` | A copy of the open `world_preset_editor` screen's working dimension stems. Stays available to screen hooks while `world_preset_editor` is open alongside the bound `PresetEditor`. |
| `project` | `fn project (&self, at: [f64; 3]) -> Option<[f32; 3]>` | World point `at` projected on this frame's screen (`GameRenderer.projectPointToScreen`): normalised device x and y, -1 to 1 across the screen (x to the right, y up), and depth z (reversed: nearer is larger). The view is the camera's rotation without bob or spin, as vanilla's. Unlike vanilla, None for a point behind the camera or a non-finite `at`; also None outside a world, before extraction, and between render frames. Supported during GUI, screen and world drawing after camera extraction. In GUI pixels of a `DrawContext` of size `[w, h]`: `((x + 1) / 2 * w, (1 - y) / 2 * h)`. |
| `push_player` | `fn push_player (&mut self, kick: [f32; 3])` | For a client mod: a push added to the local player's velocity, in blocks a tick, as an explosion's knockback is (the window's player moves itself, as vanilla's client does). Each component is clamped to -4..4; a spectator, or a value that is not a number, is left alone. |
| `raycast` | `fn raycast (&self, distance: f32) -> Option<Hit>` | The first block along the view within `distance`, in loaded chunks only; `None` on a miss or a nonpositive or non-finite `distance`. |
| `recipe_book_ids` | `fn recipe_book_ids (&mut self, recipe_id: &str) -> Arc<[i32]>` | The `book_id`s of the recipe `recipe_id` (`minecraft:stick`), the ids `transfer_recipe`, `place_recipe` and `known_recipes` use: one for each way the recipe book shows it, in the book's order. For a recipe viewer, which has the recipe from `recipes_of_type`. A server numbers its recipes itself and names none of them, so an id is given only when the server sent the recipe book entry with that number and this client's own numbering of the recipe agrees with it: the entry is the same recipe (the same result, category and ingredients), so two different recipes with identical displays cannot be told apart. Empty otherwise: a recipe the server sent no entry for (the player's book does not hold it, or it was removed), one the server numbers differently, one the book doesn't show (campfire, stonecutting, smithing, a mod's own type), an unknown id, and outside a world. The answer changes when the server adds, replaces or removes entries and when recipes reload or arrive, and no hook announces a recipe book change: query it when you use it, rather than keeping an answer from `recipes_received`. |
| `refresh_biome_colors` | `fn refresh_biome_colors (&mut self)` | Asks `biome_colors` again for every biome and, when the colours differ from those in use, rebuilds every loaded section and the far terrain with them (`LevelRenderer.allChanged`, as changing Biome Blend does). Call it when your colours change (a new season, a new day of a slow fade) and once when the player joins, not every frame: a rebuild takes the workers a few seconds. |
| `refresh_render_visibility` | `fn refresh_render_visibility (&mut self)` | Asks `block_render_visibility` again for every state and, when the answers differ from those in use, remeshes every loaded section with them. While a mod listens to `section_visibility`, rebuilds meshes in the player's current level and asks about them again, except for a first all-air compile with no section geometry contributors. Other viewed levels do not call that hook; no NeoForge counterpart has been verified. Call it when a decision changes (a toggle key, a scan finishing), not every frame. With no part answering or listener, the mesher keeps its no-mod path. |
| `release_dynamic_texture` | `fn release_dynamic_texture (&mut self, id: &Identifier) -> Result<(), DynamicTextureError>` | Vanilla's `TextureManager.release`: releases the calling mod's texture `id`. Its GPU texture is freed and the id can be created again. A mod's textures are also released when the mod unloads, and a draw of the id already submitted that frame shows vanilla's missing texture. Refused by name, with nothing changed: `Unavailable`, `NotOwned` for an id outside the mod's namespace and `Unknown` for one that names no texture. |
| `release_held` | `fn release_held (&mut self, hold: HoldId) -> Result<usize, HoldError>` | in original send order and returns its count, which can be zero. Other holds still block their packets. `Send` reports a published prefix on transport failure. |
| `render_assets` | `fn render_assets (&self) -> &dyn RenderAssets` | The render_assets service. |
| `render_capabilities` | `fn render_capabilities (&self) -> RenderCapabilities` | The optional device features render code can use, read from the adapter the game runs on (`RenderCapabilities`). |
| `request_client_quit` | `fn request_client_quit (&self) -> bool` | Ask the local client to quit, even from the title screen without a world open. The client uses the normal Quit Game path after the calling hook returns, disconnecting from any server and waiting for an integrated server to save and release its world lock before exiting. This is not a server operation. Returns `true` if the request was recorded, not if shutdown succeeded; returns `false` if there is no live client or a quit is pending or underway. |
| `right_click_delay` | `fn right_click_delay (&self) -> Option<u32>` | `Minecraft.rightClickDelay`: the client ticks before a held use key uses again. Each use (`startUseItem`) sets it to 4, the client tick counts it down by one, and a held use key uses again when it reads 0. None outside a client world. |
| `screen_exclusion` | `fn screen_exclusion (&self) -> Vec<[f32; 4]>` | Where the open screen draws outside its `rect`, and where mods draw over it: the recipe book's panel, the creative tab rows, the active effects beside the inventory, then whatever `screen_exclusion_areas` adds. Each is `[x, y, width, height]` in GUI pixels; an empty or non-finite one is dropped. A mod that lays out its own panels (a recipe viewer) keeps them clear of these. Client-side; empty with no screen up. |
| `screen_hovered_stack` | `fn screen_hovered_stack (&self) -> Option<ItemStack>` | The full stack in the slot under the pointer on the open container or creative screen (`AbstractContainerScreen.getHoveredSlot().getItem()`), including creative catalog display slots; `None` over no slot, an empty slot, or with no container or creative screen open. |
| `screen_pos` | `fn screen_pos (&self) -> Option<[i32; 3]>` | The position of the currently shown mod menu opening. Use `crate::MenuScreenOpen::pos` while constructing its screen instead. An explicit absent position from the server stays `None`. |
| `screen_slot` | `fn screen_slot (&self, menu: &str, slot: usize) -> Option<[f32; 2]>` | Where the shown mod menu screen for `menu` puts menu slot `slot` from the next frame (`Slot.x`, `Slot.y` in GUI pixels from the screen's top left, the item's top left corner): where `set_screen_slot` last put it, else where the screen declared it. `slot` ranges over `0..(container_len + player_slots)` as `set_screen_slot` documents. None for an inactive/hidden slot, a slot the menu does not have, another menu type's screen, or no shown mod screen. |
| `screen_title` | `fn screen_title (&self) -> Option<String>` | The open menu's title (the block's name, or the one it was given), as plain text. |
| `select_hotbar_slot` | `fn select_hotbar_slot (&mut self, slot: u8) -> Result<(), HotbarSlotError>` | For a client mod: chooses the local player's hotbar slot, `slot` (0 to 8), as `Inventory.setSelectedSlot` does for a number key. It sends nothing itself: the game sends the carried-item packet at the next tick's start at the latest, and before the packets of an attack, a use, an interact, a release, a drop and a continued block break (not before a block break start). Refused above 8 with `OutOfRange`, without a client world with `NoWorld`, and for a spectator with `Refused` (its number keys open the spectator menu), leaving the selection as it was. |
| `send_chat` | `fn send_chat (&mut self, message: &str) -> Result<(), String>` | Sends directly through `ClientPacketListener.sendChat` or `sendCommand` (a leading `/` selects the command path). Chat passes through `client_chat` before signing; commands run the local dispatcher before being sent to the server. This does not normalize input, check the client's Chat setting or add history. `Ok(())` means queued, handled locally or intentionally canceled; `Err` means the play listener was unavailable or local encoding or publication failed. The server still checks signatures, permissions and chat policy. API 404. |
| `send_packet` | `fn send_packet (&mut self, packet: ServerboundPlayPacket) -> Result<(), SendPacketError>` | Submits a vanilla play packet to any server, including unrelated plugin payloads. The body is validated once and outgoing credit is reserved before acceptance. At root commit the packet joins any active play holds without releasing its credit, otherwise it publishes. Rollback, disconnect or a protocol incarnation change cancels it. Registered and engine-reserved channels are refused: use their payload or service API. Unrelated plugin bodies retain vanilla's discard-codec limit. No effects are applied locally. Typed packet builders validate wire fields; sequence numbers and required client state remain the mod's responsibility. Chat and commands use `send_chat`, which owns signing. API 409. |
| `send_payload_to_server` | `fn send_payload_to_server (&mut self, id: &PayloadId, bytes: PayloadBytes ,) -> Result<PayloadAccepted, PayloadSendError>` | Queue exact application bytes to the connected server, using the frozen declaration for its current protocol. Optional absent channels return UnsupportedPeer. Frame refusal and Backpressure write no application bytes. The root transaction owns publication and cancellation. |
| `set_block_extra_models` | `fn set_block_extra_models (&mut self, pos: [i32; 3], models: &[ModelRef] ,) -> Result<(), BlockExtraModelsError>` | The standalone models (resolved with `RenderAssets::model`) drawn as part of the block at `pos` in this client's level, as NeoForge's `ModelData` lets a block's model choose parts at run time without a block state for each choice: a cable's per-side channel counts. The chunk mesher draws them after the block's own models, unturned from the block's lower north-west corner (with its offset), in the block's own light and tints. Each quad takes its render layer as a block's would (cut out, translucent), and a face with a cull direction is culled by the neighbour there as the block's own faces are. On air they draw nothing. |
| `set_blocks_dirty` | `fn set_blocks_dirty (&mut self, min: [i32; 3], max: [i32; 3])` | Marks for remeshing every loaded chunk section that holds a block of the box from `min - 1` to `max + 1` (block positions, both corners included: the box widened by one block on each axis), as `LevelExtractor.setBlocksDirty` does, so a box touching a section's edge also marks the section across it. Sections that are not loaded, or outside the level's height, are skipped. |
| `set_camera_entity` | `fn set_camera_entity (&mut self, entity: EntityRef) -> bool` | Points the view at `entity` (`Minecraft.setCameraEntity`), as a server's camera packet does; the local player's own ref puts it back. True when the view now follows it; false, with nothing changed, for a stale ref, an entity this window is not shown, or one the camera cannot follow (only the local player and mobs). While the view is off the player, the player sends no moves (`LocalPlayer.isControlledCamera`). When the camera entity is removed, the view returns to the local player. A mod can also release the view by calling this with the player's ref; this client-only selection does not notify the server. |
| `set_camera_type` | `fn set_camera_type (&self, camera_type: CameraType)` | Request the window's camera type without cycling, also from shared-host callbacks. The last request wins and `Self::camera_type` reports it immediately. Requests before or during `compute_camera_angles` apply before that frame's detached camera placement, FOV and spectator post effects. Requests during `camera_position` re-align an entity-bound camera with the already-computed angles, keeping only coordinates explicitly pinned by listeners. The selected custom camera is not re-aligned. Later requests apply at the next frame's camera extraction. No cycle hook is emitted. |
| `set_chat_history_limit` | `fn set_chat_history_limit (&mut self, limit: u32)` | Sets the chat history cap for this session, as chat-history mods raise `ChatComponent.MAX_CHAT_HISTORY`, clamped to 1 to 16384. A lower cap trims the oldest messages, wrapped lines and sent lines now; a higher one lets later ones gather up to it. The saved command history (`command_history.txt`, 50 commands) keeps its own cap. |
| `set_clipboard_text` | `fn set_clipboard_text (&self, text: &str) -> bool` | Puts `text` on the clipboard (`KeyboardHandler.setClipboard`) on the same terms as `native_clipboard_text` reads it. False, with nothing copied, at any other moment or on a server; empty text copies nothing, as vanilla's. |
| `set_collision_exclusions` | `fn set_collision_exclusions (&mut self, boxes: &[[f64; 6]] ,) -> Result<(), CollisionExclusionsError>` | For a client mod: the local player's collision exclusions, the client half of `EntityRef::set_collision_exclusions`, with the same boxes, rules and refusals (a client with no player of its own answers `Stale`). The client moves its player itself, so a mod sets the same boxes on both sides: the engine sends neither set to the other. |
| `set_debug_fps_text` | `fn set_debug_fps_text (&self, text: Option<&str>) -> bool` | Adds the calling mod's `text` to the end of F3's frame rate line (vanilla's `DebugEntryFps`, the line that reads `60 fps T: 120 ...`), after a space, where a mod shows a state of its own (Dynamic FPS's reduced frame rate) without an entry of its own that starts off. It stays until the mod sets another text, or `None` or an empty text, which takes it away; set it when the state changes, not every frame. Several mods' texts follow in mod load order, and a disabled mod's is left out. Legacy colour codes (§) work; line breaks become spaces. False, setting nothing, outside a mod's call. |
| `set_gui_status_heights` | `fn set_gui_status_heights (&self, heights: [i32; 2]) -> bool` | Sets the HUD status columns' heights, left and right in GUI pixels above the bottom edge, as assigning NeoForge's `Hud.leftHeight` and `rightHeight` does: the vanilla rows that draw after the current layer start from them, and add their own height on top. A layer placed `Below` `minecraft:vehicle_health` that raises the right column moves the mount's hearts and the air row up; a wrapper that sets them before `draw_previous` moves the row it wraps. Rows already drawn stay where they are, and both columns go back to 39 at the start of each HUD pass. `gui_status_heights` reads the new heights at once. False, setting nothing, outside the HUD layer pass. |
| `set_hud_mode` | `fn set_hud_mode (&mut self, mode: HudMode)` | Select HUD and hand visibility, including after vanilla F1 input handling. Other first-person hand restrictions and render cancellation still apply. This does not change the player's F1 preference for mandatory mod-error diagnostics. |
| `set_local_player_on_ground` | `fn set_local_player_on_ground (&mut self, on_ground: bool) -> bool` | For a client mod: sets the local player's on-ground flag. Write it from `player_tick_pre`: this tick's jump decision reads it, and the move that follows sets it again from the collision, so the report to the server carries the flag the move left. `false`, and nothing changes, with no player of its own. |
| `set_local_player_velocity` | `fn set_local_player_velocity (&mut self, velocity: [f64; 3]) -> bool` | For a client mod: sets the local player's velocity (`Entity.setDeltaMovement`), in blocks per tick. Write it from `player_tick_pre`: the tick's own move then integrates it, and the position report the move produces is checked by the server as any report is, so a velocity the server's speed test or collision check refuses is corrected there. The write replaces the velocity outright, as the client's handling of a motion packet does, and nothing is sent. `false`, and nothing changes, with no player of its own and for a value that is not finite. |
| `set_local_rotation` | `fn set_local_rotation (&mut self, yaw: f32, pitch: f32) -> Result<(), LocalRotationError>` | Turns the local player's view to `yaw` and `pitch` (degrees), as `setYRot` and `setXRot` store them: yaw as given (not wrapped; the server wraps it), pitch `% 360` then clamped to -90..90. The previous-frame angles move by the same amount, as `Entity.turn` does for the mouse, so the view doesn't jump for a frame. Sends no packet: the game's own player sync of this client tick sends the look in its one move packet, only while the camera is the player (the rotation-only packet when riding); a call after that step goes out the next tick. `NotFinite`, changing nothing, for a NaN or infinite value; `NoWorld` outside a client world. |
| `set_mouse_grabbed` | `fn set_mouse_grabbed (&mut self, grabbed: bool)` | Grabs or releases the pointer as `MouseHandler.grabMouse` and `releaseMouse` do. Grabbing does nothing when the window is inactive or already holds the pointer, with no player in a world, while connecting or loading, or on the death screen (where `setScreen(null)` does not return to the world); otherwise it closes the open screen (`setScreen(null)`, a container's menu with it) and takes the pointer, with the checks the game's own grab has: a screen a mod kept open, a prompt, a dialog and a LAN guest's action leave it released. Releasing does nothing when the pointer is not grabbed. A release in a world is undone by the player's next click in it, as vanilla's is (`MouseHandler.onButton`). Does nothing on a host without a client window. |
| `set_overlay_message` | `fn set_overlay_message (&mut self, line: Text, animate_color: bool) -> Result<(), TextFormError>` | Shows `line` over this window's hotbar (the action bar), as `Hud.setOverlayMessage` does: it replaces the current line, empty text included, and restarts the stock overlay timer and fade (`Hud.tick`, `Hud.extractOverlayMessage`). `animate_color` cycles the colour as a jukebox's "Now Playing" line does. One slot: a server's action-bar packet or a jukebox message replaces a mod's line, and a mod's line replaces theirs. Nothing joins chat or its history, no chat event fires, nothing is narrated and nothing is sent. Text the boundary refuses is an error and the previous line stays. |
| `set_pointer_grab` | `fn set_pointer_grab (&self, grab: bool) -> bool` | Takes (`grab` true) or gives back (`grab` false) the pointer for the calling mod's own open screen: the cursor is hidden and held, and the screen hears the device's relative motion only as `screen_pointer_motion`, unscaled (not as `screen_mouse_moved_pre`, and never as camera turns), while it stays a screen that draws, takes keys (Escape included) and has widgets. Vanilla's own screens never hold a grabbed pointer (`MouseHandler.grabMouse` closes the screen), so this is a Sunrise capability with no vanilla limit. A mod that wants click-to-capture grabs from its click hook and releases with `false`. |
| `set_preset_editor_dimensions` | `fn set_preset_editor_dimensions (&self, dimensions: Compound) -> bool` | Replace the open `world_preset_editor` screen's working dimension stems. Requires an overworld, valid dimension ids and type/generator declarations. Returns false outside `world_preset_editor` or for malformed data; Done commits, Cancel and Escape discard the copy. Stays available to screen hooks alongside `PresetEditorDraft::set_dimensions`. |
| `set_right_click_delay` | `fn set_right_click_delay (&mut self, ticks: u32) -> bool` | Sets `Minecraft.rightClickDelay` to `ticks`, as a mod that changes how fast a held use key repeats does after a use: 0 lets the held key use again on the next client tick's key step, a larger value waits longer. The next use sets it back to 4. It changes only this client's timing: the server checks every use packet as it always does. Unlike vanilla's `int`, it can't be negative. False, changing nothing, outside a client world. |
| `set_screen_slot` | `fn set_screen_slot (&self, menu: &str, slot: usize, pos: Option<[f32; 2]> ,) -> Result<(), ScreenSlotRefusal>` | Moves menu slot `slot` of the open screen of mod menu type `menu` to `pos`, or deactivates/hides it with None, as a Java screen that lays its slots out again sets `Slot.x`/`Slot.y` or toggles `Slot.isActive()` (Tinkers' station layouts). `slot` ranges over `0..(container_len + player_slots)`, in menu order (`AbstractContainerMenu.addStandardInventorySlots`): `0..container_len` indexes `MenuScreenLayout::slots` (including slots declared `None` at open, which start inactive and may be activated later with `Some([x, y])`), and the `player_slots` the menu declared (`MenuScreenOpen::player_slots`) follow: the main inventory's three rows, then the hotbar. A player slot starts at the screen's `inventory_x`/`inventory_y` grid. From the next frame the slot is a vanilla slot at that position: the hovered slot (the 18 by 18 box around it), its highlight, its contents, its empty-slot icon and the recipe book's ghost items; a hidden one is none of these, and is neither a drag target nor swapped by a number key. A position outside the image is allowed. The change lasts for this opening: closing the screen and opening it again (or a replacement, even one that reuses the same window id) starts every container and player slot from its declared place, shown. Any mod may read, move, activate or hide slots of the open screen for `menu`. This is client-only and the server never consults it: a locally hidden slot keeps its item and a shift-click from another slot may still fill it, which is the menu's own quick-move answer; nothing on the client filters that. It takes `&self`, so a screen callback may call it every frame; repeating a slot's current place changes nothing and costs no layout work. Each `ScreenSlotRefusal` leaves the layout unchanged. |
| `set_section_dirty` | `fn set_section_dirty (&mut self, section: [i32; 3], with_neighbors: bool) -> bool` | Marks the loaded chunk section `section` (section coordinates: block X, Y and Z shifted right by 4) for remeshing, as `LevelExtractor.setSectionDirty` does; with `with_neighbors` each loaded section of the 27 from one below to one above it on every axis, whether or not `section` itself is loaded (`ClientLevel.setSectionDirtyWithNeighbors`). Use it when your mod changes how blocks look (a tint or texture toggle, an overlay) and the meshes must follow, without unloading chunks. Returns whether `section` itself was marked: `false` when it is not loaded or is outside the level's height. |
| `set_session_clouds` | `fn set_session_clouds (&mut self, clouds: Option<CloudStatus>)` | Sets how clouds are drawn for this session only, without writing `options.txt` (`Options.cloudStatus`); `None` goes back to the player's saved option. The options screen keeps showing and saving the player's own value. The next frame draws with it. |
| `set_session_view_bobbing` | `fn set_session_view_bobbing (&mut self, view_bobbing: Option<bool>)` | Turns view bobbing on or off for this session only, without writing `options.txt` (`Options.bobView`); `None` goes back to the player's saved option. The options screen keeps showing and saving the player's own value; `graphics_options().view_bobbing` reads the one in effect. The next frame's camera bobs, or not, with it. |
| `set_session_vsync` | `fn set_session_vsync (&mut self, vsync: Option<bool>)` | Turns VSync on or off for this session only, without writing `options.txt`, and reconfigures the window's surface at once, as changing the option does (`Options.enableVsync`, `Minecraft.invalidateSurfaceConfiguration`). `None` goes back to the player's saved option. The options screen keeps showing and saving the player's own value; `graphics_options().vsync` reads the one in effect. |
| `set_setting` | `fn set_setting (&mut self, key: SettingKey, value: SettingValue ,) -> Result<bool, SettingAccessError>` | Persist and publish a setting, update Options > Mods, and synchronously notify its owner. Returns `Ok(true)` if the value changed (`Range` values compare by bits), or `Ok(false)` if it was already equal. |
| `set_sound_source_scale` | `fn set_sound_source_scale (&mut self, source: SoundSource, scale: f32)` | Scales `source`'s volume by `scale` (0 to 1, clamped), the sounds playing now included (music, a record), as the Options volume sliders update live channels (`SoundEngine.updateCategoryVolume`). It multiplies the player's slider, which keeps its own value and is never saved with the scale; the master's scale scales every source. 1 undoes it. One scale per source for all mods: the last call wins. A mod may lower the volume while the window is in the background, for example. |
| `set_sounds_paused` | `fn set_sounds_paused (&mut self, paused: bool)` | Pauses every playing sound, music and the interface's included, until `false` resumes them (`Channel.pause`, `unpause`); a pause the game holds itself (its pause menu) stays held. It takes effect on the next client tick, as the game's own pause does. New sounds still start while paused. One switch for all mods: the last call wins. |
| `set_toasts_held` | `fn set_toasts_held (&self, held: bool) -> bool` | Holds new toasts back while `held`, or lets them go: they wait in the toast queue, as vanilla's do while no slot is free (`ToastManager.freeSlotCount`), and show once no enabled mod holds them, instead of being dropped as a canceled `toast_add` drops them. Toasts already on screen go on and leave as usual. Dynamic FPS holds them while the window is in the background. Each mod's hold is its own; a disabled mod's holds nothing. False, changing nothing, outside a mod's call. |
| `shader_pack_active` | `fn shader_pack_active (&self) -> bool` | Whether a shader pack is currently loaded and active (Iris's `IrisApi.isShaderPackInUse()`). `false` on a server, when no renderer exists, or while no shader pack is running. |
| `shader_pack_state` | `fn shader_pack_state (&self) -> Option<ShaderPackState>` | The active shader pack's cloud and shadow-pass state for the current frame, or `None` when no pack is active (the same rule as `Self::shader_pack_active`: `None` on the title screen and while unloading). Taken at the start of each frame's render; read it in the render callbacks to cull shadow-casting geometry against `ShaderPackState::shadow` and to skip clouds when `ShaderPackState::hides_clouds`. Compare `ShaderPackState::generation` each frame to notice a pack loaded, switched or turned off. |
| `sign_editor` | `fn sign_editor (&self) -> Option<SignEditView>` | The sign the sign editor is on, its block position and side; None when no sign editor is open or outside a world. |
| `sound_events` | `fn sound_events (&self) -> Vec<Identifier>` | Every sound event the client's resources have (`SoundManager.getAvailableSounds`). |
| `sound_source_scale` | `fn sound_source_scale (&self, source: SoundSource) -> f32` | A mod's scale over `source`'s volume (`set_sound_source_scale`), 1 when none is set. For a source other than the master, the master's scale is included. |
| `start_local_player_gliding` | `fn start_local_player_gliding (&mut self) -> bool` | For a client mod: sets the local player's fall-flying flag (`LivingEntity.startFallFlying`) from `player_tick_pre`, so the tick's own glide detection sends `START_FALL_FLYING` as a jump-started glide does. This is the raw flag write, not `tryToStartFallFlying`: it does not check the glider, the ground, levitation or a liquid. `false`, and nothing changes, with no player of its own. |
| `text_split` | `fn text_split (&self, text: &Text, width: i32) -> Vec<Text>` | `Font.split(FormattedText, width)`: wrap a component at `width` unscaled GUI pixels. Lines retain styles, resolve the current language and use the same active font as tooltip layout. Newlines force breaks; a width below one follows vanilla's one-pixel minimum, without rejecting input. Available outside drawing. In-memory text reads do not enforce the 512-node serialization depth cap or `ExtraCodecs` profile/property bounds. |
| `text_width` | `fn text_width (&self, text: &Text) -> i32` | `Font.width(FormattedText)`: the width of a component in unscaled GUI pixels, using the current language, styles and resource-pack fonts. Available outside a draw, including client setup and tooltip-component sizing. Like `ClientTooltipComponent.getWidth(Font)`, this measures with the rendering client's active font, not a font chosen by the mod. Build a `Text` component directly and lend it to this read. In-memory text reads do not enforce the 512-node serialization depth cap or `ExtraCodecs` profile/property bounds. |
| `texture_sprites` | `fn texture_sprites (&self, textures: &[& str]) -> Vec<Option<SpriteRef>>` | Published asset/data read: available in Setup, Reloading and World. For a client mod: each texture's sprite by name (`minecraft:block/stone`, `block/stone`) in the blocks atlas, none for one it lacks. The blocks atlas holds the sprites block models name and every sprite a mod's sprite source made in it, though no model names it; a sprite only the items atlas lists is not in it. API 380. |
| `tick_rate` | `fn tick_rate (&self) -> Option<ClientTickRate>` | The tick rate state of the world this window shows (`ClientLevel.tickRateManager()`): its rate, whether it is frozen and the frozen steps left to run, as the server last sent them (`ClientPacketListener.handleTickingState` / `handleTickingStep`). It is there from `Login`, when the client level is made, before the player is placed; none outside a client world (the title screen, before login, or while reconfiguring). |
| `transfer_recipe` | `fn transfer_recipe (&mut self, book_id: i32)` | Move available ingredients for a viewer recipe into the open crafting grid, without requiring the recipe to be learned. The screen shows any refusal. |
