| `transfer_recipe_check` | `fn transfer_recipe_check (&mut self, recipe: &str) -> Option<RecipeTransferCheck>` | What `transfer_recipe_id(recipe, false)` would do now for the recipe with id `recipe` (`minecraft:stick`), worked out by the server without moving anything: the open mod menu's `menu_transfer` part asked first, then the grid, with the player's inventory, the grid and every container a mod lends the grid's recipes (a crafting station's chests, which the client can't see) counted. Any recipe the server knows, whether or not the player's recipe book holds it. For a viewer's "+", to mark the inputs it lacks (`missing_cells`) and, for a mod menu whose part names them, the ones it could craft (`craftable_cells`). The first call for a recipe asks and returns None; the answer comes a moment later and is kept for that recipe while the menu, the inventory and the grid stay as they were. On a remote server, while the answer is wanted, the client asks again once the cached answer is one second old. None outside a screen that takes `transfer_recipe_id` and on a server that doesn't answer it. |
| `transfer_recipe_id` | `fn transfer_recipe_id (&mut self, recipe: &str, max: bool)` | A viewer's "+" for a recipe by its id, into the open menu: a machine's mod menu as well as a crafting grid. The server asks the `menu_transfer` part of the open mod menu's type first, then makes the game's own grid transfer; the screen shows the result as for `transfer_recipe`. `max` moves as many sets as the items allow (Shift). Shown as `NotIntoMenu` with no container screen open, in spectator mode, and on a server that doesn't take viewer transfers. In a solo world or on a LAN host the game is the server: its own player's transfer takes the same path, part first, then the grid. |
| `translate` | `fn translate (&self, key: &str) -> Option<String>` | The selected language's text for a translation key (`Component.translatable`'s lookup), `None` when no language layer defines it. |
| `update_dynamic_texture` | `fn update_dynamic_texture (&mut self, id: &Identifier, width: u32, height: u32, rgba: &[u8] ,) -> Result<(), DynamicTextureError>` | Vanilla's `DynamicTexture.upload` of new pixels: the calling mod's texture `id` takes `rgba`, `width * height` pixels of four bytes, from the next frame on. A different size makes a new texture under the same id, as registering a new `DynamicTexture` under an id does. Writes apply in the order they were made, none merged. Send one when the texture changed, not every frame; for a part of it, use `update_dynamic_texture_region`. |
| `update_dynamic_texture_region` | `fn update_dynamic_texture_region (&mut self, id: &Identifier, origin: [u32; 2], size: [u32; 2], rgba: &[u8] ,) -> Result<(), DynamicTextureError>` | `CommandEncoder.writeToTexture`'s region overload on the calling mod's texture `id`: `rgba`, `size[0] * size[1]` pixels of four bytes, replaces the region of the texture whose top left pixel is `origin`, from the next frame on, applied in the order the writes were made. The rest of the texture is kept. |
| `vanilla_key_mapping` | `fn vanilla_key_mapping (&self, name: &str) -> Result<VanillaKeyMappingRef, MappingError>` | Resolve a vanilla mapping name such as `key.jump`, not a binding name such as `key.keyboard.space`. Keep the handle for subsequent reads and writes. An unknown name returns `MappingError::Unknown`; a host or phase without client input returns `MappingError::Unavailable`. |
| `view_hit` | `fn view_hit (&self) -> Option<(f32, Hit)>` | The first block along the view across loaded chunks only, and its distance; `None` on a miss. The shape clip does not depend on how far away other chunks are loaded. |
| `visit_text_lines` | `fn visit_text_lines (&self, text: &Text, spec: TextLayoutSpec, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (TextLine<'_>) ,) -> Result<TextLayoutSize, TextLayoutError>` | Wrap with the active font as in `Font.split`, and lend each placed line in logical order. Returns the laid-out dimensions, even outside a draw. The wrapping width and all line positions and returned dimensions are in scaled GUI pixels. Clone a line's text to keep it after the visitor returns. Invalid layout geometry (including a non-finite scaled result) refuses before visiting any line; no line or character output cap applies. Use `GuiCanvas::text_with_word_wrap` for drawing a label without replaying its lines. |
| `visit_text_visual_order` | `fn visit_text_visual_order (&self, text: &Text, visit: &mut dyn FnMut (VisualTextCharacterRef<'_>) -> bool ,) -> bool` | `Component.getVisualOrderText()`: current-language, styled characters in visual order, including bidi shaping and mirroring. Available outside drawing, including client setup. Sink indices are UTF-16 offsets within the current directional and effective-style run, not within the input component tree. Independently set equal styles do not restart a run. Custom click IDs compare by namespace and path for grouping, without rewriting each character's returned click event. A resolved color is returned as uppercase `#RRGGBB`. Action keybinds resolve to their assigned keys as in `text_string`. In-memory text reads do not enforce the 512-node serialization depth cap or `ExtraCodecs` profile/property bounds. No NeoForge hook counterpart is documented. The visitor returns true to continue and false to stop. The result is true if the whole sequence was visited. Use `character.to_owned()` to retain it. |
| `window_focused` | `fn window_focused (&self) -> bool` | The last window focus state reported by the window system. |
| `window_hovered` | `fn window_hovered (&self) -> bool` | Whether the pointer is over the game window, focused or not, as the window system last reported its enter and leave. Vanilla 26.3 has a cursor-enter event only; the leave comes from the window system. With `Self::window_focused` false, true means the window is hovered in the background. False before the pointer has entered the window. |
| `window_occluded` | `fn window_occluded (&self) -> bool` | Whether the window is fully hidden (occluded): covered by other windows, on another desktop, or minimized, where the window system reports it (some report only minimizing, and some nothing). A hidden window's frames are not seen, so a mod may draw less. |
| `window_size` | `fn window_size (&self) -> Option<[u32; 2]>` | The window's width and height in framebuffer pixels (`Window.getWidth` and `Window.getHeight`), whatever the GUI scale; none on a server or when no window exists. Readable at any time: `window_resize` reports this same size, so the two always agree. |
